# DECISIONS

Design decisions for Hungry God Inc. The spec (`docs/spec.md`) is the floor; everything here fills a "To decide" or thin spot.
Numbers marked *(draft)* are starting points: the sim builder and the balance piece may tune them against the bots, and must record the final value here.
Builders: append your own decisions under your piece's heading at the bottom.

## 0. Architecture (binding for every piece)

- **Stack:** TypeScript (strict) + Vite + Canvas 2D for the world, DOM/CSS overlay for HUD and panels. No PixiJS (one renderer, offscreen-canvas caching, composite-op lighting). No external assets: no image, audio or web-font files. Fonts are system stacks only — display: `"Bitstream Charter", "Iowan Old Style", "Palatino Linotype", Georgia, serif`; UI: `Inter, system-ui, "Segoe UI", sans-serif`.
- **Logical resolution:** 1600×900 (16:9), letterboxed and scaled to the window, devicePixelRatio-aware.
- **Layout / ownership** (one owner per directory; do not edit outside yours without the orchestrator saying so):
  - `src/sim/**` — deterministic rules core. No DOM, no `Math.random`, no `Date`. Runs in Node.
  - `src/bots/**`, `scripts/**` — bot players, headless runner, balance report.
  - `src/render/**` — canvas art: `art/` primitives, `mine/`, `town/`, `fog.ts`, `lighting.ts`, `camera.ts`, `particles.ts`.
  - `src/audio/**` — WebAudio synth (music beds, ambience, sfx).
  - `src/app/**` — boot, game loop, scene manager, input, debug API.
  - `src/scenes/**` — `cutscene/`, `bonus/`, `whisper/`, `watershed/`, `shade/`, `ending/`.
  - `src/ui/**` — HUD, panels, cards, tooltips, modals (DOM).
  - `tests/**` — vitest unit tests. `e2e/**` — Playwright scripts. `screenshots/**` — their output. `dev/**` — per-piece harness pages.
- **Sim API** (`src/sim/index.ts`): `createGame(seed, opts) → GameState` (plain JSON-serializable data), `legalActions(state) → Action[]`, `applyAction(state, action) → { state, events }`, `endTurn(state) → { state, events }`. Pure: same seed + same actions ⇒ identical state. All randomness comes from a seeded PRNG stored in the state. The UI and the bots use this same API and nothing else.
- **Map contract:** the sim owns the zone *graph* (ids, kinds, adjacency); the renderer owns the *geometry*, keyed by the same zone ids (§6, §7).
- **Debug/test hooks:** `window.__hg` (state, seed, scene jump, `advance(ms)`), URL params `?seed=N&scene=<name>&mute=1&fast=1`. Every scene must be reachable directly for screenshots.
- **Agents do not run git.** The orchestrator commits. Typecheck/test only your own directories when another piece is mid-flight.

## 1. Fiction

> **Superseded in part by §13 (user, 2026-11-23): plain names, fewer names, one seamless world.** Where this section and §13 disagree, §13 wins.

- **You:** the Hungry One, a god that fell and was buried. A mine was dug into your grave; souls of the dead harden into violet **soul crystals** around you.
- **The people:** the **Ghelm** — hunched, three-eyed burrowers with a bioluminescent **crest**. Crest colour is gameplay: pale teal = unconverted, violet = your follower (brighter with Devotion), gold = sworn to the Kiln. Never human-looking.
- **Opposing gods (factions):**
  - **Sol-Ometh, the Unblinking Kiln** — cult faction (the **Kiln Choir**). Affinity: **Crusade**. Its shrines burn **gold**; gold light is the enemy's light. The gold shrine in `mine.png` is a Kiln shrine.
  - **Vash-Tal, the Counting Iron** — government faction (overseers, the **Tally**, the garrison). Affinity: **Invasion** (lockdown, occupation). It runs the war against the other civilization (conscription events).
- **Colour language:** violet = player, gold = Kiln, cold steel-blue = Tally, teal/ochre = the neutral world.

## 2. Turn structure

One turn = one **night**. Phases:
1. **Dusk (income):** Nether for the turn is computed (§3); crystals smuggled by followers arrive; one miracle hand of 3 is dealt (§8).
2. **Night (player):** any number of Nether actions (Influence, Direct), at most **one miracle**, eat crystals freely, commit evil acts. Then End Turn.
3. **Dawn (world):** followers carry out orders; people move; Devotion drifts; Hunger and Malice rise; Suspicion updates; factions act; at most one **event** fires (some with a choice); XP is awarded; level-ups resolve.
- Nether is per-turn income. Unspent Nether carries over at 50 %, capped at one turn's income *(draft)*.

## 3. Spec formulas (exact; unit-tested)

- `V(D) = 5x + 25(x − 0.4)³ + 1.6`, `x = D/100` (V(0)=0, V(40)=3.6, V(100)=12).
- `Gain = Base / (1 + 10(x − 0.4)²)` (0→100 ≈ 193 base points; last 10 ≈ 40).
- `Faith = Σ V(Dᵢ) / N^0.15` (N = follower headcount; groups count every member at the group's average Devotion).
- `Nether = Faith × 1.5^(L−1) × (4/3)^floor((L−1)/3)`; multipliers ×4.5 at L4, ×20.3 at L7, ×91.1 at L10, ×410 at L13, ≈×923 at L15.
- `InfluenceCost = 6 · S^0.85 · T · (1 + Resistance/50)`; `DirectCost = S^0.85 · T`; T: Mortal 1, Elite 4.5, Blessed 20, Demigod 91, God 410. S = headcount of a group, or 25 % of a leader's command (individual Mortal: S = 1). **Approved as drafted.**
- Miracle cost = base (2–6 crystals) × stage tier T (Whisper 1, Shade 4.5, rounded).
- **Ember:** with zero followers Faith is 0, so the god has a small flat Nether floor, `Ember = 8` per turn *(draft)*, added to the formula. It is not part of Faith.

## 4. God stats

- **Divinity:** L1–3 Whisper, L4–6 Shade, L7 = Omen 1 (reaching it ends the build scope). XP *(draft)*: new follower +5 (group: +1 per 4 heads); Devotion crossing 25/50/75/100: +2/+4/+8/+16; miracle cast: +cost; crystal eaten: +1; zone made Active: +10; evil act: +malice vented/10; shrine desecrated: +25; Elite converted: +40. Thresholds *(draft)*: L2 60, L3 150, L4 300, L5 700, L6 1400, L7 2600. **Watershed gate:** L4 also needs a follower to have reached **the Lift** (the thread reaches the surface). **Ending gate:** L7 also needs the **City Gate** district Active.
- **Presence:** starts 1; +1 per level; some bonuses add. Reach = how many zones beyond your Active territory you may target (Presence 1 = adjacent only). Each point adds +4 % to conversion odds.
- **Conversion odds** *(draft)*: `p = clamp(0.55 + 0.04·Presence − Resistance/100 + traitFit(±0.15) + 0.05·followersInZone(max 0.15) − publicSuspicion/200 − (Malice ≥ 70 ? 0.15 : 0), 0.05, 0.95)`. A failed whisper still lowers that target's Resistance by 8 (never a wasted action) and adds +2 public Suspicion.
- **Ichor (HP):** max `10 + 2·(L−1)`. Lost only by losing Influence (a named follower killed or purged −1; per 10 heads of a group lost −1; an Active zone lost −2) or by faction weapons (Rite of Unbinding −3; Crusade/Invasion strikes). Regained only by **Gorging** (eat 5 crystals in one turn → +1) and one miracle. **Ichor 0 = the god dissolves = defeat.**
- **Hunger** (0–100): starts 30; rises `5 + L` per turn *(draft)*; each crystal eaten −12. ≥70 **Starving:** Nether income −25 %. ≥100 **Ravenous:** the god devours its most devoted follower (they die; Suspicion +10 public), Hunger resets to 60. <30 **Sated:** miracle power +10 %.
- **Malice** (0–100): starts 10; rises `3 + L` per turn *(draft)*; vented by evil acts and evil-tagged miracles. ≥70 **Seething:** conversion −15 %, public Suspicion +2/turn. ≥100 **Outburst:** an uncontrolled atrocity in a random Active zone (followers there are hurt or killed, all Suspicion tracks +20), Malice resets to 40.
- **Influence (world value):** each zone has Control 0–100 (share of its people who follow you, weighted by Devotion). Map Influence = average Control. Control ≥ 50 makes a zone **Active territory**.

## 5. People

- Stats: Devotion, Strength, Intelligence, Cunning, Health (0–100). Hidden **Resistance** 0–60 derived from trait, role and nearby Kiln light.
- **Roles (Whisper):** Digger (Smuggle crystals), Hauler (Scout/Carry far), Sorter (Skim the sorting floor — more crystals, more risk), Kiln-Keeper (Preach falsely / Desecrate), Guard (Intimidate, Shield followers), Overseer (Elite — cannot be influenced in Whisper; source of government Suspicion).
- **Roles (Shade, added):** Smelter, Hawker, Grower, Soldier, Clerk, Lector (priest), plus named Elites: **Magistrate**, **Garrison Captain**, **High Lector**, **Guildmistress**, **Smuggler-Queen**.
- **Traits** (one each): Greedy, Pious, Grieving, Ambitious, Fearful, Curious, Bitter, Loyal. Each trait has miracles that work best on it and a whisper-fit modifier.
- **Orders (Direct):** Smuggle/Skim (crystals), Preach (Devotion of others in zone), Pray (own Devotion), Scout (move, reveal), Lie Low (lower Suspicion), Evil acts (§9). Role decides which are available.
- **Zooming out (merge rules):** at the watershed, followers with Devotion ≥ 70 (max 4, highest first) stay named as **Apostles**; every other mine follower merges into the group **The Deep Congregation** with averaged stats. In Shade, people come as **groups** (crews of 8–40) with averaged stats; only Elites and Apostles are individuals. A group converts as a unit (Devotion is its average).

## 6. Whisper stage — the mine (zone graph, binding ids)

> §13: the ids and adjacency below stay binding **as code ids**. The "name" column is no longer shown to the player — the mine is one place with one name.

| id | name | kind |
|---|---|---|
| `grave` | The Hollow | god's grave; always Active at start; crystal heart |
| `heartvein` | Heart Vein | rich crystal gallery |
| `cartway` | The Cartway | central rail junction |
| `westdrift` | West Drift | tunnel |
| `kilnshrine` | Kiln Shrine | gold shrine; raises Resistance nearby until desecrated |
| `bunks` | The Bunks | sleeping quarters; many people at dawn |
| `overseer` | Overseer's Post | Tally post; Elite overseer |
| `lift` | The Lift | shaft to the surface; watershed gate |
| `sorting` | Sorting Floor | crystal carts and crates |
| `eastworks` | East Workings | crystal face |
| `lowgallery` | Low Gallery | southern rail loop |
| `sump` | The Sump | flooded teal pools; hidden cache |

Adjacency: grave–heartvein, grave–cartway, heartvein–eastworks, cartway–westdrift, cartway–sorting, cartway–lowgallery, cartway–bunks, westdrift–kilnshrine, westdrift–bunks, bunks–overseer, overseer–lift, sorting–eastworks, sorting–lift, lowgallery–sump, sump–eastworks.
About 26 named Ghelm. Layout follows `mine.png`: grave centre, Kiln shrine far west, lift north-east, rail loop south.

## 7. Shade stage — the town (binding ids)

> §13: the town is called **Deepwell**; the 13 ids below stay as code ids for unnamed blocks, grouped into 5 named districts; the town is part of the same world as the mine from turn 1.

`minehead` (your glowing point; Active at start), `slagrows` (shanties), `haulyard`, `smeltery`, `market` (Bone Market), `wellsquare`, `sporegardens`, `gravefield` (where souls crystallize), `highwarrens` (Elite housing), `tallyhall` (Magistrate), `garrison`, `kilntemple` (gold temple, bottom-right as in `town.png`), `citygate` (road to the city; ending gate).
Adjacency: minehead–slagrows, minehead–haulyard, slagrows–wellsquare, slagrows–gravefield, haulyard–smeltery, haulyard–market, smeltery–market, market–wellsquare, wellsquare–sporegardens, wellsquare–highwarrens, market–tallyhall, tallyhall–garrison, tallyhall–highwarrens, highwarrens–kilntemple, sporegardens–kilntemple, garrison–citygate, market–citygate.
The town opens mostly Void and Sensed-gray; only `minehead` is Active.

## 8. Miracles

One per turn. Each Dusk deals a **hand of 3** from the pool for your Divinity (rarity weights shift with level); pay crystals to cast one. Faith sets power: `power = 1 + log10(1 + Faith)/2` *(draft)*.
- **Whisper pool (T = 1):** Dream Directive (3; chosen followers infiltrate an adjacent zone: reveal it, plant Devotion), Bless Their Bodies (4; +Devotion and +effectiveness), Soothing Murmur (2; lower Suspicion), Crystal Bloom (3; a vein yields more for 3 turns), Night Terror (3, evil; lowers Resistance in a zone, vents Malice, raises Suspicion), Vein Sight (2; reveal 2 zones), Borrowed Tongue (4; guaranteed whisper on one Mortal), Marrow Feast (5; restore 1 Ichor, lower Hunger), Black Dew (6, rare, evil; snuff the Kiln light in a zone for 4 turns).
- **Shade pool (T = 4.5; watershed upgrades):** Mass Dream (Dream Directive upgraded: a group infiltrates an institution), Bless the Congregation, Veil of Shadows (hide a district from inquisitors), Eclipse Sermon (district-wide Devotion), Possession (act through an Elite for a turn), Plague of Doubt (evil; strips Kiln devotion), False Relic, Soul Harvest (evil; gravefield crystals), Thread of Night (extend reach).

## 9. Evil acts (vent Malice)

Ordered through followers or cast as evil miracles. Each vents Malice, pays out, and raises Suspicion: Terrorize (zone Resistance down), Sabotage (cave-in / fire: removes an inspector or guard, blocks a zone), Sacrifice (a non-follower dies: crystals + XP), Desecrate (Kiln shrine → turns violet; removes its ward; big cult Suspicion), Frame (shift Suspicion onto a rival).

## 10. Suspicion and pushback

Three tracks, 0–100: **Cult** (Kiln Choir), **Government** (Tally), **Public**. Public feeds the other two and lowers conversion odds.
Thresholds *(draft)*: 25 **Wary** (inspections; Resistance up), 50 **Alarmed** (an **Inquisitor**/**Auditor** unit enters the map and hunts toward your followers), 75 **Hostile** (purges: followers killed or broken, Ichor damage), 100 **Crusade** (cult) / **Invasion-Lockdown** (government): a multi-turn assault on your Active zones.
Whisper keeps pushback light (inspections, one overseer crackdown). **Shade is the first serious pushback:** Inquisitors walk the districts, the High Lector performs the Rite of Unbinding, the garrison occupies districts, and a Crusade is survivable only if you prepared (Elites turned, Veil of Shadows, guards, Lie Low).
Converted Elites blunt a track: Magistrate (government decays), Captain (no purges from the garrison), High Lector (Crusade cannot be called).

## 11. Events

At most one per Dawn, drawn by stage and state (≈35 total): dream-talkers, cave-ins, inspections, conscription for the war, a death in the tunnels (a soul crystallizes), a Kiln feast day, smugglers' offers, informers, omens. About half offer a 2-option choice. Events guarantee no dead turn.

## 12. Run start and end

- **Cutscene (final script lives in the cutscene piece):** five beats — the throne among stars and the fall; ages pass and a civilization builds over the grave; souls sink and harden into crystal; the god reaches up and fails; one thread of shadow slips toward the surface. God alone, no miner. The last shot **is** the mine view centred on the Hollow; the HUD fades in over it with no cut.
- **Starting bonus:** 3 offered at random from a pool of 9: Lingering Throne (+1 Presence), Hoard of the Grave (+8 crystals), Patient Hunger (Hunger −30 % rise), Sweet Voice (+10 % conversion), Old Scars (+4 max Ichor), Cruel Delight (evil acts give double XP), Deep Roots (Heart Vein starts Active), Quiet Rot (Suspicion gains −25 %), First Dreamer (start with one Devotion-30 follower).
- **Win (this build):** reach Omen 1 (L7) → ending beat: the camera lifts from Sallowreach, the road runs to a vast gray city on the horizon, a second gold light answers. **Lose:** Ichor 0.
- **Pacing targets:** Shade reached in 15–25 turns; Shade finished in 40–60 turns total.

## 13. Open world and naming revision (user, 2026-11-23 — binding; overrides anything above that conflicts)

User: "The scope of the game is more open world 2d, not just a flat progression line." / "I also don't like how everything is named. It should just be a mine with a name." Clarified: **one seamless world**; "mix of plainer names and less names. A town can have a few districts and everyone can have a name just not like floating on their heads."

### 13.1 One seamless world
- **One map, one play scene, one camera.** The mine sits inside the town on one continuous 2D world. The player pans and zooms freely at all times, from a close-up of a single miner out to the whole town. There is no "mine screen" and "town screen" and no scene switch between Whisper and Shade.
- **World coordinates = town units.** Town ≈ 6400×3600 units *(draft; the world-render builder may adjust)*. The mine interior (2400×1400 mine units) is a **nested detail layer** anchored at the mine's pit in the town, drawn at 1/k scale (k ≈ 6 *(draft)*, so the mine covers ≈ 400×233 town units and reads as a small glowing point when the whole town is on screen). Mine-local = (world − mineOrigin) × k. Rationale: keeps figures the same on-screen size in the mine close-up and in a town close-up (the sprite scale of `town.png`), and keeps baked layers within memory. Miners and townsfolk are never on screen at the same magnification; the lift is the link.
- **Level of detail, no cut:** zoomed in on the pit you see the underground cutaway (the current mine view, surrounded by black rock). Zooming out, the interior continuously shrinks and cross-fades into a dark pit with a violet glow ("the glowing point") while the surface fades in around it. Zooming in reverses it. This cross-fade is what the watershed pull-back plays.
- **Fog is one map over the whole world** (mine areas + town blocks): Active / Sensed / Void as before, no visible band at any boundary. At the start only the grave is Active; the whole town is Void (pure black) — zoomed out, the player sees a black map with one faint violet point. Fog opens wherever followers go.
- **Renderer:** `src/render/world/` exposes one `WorldRenderer` (same interface as `src/render/types.ts`, additive changes only) that composes `mine/` and `town/`. Scenes talk only to it.

### 13.2 Progression is not a line
- **The sim world is one graph from turn 1:** the 12 mine areas + the 13 town blocks, joined `lift`–`minehead`. No map swap, no `watershed` phase that halts play. Followers can be sent anywhere they can walk at any time; Presence reach is measured on the same graph.
- **Stages change what you may influence, not where you are.** Whisper (L1–3): individuals only (Mortals) — the miners, and a few lone townsfolk in each town block (beggar, gravedigger, night trader…). Shade (L4–6): groups (crews of 8–40) and Elites become targets everywhere. Leaving the mine early is a real choice: more people and richer pickings, but public and Council eyes; staying is safe but the veins run out.
- **Watershed is a moment, not a gate:** reaching L4 (needs a follower to have reached the surface) plays the pull-back in the same scene — camera eases out, the mine becomes the glowing point, the town shows mostly gray and black — then play continues. Mine followers below Devotion 70 merge into one group, **the Congregation**; up to 4 with Devotion ≥ 70 stay individual as **Apostles**.
- **Several routes to the end.** The ending still needs L7 and the gate block Active, but there must be at least three distinct, viable ways to get there — through the Keep (turn or break the Council's people), through Temple Hill (turn the High Priest or defile the temple), or through the Market (win the crowds) — plus optional side objectives in every district. Bots must show each route winning; no single route dominates. The ending beat may tint its tease by route.
- **Scenes:** `title` → `cutscene` → `play` → `ending` / `defeat`. The starting-bonus pick is an overlay on `play`'s first frame (the cutscene's last shot); Whisper and Shade are both `play`; the watershed is a camera sequence inside `play`. Music scene names stay (`whisper`, `watershed`, `shade`…).

### 13.3 Names: plainer and fewer
- **Nothing floats over heads.** No name labels, no area labels in the world. Names appear only in the inspector, tooltips, cards and the log. Allegiance is read from crest colour and shape.
- **The mine is one place with one name: Deepwell Mine.** Its 12 areas keep their code ids but have no names and no map labels. Where text must point at one (an order target, a log line) it uses a plain lower-case descriptor: your grave, the crystal gallery, the rail junction, the west tunnel, the shrine, the bunks, the overseer's post, the lift, the sorting floor, the east face, the south loop, the flooded pools.
- **The town is Deepwell**, with **five named districts**: **Miners' Rows** (`minehead`, `slagrows`, `gravefield`), **the Works** (`haulyard`, `smeltery`), **the Market** (`market`, `wellsquare`, `sporegardens`), **Temple Hill** (`highwarrens`, `kilntemple`), **the Keep** (`tallyhall`, `garrison`, `citygate`). Blocks inside a district are unnamed (descriptors: the pit head, the shanties, the graveyard, the haul yard, the smelter, the market square, the well, the gardens, the high houses, the temple, the council hall, the barracks, the town gate). District names may appear as faint map lettering only when zoomed out, and in the inspector.
- **You:** the Hungry God. **The people:** no species name in player-facing text — miners, townsfolk, people. (Code may keep `ghelm`.)
- **Factions (the three Suspicion tracks):** **the Temple** (cult; serves an opposing god called **the Flame**; gold; calls a Crusade), **the Council** (government; serves **the Crown**; steel-blue; answers with a Lockdown/occupation), **the Public**. Retired names: Sol-Ometh, Unblinking Kiln, Kiln Choir, Vash-Tal, Counting Iron, Tally, Sallowreach, Ghelm, the Hollow, Heart Vein, Cartway… ("Kiln" → "the Flame"/"shrine"/"temple"; "Tally" → "the Council"; Lector → priest; Auditor → inspector).
- **Roles:** Digger, Hauler, Sorter, Shrine-keeper, Guard, Overseer; town: Smelter, Trader, Farmer, Soldier, Clerk, Priest. Elites: the Magistrate, the Garrison Captain, the High Priest, the Guild Master, the Smuggler Boss.
- **People:** everyone has one short plain given name (one word, one or two syllables, easy to say: Odo, Mara, Ket, Bram, Ilsa, Tov, Nessa…); Elites are "Magistrate Orla" style. No surnames, no apostrophes.
- **Miracles, orders, events, bonuses:** keep approved names (Dream Directive, Bless Their Bodies); any text that used a retired name is reworded plainly (e.g. Deep Roots: "the crystal gallery beside your grave starts Active"; Black Dew: "snuff the Flame's light in an area").
- Spec terms stay as they are: Divinity, Presence, Faith, Nether, Ichor, Soul Crystals, Hunger, Malice, Influence, Suspicion, Whisper, Shade, Mortal, Elite.

### 13.4 User note on quality (2026-11-23)
"Visuals and UI are horrible but that's fine for the time being." Structure first (seamless world, naming); the visual and UI quality bar (≥ 8 from a blind critic, `mine.png` as target) still has to be met before the build is done.

### 13.5 User verdict on the round-3 mine shots (2026-11-23 — binding; corrects an orchestrator misreading)
The orchestrator first misread the user's comment and declared the shrine's stacked teal stones the benchmark. **That was wrong and is withdrawn.** The user: "That's a shit benchmark and i meant the other way around. 3's green stacked rocks look like shit compared to 2 which looks like shit."
- **The stacked green boulders around the shrine are the worst-looking thing in the mine.** The overseer's-post close-up is better than before but still bad. Neither is acceptable.
- **The only benchmark is `docs/refs/mine.png`.** What it does that ours does not (orchestrator's reading): rock is a continuous, sculpted cliff wall around the tunnels — faceted planes with soft painterly shading and fine hatching, lit by the real light sources — not piles of separately outlined cartoon boulders with uniform thick lines; floors are worn, toned surfaces, not flat fills with crack lines.
- **Never use piles of outlined blobs/boulders as the rock language** (mine or town). The art pipeline for rock, ground and buildings is being re-decided with the user; until then builders should not invest in more boulder-style art.

### 13.6 The god's thread (orchestrator)
The "one thread of shadow" that ends the cutscene stays in the game as the god's resting reach: one living strand of shadow with a violet inner glow rising from the fissure and trailing off toward the surface. It is drawn by a standalone function in `src/render/mine/thread.ts` (owned by the cutscene piece) that the cutscene and the play scene both call right after the world render with the same inputs, so the hand-over frame stays identical.

### 13.7 It is a slice; late balance is not the goal (user, 2026-11-23 — binding)
User: "it's not meant to be balanced late. Make a slice. I only scoped enough features for half of the first few days, to be honest."
- **Deliver a vertical slice:** title → cutscene → starting bonus → play in the one seamless world (mine close-up out to the town) → the Shade unlock with its pull-back → a basic Shade in the town → the ending tease. What the player touches in the first sessions must be good: look, feel, UI, the early decisions.
- **Balance effort goes to the early game** — roughly the first 15–20 turns, up to and just past reaching Shade: the first nights always offer an affordable, interesting choice; costs sit in the 15–30 % band there; pressure is felt; bonuses are comparable; texts state their numbers. That part is tuned with bots.
- **Late game: must run, need not be balanced.** After the Shade unlock the game must keep working to a win or loss with no stall, crash or dead turn, but no tuning rounds are spent on late Nether surplus, late difficulty or a 40–60-turn curve. Shade content stays lean (groups and Elites targetable, existing pushback scaled up, a few town events, the gate ending) — no large new systems.
- `BALANCE.md` reports the bot runs honestly, including where the late game is loose, and says so.

---
### 13.8 The binding reading of `mine.png` (orchestrator, after two critics contradicted each other)
`handoffs/ART-REFERENCE.md` (with 3× crops in `handoffs/ref/`) is the single reading of the reference that every art builder and art critic works from. In short: wall **faces** exist only on the north side of floors and wrap partly down the east/west sides; they are tall cliff faces of big chunky slabs/blocks (not round beans, not a polygon mosaic) with scree at the foot. The south side of a floor has no wall — just an inked contour and thin lip, then the dark rock top. Rock tops (islands) are a narrow dark-teal margin fading smoothly to near-black. Floors are calm, dark teal-gray, sparse in detail, warm only inside warm light. Light is muted gold-ochre, never lime. The reference's soft glow around the outer contour is **not** copied: the owner's rule "No visible band should exist" wins. Earlier instructions that said "walls 2–3 boulders thick on every side" (orchestrator brief for round 5A, critic r3 change 2) are withdrawn.

### 13.9 Art method verdict (user, 2026-11-23 — binding)
On the round-2 terrain shots: "the sculpted terrain is better fundamentally but lacks the detail and the polish relative to the ref image." → The sculpted lit terrain (`src/render/terrain/`) is the base for ground art in the mine and later the town; the work left is detail and polish against `mine.png`, plus the bake budget. The vector rock in `mine/bake.ts` (track B) finishes its running round for comparison and as fallback; its props, figures, dressing, fog, effects and engine work are kept either way. Integration step once the terrain passes its critic: the mine renderer draws the terrain's `lit` image under its props (and the town uses it through `town/painters.ts`).

### 13.10 Clarity pass (user, 2026-11-23 — binding; overrides earlier rules that conflict; **parts A and B are replaced by §14**)
The owner played the mine stage: "Some of the Got its don't disappear. Some things are too confusing. Tooltips and stats should be more simplified. My area that's glowing is literally staying the same and my guys are just sitting still. I don't really get it. Area stuff should make more sense." The game must be understandable without reading: one-sentence rules, visible cause and effect, few numbers on screen.

**A. Areas — one rule the player can say out loud**
1. **Lit (Active, colour):** your grave, and any area with at least one of your followers in it. The moment the first person in a gray area turns to you, or a follower walks in, the light visibly spreads into that area.
2. **Rooted:** when at least half the people who live/work in an area follow you, the area takes root — it stays lit even when nobody is there, and your violet veins show in its ground. Rooted areas are lost only through an announced enemy action (a purge/cleansing), never silently. The old devotion-weighted Control, the 50-point claim, "slipping" and grace dawns are removed from the rules and from every text. (Devotion still matters for the person: loyalty, yield, defection.)
3. **Seen (Sensed, gray):** areas touching a lit area, and any area a follower has ever stood in. **Unknown (Void, black):** the rest.
4. **Reach:** you can whisper to anyone in a lit area or in a gray area touching one. That is the whole targeting rule.
5. **Growth is the count of lit areas.** The stat formerly shown as Presence is shown as "Areas lit: N" (it still feeds reach/odds as the spec says). "Influence %" leaves the stat bar; progress lives in the goal panel.
6. An area's panel says, in this order: lit / rooted / seen; "3 of 7 here follow you" with a bar and the half-way mark; what is special about the place in one line.

**B. Followers are never idle**
1. Every follower always has a task. A new follower starts on **Pray** (feeds you Nether). A task is paid for once, when you give it, and stays until it finishes or you change it. There is no per-night re-ordering and no "idle follower" state.
2. Each night's end shows every follower doing their task (kneeling, walking the route, talking to neighbours, hiding) and what it produced, as a brief mark at their feet and a line in the chronicle.
3. **Few verbs first.** At the start a follower has four tasks: **Pray**, **Go to…** (walk to an area you can see; lights it on arrival), **Preach** (makes people in the same area easier to turn), **Hide** (lowers the heat on them). Further tasks unlock with Divinity or a role, one at a time, each introduced by one line when it first appears: level II — the role's job (Hauler: smuggle crystals; Sorter: skim; Guard: shield); level III — evil acts, shown only once Malice matters. Anything not unlocked is not shown at all.
4. The nightly decisions that remain are the interesting ones: whom to whisper to, where to send people, which miracle, what to risk.

**C. Stats — three groups, shown when relevant**
- **You:** Ichor (life; at 0 the run ends) as pips; Hunger as one meter (eat crystals to lower it).
- **Spend:** Nether with "+N a night"; Crystals.
- **Danger:** one indicator — the hottest of Temple / Council / Public by name with a word (Calm, Wary, Alarmed, Hostile); it opens to the three tracks.
- Malice appears the first time it matters (with a one-line introduction), not before. Faith is folded into the Nether tooltip's details. Level and XP are one line ("Whisper I → II").
- No stat is on screen before the player has met it; each first appearance comes with one sentence.

**D. Tooltips and texts**
- At most two short lines: what it does in plain words; then the cost and the one number that matters. Odds as a word plus a percent ("Likely · 72 %").
- Full numbers live one level down ("Details", on hold/tap), generated from the same constants the code uses (the earlier rule "texts state real numbers" is met there).
- No jargon in the first layer: say "area", "follower", "turn to you", "the surface", "heat"; never "zone", "foothold", "Control", "Presence", "Influence", "watershed" (the stage names Whisper and Shade stay as titles).

**E. Goal**
- One goal panel in plain words, at most two lines with progress: "Reach the surface — light a path to the lift (2 areas to go)" and "Grow stronger — Whisper IV (120 / 400)". Clicking a line moves the camera to the next step and pulses that area's ground.

**F. Hints**
- One hint at a time. A hint goes away when its "Got it" is clicked, when the player does what it describes, or on Esc — always — and never comes back unless "Show hints again" is chosen in Settings. A hint that fails to dismiss is a blocking bug.

### 13.11 The people must feel alive (user, 2026-11-23 — binding)
Owner: "the miners don't feel like real people they kind of just fucking stand there it doesn't feel immersive at all and they look like crap."
- **Look:** people are drawn like the figures in `mine.png` (6× crops: `handoffs/ref/ref-6x-figures-*.png`): small, lanky mine folk with pale round heads, thin limbs, work clothes and tools, in gestural working poses, inked like the rest of the art. They are small relative to the space (a corridor is 3–5 figures wide; a wall face about two figures tall). The chunky creature design ("Ghelm" figures at about three times that scale) is retired; the code id `ghelm` may stay. No species name in player text (§13.3).
- **Life:** nobody stands still. Every person is always doing something that fits who and where they are: swinging a pick at a crystal face, pushing a cart along the rails, carrying crates, sorting, filling lamps, eating, sleeping in the bunks, praying at the shrine, guards walking a beat, pairs talking, someone warming hands at a brazier. They walk between spots, turn to face what they do, and react: a head lifts at a whisper, a shiver, a kneel when they turn; nearby people glance over. Followers carry a quiet tell (a violet glint, a small secret gesture), not a floating mark.
- **Tasks show:** a follower's task (§13.10 B) is visible in the world — kneeling to pray, walking the route to another area, talking to neighbours when preaching, keeping to the shadows when hiding. Travel between areas is a visible walk along the floor.
- **Rules:** the sim's area for a person is authoritative; movement inside an area is cosmetic and deterministic from the seed and the clock. Nothing floats over heads. Allegiance reads from clothing, headgear and lamp colour at default zoom. Sensed areas show people as dim gray figures still moving; Void shows nothing.
- One people module (`src/render/people/`) serves the mine now and the town later (roles, crowds, Elites).

## 14. Gameplay loop v2 (user, 2026-11-23 — binding; replaces §13.10 A–B and every earlier rule about areas, orders and visibility that conflicts)

Owner, after playing: "Make it feel far more lively and have people go to the surface, allow you to zoom out, and have more shit colored … anywhere where your influence has reached and permeated enough should be colored, right? That doesn't mean a dude has to be there, let alone the fact that dudes don't even color shit, let alone the fact that you can't make dudes explore gray spaces … Rewrite the entire gameplay loop. Remake the whole feel. Where is the shot visual loop?"
The orchestrator's §13.10 A rule ("an area is lit while a follower stands in it") was wrong and is withdrawn. Spec formulas and stats stay exact (unit-tested); everything around them is rebuilt for feel.

### 14.1 Fantasy and feel
You are a buried, starving god. Your influence is a living **stain of colour** that seeps through rock and into people. The world is alive without you — shifts change, carts roll, the lift runs, people eat, argue, pray, go home to the surface — and every night you bend a little more of it. The player should always be watching something move and something change colour.

### 14.2 Colour is influence (the stain)
- Every area has a **Hold** from 0 to 100 %: how much of it your influence has soaked. **The coloured part of an area on screen is its Hold**: colour creeps across the ground from where it comes in (from neighbouring coloured ground, from your grave, from where followers spend their time), with living veins at its front. No follower has to be standing there.
- **Each dawn Hold changes, visibly:** it rises from (a) your grave, into the areas around it; (b) every follower who spent time in the area that day — more with Devotion (use the spec's V(D)) and with numbers; (c) preaching and some miracles; (d) **seepage from neighbouring areas that are more soaked** (so colour permeates onward on its own). It is pushed back by wards (the shrine until desecrated), by guards, overseers and priests present, and by purges. Tuning targets: one follower colours a fresh area by roughly a quarter to a third a day; two or three finish it in two days; seepage alone moves about a tenth a day; with decent play most of the mine is coloured by about turn 15–20.
- **Yours (Hold 100):** the area is rooted — fully coloured, veined, counts in full, gives its bonus (XP, and what the place offers: a seam, a cache, a route). It is lost only by being pushed back over days, which the player sees receding.
- **Seen (gray):** any area next to coloured ground, and any area a follower has ever explored. **Unknown (black):** the rest.
- **Reach:** you can whisper to anyone standing on coloured ground, next to it, or beside one of your followers.
- **Presence** (spec: reach and odds) = the total of Hold across areas (in "areas' worth"). The goal panel shows it as "Mine 23 % yours".

### 14.3 The world lives: shifts and the surface
- A turn is one day: **Night** (you act; the night shift works, the rest sleep in the bunks or at home up top) then **Day** (6–10 s living replay, speed adjustable/skippable: the bell, the lift carrying shifts up and down, carts, followers carrying out what you asked, colour creeping, enemies moving), then the next night.
- Every person has a **home** (the bunks, or a town block such as Miners' Rows) and a **workplace**, and a shift. They commute through the lift. A person is somewhere at night and somewhere else by day, and a follower stains both.
- So influence reaches the **surface** on its own feet: a follower who sleeps up top starts a stain at the pit head and in Miners' Rows; people asleep there come into reach; zooming out shows the first colour in the town. The world is one map from turn 1 and **the camera can always zoom from a close-up of the mine out to the whole town**.
- The watershed (Whisper → Shade): Divinity IV **and** the pit head fully yours. The camera pulls back; the mine shrinks into a glowing point; the town opens mostly gray and black with your stain at the pit head.

### 14.4 What you do at night (few verbs, spatial)
1. **Whisper** to a person in reach: a chance to turn them (odds as a word and a percent). Sleepers are easier. On success, a ripple of colour starts where they stand.
2. **Send** a follower somewhere: pick the follower, click a place. One verb; what they do there depends on the place and is stated in one line before you confirm — an unexplored or gray area: **explore** it (reveals it, finds what is there: a cache, a seam, a loner, a shortcut, a danger); an area with people: **spread the word** (softens them, feeds the stain); a seam or cart: **bring crystals**; the lift: **go up**; later, a shrine or post: darker options. They set off at once, walk there visibly, and keep at it every day until told otherwise. Paid once (spec `Direct = S^0.85·T`).
3. Followers you do not send **live their lives and pray** — they feed you (Faith → Nether) and stain wherever their day takes them. Nobody is ever idle. A follower can be told to **lie low** when the heat is on.
4. **Miracle** (one a night, costs crystals), **eat crystals** (Hunger), and, when Malice presses, an evil act.
There are no menus of orders at the start. More options appear one at a time, each with a one-line introduction.

### 14.5 Exploration
Every area holds something unknown until a follower has been there: a crystal cache, a rich seam, a loner who turns easily, a shortcut (a new connection), a relic (a miracle), or a danger (a post, a ward). Gray areas show a hint of it; black areas show nothing. Sending people into the gray and the black is a main way to grow.

### 14.6 Threat you can see
Heat belongs to the Temple, the Council and the Public, and it walks: guards and the overseer patrol, a guard crossing heavily stained ground notices, an inquisitor comes down the lift when the Temple is alarmed. The stain recedes where they stand. Lying low, wards of your own, and choosing where to spread are the counterplay.

### 14.7 Goals in plain words
"Turn the mine to colour" (with the percent) and "Reach the surface" (the pit head's Hold). Then, in Shade: the town, district by district.

### 14.8 The shot loop (how this is judged from now on)
- **Experience reel:** one script (`e2e/playtest-reel.cjs`) plays the real build through the real UI from the title for five nights and writes a standard reel into `screenshots/reel/`: stills of every step, plus frame strips (8 frames over 2 s) of the mine at rest, of a whisper, of a Send, and of the Day replay, plus the same views zoomed out to the town. It also writes numbers: how many people moved per second, how much colour changed per day, how many clicks and seconds a night took.
- **Playtest critic:** after every integration a blind critic plays the build the same way as a first-time player and judges only the experience: is it alive, does colour spread where I act, do I know what to do next without reading, is anything confusing, does anything look bad. Nothing is published to the owner below 8/10 from this critic without saying so.
- **The orchestrator looks at the reel itself** after every integration and before every published build, and writes what it saw in `PROGRESS.md`.

### 14.9 The whole experience is staffed (user, 2026-11-23: "you were only doing half of the experience… animations need an agent, behaviour needs one, feel and loop needs one… you're doing too little")
Every part of what a player sees, hears and touches has its own builder, its own directory and its own evidence. Map (piece → directory → brief in `handoffs/briefs/`):
- **Rules of the loop** — SIM-v2 → `src/sim` (`SIM-v2.md`).
- **Play scene: wiring, camera input, interaction** — PLAY-v2 → `src/scenes/play`, `src/app` (`PLAY-v2.md`). It integrates the pieces below through thin adapters.
- **Interface, cards, guidance** — HUD → `src/ui/play` (`HUD.md`). Shell/settings/layout → P5-fix.
- **Game feel, camera direction, the staging of every moment and of the Day** — FEEL → `src/feel` (`FEEL.md`).
- **Effects** — VFX → `src/render/fx` (`VFX.md`).
- **Miner design and animation** — P2-people → `src/render/people` (`P2-people.md`). **Town folk, Elites, enemies, crowds** — CAST → `src/render/people/cast` (`CAST.md`).
- **Behaviour (who does what, where, when; commute; reactions)** — BEHAV → `src/life` (`BEHAV.md`).
- **Ambient life, machines, air, time of day** — AMBIENT → `src/render/ambient` (`AMBIENT.md`).
- **Mine look** — terrain (`src/render/terrain`), mine engine incl. the stain (`src/render/mine`, `fog.ts`), mine art (round 5A done). **Town look** — town engine (`src/render/town`), TOWN-ART → `src/render/town/art` (`TOWN-ART.md`). **One world** — world composition (`src/render/world`).
- **Sound** — P3 round 3 → `src/audio` (`P3-r3.md`).
- **Words and the people behind the names** — WRITING → `src/content` (`WRITING.md`).
- **Opening** — cutscene (P7). **Title, bonus pick, defeat** — FRONT → `src/scenes/front` (`FRONT.md`). **Watershed pull-back and ending beat** — FINALE → `src/scenes/finale` (`FINALE.md`).
- **The shot loop** — LOOP → `e2e/playtest` (`LOOP.md`): robots that play through the real UI, the reel, the numbers.
- Later, when the loop runs: bots + `BALANCE.md`, full-run e2e, README, final holistic critic.

### 14.10 Grading (user, 2026-11-23 — replaces "every piece goes to a blind critic first")
Builders grade themselves from real evidence after every milestone and do not report below an honest 8 (`handoffs/RULES.md` rule 13). **Blind critics judge only at the gate:** the integrated build before it goes to the owner (playtest critic on the reel and a live session), and a piece when the orchestrator's own look at its evidence disagrees with the builder's grade. The orchestrator looks at every reported piece's evidence itself. Nothing is called done below 8.

## 15. Loop re-scope with the owner (2026-11-23 — in discussion; settled points are binding and override §14.4 where they conflict)
Owner: "The core gameplay loop should be about mining crystals. Some should have higher rarity. You can also feed by eating people and evil acts. Where's the miracle and acts you can perform every round — they should come in cards. You can command people to do way too many things right now. And special effects. Crystals should be equippable and craftable."
Settled so far (owner's answers):
- **Follower routines (replace "Send" and its intents):** Move (an influence attempt that can fail — lower chance the further it takes them off their standard path), Pray, Offer (a ritual that transfers their crystals to you), Lie low. These are standing routines. More unlock later.
- **Action points:** 3 a night, +1 at every minor rank; they pay for whispers and for changing follower routines (some actions cost 2 or 3). Cards do not cost action points.
- **Cards every night:** draw 3 miracles — play 1 or skip; draw 3 acts of evil — play 1 or skip. Eating a person is an act-of-evil card. (Blessings — "a buff or interesting action", pick 1 of 3 — exist as offers; exactly when they are dealt is still open.)
- **Followers' own evil:** depending on your Malice, followers sometimes commit evil unprompted; it sates Malice half as much as when you cause it.
- **Crystals are items:** many rarities; each has random stats and its own strange shape; equipped on the grave; most give stats, some have odd effects (e.g. summon copies of people who are twice as likely to raise suspicion; an extra blessing choice; a reroll). Rarity of what is mined is pure luck.
- **Special effects** for all of it.
Owner, asked how the rest should be scoped: "Sensibly. I'm talking about crystals and mining." → the orchestrator scopes everything else with judgement; **crystals and mining are the game** and everything below serves them. Items marked (orchestrator's pick) are open to the owner's correction.

### 15.1 The whole game is the mine's crystals
Soul crystals are dug out of the rock by miners every shift. Today they all go up the lift to the Council. The game is turning that flow toward your grave: every miner you turn is a share of the mine's output that becomes yours; every crystal is a small decision. Colour (Hold, §14.2), living shifts (§14.3), the day replay and zoom-out stay as the world this happens in.

### 15.2 Mining
- **Everyone who works a rock face digs on their shift**, follower or not. Each digger makes strike rolls during the Day; a strike frees one crystal. **Rarity is pure luck** — one fixed table for everyone, everywhere (only odd crystal effects, miracles and blessings can bend it).
- **You see it happen:** in the Day replay picks swing, a strike glints in the rock in its rarity's colour, an unconverted miner drops the crystal in the cart and it rolls to the lift; a follower pockets it.
- **The Tally:** at shift end the overseer counts what went up. Crystals that followers keep are missing from the count; a short count raises the Council's suspicion (searches at the lift, more guards). (orchestrator's pick)
- **More followers digging = more rolls.** The mine does not run dry in this slice.

### 15.3 Follower routines (the only commands)
Every follower digs on shift regardless. Each has **one standing routine** for the rest of their time (changing it costs 1 action point):
- **Pray** (default once turned): prays between shifts → Nether (spec Faith formula) and Hold where they are. Finds are kept hidden on them (a stash that can be found in a search).
- **Offer:** each dusk carries their stash and the day's finds to the grave in a small ritual — this is how crystals reach you. Needs a walkable way to the grave; passing guards is a risk.
- **Lie low:** hands the day's finds in at the Tally like everyone else and keeps their head down → no suspicion from them, no crystals, hard to catch.
- **Move** (a one-off order, not a routine): get them reassigned to another area — an influence attempt that can fail; the chance falls the further it is from their standard path (home ↔ work ↔ lift); costs 1 action point, 2 if far.
More routines unlock later, one at a time.

### 15.4 The night, in order
1. **Dusk — the offering:** followers on Offer arrive at the grave and set their crystals down one by one; each is revealed (rarity, shape, stats). This is the reward moment of every turn.
2. **The draw:** 3 miracles (play 1 or skip) and 3 acts of evil (play 1 or skip). Miracles cost crystals (you choose which to burn; cost counted in crystal value by rarity). Acts of evil sate Malice and raise suspicion; **devouring a person is an act-of-evil card** (feeds Hunger heavily). Pools widen with Divinity.
3. **Your actions:** action points (3, +1 per minor rank) pay for Whisper (1) and routine changes / Move (1–2). Handling crystals is free: eat, set in the grave, cut, graft.
4. **End Night → the Day:** digging and strikes, carts and the Tally, the commute, colour creeping, the opposition's moves. Depending on your Malice, followers sometimes commit evil on their own (sates Malice half as much as your own acts, raises suspicion the same).

### 15.5 Crystals are items
- **Rarity:** six tiers by luck (working names; the writer may rename): Dull, Clear, Bright, Deep, Royal, Impossible — value 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 / 16 / 32, each with its own colour and reveal.
- **Every crystal is one of a kind:** a strange generated shape (seeded silhouette and facets; rarer = stranger), 1–4 random stat lines by rarity (Nether a night, slower Hunger, whisper odds, strike luck, miracle power, lower suspicion, Ichor, an extra action point on the rarest…), and from Bright upward a chance of an **odd effect** (e.g. Echo — summons a copy of a person who is twice as likely to raise suspicion; Second Sight — one more blessing to choose from; Loaded — one reroll of a draw each night; and many more — design a long list of strange ones).
- **Four uses that compete for the same crystal:** **eat** it (Hunger down by its value; gone), **burn** it for a miracle, **set** it in the grave (sockets: 3 at the start, +1 per Divinity rank; its stats and effect apply while set), or use it in crafting.
- **[WITHDRAWN — see 15.10 A]** ~~Crafting (orchestrator's pick — the owner said "something else" than fusing three into one or blending two):** crystals come out **rough**: rarity and shape are known, stats are not. **Cut** it at the grave (costs Nether): you are shown 2–3 possible cuts, each a different stat spread; pick one; a small chance the stone cracks (drops a tier) or reveals a flaw (an odd effect). **Graft:** destroy one cut crystal to move one of its lines (or its odd effect) onto another, which visibly grows a new spur; a crystal can hold only so many grafts by rarity.
- **Blessings** (a buff or an interesting action): pick 1 of 3 at every minor rank, none at the start of a run (§43, fourth amendment); some crystals add a choice or a reroll.

### 15.6 Feeding and pressure
Hunger: eat crystals (free, any night), or play a devour card. Malice: your acts of evil, or followers' own at half value. The Council watches the count (missing crystals), the Temple watches the stain and the acts; both walk the mine (§14.6). Starving, or being rooted out, ends the run.

### 15.7 The town stage (lean, §13.7)
The watershed (Divinity IV + the pit head fully yours) follows the crystals upward: the town is where they go — the tally house, the cutters and smelters, the Council's vault. Same loop with groups and Elites as the people who handle crystals; the vault holds a guaranteed Impossible crystal; the ending is the gate.

### 15.8 Effects, sound, words
Every one of these moments gets its own effect and sound (strike glint by rarity, pocketing, the cart and the Tally, the offering procession and each reveal, cutting and a crack, grafting, setting a crystal into the grave and its aura, eating, burning for a miracle, each miracle, each act of evil, devouring) and plain words (two short lines, then Details).

### 15.10 Owner's rulings on crafting and mining (2026-11-23 — binding; these REPLACE the orchestrator's picks in 15.2, 15.3 and 15.5 where they differ)
Owner, verbatim: "Crafting, only enchanting a crystal or recombining for now. Faceting unlocks when you take over a gem workshop and crafting into jewelry when you take over a forge. Similarly other buildings grant you their facilities to create cursed items. Miners automatically give tribute randomly. You can also have them contribute crystals. Rarity is unaffected by region, but broader regions (such as different mines) have slightly different weights aka some types may be more common. Gems should also have affinities. What increases the rarity of gems is going to deeper parts of mines but especially going to larger more powerful mines. There should be tiers to mines. This one is the lowest. Gems should have crazy rarity scaling, I'm talking many random modifiers and types and bases which can get as rare as .001%, that kind of thing."

**A. Crafting is by facility.** (Cut / rough stones / Graft from 15.5 are withdrawn.)
- **At the grave, from the start: Enchant and Recombine only.**
  - *Enchant* (orchestrator's detail): spend Nether (by grade) to add one random modifier from the crystal's affinity pool into an open slot, or to reroll one existing modifier; a small chance the result is a **curse** (a strong modifier with a price).
  - *Recombine* (orchestrator's detail): merge two crystals into one new crystal — base from one parent, affinity from one, modifiers drawn from both up to the cap, grade the higher of the two with a small chance of one step up; both parents are gone.
- **Faceting** unlocks when you take over **a gem workshop** (town): re-cut a crystal — choose 1 of 3 offered cuts that reshape its numbers and which modifiers stay; the shape changes.
- **Jewelry** unlocks when you take over **a forge** (town): set a crystal into a ring / amulet / circlet — an item **a person wears** (orchestrator's detail: a follower wears it for its powers; given to someone who is not yours it works on them as a cursed gift).
- **Other buildings grant their own facility** for making **cursed items** (lean set for the slice, orchestrator's detail: the tally house → false weights; the Flame's chapel → a profaned relic; the apothecary → a black draught). A facility is yours when its building's area is fully your colour (Hold 100) or its master is turned.
- Crystals arrive with their properties visible (no rough state).

**B. Tribute.** Follower miners **give tribute on their own, at random**: each dusk each follower who found something may bring one find to the grave unasked (chance by their devotion). The **Offer** routine is "have them contribute": they bring everything they have. Pray keeps the random tribute; Lie low gives none. The count (missing crystals) still watches what never reaches the lift.

**C. Where rarity comes from.**
- Inside one mine, **which area a face is in does not matter**; **depth does**: the mine has depth levels, and each level down shifts the odds toward higher grades and opens rarer bases and modifiers.
- **Mines have tiers. This mine is tier I, the lowest.** A mine's tier shifts the odds far more than depth does, and each mine (a broader region) has **its own weights** — some types more common there. The tables are data for tiers I–V; only tier I is playable in the slice; higher tiers are what the ending teases.

**D. Crystals: deep item generation with a very long tail.**
- A crystal = **base** (its species of form, with an inherent property; dozens of bases, weights from common down to **0.001 %**) × **affinity** (its nature — e.g. Hunger, Malice, Faith, Fear, Blood, Shadow, Stone, Echo…; weights differ by mine; affinity decides its modifier pool, how it answers miracles and what it resonates with when set beside others) × **grade** (the six: Dull … Impossible — how many modifiers it can hold and how strong) × **modifiers** (a large pool — prefixes and suffixes in tiers, most plain stats, some strange; weights down to 0.001 %) × rare **anomalies / odd effects** (the copy-a-person one, another blessing choice, a reroll, …).
- Every crystal shows **how rare it is as a number** ("1 in 48,000") computed from its actual roll.
- Uses stay: eat, burn for a miracle, set in the grave, craft.

### 15.11 The spec is rewritten (owner, 2026-11-23: "edit the docs to flesh out everything further… be bold in creating new systems")
`docs/spec.md` is now version 2 and **the source of truth for the loop**; where it says more than §15 / §15.10 it wins. Owner's addition recorded there: cursed items can be given to followers for free for buffs, with a higher risk if they are exposed. New systems the orchestrator added in the spec (tagged [Design] there, open to the owner's correction): vein level = (mine tier − 1) × 3 + depth, mirroring the 15 Divinity levels; nine affinities incl. the enemy god's Flame (hallowed until profaned); resonance between set stones; "1 in N" shown on every crystal; the facilities table with a master Elite per building; items worn by followers, exposure, cursed gifts; one unlock per minor rank (Delve, Skim, Preach, Stand watch, the vault); Hunger / Malice max-out effects; the three factions' answer ladders; XP sources; the turn as four beats (dusk, night, day, dawn).

### 15.12 SIM v3.1 builder's decisions (2026-11-23, SIM successor)
- **Set powers** (spec §7.2 "three wake a set power", undesigned): three set stones of one affinity wake one — Hunger: Hunger rises 2 slower; Malice: Malice rises 2 slower; Faith: +1 devotion a day and +5 % whisper odds; Fear: −10 % heat; Blood: +2 Ichor; Shadow: searches ×0.75; Stone: +10 strike luck; Echo: one more free whisper a night; Flame: miracles +20 % (it still quarrels). Numbers in `SET_POWER` (src/sim/mods.ts); `mods(s).setPowers` lists the awake ones.
- **Strange modifiers** feed `mods()` through `fieldTotal` (tolerance, freeWhispers, seep, offerExtra, rerolls, eatNether, miracleDraw, evilDraw, blessingChoices; `searchCut` multiplies `searchMult` by 1 − v/100, floor 0.2; `tributeOdds` adds v/100 to the tribute chance).
- **"Tally house" is called "the counting house"** in player-facing text (the plain-names rule, §13.3, retires "tally"); the id `tallyHouse` is unchanged.
- **Strike rate:** 2 rolls × 0.2 = an ordinary miner strikes once every 2.5 days (spec §6 "every two to three days"); followers ×1.25 ("they dig harder for their god") before blessings, items and stones. Face richness is gone (`FACES` all 1.0, spec §6 "which area does not matter"); a found seam still adds +0.4.
- **Tribute:** at the offering (0.995), each praying person-follower holding finds brings one (the oldest) with chance min(0.85, 0.25 + Devotion/150) + `tributeOdds` → `offered {tribute: true}`; no spotting (it is quiet). Lie low: at the carts step (0.535) whatever they carry goes into their face's cart and up to the count (`crystalLost {why: 'handedIn'}`).
- **Worn powers:** `strikeLuck` adds to the wearer's luck, `strikeRate` (%) to their strike chance, `guardBlind` (%) cuts their search chance, `secondPocket` makes a search find only half (rounded up), `falseWeights` widens the count, `devotion` adds a day. A search on a wearer exposes every worn item (`exposeItems`) and the follower is taken at once. Wearers with empty pockets can still be searched. (`whisperHelp` is not yet read by the whisper odds.)
- **The Flame's price:** each Dawn (0.45) every set Flame stone costs Hunger +2 and Temple heat +1 (`flamePrice` event).
- **The sealed Deep** is not closed to walkers (the sump is on paths, a hunter hideout and Sib's post): until opened, its faces strike at depth 2 (`BALANCE3.sealedDepth`) and Move into it is refused. **Delve** (routine, Whisper II = Divinity level 2): a delving follower at a face does not strike; each delving day adds 1 to `flags.delve` (6 opens it → `flags.deepOpen`, delvers go back to Pray), 6 % chance a day of being hurt (sick 2 days); event `delved`. **Skim** (routine, Whisper III = level 3, haulers and sorters only): the old automatic skim now needs the routine.
- **Directing costs Nether:** a routine change and a Move each cost the Direct cost (S^0.85 · T, `directCostOf`) on top of the action (spec §8); `routineSet.nether` carries it.
- **legalActions bounds recombine pairs:** every pair up to 12 loose stones, past that each stone with its next 3 (any pair is still legal through `actionWhy`); `mods(s)` is memoised inside a `withMemo` scope; crafting costs read the Gemcutter blessing directly. (A 60-turn sensible bot run went from 56 s to 7 s.)
- **Bots** craft at most twice a night.

### 15.13 SIM v3.2 builder's decisions (2026-11-23, SIM successor 4)
- **Turn order v3.2** as in `handoffs/P4.md` (Night → Day 0–0.9 → Dusk 0.9–1.0; every event carries `time` + `phase`; `StepResult.day`). The bridge keeps its own JSON fold of LIVING's `DayEffects` (`LivingToday`) instead of `foldInto`'s closures, because the state must stay plain JSON; the events are merged by a stable sort on `time`, which is what `mergeFeed` does.
- **LIVING's own diggers strike too** (`hew {person, zone, rarity, value, seed}`, 0.33–0.51): the ~34 mine people who are not sim units roll on a separate seeded stream so the sim's dice are untouched; their stones go in the cart as flavour and are not part of the count.
- **Colour seeds:** `colourSeedsOf(s)` / `hearthSeeds(s)` — LIVING's haunts merged over your followers; a follower's hearth carries a seed of violet from the first night (the visual seed; the follower's night half at home stains the hearth's zone by the usual Hold rules). `GONE_WHY` adds `levied → taken`, `faded → died`.
- **Bug fixed:** a zone whose Hold reached 100 outside the Day (a whisper's colour, a miracle) never took root — the stain step skipped unchanged zones. The pit head could sit at 100 for twenty days without the watershed. It now roots at the next stain.
- **Suspicion cools faster at high Divinity:** decay per Dawn = max(1, 2 − 0.15·(L − 1)) (was max(0.5, 2 − 0.5·(L − 1))): at Shade the old 0.5 a day could never outrun "public talk", so every run ended in the Kindling whatever the player did. Public talk feeds the Temple and the Council at 3 % of public (was 5 %).
- **Ichor:** a devoted follower lost tears 1 Ichor, not 2 (spec §4: Ichor is lowered by losing Influence and by enemy weapons; a follower is one point of Influence); each level-up closes 2 Ichor (was 1).
- **XP thresholds** [0, 100, 250, 560, 950, 1800, 3400] (were [0, 90, 210, 400, 700, 1400, 2600]): with the rooting bug fixed a decent player reached Shade at turn 14 and won at 39; now Shade ~17 and the win ~50 (CLAUDE.md: 15–25 and 40–60).
- **Bots play the town:** whisper down to 12 % odds in Shade (8 % greedy, 20 % cautious; raised by (public − 35)/150 so a warm Public is not fed), weight the gate road (citygate, garrison, market, tallyhall) ×1.3; when the hottest track passes their lie-low mark, the most exposed half of the faithful lie low before any whisper; from Divinity III they send up to three followers to work the pit head until it roots. The runner computes legal actions per family (`legalActionsOf`) inside one memo scope per step.
- **Speed:** `suggestPay` uses a choice table (no list copies; same picks), `findUnit` / `findZone` use a checked id index, `kilnLight` and the graph signature are memoised per read scope, Move legality reads the cached BFS tree once per follower. A 40-turn sensible run: 6 s → 2.5 s CPU; LIVING's `planDay` (its JSON clone of the society every day) is now a quarter of the cost (request to LIVING).
- **Plain names in the sim's fallback text** (writer's list): Farmer → Gardener, Soldier → Guard, Gravedigger → Ash keeper (ids unchanged), the graveyard → the burning ground (the dead are burned), gem base "Bell" → "Bowl", blessing Grafter → "Stone Wedding", Gemcutter's text says enchanting.

### 15.14 SIM v3.3 builder's decisions — the town's contest (2026-11-23, SIM successor 5)
Orchestrator's brief: the town had one way to die (Ichor torn by followers lost to the Temple) and one way to live (XP). Contract in `handoffs/P4.md` "v3.3 ADDITIONS"; data in `src/sim/content/threats.ts`, rules in `src/sim/town.ts`; tests `tests/sim/v33-town.test.ts`, `tests/sim/sim-words.test.ts`. Spec formulas untouched.
- **Announced moves (telegraphed pushback).** In the town (Shade only) each faction whose heat is at or above a move's floor announces one move at a time at Day 0.83 (`threatAnnounced`, seen in the fast-forward), two Days ahead (one when Hostile, 75+), reminds each Day (`threatLooms`), and lands it at the Dusk it is due (0.95, `threatLanded`) unless answered (`threatBlunted {by}`). After a move lands or is blunted the faction rests 2 Days. Moves: **the writ** (Council, floor 35) — a seal on the writ to close the pit head (and −8 Council heat); **the levy** (Council, 30) — followers whose hearths are in one area and who are not lying low are taken for the war (a crowd loses a quarter; at most two people); **the naming** (`rite`, Temple, 40) — the High Priest names one follower (the brightest wick near the gold light) for the fire, and lying low does not hide them (with hide allowed the bots dodged 57 of 68 namings for one action each: the Temple's main move never landed); **the ward** (Temple, 25) — the area your colour is soaking fastest (not rooted) is warded for 5 Days (−15 Hold, then pushed back each Day); **the burning** (crowd, 50) — a mob burns a building whose craft you hold (else your strongest town area): −40 Hold, the craft lost and not retakeable for 8 Days, −10 Public.
- **The writ — the town's second way to lose.** `state.writ {seals, max: 4}`. A landed writ adds a seal; the Lockdown and the crackdown in the town each add a seal too. At 4 seals the pit head is sealed and the run is lost (`defeat {why: 'sealed'}`). Turning the Magistrate tears it up (0 seals, `writTorn`); Burn the Knot-Cords takes one seal off.
- **Answers (each costs something real).** Hide (the levied lie low) — 1 action and the Direct Nether each, and a lying-low follower gives no Nether or colour; **items** made at a held building (false weights → the writ; a profaned relic → the naming, the ward and the crusade; a black draught → the levy and the burning) — 1 action, the item is used up (it cost a crystal and Nether to make); **an Elite's favour** (new action `favour {unit}`, 1 action) — every pending move of that Elite's faction fails, the track gains nothing for 3 nights, the Kindling / the Lockdown end early, the Elite loses 15 Devotion (it can lapse) and the favour returns after 9 nights; **acts of evil** dealt only while a move of their faction is pending (or the Kindling / Lockdown burns): Burn the Knot-Cords (id `burnTheLedgers`; Council; Public +8, Temple +4, −1 seal), Silence the Preacher (Temple; Public +8, Council +5; the Inquisitor leaves), Turn the Mob (crowd; Council +10); **cooling** the track below the floor by Dusk (the move fizzles). None of this is a pending choice: nothing blocks End Night.
- **No random strikes in the town.** The Whisper stage's Hostile pressure (a random follower struck most Dawns while a track is 75+) is off in Shade: a Hostile track announces its moves a Day ahead instead. The Kindling (4 Days in town) and the Lockdown (5 Days in town) keep their crossing, but in the town their first Day is the warning and a favour or the answering act stops them. The Council's crackdown in the town takes at most 2 (was 4) and adds a seal.
- **The top of the town pushes back** (spec §10 "a lockdown, the inquisitor, and a crusade gathering as you near the vault"): at Shade III (Divinity VI) the Temple calls a **crusade** — a move of its own, announced 3 Days ahead (answers: a profaned relic, a Temple Elite's favour, Silence the Preacher); unanswered, the Kindling burns for 3 Days. Opening the Council's vault is noticed (+25 Council).
- **The town loses patience.** After 12 Days in Shade (`townLate`, event `townHardens` once) the factions rest only 1 Day between moves, every move's floor drops by 15, and the Council presses the writ whatever its heat (cooling no longer stops it; weights, a favour, Burn the Knot-Cords or the Magistrate do). Why: the slow, safe strategies (sensible 72–82 %, cautious ~78 % wins at 50 seeds) out-waited a town that never escalated; a clock on Shade makes dawdling cost without touching the fast, greedy player (who finishes ~19 Days after arriving).
- **XP from souls.** Eating a crystal and paying a miracle with crystals give XP +1 per point of soul value (`BALANCE.xpPerSoul`) on top of the flat XP. XP thresholds are pinned by the formula tests, so this was the lever for the town's length: without it the winning bots were stuck below Divinity VII well past turn 60.
- **The vault's stone heals.** Eating a crystal that came from the vault heals Ichor by a quarter of its worth (the Impossible: 8) — `ichorGained {reason: 'vaultStone'}`. Set it for its power or eat it to survive.
- **World words.** WRITING's round-5 replacement table is applied to the sim's fallbacks (bonuses' new story, the three acts' lines, town.ts, events, maps, discoveries, stages, crowds); `tests/sim/sim-words.test.ts` scans maps and the crowds too with `forbiddenStrictIn`. "Tally" is no longer a retired name in `tests/sim/names.test.ts` (§13.3): docs/theme.md §10 names the tally house and the tally clerk. The sim's event hints said "Government / Cult Suspicion": now Council / Temple / Public. Every sim fallback string (content tables, event texts, TOWN_RULES, threat views) passes `forbiddenIn` (`tests/sim/sim-words.test.ts`).
- **Speed.** `conversionOddsFor`, `effectiveResistance` (summed without building the labelled list), `wariness`, `noticedFollowers`, `presence` and the units by zone are memoised per read scope; `mods()` is cached across states by a signature of the blessings and the set stones; Dusk's card draw runs in one read scope. Outcomes are unchanged (same dice). `scripts/sim-bots.ts` caches every run per seed under `/tmp/sim5/cache/<hash of src/sim>/` and only plays missing seeds (`--fresh`, `--workers=N`, `--cache=DIR`).
- **Rooted out (spec §2 Losing) — added.** The spec's third way to lose had no check. Now, at the end of each Day (1.0, step `rootedOut`), a god with no living follower, no area but the grave holding real colour (Hold ≥ 10 — the grave's own daily seep into its neighbours does not count) and no living soul within a whisper's reach has lost: `defeat {why: 'rootedOut'}`. Nether is not counted (stones can still be burned for it). Selector `rootedOut(s)`.
- **Spec differences, ruled (docs audit).** (1) *Reaching Shade:* the sim's `surfaceReached` is set exactly when the mine's gate area — the pit head — is rooted (Hold 100), so "Divinity IV and the pit head fully in colour" is what the sim checks; no change. (2) *Winning:* the sim wins at Divinity VII with the city gate rooted; the spec's "the vault is open" is the ending beat's picture, not a second gate. The vault opens on its own (a follower or your colour in the tally hall) in 95–98 % of the sensible and cautious bots' runs; requiring it would make the end hinge on one area's soak and add a dead wait after the gate is taken. The ending beat shows the vault when `flags.vaultOpened`, and PLAY-SHADE may require it visually. (3) *Losing:* rooted out added (above). (4) *Hunger at the top:* the spec says the starving god eats one follower every night; the sim eats the brightest follower when Hunger reaches 100 and resets Hunger to 70, so it recurs every 2–3 nights unless fed (and takes a stranger in an active area, with Ichor lost, when there is no follower). Eating every night killed every run that touched the top in two or three nights (each follower is 1 Ichor and its colour) with no counterplay; the reset keeps the threat and leaves a window to feed. Recorded as a deliberate softening.
- **`npm run bots`** forwards to `scripts/sim-bots.ts` (the stub is gone).
- **Tests.** Every `tests/sim` file except `formulas.test.ts` (left untouched) sets a 60 s per-test timeout: on the shared box a Day with LIVING can take seconds under load and the 5 s default failed at random.
- **Bots.** All three scripted bots answer the moves on the last night (cautious a night early), skipping a move whose track is already more than 3 under its floor (it will fizzle): hide the levied (not greedy), spend an item, call a favour (an Elite with Devotion > 30), play the answering act; greedy answers everything except wards; the Kindling and the Lockdown are stopped first when a favour or the act is at hand; all three craft and keep one answering item of each kind their buildings can make. A follower the bot lays low stays down at least two nights and until the move that threatens them has passed (the Pray / Lie low flip-flop around the count is gone).

### 15.9 The play scene is two pieces (orchestrator, 2026-11-23)
The single integrator ran to its context limit three times. From here: **PLAY-CORE** owns the night's interface and rules wiring (`index.ts`, `hudbridge.ts`, `night.ts`, `sim.ts`, `crystals.ts`, `standin.ts`, `types.ts`, `debug.ts`, `styles.ts`, `modules.ts`, `src/app/**`); **PLAY-WORLD** owns everything drawn on the canvas during play (`world.ts`, `life.ts`, `day.ts`, `fx.ts`, `feel.ts`, `crystalart.ts` and new adapter files for AMBIENT / CAST / CRYSTAL / FINALE), calling into `index.ts` only through small lines marked `// PLAY-WORLD`. Player-facing names fixed by the writer: the Tally is "the count"; the fourth rarity (id `deep`) reads "Blazing".

## 16. A lived-in world (owner, 2026-11-23 — binding)
Owner, verbatim: "Make the game feel way more lived in and fleshed out. It should be a real world you conquer with many professions many people and events, daily life going about. I can tell that the immersion factor is low. Rain and weather appropriately too. All the zooms as well. And obviously people you influence in the mine should be able to go aboveground. Perhaps ending a turn is like a fast forward of watching people do stuff in the day, and as they happen flavor text/notifications appear and then you get the card at end-of-turn."
Recorded in `docs/spec.md` v2.1 (§3, §5, §5b), which is binding. In short:
1. **The turn:** Night (act) → End turn → **the Day as a fast-forward** of the whole world, dawn to dusk in ~15–20 s (speed control, skippable, a clock), with **notes appearing where things happen** and collecting in a clickable feed → **Dusk**: the offering, a short ledger, then **the cards are dealt** → the next night. The separate dawn summary box is gone.
2. **Many people, many trades:** ~60 mine workers in ≥ 20 trades, ~400 townspeople in ≥ 40; households, ties, homes above ground; nobody lives in the mine.
3. **Followers go above ground:** they ride the lift home; their homes and haunts seed your colour in the town and bring their neighbours within reach before the watershed; Move can target a surface place.
4. **Calendar** (payday, market day, the Flame's day, rest day), **weather** (clear, overcast, drizzle, rain, storm, fog, wind — with small real effects) and **world events** (≥ 60, most opening or closing a small door for you).
5. **Every zoom is alive**, out to the town in its valley with the road the crystals leave by.
Staffing: **LIVING** (`src/sim/living` — population, plans, calendar, weather model, events, the day's feed; brief `LIVING.md`), **WEATHER** (`src/render/weather`; brief `WEATHER.md`), a miner-animator successor (bigger gestures, more trades, weather dress), **CAST round 3** (more trades and everyday activities above ground); existing builders re-pointed: BEHAV (stages LIVING's plans), FEEL (the Day fast-forward), HUD (the day feed), PLAY-CORE (turn order), PLAY-WORLD, AMBIENT, TOWN-ART (homes, tavern, market, wash-place, outskirts and the road), world composition (the valley zoom), audio (weather, crowds), WRITING (event flavour), LOOP (life at every zoom as a verdict rule), SIM (hooks for LIVING, surface Move, homes as colour seeds).

### 16.1 The town's buildings are alien and organic, at the reference's scale and view (orchestrator's ruling from the spec, 2026-11-23)
`docs/spec.md` (Art direction, [Spec]): "`town.png` — layout, density, sprite scale; ignore human-looking sheds (buildings should be alien, organic)", and the overview: "an alien civilization". Two builders in a row built human shanty-industrial houses (Quonset huts, brick, slate) drawn as small flat front elevations. Ruling:
- **Form:** grown and built-by-hand alien architecture — ribbed shell and carapace domes, hide and membrane stretched over bone / resin frames, clay-and-spit walls, chitin plates patched with scavenged mine metal, pods clustered and budded onto each other, fungus and crystal-shard lanterns. No brick, slate, timber-frame gables or corrugated Quonset huts. Every trade's place still reads as what it is (forge, gem workshop, tavern, tally house, chapel of the Flame, apothecary, vault) through its silhouette, its clutter and its pictogram sign.
- **Scale, view, density — from `town.png`:** buildings are large, lumpy masses seen from the same three-quarter top-down view as the reference (roof mass dominant, a short front face), 6–7 figures wide, packed along dirt lanes about one figure wide, each with clutter heaped against it. Never a flat front elevation.
- **Light:** the town at night is lit in pools like the mine in `mine.png`; lit openings, not rows of identical street lamps.
- Kept from round 2: the ground, the lot fill logic, `townFacilities` / `townHomes`, the crystals' road, the claim look, the harness.

### 16.2 The owner rejects the town and the ending (2026-11-23 — binding)
Owner, verbatim, on the ending strip: "Whatever the fuck this is is NOT rated good at all. The town especially the fuck even IS that????????"
- The town must read as a town at every zoom: lit building masses, lanes, districts, landmarks you can name from far out; lamps are accents, never the picture.
- The ending is restaged once the town is redrawn: a designed gatehouse, the city as a drawn place (not a point cloud), text at readable size.
- **Rule for accepting any picture:** every thing in frame must be nameable at a glance by a stranger; if the answer to "what is that?" takes a caption, it fails. The orchestrator does not accept a self-grade of 8 on a picture that fails this, and does not record "accepted for now".

## 17. The theme — `docs/theme.md` (owner, 2026-11-23 — binding)
Owner: "Look at the reference image dude." "Do we even know the theme? These guys are aliens. Establish a more distinct theme more properly." "Like describe them and their architecture and environment. Flesh out all details (not just for this agent) that EVERYONE has been stumbling around."
The orchestrator had never written the theme down; every visual builder invented its own (human miners, a human shanty town, street lamps, dogs). Now written as **`docs/theme.md`** and binding on all pieces. In short:
- **The wickfolk:** small, pale, big-headed people with eyeshine, ear-fins that show feeling, and a **wick** — a living light on the crown whose colour is their devotion (gold: the Flame's; pale: doubting; violet: yours) and which goes out at death.
- **The colour code:** violet = you and raw crystal; gold = the Flame; cold white = the Council; teal = old rock and damp; ochre / rust = daily life; red = blood and the war; gray = sensed; black = unknown.
