- **Walls are cliff faces of large masses, not cobbles** (orchestrator: "a dry-stone wall" → mine.png's continuous faces). Per run of face (`slabs.ts` §3, `addMass`): a back tier of masses 54–130 units wide along the wall (≈ 2–5 figures; ×0.78 on faces > 90 deep, one mass for a thin island), overlapping 11–23 units, each standing on the floor edge and rising to a broken skyline at the smoothed face depth (±24 units) — one peak, the skyline falling to lower shoulders; vertices follow the contour's smoothed frame. Deep faces (mean depth > 46) carry a sparse front tier (45 % of slots, 40–84 wide, 0.26–0.52 of the depth) so the cliff steps back like a hill. 10 % of back masses on deep faces are collapsed (≈ 0.6 height) with a heap of rounded boulders; a few half-buried boulders and the scree stay at the foot (rubble only there).
- **Planes:** two ridges diverge from each mass's peak to the foot (a lit side plane, a broad front plane, a shadow side plane; wide masses get a third ridge); each plane one yaw (side planes ±0.55–0.9 rad, front ±0.17) and tilt; a top-plane strip along the skyline (3–10 units, gated so it is not a rim everywhere). Shading (`shade.ts` `mass()`): albedo × (0.6 + 0.78·max(0, n·key)), foot occlusion, the back tier rolling darker toward its skyline, long damp streaks, region gray/teal with damp rock kept teal (theme §6), shrine mound teal and lighter.
- **Ink:** outer silhouette (×1.4 at the skyline, ×1.3 at the foot); joints between masses of one tier (same `group`) only along noise-gated stretches (≈ 35 %, ×0.7); long broken lines along the ridges; a thin broken line under the top plane; cracks 1–2 long kinked lines per mass; sparse hatching on shadow planes.
- **Edge normals fixed:** `winding()` returned the inverted sign, so every object's edge normal pointed inward (stones were lit from below; the old slab rims rolled the wrong way). Fixed for all objects.
- **Shrine:** a hill (+62 units, σ 95, peak 14 units left of the niche) of masses with the recess carved into the back mass and two front jamb masses; highlights capped ×0.75 on light albedo (gold, not cream).
- **Floors:** a coarser emboss octave of the light (5-texel offset on a 2.6× sample: the noise's finest features are ~32 texels, the old 1-texel emboss was invisible), dusty/grimy mid-scale mottling and soft grime patches, so lit dirt reads at overview.
- **Glow-moss** (theme §6): faint teal emission in damp hollows (crevice band, the foot of faces).
- **Engine seam:** the engine's `MineGround` (ground.ts, engine-owned) paints our `form` layer as its rock + floor albedo; `form` is now the key-shaded albedo normalised so a flat floor keeps its albedo, without the ambient colour (the engine multiplies its own). New `bake.masks` (`masks.ts`: wallFace / floor coverage on a 4-unit grid, built in the prep worker) + `maskGrid()` to the engine's grid.

### P6 — Town render
- **World:** 6400×3600 town units (`town/layout.ts`), fog/ownership cell 16 (400×225 cells). Townsfolk stand ≈ 24 units tall (ghelm × `FIG` 0.6); doors 16–22 wide. Camera range: whole town (fit ≈ 0.25) to 2 (harness allows 0.2–2.5).
- **The pit:** `pitRect` = {x 1660, y 1290, w 400, h 233} (k = 6 → the 2400×1400 mine), exported from `town/index.ts` (also `MINE_K`). Shaft = ellipse in the rect (`plan.shaft`); the pit head around it: headframe straddling the north lip, winding house with drum wheel to the west, spoil heaps (two with violet crystal), rails from the lip to the haul street. The shaft breathes violet with `godPulse` (live light + emissive glow) and draws a "glowing point" core above the fog that grows into a slow 8-ray star when zoomed out (reads from the whole-town view and through Void).
- **Layout / block geometry:** 13 blocks (§7 ids) in the five §13.3 districts; each block = 1–3 radius-weighted seeds; every cell belongs to the block whose (domain-warped, fbm) seed is nearest → organic, continuous borders, no gaps. A noisy town limit (smoothed, holes filled) separates the town from wild ground; beyond it the cells still belong to their nearest block (the fog follows), so the town dissolves into dark scrub. Streets follow exactly the 17 §7 adjacencies (catmull through hand-set bends, gentle meander, width 64); five roads leave the map, the main one from the gate off the top-right edge toward the city. Pit near centre-left, temple bottom-right, the Keep (council hall, barracks, gate) top-right, as `town.png`.
- **Building plan (`town/plan.ts`):** landmarks first (pit head, temple of the Flame + great flame bowl + brazier processional, council hall + steel lamps, two barracks round a drill yard, the town wall traced along the Keep's limit with the gate where the city road crosses it, the well), then each block's vocabulary on a jittered lattice accepted only on own ground, clear of streets/plaza/pit/other footprints (lanes and yards stay open), then furniture (lantern-pods/steel lamps along streets, cookfires, washing lines, carts, racks, benches by doors). Vocabulary by block: shanties (slagrows), graves with pale crystal buds (gravefield), halls + heaps (haulyard), beehive kilns + glowing slag runnels (smeltery), stalls + cap houses (market), domes round the well (wellsquare), terraced spore beds + giant fungus (sporegardens), spiral shell towers (highwarrens), resin domes + shells (temple hill), iron halls (Keep). Every item carries a footprint ellipse, a draw bbox and an anatomy (door, pores, lights, flames, smoke, pods, growth points). People spots by tag (door, stall, work, garden, grave, altar, yard, gate, plaza, pit). No labels are drawn.
- **Painter interface (`town/painters.ts`) — how the terrain module plugs in:** every baked pixel (base layer and each tile) comes from `ground.paint(t)` then `buildings.paint(t, items)` with the same `TileTarget` {g (world→pixels transform, clipped), rect (gutter incl.), scale px/unit, lod 0/1/2}. Painters may be sync or sliced Jobs; `yield 'wait'` parks a tile until an async result (worker bake) arrives. `townBuildJob(seed, { ground: (model) => …, buildings: (light) => … })` swaps them. A terrain GroundPainter builds a `TerrainSpec` for `t.rect` from `GroundModel` (block owner grid + ground kinds `GROUND_COL`, streets/roads, plazas, pit, rails, channels, wall), runs `bakeTerrain(spec, { rect, scale })`, waits, and draws its `lit`/`form` output into `t.g`. Placeholder ground = smooth per-block colour field (box-blurred owners, blended into wild scrub) + world-anchored grain + streets (verge, body, worn centre, ruts) + plazas + pit + rails + channels + wall; placeholder buildings = `art/townBuild.ts` + `art/townLandmarks.ts` (lit side toward the bake light from a bucketed light field). Heaps are one sculpted mass (no outlined stone piles).
- **LOD / tiles (`town/tiles.ts`):** base layer = whole town at 0.5 px/unit (3200×1800) + its Sensed gray at 0.25, baked during the preload in 512-unit chunks; detail tiles L1 = 512 units @ 1 px/unit (used for device px/unit 0.75–1.4, LRU 30) and L2 = 256 units @ 2 px/unit (> 1.4, LRU 40), each with a 2-px gutter and its own half-res gray. Tiles are requested for the view (+15 %), baked nearest-centre first as Jobs pumped after paint within `tileBudgetMs` (3 ms) per frame; a finished tile fades in over 0.3 s over the level below (L2 over a finished L1, else the base) and is drawn at integer device rects (no seams, no pop). `renderer.flushTiles()` / harness `flush()` bake everything for deterministic shots.
- **Lighting:** night. Two static lightmaps at 0.25 px/unit (ambient `#5d6474` + every source as an additive pool, squashed 0.7 for the 3/4 view): `lmColour` (static sources) and `lmGray` (all but the shaft, for the Sensed bake). Live lights (drawn per frame, visible only): lantern-pods (turn violet with control in a fixed scattered order), temple lights (violet when desecrated), the shaft (god pulse), Council checkpoint lamps when occupied. Frame: albedo × half-res lightmap (multiply) + soft-light pass 0.55, then people (unlit), then emissive (bloom glows from a 400-unit grid of sources, only big ones at far zoom; flames; slag runnels; shaft; motes; vein glow; people points). The colour pass is clipped to the bounds of the Active blocks' fog reach.
- **Fog:** the shared `fog.ts` with owner = the block grid (wild included), cave = all, blend 5, warp 6, sharp 1.5. Sensed = gray tiles (albedo × `lmGray`, desaturated, 11 + 0.35·L cool, lower contrast) composed in a half-res screen buffer with `voidCover` and cut by `inactiveMask`, haze snapshotted in; rebuilt only when camera/fog/tiles change. No rim/halo code exists. Measured (fit view, mid & start presets): pixels where the fog is Void are #000 except a ≤ 2/255 bilinear fringe one fog cell wide; mean luminance of Sensed pixels rises monotonically with s (0 3 5 8 10 13 16 18 21 27 by tenths — no band at Sensed→Void); across Active→Sensed it varies smoothly (24→32), no peak (no rim).
- **Zone state:** control = violet wash over the block footprint + vein strokes from the block's growth points in 10 buckets (+ glow) + pods turning violet (≈ 15 % at 20, nearly all at 80). Flags (`TownZoneFlags` in `town/types.ts`): desecrated (temple flames/lights violet), warded (gold wash + rising gold motes), occupied (striped pole barriers + banner posts at every street mouth, three steel-blue banners round the plaza, steel lamps). Affordances = the block's soft fog-membership footprint (× townness, so it never shows a canvas edge) tinted and × seenMask (never over Void): selectable faint breathing lavender, hover pale, selected violet.
- **People (`town/people.ts`):** `TownUnitView extends PersonView` {size?, elite?}. Individuals take a role spot in their block; groups (8–40) stand on open ground round the plaza with a footprint radius of 12.5√n + 12 (their members are not drawn here); Elites (magistrate, captain, highpriest, guildmaster, smuggler) are 1.35× with regalia (mantle + staff with badge + chain; spear and pennant; gold sun-disc + flame staff; mantle + ledger; hood + lantern). Moving units walk the street graph (BFS over §7 adjacency) at 70 u/s. Crest colours: neutral pale teal, follower violet brightening with devotion, Temple gold, Council steel-blue (+ ghelm shape cues). Far LOD: below device 0.34 px/unit units are crest-coloured light points (groups a cluster glow sized to the crowd plus specks ∝ headcount), cross-fading into figures by 0.6. Nothing over heads except a small order badge (diamond with an abstract glyph) when `order` is set; selected/hover = ground ring; markers = dashed ground ring. `personAt` hits groups by their crowd ellipse.
- **Effects:** the shared `effects.ts`, two instances: individuals at 0.62 scale, groups/blocks at 1.15; the god's end is the shaft. Convert/miracle/death on a group also lift a mote from every member.
- **API:** `createTownRenderer(seed)`, `preloadTownRenderer(seed, budgetMs)` → `TownRendererLoad` {progress, done, renderer, promise, stats, finishSync}, `createTownRendererAsync(seed)`, `townBuildJob(seed, painters?)`; renderer implements `WorldRenderer` + `pitRect`, `settle()`, `flushTiles()`, `memory()`, `zoneVisibility()`, `tileBudgetMs`, `prof`.
- **Dependencies:** none added.

#### Round 2 (P6a — town renderer fixes after the r1 critic at 6/10; builders #3–#5)
- **World edge:** the layout is squeezed toward the pit so the town sits well inside the 6400×3600 world; `geo.reach` (an organic, noisy falloff) is baked as black into every tile and gray tile, and everything beyond the world rect (tile gutters included) is painted black, so no non-Void pixel ends on a straight line at any zoom or camera overscroll. The wall runs continuously W→N→E along the Keep with towers; the gate sits on the city road, which leaves through it to the NNE into the dark.
- **Frame cost:** tiles are baked LIT (albedo × static lightmap + block-state lights, soft-light, emissive glows) with a half-res Sensed gray copy, so a frame is a few blits. The Sensed + Void composite lives in a reduced-res buffer whose origin is snapped to whole buffer pixels: a pan scrolls it and recomposes only the strips that came into view; zoom/state changes recompose it (quarter-res while the zoom moves). Tile bakes run in individually flushed steps (light buffer / multiply / soft-light / saturation / per-block decals / props / glows) with a 3 ms budget, lent to the next frames when a frame is already > 9 ms (never starved > 3 frames).
- **LOD:** level n+1 cross-fades in over a zoom band (`LEVEL_A`/`LEVEL_B` 0.7–0.92 and 1.32–1.62 device px/unit), new bakes fade in over 0.35 s; tiles are pre-warmed ahead of a pan (45 frames of camera velocity).
- **Sensed:** gray tiles = the same ground lit by the static lights, desaturated, × `SENSED_K` 0.6; the drifting haze is faint (`hazeGain` 0.12) so it cannot lift dark Sensed ground toward its Active level. Measured 0.51–0.66 × Active per block, chroma 0, in every preset with a seen town.
- **Fades:** the town's fog runs at 0.6× the shared rate (`TOWN_FADE_K`, ≈ 1.8 s smoothstep, ≈ 0.7 s of visible ramp, no first-frame step); `settle()` finishes fog, stain and tile fades.
- **People at town scale:** crowds are one cached composite with members at fixed offsets (no reshuffle across zoom), radius 7√n + 12 so 8 vs 30 reads by footprint; far zoom gives people a signature lamps never have (a slowly pulsing crest ring round a bright core; crowds a disc of their real footprint + rim ring + a speck per member; Elites a double ring and a four-point star). Order/apostle marks lie on the ground in front of the unit; nothing floats over heads.
- **Effects at town scale:** every effect also gets a beacon sized in screen px at far zoom — an expanding ground ring (1.6 s), a short column of light (1.8 s) and a lingering ground mark (5 s), coloured per kind.
- **Zone state:** control veins are painted on the ground under the buildings only (with a soft violet underlay); ward = a slowly turning gold seal on the ground with standing light at six rim points + rising gold motes.
- **Haul yard:** the pit-head line runs on into the yard and fans into two sidings with ore carts waiting, ore heaps at the buffers and a crate platform.

#### Round 4 (P6a — the town re-planned to docs/refs/town-target.png; builders #7–#8)
- **Frame:** the whole-town view frames `TOWN_FRAME` (layout.ts) = the target image mapped to town units ({1800, 880, 2800, 1866}); the light reach runs 350–1150 units past the last bench with a ±300 noisy front so the terraces fill that frame and the edge of the known ground is organic, never the world rectangle. Requested P6b to use it for `townExtent()` / `frameAll()`.
- **Density:** ~150 buildings incl. landmarks (target "about 100–130" ordinary + landmarks), 3–6 figures wide (72–156), packed one-cell apart along the lanes, a ≤ 10 small-home gap pass; homes also on the flat tops of the high benches when on a pad, inside the frame (≤ 18, no door spots — no stair reaches them). Long rows are cut into blocks by **alleys** (`geo.alley`: borders of a jittered 330-unit Voronoi, only ≥ 84 from a street), kept clear by the plan and drawn as lane.
- **Lanes read:** block insides (≥ 20 from a lane) get a darker trodden-yard overlay (rock.ts `yard`), so the ground painter's ochre lanes read as ribbons; lanes on bench tops and alleys are drawn by the rock pass in `RAMPS.lane`.
- **Light (mine model):** day = albedo × (AMBIENT #827b6c + 0.5·pools); night = albedo × (AMB_NIGHT #7a8798 + 1.25·pools) × NIGHT_COL #d4dae4; pools come from TOWN-ART's `itemLights(it)` (≤ 2 per home, 75 % of homes lit) + the landmarks' own; live night lamps are tight cores (≤ 14 units), never orbs; the painters' lit openings are ×0.45 at town zoom. Measured whole-town lit-pixel luminance: day median 53 (q 44–63), dusk 41, night 45 (q 36–53, p95 91). Void: 0 non-black pixels over 133 789 Void samples.
- **Ground:** terrace tops / faces from `RAMPS.terraceTop` / `terraceFace`, crack lines with a lit chip and hatch clusters on every top; outer benches lifted toward the world's land colour (no ring); pits get a ledge rail with crystal carts, prop frames, a lattice crane and lip rubble.
- **Caps:** stalks in the buildings pass, caps on the live canopy layer at `capAlpha(24·A)`, ground shade `1 − capAlpha` (strip `screenshots/town-capfade-strip.png`).

#### Round 6 (P6a — blocks, crisp lanes, terraces with form; builder #9)
- **Blocks:** homes in rows along both sides of every street then lane (tangent to the lane edge, a shoulder apart, narrower widths tried before a gap; streets carry the bigger homes), widths 1.42× round 4, footprints = the drawn body, a doorstep kept free in front of every floor home, a small-home gap pass, a terrace share on the pads (not routed), fenced back yards (`yard`, engine-drawn: washing, racks, crates, shrub beds) in what is left; plaza apron r + 150. Voronoi alleys retired.
- **Lanes:** vector ribbons (verge, packed body `RAMPS.lane.mid`, worn crown, ruts, kerb stones on streets) — no per-cell rasters. Bench tops: 3× crack density in the frame, broken ledges with scree. The Keep: paved cast court, wall with corner towers and a west gate, cold lamps.
- **Light:** day AMBIENT #80817a, POOL_DAY 0.36, cap sun shadows by day; night AMB_NIGHT #4e5769, pools 1.5×; sky grade + night map one cached multiply. `setSky.wind` sways caps.

#### Round 5 (P2T successor 6 — cliff columns; adoption first, owner §19)
- **Walls are cliff faces of columns**, not boulders or single-peak masses: each stretch of face is cut by
  near-vertical chisel breaks into columns 10–76 units wide; neighbours share the exact break polyline (no gaps,
  unit-tested); heights random-walk so the skyline steps; each column has a lit chiselled top bevel (straight lower
  edge, deep at one end, shallow at the other, sometimes split), a side plane where it stands above its neighbour,
  an inner slanted chisel plane, and maybe a ledge or overhang (shadow under it) or an alcove. Breaks are firm ink
  (thinning in places); skyline and foot heavy. Fallen blocks + scree at the foot as before.
- **Depth shows in the rock** via new optional material fields `depth` (0 upper, 1 lower, 2 the Deep — from the
  sim's `depthOf`), `worked` (pick marks on cut faces, by ground type) and `veins` (violet crystal seams glowing
  into the rock, rich round heartvein / grave / sump): upper rock paler and dustier, lower darker and wetter (more
  damp streaks with a cool sheen, more glow-moss), the Deep darkest and colder with the most veins.
- **Floors:** depth darkens them; bare-rock floors (stone/hollow/flag/gallery styles) get embossed stone relief;
  footprints along walkways and beside rails; puddles in wet/low places (dark still water, wet ring, no rim).
- **Adoption:** `setMineGround(terrainMineGround)` (one call); proof page `dev/terrain.html?engine=1`.
- Owner §19 arrived mid-round: polishing stopped at this first cliff pass; no side-by-side refinement rounds.

#### Round 2 (P2T — sculpted terrain, look rebuilt after a 4/10 critic verdict)
- **Rock = lumps, not a cell lattice.** The Voronoi block faces, chamfers, brush streaks and sheen are gone. Rock is built from a list of *lumps* (core.ts §2): each a rotated ellipse blended 70/30 with a rounded six-facet polygon (12-norm of jittered cut planes) plus low harmonics, so outlines are irregular stones with flattish sides and soft corners; a pillow/dome height profile (1 − ρ²)^q (q 0.2–0.45: flat tops, round shoulders); draw order = its bottom edge (lower on screen = nearer = drawn over the farther one). Normals are analytic (dome gradient + the tilt of the face it sits on), so nothing is blurred at zoom 2.
- **Hierarchy:** north (visible) faces get tall columns standing on the floor (half-height 0.36–0.6 of the 62-unit face) and a smaller tier behind them near the lip; side faces get rounder masses sized by the band; **south lips get no lumps** (we look onto the rock top: an inked edge and a lit rim, as in the reference); medium lumps in front at the foot (sparse); a skirt of pebbles/small boulders at the foot in clusters; pebble clusters on floors (none on rail ballast, few on paths); stones on pool banks.
- **Between and above lumps:** gaps on north faces are a darker body of the same rock deepening into crevices next to lump edges; the rock top is a crisp flat dark-teal fill (`rock.top` × 0.62) with a crisp lit rim (3–5 units, two value steps) where it meets the lumps or a floor. Islands (enclosed rock) are solid tops; exterior rock is darker (× 0.35) and sinks into black smoothly (no band, no halo: the owner's rule).
- **Ink:** each lump's contour is centred on its edge, weight 2.3/1.8/1.25/0.85 units (big/mid/foot/pebble) × a swell along the line; lighter and broken on the side facing the key light; the outer half drawn over the farther lump is heavier (overlap), heavier still over the rock top (the outer silhouette); light broken inner creases on the lower side of some big lumps; ink colour is a dark teal (#06100f-ish), not black.
- **Form and light:** cool key from the upper left quantised into three soft steps per lump (painted, not rendered), occlusion just behind a nearer lump's edge (≤ 7 units, shaped), real lights by Lambert^1.3 on the lump normal (faces turned to a fire catch it, far sides stay teal) plus 42 % of the light's own colour on rock (teal catches gold, not only olive). A few crisp hatching strokes (0.64-unit lines, 2.1 apart, broken) on the shadow side of half the wall lumps.
- **Colour:** one gray-teal rock everywhere (`TEAL` in terrain/mine.ts); colour on rock comes only from light. Grounds muted (brown west/haul, gray-brown work, gray gallery/office, teal-gray crystal/damp, violet-gray hollow); patches have painted edges (smoothstep windows of ~0.05) and low contrast.
- **Floors:** tonal patches, worn darker stains, sparse grit, a darker ballast bed exactly under each rail (exact segment distance to `routes` of kind rail via `SegIndex`, 17 units half-width + gravel) with ruts at ±9 (the rails' gauge in `drawRail`); walking paths get only a lighter trodden centre with scuffs along the walking direction; grime at the wall foot (9 units); a few faint broken hairline cracks. Office paving: irregular slate stones (stretched Voronoi), raised lips, chipped joints, stones missing in patches and breaking up toward the walls.
- **Water:** deep dark teal, a lighter shelf near the shore (5–8 units), an inked bank with stones, soft broken reflections of nearby lights, sparse faint ripples.
- **Lights (terrain/mine.ts):** small and local: reach × torch 0.36, lamp/tally 0.38, shrine 0.56, crystal 0.45, god 0.4; falloff (1 − d²/r²)²; lights sit 26 units above a receiver plane capped at 26 (faces are painted, not 110-unit cliffs), so walls facing a light are lit and far sides stay in shadow; shaped cast shadows (penumbra 2 + 0.04·d units).
- **Domain:** `terrainDomain(spec)` = floor bbox + the rim on every side + 16; the default bake rect is that domain (it can start at negative coordinates — use `bake.rect` / `bake.dataRect` to place layers). Fixes the clipped north rock.
- **Performance:** each worker still builds the global fields and lumps, but the heights only within a light's reach of its band and the lightmap only under its band; data layer per band; lit/form leave the worker as ≈ 1 MB `ImageBitmap` strips and are drawn into the canvases ≤ 10 ms per frame; workers = min(4, cores − 1) so the main thread keeps a core; the harness builds the mine geometry in time slices.
- **API changes (compatible):** `TerrainBake.dataRect`, `BakeBuffers.dataRect/dataY0/dataFullH`, `BakeOptions.dataBand`, export `terrainDomain`. `GROUND_MATERIAL`, light reach/gain and the rock colour changed in terrain/mine.ts.

#### Round 3 (P2T — slab cliff faces, tiled worker bake; builders r3b + r3c)
- **Pipeline:** `prep.ts` (global 2-unit fields once: floor mask, signed distance, exterior, crevice band, rock-top distance, contours, light visibility as a polar map per light) → `slabs.ts` (contour loops → slab polygons on faces whose normal does not point south, scree, loose stones, niches) → `shade.ts` (class `Shader`, per-pixel shading of one tile from an object-ID raster and analytic polygon edge distances; no ray-marched shadows). `index.ts`: one worker prepares and clones the fields to a pool; tiles of 384 units come back as transferred `ImageBitmap`s; `drawTerrain()` draws the visible ones; `lit`/`form` are lazy whole canvases kept only for compatibility; `form` off by default.
- **Budget (measured, harness `?stats=1`):** whole mine 4564×2824 px at 2 px/unit, median wall 3.2 s at load 6–9 (6.0 s at load 14–16); no long task from terrain code (only a ~52 ms harness geometry task in setup); peak 61 MB worker arrays + 51 MB tiles = 112 MB.
- **Faces:** HC = 64 world units (the reference's north walls are about three figures tall); `kilnshrine` × 1.3. Slabs are chunky (narrowest ≈ 21 units), a minority reach the lip, most are mid or low blocks with a block stacked on top; broken top silhouettes, rounded corners (chips on most corners, a 3.2-unit bevel).
- **Colour regions:** one broad field (west/north-west → teal stone and teal-gray floors, east/south → neutral gray stone and floors) decides each slab's colour (per slab, never mid-face) and the floor tint; floors' albedos are set so that under the cool ambient they land on the reference's floor colours. Warm light desaturates the albedo first (rock 0.74, floor 0.85), so lit stone goes olive-gold / ochre, never lime; rock answers light 1.5× the floor.
- **Light visibility for faces:** a wall receiver is tested from above its foot with a margin that grows with its height (else every face lit from the front was cut off by its own floor edge).
- **Floors:** finer grain, calm mottling, hash-placed grit (pits and tiny pebbles with contact shadows; density higher near walls), cracks only on dry floor. Lamps broader and weaker (reach ≈ 0.5–0.55 of the lightmap radius) so pools read as lit areas, not spots; crystal 0.3, fungus 0.45; the old `water` light is off (no glow at pools).
- **Pools:** irregular bank (two-scale wobble), bank face under the north shore, depth gradient, broken sheen lines, glints.
- **Niche:** a rough-hewn opening (jittered outline, square-ish shoulders), hewn reveals, fire glow low at the foot, an inner ink line.
- **Boundary:** outer contour ink → exterior rock fades to exact `#000` within 4 units; outward profiles in the final exterior run (script `lum.py` with the shader's `ext` debug mask): S/W max rise ≤ 0.5, N 3.9, E 7.0 (≤ 0.8 % of profiles > 3 levels, anti-aliasing at slab corners).
- **Palette (non-black pixels, ours vs mine.png):** teal 33.7 / 26.4 %, neutral 47.8 / 50.5 %, warm 13.7 / 16.7 %, lime 0.0 / 0.3 %, mean saturation 0.26 / 0.257, p90 luminance 91.9 / 98.9.
- **Light levels (r3c final):** torch gain 1.1, lamp 1.0 (reach 0.52 / 0.55); lit rock answers 1.25× the floor; the shrine floor albedo is brown (`#88624a`) and warm-ground zones get no teal tint.

#### Round 3d (P2T — rock masses of stacked rounded blocks; builder r3d)
- **Walls are masses, not fences.** The reference (3× crops) builds a wall as a rounded mound of fitted, bulging, rounded-angular blocks about as wide as tall, stacked 2–4 deep, set back and darker toward the top. `slabs.ts` section 3 now lays rows of such blocks; the slab layout of round 3 is gone.
- **Frame:** blocks are placed in the contour's own frame (s along the wall, v into the rock by the visible face depth `e·n`). A side wall therefore carries round blocks seen edge-on, not vertical shards. The frame uses a separate, heavily smoothed normal (±48 units, never more than ~50° from the local one). With the ±15-unit normal, the (u, v) frame spread 2–3× at v = 50 on wiggly contours, and rows either broke into gaps or crushed into shards.
- **Stacking:** each pass lays one row, and every block rests on a support height map (per unit of arc length) left by the blocks below it, foot tucked 3–6 units behind them. So rows are uneven and no joint falls within 5 units of a joint below. Rows step in world units at the block's height.
- **Block sizes:**
  - Row heights are 46/37/32/28… units, times 0.8–1.5 for the local face depth, so taller faces get bigger blocks.
  - The floor row has 20 % anchor boulders 1.4–1.8 rows tall; about 22 % of all blocks are small fill.
  - Widths run 0.62–1.07 × height.
  - Tops stop 1.5–5 units past the lip, and squat cap stones are narrowed, so there are no planks and no spikes.
- **Outline:** 5–8 corners on a squarish superellipse frame, flatter at the foot. Each corner is rounded by a short bezier, with low-order bulges and a little shear. Blocks are rigid (they do not bend round corners).
- **Shading:** blended top, front and side planes along slightly wandering soft boundaries, plus a convex body term and a rolled rim. A painter's stronger key on rock (albedo × 0.66–1.16 by n·key) gives a lit upper left and a dark lower right. There is soft occlusion at the foot and in the lower and right joints. Upper rows are darker, and skyline blocks roll back into the dark. Ink is heavy at the foot and on the skyline, light on the lit left. 40 % of blocks get a plane-break line, ~30 % a crack, and 26 % hatching (side plane and foot only).
- **Warm highlight:** each light adds a highlight in its own warm colour on rock planes facing it (n·l > 0.45, squared). Strength: blocks 1 (falling by row), big stones 0.5, pebbles 0.12. Lit rock now goes properly gold.
- **Shrine:** `kilnshrine` wall scale is 1.8 and the niche 60×74. Two anchor jambs flank the opening, and the rows above rest on a support preset over the arch (lintel boulders), so it reads as a recess carved into a mound.
- **Floors:**
  - **Stones:** drawn stones with contact shadows, clustered on a 14-unit hash grid. Pebble clusters sit at wall feet; flat chips sit round the warm work lights (new optional `spots` argument of `buildObjects`). Stones are reference-sized (r 1.2–6.2). Rail beds and the centres of paths stay clear.
  - **Texture:** soft darker blotches and two faint broken ruts along paths. The light is embossed on the grain, so lit dirt shows its texture instead of a cloud.
  - **Colour:** floors in the gray region are neutral, with no teal cast.
- **Dark areas:** the island-top fade and the crevices desaturate as they darken.
- **Light levels:** LGAIN 0.82, ambient base 0.41, rock light response 1.5, torch/lamp gain 1.3/1.1, shrine reach 0.48. The exterior fade is `(1 − dT/3)²`.
- **Measured:** see `handoffs/P2-terrain.md` round 3d (palette, boundary profiles, bake).

#### Round 2 (P3 audio rework, critic round 1 at 6.5/10)
All sound stays synthesised at runtime (no files, nothing fetched; the sfx-skill at `owoevan-dev/sfx-skill`, MIT, was used only as a design and measurement reference — its loudness conventions, "brief" = loudest 200 ms, are borrowed). Nobody can listen here, so every claim below is a measurement made by `node e2e/audio-check.cjs` (offline renders through the real graph) and recorded in `screenshots/audio/report.json`. This section supersedes the round-1 loudness numbers above (beds "−18 to −14", sfx peak targets).

**Measurement rules.** OfflineAudioContext at 48 kHz through the real `AudioCore` graph at default volumes (master 0.85, music 0.8, sfx 0.9). A **0.6 s pre-roll** of silence runs before anything is measured: Chromium's `DynamicsCompressor` attenuates ~12 dB for its first ~200 ms, which is what made the round-1 trims meaningless. Beds are pumped in 0.5 s chunks with the live 0.7 s look-ahead. Loudness is BS.1770-4 (K-weighted, gated integrated; EBU 3342 LRA; "brief" = loudest 200 ms for one-shots). True peak is 4× oversampled (`measure.truePeak`). "Small speaker" = a 24 dB/oct high-pass at 200 Hz.

**Loudness targets.**
- **Beds:** −21 LUFS integrated ±1 over the window each scene really plays (cutscene 74 s, bonus 150 s, whisper 180 s, watershed 40 s, shade 180 s, ending 60 s, defeat 60 s), true peak ≤ −1 dBTP, quietest 1 s window ≥ −49 dBFS (no dead air), LRA ≥ 3 LU (shade ≥ 5), envelope autocorrelation ≤ 0.5 at every lag from 5 s to half the window (no audible loop), ≤ 6 LU lost through the 200 Hz high-pass. `SCENE_TRIM` (linear gain) is calibrated to this: cutscene 1.16, bonus 1.32, whisper 1.85, watershed 0.83, shade 1.6, ending 1.0, defeat 1.3.
- **Danger is colour, not level:** intensity and hunger layers play on the bed's `tensionOut`, which bypasses a core duck that steps the bed back as they rise (`duckIntensityDb` 3 dB, `duckHungerDb` 2.2 dB at full, both scaled by level² so the duck follows the layers' own steep gain curves — with a 1.2 exponent a half-danger bed sagged −1.6 LU), so at intensity 1 / hunger 1 / both / half the bed moves ≤ ±1.5 LU while its fingerprint changes (tremolo, pulse, high section, heartbeat, gurgles). The long-lived danger layers are only started while their level is above zero, and stop `LINGER` 6 s after it returns to zero.
- **Sfx ladder** (`SFX_TIER_LUFS`, brief loudness alone, mean of 3 seeds): tier 0 soft UI **−31** (uiHover, textBlip) · tier 1 UI **−26.5** (clicks, cards, choices, denied) · tier 2 routine actions **−22** (≈ the bed, so they read as part of the music) · tier 3 events **−17.5** (eventOpen, miracle(s), gorge, purge, hungerPang, levelUp) · tier 4 danger and stage moments **−13** (crusadeHorn, inquisitor, thresholdAlarm, maliceOutburst, ravenous, stageUp, victory, defeat). Each sfx within ±1.5 LU of its tier, spread inside a tier ≤ 1.5 LU, gap between adjacent tiers ≥ 2.5 LU, true peak ≤ −1 dBTP. Per-sfx `trim` in `SFX_META` holds the calibration. Over the loudest gameplay bed (shade at full danger) the tier-4 alerts must raise the loudest 200 ms of the mix by ≥ 3 LU, and UI ticks must raise the 1–5 kHz presence band (25 ms windows) by ≥ 3 dB — so ticks stay quiet but are never masked.

**What changed per defect.**
1. *No loudness hierarchy* → trims re-measured after the pre-roll; the five-tier ladder above, enforced per sfx, per tier and between tiers.
2. *Pitch applied twice (10 call sites)* → each recipe receives the already-jittered pitch `k.p` once; drip/heartbeat/gurgle/crystalGain/inquisitor take it as an argument instead of re-reading it. `pitchCheck` renders every sfx at pitch 1 and 2 with the same seed and requires every strong partial to land at exactly 2× (±17 cents).
3. *`whisper` was hiss* → `synthbuf.ts` resynthesised it as a voice: tilted breath noise through four gliding formant band-passes (F1–F4, audible F2/F3 motion) with a second, lower formant set mixed under it (a tract ~1.3× longer: the slightly inhuman god), sibilance band-limited to ~2.5–7 kHz, a soft aspirated 'h' and a breathy 'k', a reversed-reverb swell into the first consonant, a 24 dB/oct low-pass at 8 kHz, every variant RMS-normalised. Required: ≥ 60 % of its energy in 300 Hz–4 kHz and ≤ 8 % above 8 kHz, both for the synth buffer and the rendered sfx.
4. *Hunger inaudible on small speakers* → the heartbeat is a stack (a lightly saturated falling thump, a chest resonance at ~150–260 Hz, a damp noise band, a soft skin click); the growl is two beating saws around 52 Hz saturated so their harmonics fill 150–900 Hz, through two wandering gut formants (~300 and ~560 Hz) with a ~30 Hz rasp; the gurgle is resonant brown noise in the low mids. The hunger layer alone must lose ≤ 6 LU through the 200 Hz high-pass.
5. *Beds +5 LU at maximum danger* → tension bypasses the core duck (above); ≤ ±1.5 LU enforced.
6. *Bonus 12 s loop* → the held breath has no clock: the drone's filter and level wander, held voices arrive at random gaps with random lengths and voicings around the frozen sigh.
7. *Shade never rests* → a `FormPlan` of sections (pulse, halftime, procession, drift, swell, hush; 20–70 s, never the same twice in a row, the first six composed so every kind is heard once). Each sets drum pattern, choir, wind, drone, crowd and melody levels; `hush` is wind and bells alone.
8. *Motif fatigue* → new variants (sighs, climb, diminish, mirror, free lines of 4–8 notes that end on a tonic chord tone), section flavours that weight them, long rests; a unit test over 30 min of mine and town music (3 seeds) bounds how often the literal motif appears.
9. *Defeat silent tail* → the collapse is followed by a long aftermath (mourning choir chords every 8–22 s, wind swells, deep glass echoes, drips) that keeps breathing at a lower level; enforced by the no-dead-air window over 60 s.
10. *Check too loose* → `e2e/audio-check.cjs` rewritten (below).
- Minor: `soulCrystallize` became a soul hardening (a soft exhale thickening into detuned inharmonic glass partials that converge, then a tight ping and a settling thud); `textBlip` a quill-on-parchment breath consonant (one soft noise band and a faint body, low variance because it plays every few letters); `crusadeHorn` reaches −4 dB in 90 ms (lip blat + scoop); per-name voice caps (`maxVoices`, default 4, long sounds 1–2) plus the global 24-voice cap, and a **repeat attenuation** for streams of short sounds that never overlap enough for a cap to bite (each other play of the same sfx in the last 150 ms takes 0.75 dB off, at most 6 dB; the cutscene's typewriter blips at ~17/s sit ~1.5 dB under a single blip); spam of 8 plays in 400 ms must keep simultaneous voices ≤ cap, true peak ≤ −1 dBTP and the loudness rise ≤ 6 LU over one play; at most 2 beds exist at any time (`MAX_BEDS`), also while the context is suspended and its clock stands still; the dev `SOLO` global is gone (solo is a `CoreOptions` field used only by the offline probes); the seed comes from `createAudio({ seed })`, `setSeed(runSeed)` (hashed by `audioSeedFromRun`) or a fresh random seed per session.

**How to re-measure.** `NODE_PATH=/usr/local/lib/node_modules_global node e2e/audio-check.cjs` (full, ~5–15 min depending on load; `--quick` shortens the bed windows to 60 s and is for iteration only — its shade LRA and loop checks are not meaningful). It starts its own Vite in-process with HMR and watching off on port 5203 (`PORT` overrides), renders ~235 jobs on 4 pages, prints one summary (sfx ladder by tier, whisper bands, closest sfx pair, bed table, danger deltas, headroom, crossfades, live engine) and exits 1 with one line per failed threshold. All thresholds live in the `T` table at the top of the script. Pure measurement code (`src/audio/measure.ts`) is unit-tested in `tests/audio/round2.test.ts` (997 Hz sine = −3.01 LUFS, the HPF slope, loop detector, LRA, true peak on an fs/4 sine).

**Measured (full check, 2026-11-23, all 235 jobs pass, 0 console errors/warnings).** Sfx tiers: 0 −31.3..−31.1 · 1 −27.1..−26.4 · 2 −22.4..−21.8 · 3 −17.8..−17.1 · 4 −13.9..−12.9 (spreads 0.2–1.0 LU, gaps 3.2–4 LU), every true peak ≤ −1.1 dBTP. Whisper: 88 % of the voice buffer's energy and 61 % of the rendered sfx's in 300 Hz–4 kHz, ≤ 0.2 % above 8 kHz. Pitch doubles every strong partial of all 34 tonal sfx. Beds: −21.2..−20.5 LUFS, true peaks −6.2..−3.2 dBTP, LRA 5.7–9.2 (shade 8.8 over 3 min), loop autocorrelation ≤ 0.34, HPF loss ≤ 5 LU. Danger deltas (90 s): whisper −0.7 / −0.1 / +0.1 / −0.8 and shade −0.5 / −0.1 / −0.2 / −0.5 LU (intensity 1 / hunger 1 / both / both 0.5); the hunger layer alone loses 2.2–2.3 LU through the HPF (round 1: 15.9). Alerts rise 7.0–8.4 LU over shade at full danger; UI ticks +3.0–6.5 dB of presence. Spam: ≤ cap voices, rise ≤ 5.4 LU.


#### P3 round 3 (critic r2 at 6.5/10; builder + successor)
- **Ramp anchoring:** every automated gain is tracked (`dsp.ts` anchor/setAt/linTo/sweepTo/glide); a move always starts with an explicit `setValueAtTime` of the modelled current value, so no ramp ever starts from a stale anchor. Fade-step probe: every fade start ≤ 0.07 % of local level (was 4.5–84 %); unit test `tests/audio/round3.test.ts` fails on any step > 1 %.
- **Brightness:** instrument cutoffs and partials opened by the round-3 builder; the successor added `SCENE_AIR_DB` (2.8 kHz high shelf on all three bed outputs: whisper 2, watershed 9, defeat 5, shade 2, ending 3 dB). A 10 dB shelf on the mine put its 4 kHz octave above the town's, so the mine got only 2. 4 kHz rel 250 Hz now: cutscene −17.5, bonus −10.8, whisper −17.2, watershed −13, shade −13.4, ending −15, defeat −22.9 (r2: −27, −24, −31, −27, −28, −36, −47); centroid whisper 243 Hz vs shade 479 Hz, so the town is clearly the brighter one. Below 60 Hz ≤ 11 % everywhere.
- **Loudness:** `SCENE_TRIM` recalibrated (cutscene 1.229, bonus 1.32, whisper 1.97, watershed 0.652, shade 1.213, ending 0.966, defeat 1.38) → every bed −21 ±0.6 LUFS over its play window (shade 240 s −21.3, short-term −27.3…−17.9, LRA 5.6).
- **Noise loops:** wind driven by `wander()` per side, fresh noise per side → wave autocorr ≤ 0.02, L/R corr ≈ 0; rumble ≤ 0.06, L/R ≈ 0.
- **Danger in the town:** shade `dangerTrim` 2.1, core duck 1.8/1.4 dB.
- **Phase:** `setPhase('night'|'day')` crossfades day-only layers (work rhythm on the bed's grid, carts, lift wheel, brighter layer) and a +2.5 dB shelf; day measures +2.2 LU (mine) / +0.7 LU (town) over night, 2–5 kHz share ×3.6 in the mine.
- **The crystal loop (§15) sound set:** a six-step strike ladder (`strikeDull`…`strikeImpossible`; one shared recipe, every step adds a layer; tiers 1/2/2/3/3/4; the top step ducks the music −24 dB for ~1 s via the new `SfxMeta.duckHold`); `pickSwing` self-varied (clean / glancing / stuck); pocketing, cart, Tally, search, offering, reveal (caller passes the rarity as pitch), cards (draw, hover, miracle vs evil play, skip), crystal burn, cut, crack, flaw, graft, socket, AP, routines, Move, blessing, a follower's own evil. Continuous moments are ambience streams, not one-shots: `procession` (a hummed chord that grows from one voice to five with the walking followers) and `grave` (one soft high tone at a time, denser with more set crystals; at most about two overlap, so it never becomes a wash). Stinger `bestFind`. Every new name is calibrated on the tier ladder (mean of 3 seeds, within ±0.1 LU after trim). Send sounds stay playable but are unlisted. FEEL's four-rarity names alias the ladder (common → Clear, uncommon → Bright, rare → Royal, legendary → Impossible).
- Everything is still synthesised at runtime; nothing is fetched; no new dependency.
- **Successor 2 — sound design judged from pictures, not code.** Every sfx is rendered (3 seeds) through the real offline graph (`src/audio/devtools/audition/render.cjs`) and drawn as waveform + log spectrogram with numbers (`audition/sheet.py`: attack, length to −40 dB, centroid, band shares, noise share, pitch track, cuts, seed variation, loudness vs tier) → `screenshots/audio-sfx-sheet-<family>.png`. Families are one list (`devtools/families.ts`) shared by the sheets and the bench's sound board (`dev/audio.html`: every sound grouped by family, plus sequences: the ladder, a shift of swings, the count, the offering, the hand, affinities, tail finds, a storm, the Day at ×4).
- **What the pictures showed and what changed:** the strike ladder got *darker* above Bright (centroid 1813 → 658 → 754 → 375 Hz; Impossible 51 % below 150 Hz from a 36 Hz drum and a D2 pad) → re-voiced so every grade adds brightness, ring and partials (Blazing: a beating octave pair and sparks; Royal: a five-note arpeggio, a bright bell, a high choir; Impossible: a D-major struck-glass chord D5–A7 over a short blow, a cascade to D8, a far bell). `pickSwing` rang at a steady 1.4 kHz (pickHit's sine ring = an audible pitch; seeds alike; the stuck kind −11.5 LU) → `pickBlow`: a noise impact at a random centre, an inharmonic iron tick under 30 ms, chips, a light body; `pickStrike` is its clean kind. `tallyShort`'s low reed chord (centroid 236 Hz, 1.2 s) read as a buzzer → two soft organ tones a semitone apart in the middle register sagging together. `crystalReveal` was a bare two-note ping → a breath drawn in, then the note in the crystal's material with its fifth and octave opening, a warm under-note and glints. `devour` (3.6 s, 57 % below 150 Hz, a saturated growl and a choir moan) → 1.7 s: a caught breath cut off, a wet crush and bone, one chew, a swallow, a low exhale. `scream` and the follower's far cry are cut off mid-breath (30 ms) and the room carries the rest. `crystalBurn` crackles louder.
- **Affinity (§15.10 D):** `SfxOptions.affinity` picks the ring's material (`materialOf` + `ring` in sfx.ts): hunger wet, malice inharmonic metal, faith bell, fear trembling breath, blood pulsing, shadow hollow and reversed, stone wooden knock, echo repeats, ember crackling, frost very high; other strings hash onto one. Same pitch ladder by grade. `oneIn ≥ 10000` adds a tail-find layer (a climbing shimmer; + a choir from 1e5; + a far bell from 1e6) and ducks the music 10 dB.
- **New sounds:** enchant, curse, recombine, tribute (tier 0, furtive), facilityGained, forgeJewel, giveItem, itemExposed (+ FEEL's aliases facility / forge / give / exposed; `SFX_ALIASES`); thunderNear / thunderFar, chapelBell, shiftWhistle, dayNote / dayNoteHeavy. `crack` / `flaw` / `graft` and the Send sounds are `SFX_DEPRECATED` (still playable).
- **Weather and daily life (§16):** ambience kinds rain, rainMud, storm, fog (also a high shelf down to −10 dB on every ambience stream: the world goes muffled), seep (the mine's answer underground), tavern, hymn, road, dogs, night. The Day fast-forward: `setPhase` fades scaled by the caller to the speed; reversal mid-fade and skipping are anchored (unit test + fade-step probe scenario).
- **Watershed entry:** the outgoing bed fades over 5.5 s (`WATERSHED_OUT` in core.ts) under the rise, and an extra glass lands at 1.3 s; `transitionProbe` now reports where the minimum is (`minAt`) and a momentary series.
- **Procession:** its 60 s picture showed a 4.3 Hz beat at 20 dB (the choir's ±8.5-cent chorus + per-event jitter) → `choir(..., spread)` and exact pitches for the hum (spread 4 cents).
#### P3 theme round (successor 3, 2026-11-23; docs/theme.md §9, DECISIONS §16, §19)
- **The wickfolk voice** (`src/audio/wick.ts`): five synthesised phonemes — a tongue *click* (a 3–6 ms burst with a tiny ring), a *chirp* (a voiced glide up = a question, down = a statement), a *trill* (the voice chopped 18–30×/s; rising = laughter), a closed-mouth nasal *hum*, a *gasp* — through a big head's narrow formants (≈ 760 / 1850 / 3300 Hz, higher than a human's), from speakers drawn by age (children 560–860 Hz, adults 260–470, elders 200–290) and distance (lowpass + room). Four moods shape it: **calm** (hums and chirps, slow, the odd laugh), **uneasy** (click runs, short chirps, wide wavering vibrato, quieter), **fervent** (dense, trills, rising, louder), **afraid** (high, quick, falling chirps, gasps). `setMood` glides from one to the next over ~4 s by mixing phrases from both. Grief is one long low hum taken up by others (`griefHum`); many voices humming one chord (`humChord`) are the Temple, a crowd taking up a hum, the procession's cousin. No human murmur, no words, nothing pitched like a human voice.
- **Re-voiced to the theme:** no dogs (the kind is removed; a `dogs` key plays `beasts`: pit-beast lowing — a low nasal groan through a shell, rough with a throat flutter — shell scrapes, skitters scuttling with chitter); the market and tavern are wickfolk talk, clay chits and cups, a shell-lyre instead of a fiddle; the road is a pit-beast train, not a horse; the night is near silence (far drips in a big space, a breath in the eaves, a rare skitter or moth; no crickets, no owl); the Temple (`hymn`) is low hummed chords, braziers' roar and crackle and a brass bowl, never an organ; the Council's quarter is the cold beating hum of shard-lamps, guards stamping, iron, a far struck plate; the mine's shard-lamps hum thin and cold with a lamp-moth now and then. One-shots: the shift signal ("the whistle" in the text: `shiftWhistle`, `dawnBell`, `duskBell`) is a horn-pipe — a hollow, breathy blown horn that scoops into its note; the Flame's call (`chapelBell`) is three strokes of the Temple's great brass bowl over a held hum; `crowdTurned` is a ripple of wickfolk voices gathering into a hummed chord; `scream` / the follower's far cry are a wickfolk shriek (higher, wick formants, a trembling trill) cut off. New: `councilPlate`, `wickCall`, `wickLaugh`, `wickGrief`, `beastLow`.
- **The grave's own voice** (`nether` ambience): a D1/D2 sub pair and a brown rumble breathing on two incommensurate slow cycles, with a throat of harmonics (110–220 Hz) that comes in above ~0.3 so small speakers hear it; loudest at the grave, under the town faintly.
- **One call for the beds** (`audio.setView`, pure mapping `view.ts` `mixForView`): place (mine / grave / town / valley), zoom (far = less detail, more wind), the Day's clock (`phase`, `t`, `rate`), weather, what is near, the lift moving, the Flame's day, the mood, Nether → every ambience level, the Night/Day form of the bed (dusk closes it), glide and crossfade times scaled by the Day's speed. The market peaks at midday, the tavern fills towards dusk, rain empties the lanes and fills the tavern. WEATHER's own levels (`setWeather(weather.state.audio)`) override the guessed ones; `thunder(strength, delay)` schedules a crack (≤ 0.3 s) or a roll.
- **Mixer:** ambience has its own bus and volume (`setVolume('ambience')`, default 0.8, persisted with the others in `hungrygod.audio.v1`), with its own copy of the music bus's reverbs and tilt so it sounds as before; a duck takes the music fully and the ambience at 70 % of its depth in dB. `dayNoteHeavy` ducks −5 dB (music) / −3.5 (ambience) for ~0.6 s. Every ambience kind has a calibration trim (`AMB_TRIM_DB`) to a place on `AMB_TARGET_LUFS` (washes 6–8 LU under the −21 bed, sparse things far under); sfx trims recalibrated on a three-seed mean for every name that was off by ≥ 0.6 LU (the predecessor's pending names: picks, strikes, thunder, horn, notes; and the new ones). Limiter ceiling lowered to −1 dBFS (soft from −2.2; the 4× oversampled shaper after the bus compressor): with the strike ladder, picks and thunder at their tiers a ×1 Day could touch −0.3 dBTP.
- Everything is still synthesised at runtime; no external content (the sfx-skill fallback was not needed); no CREDITS row.

### P5-fix — Settings and layout fit
Owner, after playing the hosted (iframe) build: "I need settings to adjust volume and stuff and the bottom cuts off."
- **One settings panel for every scene, owned by the app shell.** `src/ui/prefs.ts` is the store (one localStorage key `hg.settings.v1`, sanitized, every access guarded, applied live through `subscribe`); `src/ui/settings.ts` is the panel. The shell (`src/app/app.ts`) keeps one always-present button in the top-right corner of the stage: gear + the word "Settings" on the title and defeat screens, a gear with a tooltip everywhere else, dimmed to 62 % during the cutscene (its Skip stays bottom-right). It opens by mouse alone; Esc is only a shortcut (the cutscene keeps Esc = skip; the play scene uses Esc to cancel first, then opens settings).
- **Opening it pauses the world:** the shell stops calling `scene.update` and freezes transitions (the cutscene's timeline and its cue sounds stop with it), and music ducks to 35 % until the panel closes or the Music slider is touched. Destructive run actions (Restart run / New run / Title screen) confirm in-page; `confirm()` is never used (blocked in the host).
- **Contents and defaults:** Sound — Master 80, Music 70, Effects 80 (on first run the values the audio engine remembered are adopted), Mute off, Mute in the background on. Display — Fullscreen (live, not stored; disabled with a note where the host forbids it), Interface size 90/100/115/130 % (default 100), Brightness 80–130 % (CSS filter on the canvas, default 100), Reduce motion off (kills HUD pulses/shakes, dims screen flashes to a third, and makes every camera move land at once). Game — Dawn replay Normal / Fast (×2.5) / Skip, Skip the opening off, Show hints on + "Show hints again", Pan speed Slow/Normal/Fast (arrow keys ×0.55/1/1.7), Invert zoom off. Keys — the active scene's own list (`Scene.keys()`). Run — only while a run is active. The audio engine has three buses (master/music/sfx), so there are no Voices/Ambience sliders.
- **Two units:** `--su` = CSS px per canvas unit (positions tied to the picture, e.g. the title column under the eye) and `--u` = the HUD unit (sizes). `--u` = stage scale × Interface size, but never below 0.9 px, so the smallest HUD text (10 units) is never under 9 CSS px; it is capped so the HUD's virtual stage stays ≥ 760×420 units. The shell publishes the HUD's virtual size as `#ui[data-fit]` — `wide` (≥ 1480 units), `mid` (1100–1480), `narrow` (< 1100) — and `data-short` (< 640 units tall); scenes switch to compact layouts from those, never from window size.
- **The stage sizes from `#app`'s real box** (ResizeObserver + visualViewport + orientationchange), not `window.innerWidth`; `#app` is an in-flow 100 % box inside the root's content box, so a host page's `:root` safe-area padding or an iframe is respected. The 16:9 stage is always fully visible (letterboxed) and nothing in the HUD may leave it — `e2e/layout-check.cjs` asserts this numerically for 10 viewports × every scene.
- **The miracle hand is compact and always fully on screen:** each card at rest is a face (glyph · name · cost) inside the stage; hovering, keyboard focus, being the card in targeting, or a first tap (touch) opens its details upward (summary, flavour, power, target, affinity, Malice, rarity, blocker, key). The rich tooltip on cards is gone (the card is the tooltip). Narrow HUDs put the hand at the left, the End Night block at the right; the inspector always stops above the End Night block; the left column (objectives + chronicle) stops above the hand.
- **Hints point at HUD elements** (the hand, End Night, the Suspicion gauges) and are clamped inside the stage, instead of fixed canvas coordinates.
- **Very short frames (round 2): a tiny layout, not smaller text.** When the HUD's virtual stage is under 400 units tall or 640 wide (`isTiny`, e.g. 1015×240 → 473×267, 640×300 → 592×333) the shell sets `#ui[data-tiny="1"]` and text stays ≥ 9 px. Play: the top bar drops its labels and sub-numbers (icons + values; every section keeps its tooltip) and fits in two short rows; the goals and the chronicle fold behind one "Goals" button under the top bar (C toggles it; opening it closes the inspector and selecting someone closes it); miracle cards become chips (glyph + cost) whose details — name first — float upward on hover/focus/tap; Eat and End Night sit side by side bottom-right without their sub-lines and key badges (the keys still work and are listed under Settings → Keys); the inspector is narrower and drops the bio. Title: smaller name, no tag line, small buttons; and on any frame the title column measures itself and is pulled up just enough that its bottom stays inside the stage.
- **Hints (owner: "Some of the Got its don't disappear").** The cause was the hint's × button, which carried a tooltip reading "Got it": clicking removed the button, `mouseleave` never fired, and the "Got it" tooltip stayed on screen. Now: (1) a tooltip hides itself as soon as its target is clicked, removed from the page, or slides away (a per-frame check while one is open), for every tooltip in the game; (2) the hint's button is a plain labeled "Got it" with no tooltip; (3) a hint that has been on screen is remembered as seen whatever closed it — Got it, Esc, doing what it says, or dawn — so it never comes back until Settings → "Show hints again" (§13.10 F); (4) Esc closes an open hint before anything else; (5) the next hint never replaces one in the same instant (hints are checked only after an action); (6) a hint is placed where it covers no HUD panel (hand, End Night block, inspector, goals, chronicle, top bar), choosing the least-overlapping spot nearest its anchor; an open miracle card's details draw above a hint. `e2e/layout-check.cjs` plays three nights through the real UI (Got it on every hint, settings opened and closed, dawn, title → Continue, reload → Continue), then forces every remaining hint id through the debug hook: each must be gone within 300 ms of Got it, none may return, Esc must close one without opening settings.
- **Reading a miracle card takes the floor (round 2):** while a card's details are open (hover, focus, tap, or the card being targeted), a hint steps aside, and on narrow HUDs the goals/chronicle column fades out so the details never sit on top of it; everything returns when the card closes. Narrow details are capped to the space under the top bar and scroll. The hint component (`src/ui/hints.ts`, reusable by any scene) re-places itself every 200 ms while open, so it moves out of the way when a panel opens; when no free spot exists it yields (hidden, not dismissed) until there is room. Final matrix: 12 frames × 15 scene states + the hint run, all pass (`e2e/layout-check.cjs`, run on a static build of the round's code). The Whisper scene was then removed by PLAY-v2; what carries over is the shell, settings, layout units/flags, the hint component and the tooltip fix.

### VFX — Effects
- One module, `src/render/fx/` (`createFx()`, `fx.spawn(kind, params) → handle`, `fx.update(dt)`, `fx.draw(g, host, 'ground'|'above')`); the binding list of kinds and params lives at the top of `handoffs/VFX.md`. World units are town units; every size inside an effect is in **figure heights** (`host.figureAt`, default 6 town units inside the mine pit, 24 in the town), so one effect reads the same next to a miner and next to a townsperson.
- Look: hand-inked. Every light shape has a dark ink body or edge (tapered ribbons stroked in ink, wobbly broken rings, branching veins, inked sigils), light is additive glow on top. Families: violet/ink = the god; ochre = lamps and the lamp-world; steel-blue = the Council; gold/kiln-orange = the Temple; blood-red/black = evil acts (heavier, darker, shuddering). Ground effects are squashed by the 3/4 view (`GY = 0.58`).
- Every effect has anticipation → hit → settle; persistent ones (`whisper` until `resolve()`, `sendRoute`, `lampSweep`, `hunger`, `lieLow`, `inquisitor`, `lockdown`) settle out on `end()`.
- Far zoom: when a figure is under ~26 px on screen the effect cross-fades to a fixed-screen-size inked beacon (pulse rings, pip, optional sigil and light spike), coloured by family; Divinity's veins keep a fixed screen width so they read at whole-town zoom.
- Void: every primitive tests `host.visibilityAt` (ribbons/rings split at Void points, glows shrink to fit, cones shorten); the gallery pixel-checks that no effect pixel lands in the Void. Nothing is drawn above heads permanently; result sigils stand at the feet or rise and fade.
- Budget: struct-of-arrays particle pool (cap 900), pooled effect instances, cached glow/cone sprites and glyph paths, no per-frame allocation in the hot paths. Reduce motion: peak flashes capped (`D.flash` ≤ 0.18 alpha), expanding beacon pulses replaced by steady rings, fewer particles.

#### VFX round 2 (the mining loop, §15)
- **Six rarity looks, four axes each:** Dull gray-violet, Clear ice, Bright violet, Deep indigo, Royal rose with gold sparks, Impossible white with violet. A tier is never told by colour alone: size, ceremony length (held beat from Bright; the room darkens from Royal; Impossible holds ~1.6 s before the break) and light rays (0/0/3/4/6/16) escalate together.
- **The offering reveal is the reward beat:** every crystal is set down as a rough dark husk (§15.5 "crystals come out rough"), the grave inhales (darkness pools, motes drawn in, light leaking through cracks, the husk trembles), then it breaks and the stone rises into its tier's light. Staggered with `delay` when several arrive.
- **Crystal bodies have one drawing function** (`shape.ts`, `setCrystalDrawer`) so HUD's seeded shape can replace the stand-in shard everywhere at once.
- **Cards are data-driven:** a card id plays its own effect if it has one, else its family (`light`, `growth`, `sickness`, `fear`, `fire`, `silence`, `blood`), registered with `setCardFamily`. A card in flight from the hand is a UI-space object (it may cross the Void like the HUD does); it lands in the world and from there every pixel obeys the Void rule.
- **The Tally is chalk:** the overseer's tally strokes on the ground at the lift (fives with a slash) are diegetic marks, not labels; a short count ends in a sour slash and the Council's steel ring.
- **Void check is a diff:** the gallery compares each frame with and without effects, counting changes on pixels whose whole footprint is Void; primitives test visibility with a margin for stroke width and antialiasing (`D.visR`), and lifted shapes test where they are drawn, not their ground point.
- **Perf:** veins batch into one path per call and hairline veins draw as one shared polyline. The stress dawn (78 effects, ~750 particles, 1600×900) is fill-rate bound on this software-rendered box; see `handoffs/VFX.md` for numbers.
- `sendRoute`/`sendPreview` dropped per §15 (still registered so old callers do not break).

#### VFX round 5 (feedback where the player looks)
- **A wick moment is told on the floor, not only on the crown.** At play zoom a figure is ~36–45 px and the bud ~3 px, so each wick effect also lights the ground round the feet the way lamps and crystals light the floor in `mine.png`: turn = the gold pool flickers and dies, a beat of dark, a violet flood with two rings spreading ~2.6 figure heights and a flick of light running off toward the grave, then the violet pool with short veins pointing home stays ~6 s (`wickPool`); death = the pool swells once and pinches out, a pale ring goes out across the floor, unlit ground remains ~4 s (`wickStain`); fear = a strobing pool and broken rings of their own light pulling IN (a flinch; the turn's rings go OUT), neighbours' lights flicker with it; prayer = a column of light from the floor well over the head and a pool twice the size; Lie low = the violet pool flattens to a slit while a ring closes in.
- **Screen-px floors, not just zoom-scaled sizes:** every ring ≥ 3 px line and ≥ 40 px radius, every pool ≥ 30 px, the flick ≥ 150 px, the prayer column ≥ 96 px, whatever the camera; the same forms draw at whole-mine zoom (they are the far form there), so one effect reads from close-up to the whole mine.
- **Ownership of the light stays with the caller contract:** the lasting pool and stain are separate effects spawned by the moment, so the `wickTurn` / `wickOut` handles keep their round-4 lifetimes and hand-back times.
- **Grades at play zoom:** the shared strike/reveal burst has per-grade screen floors (ground light 24 + 14·grade px, rays 14 + 8·grade px long, 1.6 + 0.35·grade px wide). The dusk's best stone (staged Royal+, or `best: true`) floods the whole grave chamber for a breath (≥ 280–340 px, a wave racing out across the floor) — the loudest moment of the dusk.
- **Effects keep the violet stand-in stone for now.** The drawn crystal look (`crystalLook`) is violet in every grade and affinity (only the exported `RARITY_LOOK` table keeps the old six hues), but drawn through `fxCrystalDrawer` at reveal size the real stone reads as a dark curved hook while the stand-in reads as a lit violet crystal (`screenshots/vfx-crystal-ab.png`). `setCrystalThemed(null)` switches to the real stone (with the drawn-look hue check) once CRYSTAL fixes it.
- **Governor:** per-frame ring cap (36 at level 1, 14 at level 2); level 2 sheds faint rings and all ring ink.

#### VFX round 6 (the effects in the live game; perf to budget)
- **One call per sim event.** `fx.onSimEvent(ev, places)` (`src/render/fx/sim.ts`) is the only mapping from the sim's events to effects: `ROUTES` is typed `{ [K in GameEventKind]: route | { silent: reason } }`, so a new sim event kind does not compile until it is routed or declared silent with a reason (UI-only, numbers, Day-film, scene cinematics). The router owns the persistent effects (one aura per socket, the cart glows, the hunter's light, Hunger, Lie low); `fx.graveState(sockets, places)` keeps the grave's standing state idempotently. `places.drawsStones` = the caller draws and moves the grave's stones (the play scene's hoard): then the grave effects draw light only, never a second stone.
- **The grave's moments read on the floor at play zoom.** Set, enchant, curse and recombine all share one shape: the grave inhales (a dark pool, the contrast beat: the grave's own crystal art is already lit violet, so light alone does not read on it), then a star of light rays bursts from the stone (≥ 200 px), its light floods the chamber floor (≥ 190 px wash with an afterglow that holds ~2.5 s), and an inked shock ring races out to ≥ 250 px. They play at the stone's own seat (the socket, or its place in the hoard), never at the grave's centre. The standing aura of a set crystal is a breathing pool of its grade's light (≥ 54 px, outer pool ≥ 90 px), a slow inked broken ring, low orbiting motes and a pulse ring every 3.4 s — a presence you notice, never a label.
- **Perf: cull first, batch second, govern last.** Effects are culled by the view rect (their box of points widened by their largest screen-floored form, ~380 px) and skipped whole when every point is in the Void (tested once per effect, not per particle); the ground layer's ink (rings, ribbons, veins) of all effects shares one path per style (colour, ink, alpha step of 1/16, blend), painted once; particles batch into one path per (colour, alpha step of 1/8[, line width]); sigils compose their transform instead of save/restore. The load governor stays as the last resort.

### WRITING — Voice and text
- British spelling with -ize (colour, grey, terrorize).
- Typesetting (curly quotes, dashes) happens at output, in `say()`; source strings stay plain.
- "Faith" replaces Devotion and "heat" replaces Suspicion in all player-facing text.
- Short layer: at most 2 lines of at most 60 characters. Details carry the numbers.
- Hunger speaks as "we" in warnings, matching the cutscene.
- Temperament comes from the trait (greedy -> sly, pious -> devout, and so on); moment lines are written per temperament.
- The picker never repeats a variant back to back for the same key.
- `src/content` imports nothing from the sim; everything is keyed by plain strings.
- Pronouns per named person (`pronoun` on their text, taken from their own card line); 'they' for generated people, loners without indication, groups and crowds.

### FRONT — Title, bonus pick, defeat
- **Title is a living picture**: the real mine renderer draws the grave alone (everything else void), breathing; a baked still of the same shot (`src/scenes/front/title-still.webp`, 1920×1080, 47 KB, re-baked by `BAKE_STILL=1 node e2e/front-shots.cjs`) shows at once while the live mine bakes, then cross-fades to it. The name is ink-lettered on canvas (IM Fell English, OFL — `CREDITS.md`). Menu: Begin (or Continue + Begin anew when a run is saved) / Settings, and a small seed line with a dice.
- **The shell's corner Settings button is hidden on the title** (`#ui:has(.ft-root) .hg-gear`): the title has its own Settings word, and on short layouts the corner button sat on the name.
- **Title → opening without a cut**: a 2.6 s camera push into the god's eye (×4.6, log-space ease), the dark closes on it, and the spark lifts to `OPENING_SPARK`, where the cutscene's first frame holds it. With "Skip the opening" on, the camera settles on the first gameplay shot instead and hands its world to the pick.
- **Starting pick**: no reroll (the spec offers three, you keep one). Offers are the sim's seeded `bonusOffer`. Cards rise out of the eye; first tap looks and second tap keeps on touch; 1/2/3, ←/→, Enter, D for Details. The kept card sinks into the grave, the eye flares, the world answers (renderer effects; Deep Roots lights the gallery at once), the god says one line, then `goto('play', { session, world, from: 'bonus' }, cut)` with the clock continued.
- **The god's thread retracts during the pick** (orchestrator: "the god's thread must not persist"): the first pick frame is identical to the cutscene's last, then the thread draws back into the eye while the offers rise out of it; the world is handed on with `thread = { reach: 0, rest: 1 }`, so the play scene draws none and nothing pops back.
- **Cards keep clear of the settings button**: on short layouts the row moves down under it (measured at layout time, not guessed).
- **Defeat**: one plain sentence and one inked picture per cause (starved, the Temple, the Council, the last follower lost, the ground scrubbed), the run in five lines from a replay of the action log (nights, followers ever/at most, peak share of the mine, how far up, whispers), Again (new seed) / Same seed / Title. The world drains to gray from the edges toward the grave, then the dark closes the same way, then the god's spark gutters out. A soft wide pool of dark sits under the words while the gray world is still visible (legibility; no edge). The save is cleared.
- **Pause**: the shell's settings panel is the pause; FRONT adds `PauseVeil` (the frozen world sinks back while paused, lifts in 0.25 s) and `autoPause` (tab hidden / window blur during a run opens the panel) for the shell owner to wire.

### FEEL — Game feel
- **One conductor, no drawing of its own.** `src/feel` stages moments over a host (camera, VFX, audio, HUD, people, hit-stop, atmosphere); it never draws effects, sounds or panels. Every member of the host is optional, so the play scene can wire pieces one at a time.
- **Every input answers in the same frame** (first response 0 ms, budget 100 ms) and nothing blocks input except Divinity up (once per rank, End Night only, ≤ 2.2 s). Big moments never pile on each other: a second big moment waits (≤ 0.9 s) for the first one's hit to settle.
- **Camera never fights the player:** any pan/zoom input cancels direction for 3 s; recipes only nudge a target on screen (no zoom) unless the moment is rare (legendary crystal, eating a person), and then they glide back to the player's framing afterwards. Far moves cut through a short dip instead of a long glide. Shake ≤ 6 px, punch ≤ 2 %, idle breath ≤ 3 px after 6 s; none with reduce motion.
- **Re-scope verbs are data** (`src/feel/scores.ts`): cards (draw/hover/pick/play/denied/discard), mining a crystal per tier, feeding a crystal, crafting, equipping, eating a person. A beat list per moment; the interpreter is `score.ts`. When §15 settles the verbs, re-pointing is a table edit.
- **Sound lands with the picture:** scores spawn the effect at 0 and put the sound, hit-stop and reactions on the effect's own beats (`VFX_BEATS`, mirrored from VFX `FX_TIMING`, checked by a test). The pick bites before the crystal shows; the anticipation grows with the tier (reveal at 0.33 / 0.37 / 0.65 / 1.05 s).
- **Crystal tiers read at a glance by escalation:** common = a tick; uncommon = chime + punch; rare = held breath, 80 ms hit-stop, onlookers look, camera nudges; legendary = the world dims, the camera leans in 1.3×, 140 ms hit-stop, onlookers kneel, framing returned. In the Day film small finds ring out in a quick rising run and the best two rare+ finds escalate (rare → legendary) to the day's headline.
- **Eating a person is the heaviest non-unique moment:** recoil + scream at 0, the maw opens with a 120 ms hit-stop, the fall when the person is gone (1.05 s, matching the effect), what was eaten reaches the grave as Nether, then Malice/danger pulse.
- **The Day frames the shift** (FEEL, 2026-11-23): the film's base pose is the bounding box of the seen area plus a margin, with figures ≥ 36 logical px (the reference's size) and ≤ 72 px, a thin box zoomed to fill ≥ 55 % of the view, a big box framed on the work and kept inside the box; strike pushes multiply that wide, nothing pulls out past it; the player's framing returns at the end. The player's own zoom no longer decides what the Day shows.
- **1 in N staging** (FEEL, §15.10 D): a reveal is staged at max(grade, band(oneIn)) with bands 1 in 30/150/700/3,000/10,000 → Clear…Impossible; the chime stays the grade's. A Day find ≥ 1 in 10,000 stops the film 1.8 s; ≥ 1 in 100,000 stops it 3.2 s and adds a once-in-a-run layer. Holds are outside the 6–10 s plan (the plan stays 6–10 s).
- **Crafting moments** (FEEL, §15.10 A–B): enchant (gather → land → read; a curse = dread then temptation), recombine (two in, a held breath, the new stone revealed at the fuse), facet / forge only with their facilities, give, facility gained (big; the verb appears), unasked tribute (quiet, no camera) vs the contributed haul (the offering procession), exposure of a worn item in a search (big; the worst small moment). Cut-with-crack and graft removed.

- **The Day is a fast-forward, not a highlights film** (FEEL round 3, spec v2.1 §5, DECISIONS §16): one clock 0 (dawn) → 1 (dusk) in **18 s at ×1** (×2 / ×4, pause, skip to dusk, skip dusk; Settings daySpeed multiplies), which everything that moves with the day reads (`feel.dayClock`). Every sim event takes effect at its own `time`; nothing is reordered for drama. The old 6–10 s film is `feel.highlights` (deprecated).
- **Notes**: one per thing worth a line (sim events in FEEL's plain words unless they carry `text`; LIVING's feed; weather changes when the feed has none). Weight: light (strikes Dull–Bright, pockets, carts, colour, weather, most town life) · normal (the count right, a clean search, first colour, a loss, heat ≥ 3, LIVING `normal`) · heavy (strikes staged Deep+, a short count, a search that finds, an exposure, followers' own evil, a death of a follower, rooted, a big loss, an attack, a hunter, an offer spotted, LIVING `heavy`). LIVING weights: strings, or 1/2/3, or 0..1 (≥ 0.7 heavy, ≥ 0.35 normal).
- **Density**: ≤ 3 notes on screen; a light note shows 3 s, heavy 4 s; light notes of one kind at one spot (same place, or within 130 px on screen — every mine face at town zoom) within 0.9 s are one note ("— and 2 more stones here."); with no room a note waits ≤ 1 s, then joins the feed only, folded into a note on screen; a heavy note always shows (the oldest light note makes room). Every note joins the feed exactly once.
- **Heavy notes** slow the clock to ×0.25 for 1.5 s (2 s for an Impossible, an exposure, a death, the vault) with 0.15 s / 0.3 s ramps; overlapping ones extend it. Only if the player has not taken the camera, the camera leans toward the place (in from the wide by the note's push: Deep 1.25, Royal 1.45, Impossible 1.7, others 1.15–1.35; 60 % of the way when the place is in the wide, onto it when not), holds through the slow-down + 0.6 s, then returns to the wide; leans are ≥ 1.1 s apart. A strike ≥ 1 in 10,000 stops the clock at its reveal (1.8 s; ≥ 1 in 100,000 3.2 s + the once-in-a-run layer); taking the camera ends a hold.
- **The camera**: the wide of what the player can see (the shift wide; town zones in `seen`/`surface` make it the town wide — town figures 32 units, specks ≥ 12 px allowed; a mine + surface mix keeps figures ≥ 18 px), drifting in 6 s legs (≤ 2.5 % of the view, ≤ 1.5 % zoom; none with reduce motion). Any pan/zoom/drag — or a click on a feed line (`feel.focusNote`, which glides there) — hands the camera to the player for the rest of the day; nothing moves it again until the night.
- **Dusk** (≤ 12 s, ~6–8 s ordinary): light closes (`dusk`), notes leave the screen, the dusk's events apply → the offering (procession + reveals, or the quiet tribute for one unasked stone; on a town wide it does not take the camera) → the count (`duskCount`) → the ledger (≤ 5 lines, best and worst first) with the stat moves → level ups / unlocks (≤ 2) → `hud.deal()` + the miracle and evil hands fan in → night. The dawn summary box is gone (§16); `plan.summary` keeps the ledger's top 3 lines for old callers.
- **Round 3b (builder #4) — density**: light notes go to the feed only; normal and heavy news stand in the world (≤ 3, heavy evicts), each with a small ground mark at its place (VFX `dayMark`, never over a head). Same-kind normal notes at one spot within 0.9 s merge. The `dayNote` cue speaks the HUD's shapes (weight 0/1/2, `anchor` = FocusTarget, omitted for feed-only lines).
- **Round 3b — Skip is a catch-up, not a cut**: the rest of the day races to dusk in ≤ 1.2 s real (the world takes effect as the clock passes, so people visibly hurry through it); heavy news still shows and stays up to 2.4 s into the dusk; no slow-downs, holds, leans or recipes. A second Skip (or `skip()`) is instant. Reason: the owner's "watching people do stuff" — even a skip should show the day passing and never hide a death or a find.
- **Round 3b — the camera belongs to the player first**: any pan / zoom / drag / note click takes it for the rest of the day; a "Follow the day" toggle (`dayFollow` cue, `feel.followDay(true)`) gives it back and the Day glides to its wide (or the grave shot at dusk). The wide's drift now eases a quarter of the way toward each worked place in turn (≤ 12 % of a view, ≤ 6 % closer) so the shift reads as a tour of the work, never leaving the wide's centre out of frame.
- **Round 3b — the Dusk is one piece**: the walk starts the moment the shift ends (no lead pause); the camera eases 60 % of the way to the grave (1.22× the wide; stays on the wide when the grave is not in it, and on the town at Shade); each stone is set when its bearer's feet come within 1.6 figure heights of the grave (else at its planned time + 1 s; every bearer down by 3.3 s); reveals go up the staged tier (`revealTier(grade, oneIn)`), each ≥ 0.35 s after its stone was set, the best last after every stone is down, with anticipation and hold; late bearers close up the remaining gaps so the offering stays ≤ ~7 s. A single unasked stone is the same piece with one walker (`how: 'tribute'`). Beats go out as `offerBeat` cues and `film.onOfferBeat` (for the HUD strip / VFX / audio).
- **Round 4 (builder #5, live game) — the Day's wide is the lit mine**: figures ~40 px (`DAY_FIG_PX`; up to 56 px), the frame searched over the seen mine zones so ≤ 12 % of it lies off them (`litWide`, bar 15 %), holding the day's work; the surface no longer widens the day (that made the mine a lit dot in black). The surface's share: when the shift rides up for the count (first `lift up` ≥ 0.6, 0.05 early) the camera rises to the pit head (town figures 24–44 px) and stays to dusk; the dusk shot takes it back to the grave even when the scene stages the dusk (`dusk: false`). Leans start 0.7 s (nominal) before a heavy note lands.
- **Round 4 — the turn holds through the violet**: the whisper's turn leans ×1.14 onto the person at once and holds until the wick catches violet (VFX `wickTurn`, 1.55 s) + 0.55 s; the hit (hit-stop 80 ms, the chime, the goal pulse, neighbours look) lands on the violet, not the click (the click keeps a light 50 ms tick). `evil {act:'devour'}` is staged on the devour score (maw 0.42 s, gone 1.05 s, close ×1.25 held to 2.35 s).
### P6b — World composition
- **One renderer, one camera** (`src/render/world/`, `WorldComposite`): world = town units 6400×3600; the mine (2400×1400 mine units) is nested at the town's `pitRect` {1660, 1290, 400×233}: world = pit + mine / K, K = 6. Scenes talk only to `WorldComposite` (implements `WorldRenderer`, additive `WorldView`: one list of the 12 mine areas + 13 town blocks, one list of units incl. groups/Elites, `lift`, `thread`, `pitGlow`, selection/hover ids, one-shot `effects` with ids).
- **LOD band in world scale** (logical px per town unit): `LOD_S0` 1.65 … `LOD_S1` 3.3 (mine scale 0.275 … 0.55), descent p = smoothstep in log-scale × pit proximity (a camera whose view is > 600 px from the pit stays on the surface).
