Below the band only the town is drawn (its own renderer, straight onto the screen); above it only the mine — **pixel-identical** to the mine renderer alone (identity test max 0/255 at 6 mine views incl. the cutscene's hand-over pose, and the town view). Inside: each pixel = town·(1−u)·(1−d)·(1−h) + mine·h, two soft concentric fronts on an ellipse with the pit's proportions (`descentRings`): the shaft's darkness spreads out over the surface, then the mine's light rises inside the darkness — the two pictures never overlap (no doubled image, no rectangle; sweep: mean-luminance steps ≤ 3.5/255 between 37 zoom steps, rect-edge contrast ≤ 4/255).
- **Band cost:** the town is clipped to outside the fully-covered core ellipse (filled black first), the mine renders only inside its content box ∩ the ring's ellipse into one screen-size scratch, masked by a 256² ring sprite (multiply) and added (`lighter`). No extra full-world canvases (scratch 5.5 MB). Measured (delivered, medians, load 7–12 on 4 software-rendered cores): outside the band ≤ +3 ms (0.5–1.0× of the larger layer); in the band 1.1–1.4× at p 0.2 and 0.76, but **1.76–1.85× at p 0.37–0.56** (world ≈ mine + town + 4–10 ms: both layers really are drawn there) — over the brief's 1.6×. The band is crossed in well under a second; zoom sweep 0.25→15→0.25 has no frame > 100 ms (max 76), pull-back max 68 ms. Cheapest next fix: render the mine into the scratch at half resolution while its ring weight is < ~0.7 (it is faint and moving there), switching to full resolution before it dominates.
- **Camera:** `ZOOM_MIN` 0.25 (whole town) … `ZOOM_MAX` 15 (mine zoom 2.5), the full range from turn 1 (an unbaked town is black with the pit's glowing point). `constrainCamera`: away from the pit the zoom stops at the town close-up (`LOD_S0`); with the pit on screen the camera descends and its centre is drawn toward the pit, ending in the mine camera's own clamp. `setPose` never clamps (FINALE may look one screen beyond the town's NNE edge; outside the town the world is #000).
- **Watershed:** `pullBackPath(from, to)` eases log-zoom with a time-warp that lingers in the band (≈ a third of the time) while the pit's screen position glides to its end place — the mine shrinks into the glowing point, the town opens around it (12-frame sheet `screenshots/world-pull-sheet.png`).
- **Crossings** (`crossing.ts`): changing a unit's `zoneId` between `lift` and `minehead` walks it to the cage (mine units 1995, 262) / the pit head, fades it out over 0.6 s, fades it in at the other end and lets it walk on; a stuck route gives up after 20 s.
- **Stain pass-through:** `hold` / `holdFrom` / `holdAt` go to each layer as is; `holdAt` is in world units for both layers (converted to mine units for mine areas); a `holdFrom` naming the other layer's gate (`minehead` for `lift`, `lift` for `minehead`) becomes a seed where the link opens (the cage in the mine, the pit mouth on the surface) — measured: at hold 0.25 colour 1.0 at the gate, 0 at the far side (`screenshots/world-stain-link.png`).
- **Effects:** routed to the layer of the person/zone; a target in the other layer is replaced by the gate; a whisper to a surface person also runs up the lift shaft in the mine. Mine effects get a world beacon (screen-sized ring/bloom on the ground, never a label, never over Void) so they read at whole-town zoom; the town draws its own far beacons. New kinds (ripple, bell, discover, mark) are drawn by the beacon at every zoom until a layer reports `supportsEffect(kind)`.
- **Overlay seams** for AMBIENT / VFX / people: `addOverlay(seam, fn, { layers, order, inColour })` in world units in both layers. Default = after the layer's fog (the caller applies `host.fog(x, y) → { colour, seen }`); `inColour: true` = inside the layer's colour pass (mine `addLayer`, town `layers.*`, chained with whatever is there), so the layer's own Sensed gray covers it (town: Void too, leak ≤ 3/255; mine: Void is NOT covered — weight by `host.fog().colour`, as the mine's own `addLayer` contract says). A layer that is not drawn does not call its overlays.
- **Dependencies:** none added.
- **Round 4 (builder #4) — the descent is one opening, the shaft mouth** (supersedes the two-ring band above): the rings are measured on the dark oval the town draws in the pit (`MOUTH`: centre 6 below the pit's, radii 0.36 × 0.3 of the pit rect; `mouthE`). Coming up from the mine, the mine is seen through an opening that closes from beyond the screen's corners onto the mouth (p 1 → `P_MOUTH` 0.3) while the town fades up round its black rim (`surfaceDark` = smoothstep(0.12, 0.8, p)); then the mine dims inside the mouth (a = smoothstep(0.02, 0.42, p)) and the town's own shaft, collar and violet point fade in over it (rim strength smoothstep(0, `P_SPLIT` 0.2, p)). No moment shows two unrelated pictures overlapping: for p ≥ 0.2 the mine and the town partition the screen along the opening's edge (mine straight onto the screen inside, dimmed by one black sprite toward its soft rim; town clipped outside; one 2-px black stroke hides the two anti-aliased clip edges — black on both sides). Only the faint end (p < 0.2) uses a scratch: the mine at **half resolution** × its weight, added. Evidence: `screenshots/world-pull-band.png` (24 frames, p 0.85 → 0), `world-pull-sheet.png`.
- **Pull-back pacing:** the band gets ≈ 22 % of the path (was 9 %), `BAND_LINGER` 4.
- **"The whole town" = the town plan, not the world rect:** `frameAll(pad = 50)` fits `townExtent()` (every block's seed discs + the pit, + 220 units of walls, roads and the lit edge: x 387…5738, y 289…3201) centred, filling the view → (3062, 1745) @ 0.275. The 6400×3600 rect at 0.25 left the town small and off-centre in black. FINALE's overview/far poses, the play scene's "Whole world" and its pull-back follow automatically.
- **Mine in-colour seams cannot paint on Void:** the world draws them into a scratch, cuts it with the mine's own fog `voidCover` twice (content × (1 − a)²), then composites — with the composite's own cover the soft Void edge reaches exact #000 (only while such overlays exist; the play scene's grave uses one).
- **`sky` seam (WEATHER):** once per frame after the whole composite (both layers, band, beacons), world units, `host.fog` blended across the descent; `host.flash(k)` brightens the finished picture by (1 + k) (the canvas added onto itself) and `host.tint(colour, k)` multiplies it — both keep exact #000 black, so a lightning flash or an overcast grade never lights Void.
- **`frameSurface()`:** the pit head + miners' rows at a town zoom where people read (≥ 0.75), the pit kept inside the middle third (followers go home above ground from night 1, §16.3).
- **The valley (§16.5, the furthest zoom):** `ZOOM_FAR` 0.1 (constrainCamera and `setupCamera` allow it; `ZOOM_MIN` 0.25 stays the name for "the whole town"); `frameValley()` → (4100, 700) @ 0.1. `valley.ts` bakes once (slices of a few ms after the town attaches) a 2000×1500 canvas at 1/10 unit over x −7000…13000, y −5600…9400: mottled night ground, strip fields in turned parcels with inked hedgerows and trees, woods, a river south of the town with a ford track, the road from the town's gate north-east to a notch in the far ridge, farmsteads; beyond the ridge night, with the city's cold glow breathing live. Live on top: four cart lanterns on the road (three out, one back, ~140 s a trip), farm windows that come and go, river glints. Added (`lighter`) onto the town frame with weight smoothstep(0.42, 0.2, scale), so the whole-town view already shows the land at its corners. **Fog decision:** the valley is land, not a zone, and is never Void; it is cut away softly round every block's ground (seed disc + 120 … 520), so Void blocks stay exact #000 and the town's own fade meets it with no band. On night 1 the town is a soft black hole in a living valley with the pit's point at its heart. `world.valley.alpha` lets a scene dim or hide it (FINALE's far city shot).
- **Round 5 (builder #5b) — the land redrawn (theme §6, owner §18/§19: "something in place, right in kind"):** the
  land's design and painters live in `valleyArt.ts` (`planLand` places everything once and collects the live lights —
  the old painters ran per canvas and pushed every light twice); `valley.ts` keeps the bake, the fog mask, the
  compose and the live lights. Content: teal-gray rock with a north-west hillshade, lichen/rust mottle, damp hollows;
  sparse faceted crags; ~29 cap-tree groves (teal in the damp along the gorge and round a dark tarn, amber on the dry
  uplands, caps 100–380 units, THEME's `drawCapTreeStalk`/`drawCapTreeCap`); glow-moss beds (lit at night); a dry
  gorge (the far wall's face in view, a rubble floor, still black pools with teal glints) with a stalk-timber trestle
  where the south road crosses; spoil heaps and a tip track out from the rim nearest the pit head, two old abandoned
  stepped cuts (all placed from `FLOOR` / `PITS[0]` at bake time, so they follow a town re-plan); pit-beast herds
  (THEME's `drawPitBeast`) with herders' hide camps; four waystations of shell houses round a yard with a hearth, a
  beast pen and carts on the cart roads; mountains as inked peaks (lit west face, dark east face) along the north and
  east, the road's pass and the city's basin cut out; the city at `CITY` (10100, −2500): a walled basin of cast blocks
  and cylinder towers round one huge gold dome on a stepped drum; past the north range scorched ground, smoke columns
  and fires with the red glow.
- **Day light measured** (frameValley, noon): lit-pixel luminance median 53, quartiles 38–66 — the mine reference's
  53 / 37–71 (owner §18). Night: the hour's multiply (0.1, 0.11, 0.145): near-black land with fires, moss, the city.
- **One light for the town and its land:** a `WorldView.dayT` now also sets the town's daylight
  (`town.setDaylight(Valley.daylightOf(dayT))`); without one, the land follows the town's own daylight
  (`Valley.dayTOf`). The play scene feeds `dayT` to AMBIENT only today — requested to pass it in the view too.
- **The land meets the bowl past its rim, over the town:** the land is composed `source-over` (alpha = known × a rim
  cut on the floor's distance, 230 → 560 units, noise-warped), so beyond the benches it replaces the town's dark
  plateau and its black cover instead of being added under them (that left a dark moat round the town). Unknown land
  has alpha 0 (the town's own frame, Void #000, stays).
- **`townExtent()` follows the floor** (`FLOOR` bounds ∪ the pit rect + 330 units), not the blocks' seed discs (the
  town engine's re-plan moved the bowl); `frameValley()` is placed relative to it.
- **The land's fog before the town sends one is unknown** (was: all Sensed gray): the last block fog the world sent
  is kept through the bake and applied when the mask is ready, so a frame rendered between the bake's end and the
  next update can never paint gray land over Void. The plan runs in 4 bake steps (≤ ~120 ms each unloaded).
- **The world checks follow the live pit** (`e2e/world-shots.cjs` identity / sweep / void / seams read `lib.PIT` and
  `mineCameraFromWorld`, not the old constants): the town engine's re-plan moved the pit to (2125, 1083).
- **Cap fade** (owner §18): the land is only drawn at scale ≤ 0.42 where `capAlpha(FOLK.H × scale)` ≥ 0.95, so its
  caps are fully in and meet the town's canopy without a step; `capfade.ts` is the one adapter to THEME's rule.

- **Round 6 (builder #6) — night, the rim, the shaft, the massifs:**
  - **Night is never near-black (§18):** the land's light of the hour is `Valley.lightOf(dayT)`: by night 62 % of the
    colour is drained (a `saturation` pass) and the rest × (0.5, 0.62, 0.66) — dim teal-gray rock with its ink line
    (target known-land median 30–40); hearths get broad warm pools (r ≈ 520 units), glow-moss and the tarn glint, the
    city and the war glow on the horizon. Dusk copper weight 0.7 (it must read over the brighter night base); dawn is the
    same curve. (Was × 0.1/0.11/0.145: the land vanished at night.)
  - **One clock:** `WorldComposite.dayT` (the view's `dayT`, else the town's daylight) lights the town (`setDaylight`)
    — now even before the land exists — the land, and every overlay host (`host.dayT`, `host.daylight`, additive).
  - **The rim is crossed, not outlined:** footpaths run out of the bowl's rim (starting under the town's edge, where the
    land's cut fades them in) to outlying hamlets of shell huts with lamps, cap-tree clumps and small tips; lone cap-trees
    and boulders stand 260–900 units past the floor; groves come to ~650. The flat plateau-colour ring round the bowl
    was cut to a noisy 0.2–0.45 mix. (A bare 900-unit ring of plain ground round the town read as a moat.)
  - **The shaft's walls (the watershed's middle):** in the band's split phase the black inside the opening round the
    mine's box is the lift shaft from above (`shaft.ts`): log-spaced rings of strata (teal rock, damp, ochre and rust
    bands, cribbing timber), each lip inked with a catch of light on its north arc, cracks, the cage's guide rails,
    old adits (a lamp left in some), violet veins; lit from the mine below, faded at the opening's edge and with
    smoothstep(0.995, 0.86, p) (so p = 1 stays identical to the mine). Drawn only if the lift or the pit head is seen
    (Sensed: gray; both Void: nothing — Void stays #000).
  - **Massifs, not pebbles:** crags are ~25 ridges of 3–7 outcrops (the biggest in the middle, scree, a crest and
    cracks) + lone boulders (~350 in all, was a 150-unit grid). Mountains are ~100 massifs of 1–4 summits (some
    flat-topped), placed biggest first and kept apart so valleys show; each summit's east face in shadow down its spur.
    Spoil heaps are pale tailings fans tipped outward from the rim (radial streaks, a dark toe).
  - **`townExtent()` = the town engine's `TOWN_FRAME` ∪ the pit + 120** (its request: frame the town as the target
    does) → `frameAll()` ≈ 0.37 on 1600×900 (was 0.275), so the whole-town view is the town, with the land just
    showing at its edges.

### FINALE — Watershed and ending
- **Round 3 (2026-11-23, after the owner's rejection §16.2 and the theme §17–18): no art of our own.** Both sequences
  direct the camera, the light, the fog and the words over the real mine, town and land; FINALE's procedural city,
  gate veins and crystal streams are deleted. One deterministic timeline each (`Script` played by `SequenceRun`: pose,
  view edits, cues, overlay as functions of t; `seek` replays from 0 on a 1/20 s grid), keyed camera moves through a
  monotone cubic (C1, no overshoot, at rest at both ends; zoom in log space).
- **Watershed (22 s):** grave (settle 0–1.6, the god stirs 1.8) → the camera lifts, the chambers fall away (3–8) → up
  the shaft with the cage and its violet light (5–11; the world's descent rings shrink the mine into the mouth) → the
  open pit floods with colour (11.5) → the mine is one violet point in the pit floor and the town the god knows opens
  outward from the pit, block by block, exactly as the sim's fog has it (gray Sensed, black Void, colour where the
  followers' lives carried it) (14.5–17) → settles on `frameAll()` at night light; the HUD rises 1.4 s before the end.
  The run's last pose is Shade play's first: PLAY-CORE keeps it (no re-frame). Two lines from WRITING (`text.stage()`
  lines 0 and the last sentence of 1). The `watershed` bed starts 3 s in so its bloom (11.5 s) lands on the point.
- **Ending (33.5 s + a wait):** over the town's lanes (violet wicks, "Deepwell is yours.") → rise toward the gate as
  dusk falls → along `MAIN_ROAD` over the land, which opens gray past what the god knew (`valley.reveal`) while a
  procession of violet wicks walks out of the gate and stops at the road's first waystation (its yard lights violet)
  → the city (the land's own `CITY`: walled basin, cast towers, gold dome) at night; the land takes colour
  (`revealColour`), a gold light opens over the dome and turns its gaze down the road, a low red war glow on the
  horizon → dark → closing card: the title, three plain rows (nights, followers, the best stone found — the sim's
  crystal with the highest grade, named by WRITING), "You are still hungry." → a click → the title. Lines from
  `text.ending()`.
- Reduce motion: the long moves become held shots joined by slow cross-fades (watershed: two renders blended; ending:
  through the dark); no shake.

### PLAY-v2 — Play scene (integrator; successor #2, 2026-11-23)
- **The HUD is real:** the play scene talks to `src/ui/play` (`mountHud`) through one adapter, `src/scenes/play/hudbridge.ts`, which maps sim selectors to the HUD's view models and the HUD's handlers to scene verbs. The scene's own stand-in HUD/card/hints are retired. The robots' stable `data-action` names are kept as aliases on the HUD's buttons.
- **Hints only at night:** the guide is switched off during the Day replay and the dawn lines, so nothing covers the moment the player is meant to watch.
- **The close-up:** after the cutscene hand-over (first frame unchanged), the camera leans in (scale ≥ 13) on the grave and the first miner in reach, so a figure reads at about 25–30 px on a 900-px view; "Whole world" is one click away (top right, `Z`).
- **Stain seeds:** `holdAt` = where followers stand, captured once at dusk (stable through the night) — colour visibly grows from where followers spend their time (§14.2).
- **v3 night stand-in (§15, until SIM-v3 implements its contract and the HUD ships hands/crystal panels):** `src/scenes/play/crystals.ts` (rules) + `night.ts` (controller + panel). Night order: dusk offering (each crystal set down at the grave with a strike glint by rarity) → two hands of 3 (miracles paid by burning the cheapest crystals that cover the cost; acts of evil incl. Devour; play 1 or skip each) → 3 action points (+1 per rank, +AP lines): whisper 1, routine 1, Move 1–2 (chance 80/60/40 % by distance, +15 % onto home/work/lift) → End Night. Crystals are free to eat (Hunger −value×6), set (3 sockets), cut (Nether 1+tier; 2–3 seeded cuts; 12 % crack) and graft (best line or the odd effect; cap 1 + tier/2). The Day: diggers make strike rolls (rarity 50/27/13/6.4/2.8/0.8 %); carts go up, followers keep (Pray) or offer (Offer) theirs; each kept crystal is a short Tally → Council +2.5. Stand-in numbers only; SIM-v3's contract replaces them one adapter at a time. The Send chip and route flow are removed (§15.3).
- **Burn choice (stand-in):** a miracle burns the cheapest loose crystals that cover its cost; SIM-v3's `payOptions` (player picks) replaces this when the HUD's pay picker lands.
- **§15 re-point (builder #2):** Send and its intents are gone from FEEL (no `sendPreview/sendConfirm/recall/arrival/explore/craft`); routines (`routine`, 1 AP pip) and `move` (ok/failed) replace them. Six crystal tiers (Dull … Impossible) everywhere; strike sounds read VFX's own `FX_TIMING` (one adapter file) so the pick sound lands when the pick bites and the tier sound when the stone shows.
- **The dusk offering is the reward beat of every turn** (`offering`, `src/feel/offering.ts`): procession → reveals rising through the tiers with the best find last → anticipation before a Deep+ best → best-find hold. Gaps by tier (Dull 0.3 s … Impossible 1.3 s) compress to fit 7.5 s for a normal haul (≤ 8), 8 s for any; always skippable (`moment.skip()` jumps silently and hands the HUD every reveal).
- **The Day is a film about digging:** picks swing across the mine first; Dull/Clear/Bright strikes ring out in the wide (no camera); the best two Deep+ finds rise to the day's headline, each with a shot that pushes in from the player's own zoom (1.25 / 1.45 / 1.7×) and a held beat; pockets land right after their strike, carts roll; the Tally plays as a count with a held breath (a short count gets a shot on the lift); then searches, followers' own evil, the opposition. Still 6–10 s.

### CAST — Town folk, Elites, enemies, crowds
- **One rig, one hand.** Everyone who is not a miner is built on the miner animator's rig (`src/render/people`) through
  one adapter (`cast/adapter.ts`): 16 town-folk designs, the five Elites as individuals (Orla the magistrate, Harl the
  captain, Aldo the high priest, Petra the guildmaster, Vix the smuggler boss), 13 authority/enemy designs, 4 animals.
  Same 40-unit rig, same ink, same lanky proportions as the mine reference; town scale 0.6 world units per rig unit.
- **Recognised at a glance, no icons:** each design has a silhouette mark (pike, mitre, tall hat, hood, plume, robe
  flare, brim) and one accent colour. At far zoom (figure under 7 device px) people become pixel-snapped, inked role
  specks; the five Elites get five hues that never meet (blue, crimson, gold, rust, plum) and a body one pixel wider.
- **Allegiance on the body (§13.11 quiet tell):** follower devotion < 25 a violet glint in the eye; 25–59 a violet
  wrist thread and the secret sign (two fingers to the lips) now and then; ≥ 60 sash, face mark, chest hand-print,
  carried flames and lamps burn violet, upright posture. Temple = gold sash; Council = a steel-blue armband on
  townsfolk (soldiers already wear the blue). Moods fear / zeal / doubt bend any clip (shrink, chest out, look down).
- **Elites act as themselves:** a signature idle each (Orla reads and stamps, Harl paces hands behind his back, Aldo
  lifts the Flame, Petra counts coins, Vix leans and flips a knife) and their own whisper / turned reactions.
- **Crowds are groups with internal life:** 11 formations (market, congregation, procession, patrol, workgang, mob,
  queue, funeral, drill, crowd, hunt). Members are spread by best-candidate sampling with a one-body minimum gap so a
  crowd reads as people, not a heap; each runs its own deterministic schedule; conversion passes person by person from
  the front back, each lifting their head as it reaches them.
- **Cost:** every figure is a cached sprite (look × activity × frame × facing × paint × size). Townsfolk share 8 looks
  per role so crowds share sprites; looping clips keep at most 16 poses per cycle; at most 14 new sprites are painted
  per frame (over budget a person holds its last cached pose for a frame); each sprite is trimmed to its painted
  pixels (a figure fills about a fifth of its pose box), which cuts blit area and memory.
- **Integration seam:** `CastLayer.draw(ctx, entries)` draws a whole town per frame (cull, y-sort, walk detection with
  planted feet, reactions, schedule, Sensed gray, far specks). The town renderer can hand it `layers.props` and set
  `ownPeople = false`.

- **Round 3 successor (wickfolk, "get something in place", §19):** the cast body takes THEME's proportions but draws
  the trunk in side depth (THEME's widths are front-view: depth = 0.62 × width, trunk 0.8 × THEME torso under the
  top-down foreshortening); legs in pale felt wraps so they read; the head turns half as far as the body so both
  eyes show (as the references' figures). **Groups have a mood** (calm / uneasy / fervent / yours) that bends every
  non-authority member (gather, face in, clip, fins, wick colour and strength) and passes through the group from the
  front over ~2 s; at town zoom a group also gets one ground pool — trodden shade plus its wicks' gathered light in
  the mood colour — so a crowd reads as one body; nothing floats over heads. **`drawPerson(ctx, person, pose, t)`**
  is the one-at-a-time seam (shared module bank; far zoom → head + wick speck). Levy, escort and stoke clips added
  for BEHAV's levy and burnings.
### P2-people — Living people
- **Scale:** a mine figure is 36 world units feet to crown (`MINE_FIG_SCALE` 0.9 × the 40-unit rig), measured
  against `mine.png`: ≈ 2.1 rail gauges, ≈ ½ a wall face, about a cart's height; a corridor is 3–4 figures wide.
- **Look (vs `ref-6x-figures-b`):** the pale round head is the read — drawn ≈ 24 % of the figure's height, skins
  near-white, caps cover only the crown so the face stays bare; mine work clothes are pale-to-mid grays (the figures
  are the palest thing on the floor, as in the reference), legs and boots dark; thin inked limbs (one ink stroke per
  limb), flat tones with one shadow step. Allegiance reads from the cap lamp's colour and the violet eye glint.
- **Low detail level:** a figure under ≈ 64 px tall (`LOW_DETAIL_PX` 1.6 px per rig unit) skips cuffs, wrap bands,
  patches, highlights and hand outlines — sub-pixel there, and ≈ 20 % fewer canvas calls.
- **No sliding feet, by construction:** behaviour joins segments at event time (`Life.joinSeg`): a walk starts where
  the person stands; an activity point more than 0.5 units away is reached by a short stroll (no stepless glide).
  Animator blends move a foot by stepping it on an arc (near foot, then far foot), which also gives start/stop steps;
  a standing person who turns round shuffles two steps in place (`turn`) and keeps the feet's ground positions.
  While walking, a stance foot is integrated in place (it moves back by exactly the body's displacement, also
  through path corners and facing flips) and eases into the gait's swing after lift-off; walks toward / away from
  the camera take shorter, quicker steps (foreshortened stride) and a leg may stretch up to 1.3× to keep a planted
  foot on its point. Measured in the real mine (life capture, 16 people, 10 s): 0 of 127 one-second windows still;
  planted-foot slide median 0, p99 0, mean 0.07 u/s, 7 of 2399 samples over 8 u/s (was mean 6, max 370).
- **Draw path for many people:** `LiveSprite` — each person's own sprite, repainted at 10 fps (phase-staggered), blitted
  every frame from a tight rect; walkers are blitted at their last repaint's feet position, so the planted foot is
  exact and the body advances in small steps. Measured 30 people (final run, load 16.8): 2.2 ms over a blank control at zoom 1 (figure
  ≈ 32 px; budget 2 ms), 4.5 ms at zoom 2.2 (≈ 71 px) on the shared 4-core software-rendered box; a full vector redraw
  is 7.7 / 12.1 ms. Idle/work clips repaint at 8 fps, walkers at 10. The mine's own throttled sprite cache is asked to adopt the anchored blit (requests #6–8).
- **New clips:** `turn`, `start`, `stop`, `board`/`ride`/`alight` (the lift: step up, hold the overhead bar and sway,
  step out), `cartIdle` (forearms on the tub rim, a look back down the rails). `attachPoints(anim, out)` gives head,
  cap lamp, both hands and the cart tub centre for lamp glints, sparks and the cage.
- **Builder #4 (wickfolk body, §17/§19):** the rig keeps all motion; `drawFigure` draws its skeleton as THEME's wickfolk (`src/render/people/folkfig.ts`): THEME `torsoPath` trunk in a trunk-local frame (so the upright kit follows any lean), wrapped thighs and bare pale shins, `drawFoot` / `drawHand` (four fingers), THEME's cached head with fins, eyeshine and wick, and the trade's kit from THEME's `TRADE_KIT` (one species and one dress code with the town). Three-quarter view (turn ≈ 1.02 rad toward the facing) so both eyes and fins read from the mine's camera. The wickfolk have no neck: the head centre sits ≈ 0.8 R above the shoulder line (rig solve with a body remap). Carts are stalk-timber tubs with violet crystal. Per §19 this is "something in place": no polish rounds.

### HUD — Play interface (builder #2, re-scope §15)
- **Cards are cards:** two fanned hands each night at the bottom centre — Miracles (left) and Acts of evil (right), each "play 1 or skip" with a Skip button on its label. Rest card = art window, name, cost gem, rarity pip and rarity-metal frame; hover/tap opens the big readable face above it (two lines, Play, Details); drag up to play, drag a targeted card onto the world (the scene answers `onCardAim` with a live line), or keys 1–3 / 4–6 then Enter. Deal/draw/play/discard animate; reduce-motion = fades.
- **Pay step:** a miracle costing crystal value opens a small panel above the card: pick crystals (tiles with their value) until the bar fills, then "Burn and cast". The HUD never chooses for the player (the scene may preselect).
- **Rarity colours (six tiers, cards and crystals alike):** Dull grey · Clear ice · Bright jade · Deep sapphire · Royal crimson · Impossible gold (iridescent). Violet stays the god's own colour and is never a rarity.
- **The grave** is the inventory: a belt at the bottom left shows the sockets (what is set) and a Grave button with the count; the panel holds sockets and the crystal grid; the crystal card shows two plain lines, then stat lines / odd effect, then Eat · Set/Unset · Cut (rough only, shows the 2–3 offered cuts and the crack chance) · Graft (pick donor, pick a line or the odd effect; donor destroyed). Rough crystals cannot be set. Hover a tile for its two lines.
- **Dusk offering strip** under the top bar: crystals flip in one by one (rarer = slower, with a burst from Deep up), running worth, then Continue; click = faster.
- **AP** are violet pips in the Spend group; **Tally** is one word in the Danger group ("Tally −3") whose tip says expected / counted / short and what the Council thinks.
- **Follower card v3:** routine as three segmented buttons (Pray / Offer / Lie low, current one marked, others show their AP), crystals hidden on them, one Move button with AP and a chance word; the live chance shows in the cursor chip while targeting. The Send chip is gone (SendVM ignored).
- **Crystal drawing** is owned by the CRYSTAL piece; the HUD has one adapter (`src/ui/play/crystal.ts`) with a stand-in painter until it lands.
- **Generic card component** for other screens: `pickCard(host, cards)` / `cardFace(card)` (FRONT's bonus pick).
- **Re-pointed to the crystals (§15.1, §15.7):** the watershed follows the lift's load — a cage of crystals (rarity colours of the HUD) rides up the shaft as the camera pulls back, and when the town opens a stream of crystals runs out of the pit head into the smelters and the Council's vault (and into the vault again on its beat). Words: "So this is what they built with my crystals."; beats Crowds ("Whole crews carry the stones up here…"), Elites ("The few who keep the count…"), the Temple, the Council's vault ("Where every crystal the mine gives ends up. One in there is Impossible."). The ending: once the gate is soaked the crystals go on through it and up the road to the city (a stream of glints the whole way, visible from the far view); the god: "Every crystal they dug went up that road. Something there is looking back."; the summary's crystals row (eaten, burned for miracles). Hand-back defaults to the pit head at a play zoom; `opts.handback: 'whole'` hands back on the composed whole-town frame instead.

### WRITING round 2
- **Rarity names:** Dull, Clear, Bright, **Blazing**, Royal, Impossible (sim id `deep` shows as Blazing): three steps of light, then too much light, then the Crown's, then what cannot be. "Deep" did not rank after "Bright" by eye. Every rarity line ends "Worth N"; Details say "Rarity n of 6". No loot words (tested).
- **"The Tally" is "the count" in every player-facing string:** "Tally" is a retired name (§13.3). Levels use the sim's `tallyView.word` (Content, Grumbling, Counting twice, Searching) and each says what the Council thinks and what comes next. The intro names the rule plainly: "What your followers keep is missing from it."
- **Crystal names:** seeded, two or three plain words, a pattern per rarity (Dull "Sooty Thumb" … Royal "Banner of Widows" … Impossible "Double Without Wax"). The base name depends only on the seed, so a stone keeps its name when cutting reveals an effect; the effect then swaps exactly one word for one of its own (Echo: Twin/Doubled/Echo/Double). Effect words appear in no rarity pool and belong to one effect each (tested). Hashes go through a murmur finaliser (FNV keys that differ by one character gave correlated picks).
- **Text follows the sim's rules, not the other way round:** odd effects, stat lines, cards and blessings say what `src/sim/crystals.ts` and `src/sim/content/{cards,bonuses}.ts` do, with their numbers as Details defaults; a test compares names, targets, costs, Malice and Hunger with the sim's tables at runtime. Evil cost line: "Hunger −35 · Malice −12 · the Public and the Temple notice".
- **Devour is "Devour a Person"** on its card (the owner's words); the sim's fallback says "Devour".
- **Move preview** is a question and a reason: "Ask for Ket to be moved to the crystal gallery?" / "Risky · 45 % · off his path · 1 action", with the brief's words Likely / Risky / Almost certainly not (≥ 65 %, ≥ 30 %, below).
- **Retired:** the Send / intent / recall strings (`btn.send`, `btn.sendConfirm`, `btn.recall`, every `send.*` key, the `send`/`intent:*`/`recall` unlocks, `text.sendPreview`, the "Send" guide beat and `hint.sendMode`), removed after a grep showed nothing outside `src/content` used them. `text.task` / `TASKS` and the ledger's `sent`/`recalled` cases stay, deprecated (harmless; the events are no longer emitted).

### TOWN-ART — The town's look
- Painters only, through the engine seam `town/painters.ts` (`ArtGround`, `ArtBuildings`, `artPainters` drop-in for `townBuildJob(seed, artPainters)`), plus `paintLitWindows` and the state overlays `drawBarricade/drawBanner/drawPyre`. Code in `src/render/town/art/**`; harness `dev/townart.html`, shots `e2e/townart-shots.cjs` → `screenshots/townart-*.png`.
- Reading of `town.png`: same 3/4 top-down as the mine; the roof is the big shape, a short south wall strip under it; patched teal/rust corrugated sheet, mustard tarps; charcoal-gray stony ground with soft **ochre dirt lanes** between doors; inked stone clusters; narrow value range except at lamps.
- Ground: charcoal-gray albedo per block (temperature shifts by district), ochre lanes with ragged verges and no dark verge line (no band, owner's rule), wear paths door→street, paving decided per stone by a continuous field (no grid steps); puddles only as rare teal water in the Works/Rows.
- Density: the plan places the buildings that matter (doors, lights, spots); TOWN-ART's `fill.ts` fills the free lots with scenery (huts, sheds, fenced yards with clutter and washing, woodpiles, ore heaps, crate stacks, garden plots) painted by the ground painter. A filler never overlaps a plan item's extent, a street, a spot where people stand, a plaza, the pit, the wall, rails or slag channels, so draw order never matters and no one walks through scenery. Two passes (big lots first, then small).
- Houses: deeper roofs (depth ≈ 0.48 × width) over short front walls; silhouette ink at least 1.2–1.3 device px at any zoom.
- District languages: Rows = patched teal/rust corrugated barrels and terraces; Works = soot iron + brick, kilns; Market = striped awnings, terracotta/ochre tile, timber; Temple Hill = pale stone, terracotta, the kiln-gold dome; Keep = gray ashlar, steel-blue slate.
- The pit collar is irregular dark rubble, not a clean pale ring (it read as a UI ring at far zoom).
- **Carried fire is exposed as light:** every flame or lit lamp a cast sprite paints is recorded at paint time;
  `CastLayer.lights` (= `bank.lights`) lists them in world units each frame so the renderer can pool light under the
  inquisitor's lantern, censers, torches and candles. (The miner rig's own `lantern` prop is not captured.)
- **Measured (shared 4-core software box, load 5–9, 1600×900, figure ≈ 38 px):** 300 figures through the bank warm
  4.8 ms median / 8.2 p90, cold 9.6 / 14.7 (blank control 0.5); 300 people + 24 in groups through `CastLayer` with
  activity changes and a Sensed strip 9.7 ms, 120 + 24 → 5.4 ms (both include the harness ground); sprite memory
  17.7 MB for ~4000 sprites. Before this round: 22–29 ms, cache never warmed, ≈ 27 KB per sprite.
- **The hook is the greater mines (§15.10 C, owner's ruling):** in the far view, after the gold light looks back, a great pit opens under the city itself (tier II: a dark mouth ringed and lit from far below in Royal pink, stones rising out of it) and a second on the horizon past the city (tier III, Impossible gold). The Hunger: "Ours was a tier I hole. Theirs go deeper. {grade} stones, by the cartload." — {grade} comes from the sim's `MINE_TIERS` (tier II's notable grade) through the finale's adapter (`host.tierTwo()`, stand-in "Royal" until the table exists). The gate's own stain is not re-animated during the ending (doing so re-rendered the town's ground tiles in visible rectangular steps); the climb is the veins and a violet wash in the arch.

- **Round 4 (2026-11-23, §18/§19): one hand for everything that stands in the town.** A single fixed three-quarter
  projection for all kinds (`art/k3.ts`: x across, depth leaning up and 0.2 to the right so right-hand walls show as a
  shadow strip, z up), solids painted as **stepped flat planes** (hi / lit / mid / shadow / deep by how a facet faces
  one key light from the upper left), heavy teal-black silhouette ink (~1.8 % of the width, never under 1.1 px), thin
  ramp-coloured inner ink for ribs and seams, warm gold doorways with a floor glow. Shell houses are ribbed half-barrel
  vaults (axis running back) with a darker shell end, lit rim and clay sill, or lumpy hide huts (rust / madder /
  ochre) on a short clay wall; cast houses are rounded-corner cream prisms, drums with domes, tanks, black pipes and
  stacks with the Council's mark and cold shard-lamps. Places are composed from the same parts (`kplaces.ts`).
- "Red hide" is a **madder rust** (`#7a3f31`), not the war red: theme §2 keeps red for blood and the war.
- Each plan item is painted **inside its own drawing box**: the kind's extent is measured once (offscreen, w = 100)
  and the width shrunk (never grown) to fit `it.bb`, so no tile seam can cut a painting.
- Every kind publishes its **light sources** (`itemLights(it)`: hearth / shard / crystal / flame / forge, world units)
  for the engine's light pass; `paintLitWindows` draws them as additive accents.
- The scenery fill no longer adds huts (P6: the plan holds the buildings): free lots get yards, heaps and lean-tos.
- Cap-trees, lamps, braziers, pit beasts and carts come from THEME's `props.ts` (`art/themeprops.ts` re-exports).

- **Round 5 (2026-11-23): homes are seeded builds from parts, at the plan's width.** No two neighbours match:
  `khome.ts` picks a body (shell: ribbed vault end-on, vault side-on over a rubble wall, beehive shell on a drum, lumpy
  hide, sagging saddle hide; cast: stacked blocks, drum + dome, twin blocks, roof tank, iron stack), a roof colour
  (teal, deep teal, pale, slate blue, bleached cream, rust, madder, ochre), a state (patched, lashed and weighted,
  sagging, propped), additions (lean-to, budded pod, porch cloth, outside stair, vent or flue, water tank) and a yard
  on the free side that names the trade (tanner's frames, potter's kiln and jars, cord walk, beast pen, washing,
  fuel, cap-garden). About 45 % of shell homes have a rubble storey: a door a figure walks through, windows at head
  height, the shell roof as a loft. Homes keep `it.w` and fit **depth, then height** into the drawing box (`bb`);
  width never shrinks. The hand (§20) now applies to everything drawn with `k3`: ink 1.75× heavier on the underside,
  short broken hatching on planes turned from the key light (skipped below ~2.4 px spacing), cracks and chipped
  corners on cast and rubble walls, values muted (highlights mixed 13 % toward cream instead of 22 %).
- Market stalls are cloth grids seen from above (front edge high enough that the counter and its goods show under
  it); the Temple is a carved front in the cliff with columns, arch within arch, two gold figures and wide steps.

### BEHAV — Life director
- **Builder #2 (2026-11-23).** `src/life` decides who does what, where and when; it is headless, deterministic and presentation-only. The call sequence and interface are at the top of `handoffs/BEHAV.md`.
- **Night lift:** the cage runs all night on a 54 s timetable that is pure in t: ore carts go up and empties come down (`lift.cargo`, `liftBell`). A night-shift worker in the lift area is the lift hand and meets each cage.
- **§15 mining Day:** a post-pass on the Day plan stages `strike` (kept → pocketed; otherwise into the cart or basket), `carted`, the Tally, `search` (with exposure of worn items) and followers' `evil`. Each beat replaces a window of the timeline and rejoins the old plan, so the Day stays continuous. The Tally is always staged at the end of the day shift (t 0.45–0.6), before the evening lift, whatever the sim's `time` says. A short count past the sim's `tolerance` (and not `covered`) makes the overseer flare.
- **Dusk:** followers on routine `offer` form a procession to the grave. At nightfall they are already on the path, at most 280 u out, so the reward lands 10–20 s into the night. They kneel only in front of the grave. Tribute (`LifeState.dusk.tribute` or a `tribute` event) is one furtive trip by one person: work until slipping off, walk, sneak the last 100 u, look back, set the stone down, leave. Rituals are protected from moments, talks and joiners.
- **Worn items** (`LifeUnit.worn`): bolder nerve, watchful look-rounds, touching the charm when the law is near, and choosing spots away from the guards' beat. **Depth:** an area's depth (from `zones[].depth`, otherwise from how far down it lies) adds "listen to the rock" pauses.
- **Robustness:** a new turn, or the night clock going back by more than 0.5 s, starts a fresh night. Every frame checks that each person has a plan covering now and re-plans them otherwise. Night events are applied in planner time order, so frames do not depend on how often the director is queried. Each person walks at their own pace (0.88–1.12×).
- The harness and tests fall back on frozen presets (`tests/life/fixtures/presets.json`) while the sim is mid-edit.

- **Builder #3, round 3 (2026-11-23) — the staged living world (spec v2.1 §3, §5, §5b; DECISIONS §16).** New pure call
  `life.stage(input, t)`: LIVING's `{ society, plan }` (or the stand-in `life.standIn(seed, day)` in exactly that shape, until
  LIVING's planDay lands — one adapter, `src/life/living.ts`) → every person's position and activity at plan time t ∈ [0, 2).
  Decisions: (1) **one clock = plan time**: 0 dawn … 1 dusk (= `feel.dayClock.t`), 1–1.25 the evening (`duskClock`), 1.25–2 the
  night (`nightClock`: real-time pace 1/300 plan units per second, holds at 1.97; awake routines loop on). (2) **Walking pace is a
  time-lapse**: 11 700 mine / 7 800 town units per plan unit (≈ 18 figure heights per film second at ×1; ≈ a walk at night);
  a plan's walk is fitted between its stops, sped up to 3× when the plan is tight, otherwise the person arrives early and waits.
  (3) **The lift is a discrete-event schedule** built with the timelines: capacity 12, ride 0.015, boarding 0.005; FIFO queue
  lines at the pit head and the bottom, people step forward as each cage leaves; the storm stop lets the last cage up crowded.
  (4) **Standing room**: every (person, place, purpose) claims its own point on a body-room lattice (13×8 mine, 9×6 town) on
  standable ground in the right zone near the place's real spots (`slots.ts`); queue lines and cage doors are reserved. So
  people standing still never overlap and always return to the same bench, stall, face or door. (5) **Trades** (`trades.ts`):
  each of LIVING's 24 mine + 51 town trades has a work spot tag, a mode (still / shuttle / rounds / browse / play), a clip
  cycle, a carry and a gait — tested unique. Meanings ("knead", "pump", "wind") stay in `doing`; the clip falls back per
  renderer (miner animator vs CAST) until the animators add them (Requests). (6) **Crowds** stage as one timeline with
  per-member lattice slots at every stand and a walking blob between (offsets blend over the first/last 20 % of a walk).
  (7) **Indoors**: sleep is indoors at home; eating, the tavern, chapel, school are partly indoors; work at a workshop is done
  at its door (visible). Indoor individuals still get a patch (`indoors: true`) so sleepers can be whispered to. (8) **Events
  are overrides**: a beat over [a, b] starts where the person is at a and walks back to where the plan has them at b (b found
  from the real route length); participants are placed before bystanders; 15 kinds staged, the rest gather-and-look.
  (9) **Weather edits the plan before staging**: rain splits outdoor leisure (and 40 % of outdoor work) into shelter under
  the eaves or a walk home (the market thins), walkers jog with hoods; fog: walkers carry lamps; cold: stamping at the work
  spot; storm: the lift stops 0.2 after the storm's onset, mine work ends before it. (10) **Nobody lives in the mine**: the
  stand-in keeps 5 of 60 on the night shift (pumps, winder, a guard, a lamp man, a vent man).
- **Round 4 (BEHAV, 2026-11-23).** (1) **LIVING's real days are the input** (`livingDays` / `livingDay`); the stand-in stays
  as a fallback and for old tests. (2) **Legal ground**: every town walk is checked every 2 world units against the plan's
  open test, re-routed by A* where blocked and string-pulled (no doubling back along a street); only the doorway step at
  either end may touch a building. (3) **People named in an event step out of their crowd for the day** (and so do the
  hearths of the dead, the levied and a newborn's mother) so a burning, the levy or a blessing is acted by those people,
  not by a whole crowd. (4) **LIVING's kinds are staged as themselves**: a burning is carried in on a bier by the dead's
  hearth, who stand with covered wicks while the ash keeper stokes; an empty burning has no bier; the levy musters at the
  drill yard and marches out of the gate while each hearth sees its own off; the Flame's procession gathers the faithful
  (three in the rain); a crier reads out the levy / tax / war news; blessings, moults, runaway trains, refugees, the
  wounded and the tithe each have their own few people and beats. (5) **Indoors is not idle**: someone at indoor work is
  still doing their trade (nothing drawn outside; a cutaway or a card can read it); selling at a stall is the trade's craft
  between haggles. (6) **Only heavy rain (≥ 0.7) or a storm stops outdoor work**; light rain = hoods up, work goes on;
  LIVING's own `shelter` stops are kept. (7) **v3.2 offering**: a bearer stays below after the shift and sets the stone
  down at the sim's `offered` time; the frame lists bearers with arrive / leave times. (8) **Counters are per layer and
  outside / inside**, and outside always equals what is drawn. (9) The legacy `day()` path's tests are skipped under v3.2
  (the binding Day is `stage()`); the night tests now expect the sleepers above ground.

- **Round 5 (BEHAV, 2026-11-23) — how people stand together.** (1) **No lattice anywhere**: a standing point is a
  lattice cell plus a seeded nudge of up to half a body room, kept only when nobody else's point is within a body's
  room; in the town it also keeps half a room clear of walls. (2) **Groups stand in shapes by what they do**: talk /
  eat / drink / idle → knots of 2–5 facing each other (now and then one alone); lift line / pay → a loose queue with
  uneven gaps facing the head; chapel / sermon / burning / crier / wedding → an arc round the focus, in front of it;
  drill / muster → ranks (the only straight lines); a crew at work → spread out; sleepers → a heap. Every crowd member
  faces its own way. (3) **Walking crowds are a ragged stream** (uneven gaps, some two abreast). (4) **Milling**:
  everyone standing about shifts weight, steps, looks round now and then, on their own phase and pace; cycles of
  meanings run on per-person phases (no two neighbours change in step). (5) **People standing about turn to the nearest
  person near them** (pairs face each other). (6) **A market crowd breaks up**: members go stall to stall on their own
  (legal routes), stop at shared browse spots round each stall taken in turns, haggle / weigh / point, ~70 % carry a
  sack or bundle away, children run about, a few only talk; all are back with their knot when the crowd leaves.
  (7) **Doors are the plan's**: facilities use TOWN-ART's real door and work spots; every household gets its own
  dwelling door in its zone. (8) The legacy `day()` is now staged by `stage()` on the stand-in day (no skipped suites).
  (9) **The levied do not come back**: on levy day the levied (not the captain, not a whole work gang) muster at the
  drill yard, march out of the gate and are out of sight for the rest of the day; their hearths stand at the gate
  covering their wicks until well after the column has passed. (10) **Stalls are solid** for the director (counter and
  awning, 80 % of the footprint): buyers browse round them and sellers stand at the edge, not on top.

### AMBIENT — Ambient life and time of day
- **Scope and seams.** `src/render/ambient` draws everything that moves that is not a person, an action's effect or the terrain. One call, `amb.attachWorld(world)`, registers the mine renderer's in-colour `addLayer('ground'|'props'|'above')` (the fog covers it like the art), the world's overlays for the mine's Sensed part in gray and for the town, an **in-colour** town ground overlay for mist (the town's fog grays it and keeps the Void exact #000), and sets `mine.builtinMachines = false`. Generic `amb.draw(g, host, layer)` serves any other renderer.
- **One clock.** Every motion is a pure function of the `time` given to `update()` plus `dayT` (FEEL's day clock, §16): carts, lift wheel, smoke, birds, windows, the grade; ×2/×4 just advance it, paused freezes it. Offsets that grow with time (cloud shadows, mist banks) advect along the day's fixed wind direction so a veering wind never makes them jump.
- **Crystal flow (§15.2/§15.7).** Six grades (spec §7.1 names; 'deep' kept as an alias) with ambient colours — dull bone, clear ice, bright jade, blazing flame, royal azure with a gold glint, impossible prismatic; violet stays the god's. Mine carts carry the play scene's per-cart loads (`setLoads`, by cart or by zone: the next cart loading at that zone's stop takes it), heaped and glinting one stone at a time, rarer stones more often. Worked faces (`setWorking`, default: every work face by day, two at night) show a pick beat per digger: a dust puff rolling out, four chips, a spark, a hand lamp set down. SIM-v3 pulses (`event('strike'|'carted'|'tally')`) glint in the rock in the grade's colour, hop into the nearest cart, and dip the Tally's balance. **The Tally** is the overseer's table beside the lift (balance, ledger, a lamp that flares on the count and gutters red on a short count). **Town route:** pit head → haul yard → market → Tally Hall (the Council's vault is in the Tally Hall, the garrison next door guards it); two mule wagons with crystal loads and a lantern on a pole (lit from dusk, reads at the whole-town zoom); a handcart takes the cutters' share from the haul yard to the smeltery (BEHAV attaches a porter at `pushX/pushY`).
- **Liveliness pass (owner: "far more lively").** Windows gutter and somebody crosses a lit window now and then; street lamps breathe; moths circle lit lamps; ground mist banks drift through the town at night and on misty mornings; moonlit cloud shadows cross the town by night too (multiply: black stays black); lighter night smoke so plumes read. Measured share of a "nobody acts" frame changing within 2 s rose to: whole town at night 1.2 % (was 0.58; renderers alone 0.28), market 4.7 % (3.3), pit head 9.1 % (6.8), mine heart 10.3 %, a worked face 8.4 %; every screen quarter of every subject has moving tiles.
- **Time of day.** Night deep blue-black (multiply), dawn gold from the north-east wall, dusk red-orange from the west, working daylight as an exposure lift (color-dodge by a mid gray: a linear gain that keeps black black) plus a warm balance. The town is baked night-lit, so day is a grade only and baked lamp glows remain by day — a day lightmap is requested from the town engine. The mine barely changes: a faint daylight shaft down the lift shaft.
- **Weather hand-over (§16).** WEATHER owns rain, fog, wind and storms. `amb.setWeather(weather)` reads `weather.state` (wetness, wind vector, flash, visibility): smoke, flags and mist follow its wind, its flash lights the grade, and the ambient's stand-in rain and mist switch off. The ambient publishes `amb.sky` (dayT, sun, lamps, night, dawn, dusk, elev).

- **Successor 3 (§18/§19: in place, no polish).** One light: the town engine's own sky (`setSky`: day lightmap / night
  multiply / dusk copper / flash) is driven by the ambient's clock every frame (`daylight = Valley.daylightOf(dayT)`,
  lamps = the lamplighter's round); the ambient grade no longer tints or lifts the town (that colour-dodge exposure
  field cost ~40 ms a frame on the software canvas) and only adds the brief dim cue. The engine draws the lit windows
  and door lamps live, so the ambient no longer doubles them; it keeps the moths. Mist is composed small and drawn once.
  **Places from the plan, never constants:** the crystals' road is shaft → sorting pens → tally house → the Keep's
  vault along a graph of every street, road and lane; the cutters' handcart pens → gem workshop; headframe / engine /
  market / wash / forge / smelter by `TownItem.place`; a re-plan rebuilds the town ambient. **Theme props:** every
  cart is THEME `drawCart`, the mine loop carts, pit-head carts and road wagons go behind a walking pit beast; skitters,
  lamp-moths and a gold brazier at each shrine nook are THEME props; shard-lamps breathe cold white and never gutter;
  the pit-head engine has a pale beating steam plume; no pennants on the wall (gate and Keep cold, Temple gold).
### CRYSTAL — The crystals
- **One renderer for every crystal** (`src/render/crystal/`): rock, hand, cart, grave socket, inventory icon, inspect view, jewel setting. Each stone is a seeded 3D convex-mesh form, drawn in a fixed 3/4 orthographic view (pitch 0.5 rad, a yaw per seed) and painted as glass: back faces, inner phantoms, front facets with thin facet ink and a heavier hull ink, then inner light clipped to the stone's own silhouette. Nothing glows outside the stone (no halo at the void) except an optional ground pool (`glow`) and the transient sparks of one-shot animations.
- **Four axes, each readable at a glance** (§15.10 / spec v2 §7):
  - **Base** sets the silhouette: 45 forms in five classes, from common (shard, chip, fang, bead, needle, cluster, book) to mythic (eye, hand, heart, seed, door, egg, möbius, orbit). All 35 sim bases map to a form explicitly (`BASE_ALIAS`); an unknown id maps by hash.
  - **Affinity** sets colour, inner light, pulse and a material overlay, all nine of spec v2:
    - hunger: orange smoulder with embers
    - malice: venom green with black veins
    - fear: ice with frost
    - faith: pale violet
    - blood: garnet heartbeat
    - shadow: smoky glass
    - stone: grey
    - echo: azure with a ghost outline
    - flame: hallowed gold-white with fine rays, visibly wrong beside the violet stones. A profaned flame stone is tarnished gold with a violet core and defiled veins.
  - **Grade** sets size (0.8–1.0), facet count (mean about 37 at Dull, about 230 at Impossible), clarity, emission and glints. With no base given, rarer grades lean toward the stranger bases.
  - **Modifiers** each leave a small mark on the largest visible piece of the stone. A mod family always gets the same mark (hash of the id without its tier digits), one of ten kinds (inclusion, vein, frost, band, speckle, bubbles, hairline, motes, star, ripple) in a second colour, sized by tier. A curse is black veins welling with red light, a darkening and an inverted-triangle brand. The 22 odd effects keep their own signs (`EFFECT_SIGN`).
- **The rarity tail (`oneIn`)**:
  - ≥ 1 in 1,000 adds orbiting motes.
  - ≥ 1 in 10,000 adds chromatic split edges.
  - ≥ 1 in 100,000 makes the stone a night sky inside: a dark interior with turning stars and a nebula, a white rim, and motes passing in front of and behind it. Nothing else in the game looks like this.
  - The reveal animation scales with log10(oneIn): a longer dark, a slower turn, a bigger flare and, at the top of the tail, a ring of light.
- **Craft states are pure animations of progress**: enchant (light gathers and the new mark appears), curse (the light gutters and the curse takes with a red pulse), recombine (both parents swing in and fuse; the fused form keeps the other parent grown into its side, in its own colour), facet (the wheel spins the old geometry out and the new one comes clean), jewel (the stone lowers into its bezel). Legacy cut, crack, graft and rough still draw but are not used by the base game.
- **Jewelry** (`drawJewel`): ring, amulet or circlet in dull worked gold, so the stone stays the brightest thing. At figure scale (≤ 10 px) it is a metal fleck plus one twinkling pixel of the stone's colour.
- **Resonance** (`drawResonance`): set stones of one affinity are joined by a braided, slowly flowing link. A Flame stone quarrels with any non-Flame stone beside it, shown as a snapping gold-white arc with sparks.
- **Performance**:
  - Idle world crystals blit a cached sprite (bucketed by size 7 %, light direction ×8, level and warmth), with a live pulse on the emission layer and live star glints.
  - A crystal is painted live only when it is animated, larger than 96 px, orbiting at ≥ 32 px, floating at ≥ 40 px, has a moving effect, or is in the top tail.
  - Icons are cached per crystal, size and dpr.
- Facilities and places (§15.7, §15.10 A, §16): six loop facilities (tally house, gem workshop, forge, the Flame's chapel, apothecary, the Council's vault) and six everyday places (tavern, bakery, guard post, carters' yard, mill, wash-place) are ordinary plan buildings picked deterministically (own block first, else nearest) and dressed in front of their facades with pictogram signs; `townFacilities(plan)` / `townHomes(plan)` publish ids, doors and work spots; `facilityRoute` is the crystals' road (pit → tally → gem workshop / forge → vault) along the street graph, dressed as a cart track with violet grit and tally-board waymarks; `drawFacilityClaim` is the "now yours" overlay (violet veins and crystal clusters).
- Open: spec v2 says town buildings should be alien and organic; the current look is human shanty-industrial — awaiting the orchestrator's ruling.

- **Grade ladder, round 2 (successor, 2026-11-23; stopped at owner §19 "just get something in place").** Grade is read without hue:
  the grade (not the affinity) owns the idle motion (Dull still · Clear breathes · Bright twinkles · Blazing beats · Royal a sweep ·
  Impossible drifts out of step) and the number of star glints (0..5; icons draw them all, still; in the world they never fade
  below a resting glint). Signs outside the stone (`GRADE_SIGN`): UI (icons, anchor 'center') — Bright a soft backlight, Blazing a
  slow burst of eight rays, Royal a thin orbit ring (far half behind, near half in front) that its motes ride, Impossible a dark
  disc that drinks the light, edged in a prismatic ring; world (anchor 'base') — the ground pool (Bright small, Blazing strong),
  Blazing a four-pointed core flare, Royal its orbit, Impossible a shadow pool with a prismatic edge on the ground instead of light.
  Colour code: affinity pulled back inside (≈12–28 % of facets, the depths, the core tint); a test checks every body is violet for
  all nine affinities × five grades. Painterly depth: every facet is a lit→shadow gradient; ink 0.75–3.4 px; Blazing's seams burn.
  Blind test (`sheets blind`, `BLIND_SALT` reshuffles 24 icons + 24 world stones, random base/affinity): icons 24/24 on a fresh
  shuffle; world 19/24 at 16–22 px (Clear vs Bright remain the confusable pair).


#### CRYSTAL follow-up (2026-11-23) — every grade is violet; a stone without an affinity is its grade's own violet
- `RARITY_LOOK` is rebuilt from the soul violet for all six grades (glow brightness rises Dull → Royal, saturation Dull → Blazing, core whiteness through every grade; Impossible is dark violet glass with a white core, its prismatic edge only in `gradeAura()`). No gold, red or blue grade colours; the old tables survive as `PALETTES` for affinity bases and the magpie flecks only. A test holds it (`tests/crystal/raritylook.test.ts`).
- A stone drawn with no `affinity` takes its grade's own violet, not a default affinity by grade (a bare Royal used to come out orange). Stones that carry an affinity keep their tint.
- The effect drawer (`fxCrystalDrawer`) draws the lit stone as the UI icon does: centred, turning about its middle, the grade's signs behind it, no ground pool; only `state: 'rough'` draws the husk.

### WRITING round 3
Spec v2 / v2.1 words (mining, long-tail crystals, crafting by facility, items, the lived-in world). `src/content/{gems,craft,mining,ledger31,living}.ts`.
- **Player words.** A modifier is a **line** on a stone ("Add a line", "3 of 4 lines"). A curse is a **cursed line** that states **its price** plainly ("The price: all heat 10 % higher"). An item is **worn**; a cursed item is **slipped** to someone not yours. Grades read "Grade n of 6". Rough / Cut / Graft are retired; cut choices are now Faceting ("A deep cut · keeps every line · 10 % weaker").
- **Tribute labels.** A find a follower brings on their own is "Brought unasked". The Offer haul is "As you asked". The dawn summary counts them apart.
- **"1 in N".** Two significant figures with commas, then "million" / "billion" ("1 in 48,000", "1 in 8.1 billion"). It has seven named bands: Everyday < 25, Unusual < 250, Scarce < 2,500, Rare < 25,000, Very rare < 100,000, Once in a run < 1M, Never seen. Spoken reveal lines start at Scarce. The dawn summary names "the find of the day" from 1 in 2,500.
- **Names.** A name is built from base + affinity + grade + lead line (mods[0]) + effect. A curse lends its word first (60 %). The effect swaps at most one word. Stones at 1 in 25,000 or rarer draw from their own word pools ("The Inward Grudge", "Fork Without Hands"). Names are 2–4 words and ≤ 26 characters, with no repeated stems and no rarity words; this is tested over 5,000 real rolls. After "the", a name that starts with "The" drops it ("the Ninth Blade").
- **Bases.** `BASE_MIRROR` copies the sim's weights and inherent properties, so the base lines can state numbers; a drift test fails if the sim's table changes.
- **Blessings.** 'Grafter' is shown as **Stone Wedding** ("Recombined stones keep more, and grade up more often."). Gemcutter reads "Enchanting costs half the Nether, and never curses."
- **Resonance.** Two of a kind are named and described. For three of a kind, the text proposes set-power names and effects (`resonanceText(id, 3)`), which the sim does not yet implement.
- **Lived-in world (§16).** Day notes are one plain line each: what happened and where, ≤ 70 characters with an 8-letter name and "the market square" (≤ 74 at the longest place). There are 66 world events × 3 variants, the sim's own Day events, and everyday-life notes. A per-run `Notebook` walks every variant of a key before it repeats one, so a week never reads the same twice. Keys are `FeedItem.textKey` (`event.* sim.* life.* weather.* calendar.*`). Any LIVING kind id that differs maps through `NOTE_ALIAS`, and unknown keys fall back to the feed's own `text`.
- **Trades.** The 75 LIVING trades get plain English names where the code id is jargon (powderman → "powder keeper", ventman → "air-door keeper", ashboy → "sweeper", chaplain → "shrine keeper", trader → "stallholder"). Each has one line on what they do and one on what they can do for you.
- **Calendar and weather.** Each rule is stated in one clause that matches LIVING's effects ("Half the diggers go down; the Temple watches closer.", "Guards and searches see less.").
- **Spelling.** British, with -ize: jewellery, colour, rumour, grey.

### PLAY-WORLD — the living world on the canvas (builder #1, 2026-11-23)
- **One shift list.** The Day's mining events (SIM-v3 shapes: `strike`, `carted`, `tally`, `search`, … with `time` 0..1) are handed over once at End Night (`shift.begin`, `src/scenes/play/shift.ts`) and read by every world-side adapter: BEHAV stages them (miners stop and look, pocket or carry to the cart, the count queue), FEEL's film shoots them, AMBIENT pulses at the faces (cart loads per face, working faces, the count's lamp), and the world fires the effects itself only when FEEL's film is off. The stand-in's strikes are converted by `shiftFromStandin`.
- **The film shows what the player can see.** FEEL gets `seen` (mine areas that are not Void) and `figureH`; strikes and carts in Void areas are heard (AMBIENT, the count) but kept out of the film, so no shot ever pushes into black.
- **FEEL's effects are VFX-native.** The play scene's effects adapter passes VFX kinds with world points straight through and returns the handle (earlier it dropped every one it did not translate — strike glints, pockets, auras, offer reveals, card effects).
- **Default close-up:** scale 7.5 (a figure ≈ 45 px on a 900-px view, the grave with its neighbours in frame); "Whole world" stays one key away.
- **The grave's hoard is in the world:** every loose crystal lies at the grave's lip (drawn by CRYSTAL, cut look — §15.10 has no rough state) and drops in when it arrives, so eating, burning, recombining and setting visibly change the grave.
- **Tribute vs Offer at dusk:** Offer followers walk to the grave in BEHAV's procession; a follower bringing unasked tribute walks alone (`offer` life event per bearer).
- **Depth mark:** inside the mine a small fathom gauge at the left edge (upper galleries / lower workings / the Deep) with the level's name for three seconds when it changes; screen space, never over a head. The depth look of the rock itself is the mine engine's.
- **Time of day:** AMBIENT's clock follows the Day (dawn ≈ 0.22 → dusk ≈ 0.84 over the replay), deep night while the player acts; the bell rings at the start of the shift.
- **(successor 2) The film's clock is the shift, not the calendar day.** LIVING's plan time 0 is the dawn bell with everyone at home in town, and the mine is empty again from ≈ 0.86. The Day film is about the mine, so film 0 = plan 0.095 (the first cage going down full) and film 1 = plan 0.9 (the last cage up); BEHAV is staged on the mapped time, and LIVING's feed and weather reach FEEL in film time (`shift.ts planT / filmT`). What happens at home before the cage goes down is noted at dawn.
- **(successor 2) "Whole world" frames the known world:** the pit head plus every town block that is not Void (Sensed along followers' routes, their hearths, colour); the surface frame when that is ≤ 3 blocks, the town round them when more, the whole town once everything is known. Never a black town with a dot.
- **(successor 2) The ambience follows the view** (4 Hz, changed beds only): lift machinery when the shaft is on screen and the cage moves, carts while the shift works the faces in view, water by the sump, smelter / market / wind on the surface, the weather of the Day's clock, the night's hush. `grave` and `procession` stay the scene's (the offering).
- **(successor 2) The dusk's bearers are BEHAV's walk and the film waits for them:** bearers (the Day's `offered` units, FEEL's `duskWalk`, the scene's dusk list) go to BEHAV as `StageOpts.bearers` on every stage call of that Day; they stay below after the shift and kneel at the grave by ≈ 0.93. The film's end (plan time) moves to the last bearer's arrival (0.9 … 0.985), so at dusk every stone's bearer stands at the grave; dusk then runs on from there at film pace to the walk home. Everyone, sim units too, is placed by `stage()` at night.
- **(successor 2) A wick changes only with its effect:** every conversion is caught where the world sees the follower flag flip (whatever path made it) and plays `wickTurn` at the crown; the figure keeps its old light, out, until the effect reaches violet (1.55 s). Fear reacts → `wickGutter`, Pray → `wickFlare`, Lie low → `wickHood`, death → `wickOut`.
- **(successor 2) Weather in play:** WEATHER at quality 'low' on LIVING's timeline (today's and tomorrow's plans), at the plan time the stage shows.


#### VFX round 3
- Perf: big glows (≥ 90×90 device px) go to a half-res light layer (32 px dirty tiles, composited with 'lighter'). Ribbons and rings batch into one Path2D per style; ink is a widened outline fill. A deterministic load governor (effects + particles/60 → level 0/1/2) sheds washes first (budget 0.9 × canvas area, radius cap 240), then faint glows, ring segments, thin ink and dust strides. Readable cores (pins) are never shed.
- Crystal light: shape.ts wraps CRYSTAL's drawCrystal. Colour comes from grade (RARITY_LOOK); scale and drama come from staged rarity. Affinity sets colour and burst form (maw, thorns, halo, flicker, drip, eclipse, shards, echo, fork, star). Depth darkens and enriches. A tail find (oneIn ≥ 10,000) triggers a once-a-run tailFind moment on strike and on reveal.
- Small effects carry a 3–6 px ink-backed four-point pin at zoom 1. Icon glyphs (eye, flame, danger, runes) were replaced with inked light strokes. CARD_TABLE maps all 30 card ids to an effect.
- Time scale: fx.setTimeScale(k) for the ×1/×2/×4 Day, plus a per-spawn `speed`. sendRoute/sendPreview are deprecated. cut/crack/flaw/graft are not used in the mine; cut stays for the town's Faceting.

#### VFX round 4 (successor 4; owner §19: "just get something in place for now")
- **Colour code (theme §2/§8), no exceptions:** every effect colour comes from `src/render/fx/pal.ts`, which derives everything from `src/render/theme/palette.ts` and the wick colours in `folk.ts`; `tests/vfx/palette.test.ts` fails on any hex, `rgb(`/`hsl(` literal elsewhere under `src/render/fx` (allow-list: the flat dev gallery backdrop). Grades are all violet, read by brightness, saturation, size, ceremony and rays (0,2,3,4,6,8); affinity is an inner second colour only. Hunger / evil = violet going to black; red only where blood is spilled; Temple = gold fire; Council (searches, exposure, tally, lockdown) = cold white.
- **The wick effects** (`wickTurn`, `wickGutter`, `wickFlare`, `wickOut`, `wickHood`) sit at the crown point the caller passes. While the handle is alive the effect owns the light: the caller draws the figure's own wick out (`state: 'dead'` until THEME offers a no-wick option), then switches to the final state (turn → 'yours'; out → 'dead'; hood → hood up from `FX_TIMING.wickHoodOn`). The bud is never faded by the effect, so the hand-back has no pop.
- **`dayMark`** (FEEL's Day notes in the world): a ground ring in the event owner's colour (violet / gold / cold white / red / tan), heavy notes pulse a second ring, 2.2 s, only where visibility is 'active'; owner from `owner`, else guessed from the note `kind`, else daily life. Offer beats: `best` is staged one ceremony up (`rarity: max(grade, royal)`, real `grade` colour) — the loudest moment of the dusk.
- **Void check:** the remaining black-pixel changes (rootBloom, soothingMurmur, bell, crusade, silence: 4–308 px over 24 cells) all fall in areas the world reports visible (black the mine paints, not Void); every effect is clipped where `visibilityAt` says Void.
- **Hosts may reuse one point object** from `toScreen`: the draw context now copies each point before asking for the next (the round-3 mine harness reused one and drew every effect at scale 0; the play camera returns fresh points and was never affected).

#### PLAY-WORLD successor 3 (2026-11-23)
- **The grave hoard is the grave's state, every change a movement** (`src/scenes/play/grave.ts`): loose stones lie in two heaps either side of the grave's hole; set stones stand in seats on the hole's front rim (the hoard draws them; the scene's old socket ring stands down). Set / take out = the stone flies between heap and seat; recombine = two fly together and one new stone is struck; eat / burn = it sinks into the hole; an arrival drops out of the dark onto its heap. The grave answers each change (a violet wash that holds ~2 s, a column of light on the lock) until VFX round 6's grave moments carry that light (`lightByFx`). The hoard runs on wall time so its movements play while the grave panel is open.
- **The empty dawn:** the Day film opens at plan time `shift.planA` — PLAN_A normally; on a day the cage is held up (LIVING's liftJam, staged by BEHAV, invisible in the plan) the first plan time with ≥ 10 people standing in the mine (≤ 0.3). No Day opens on an empty mine.
- **The Day is watched where things happen:** FEEL's framing gets the seen mine zones that have today's events, the coloured zones and the grave (not every Sensed corner, not the surface while the mine is seen) — the mine fills the frame with ~40-px people.
- **The surface's daylight follows the Day:** every WorldView carries `dayT` (the same clock AMBIENT gets).
- **Followers' colour seeds come from the sim** (`colourSeedsOf`): hearths and strong haunts carry a violet seed point.

### HUD — round 3 (builder #3, v3.1)
- Miracle cards are arched, evil cards notched: the kind reads from the silhouette before colour. Each card has its own inked emblem (`src/ui/play/cardart.ts`), drawn procedurally so it ships with no asset licences.
- The grave is the home screen: one item card on the right shows everything about a stone (odds as "1 in N", ordered lines, curses marked); verbs state their consequence in one line. Facilities not yet earned are listed, locked, with a plain unlock line.
- Day notes stand at the place for 2.6 s (heavy: 4.2 s), at most three, pushed off the grave and panels, then live in a "Today" list; the dawn/dusk ledger sits low-right, never centre.
- The top bar shows the count as a word ("Count · Uneasy"); the numbers live in the tip. The count is Council property, so it is cold white (theme §2).
- End Night's preview first names what is still unspent (unplayed hand, actions left).
- **Theme pass (docs/theme.md, §17).** No Earth animals: rats in the mine and every town critter (the old chickens, dogs, cat) are now **skitters** (pale, segmented, eight rippling legs, feelers); the bats at the shaft are **lamp-moths** (pale, fluttering in the shaft's light); the town's birds, crows and bats are off; the road wagons are pulled by **pit beasts** (ribbed teal shell, six stub legs, blind head, two sweeping feelers). Sky: a truly black night (deep blue-black multiply), a warm dim amber day (lower exposure, amber balance), a copper dusk. Smoke is pale (fuel-cakes). Still to do: eels in the pools, pit-beast cart trains in the mine, shard-lamp hum/cold-white flicker on mine gallery lamps, the crystal-fired lift engine's pale steam plume, the pump's rocking beam (see handoffs/AMBIENT.md next steps).
- **Grade cost.** The grade is composed on two small canvases (a multiply field and a colour-dodge exposure field, 192 px wide) and applied with ≤ 2 full-screen draws: the per-wash full-screen soft-light/dodge fills cost 10–20 ms delivered on the software-rendered box.

### HUD — round 5 (builder #5, function first per §19)
- **One place for "what just happened": the left column under the goal.** Night tickers and the Day's feed both live there; during the Day a ticker line joins the feed instead of stacking separately. Notes still appear briefly at the place (spec §5); the feed list is the record. Each row/note carries a pictogram (theme §4: pick, cup, stone, rain, sun, hearth, people, basket, whistle, knot-cord, flame) in its owner's colour only when the news is the owner's (Council cold white, Flame gold, yours violet, blood red); weight 2 rows are drawn heavier (display face, owner bar). The header reads "Today · <calendar>" with up to three "why today" lines (WRITING `effectWhy`).
- **The grave is the home screen and takes the left side:** while it is open the hands tuck below the frame, and the panel runs down over the belt and chronicle (its own sockets are inside it). The item card's lines scroll; its verbs never leave the card.
- **Hand cards stand side by side** sized to their half of the free band (floor 96 px; overlap only below that) and show the name and what they burn at rest; the full line is on the big card (hover / click). Under 1440 px wide the belt keeps only the Grave button so the hands get the room. A card on the right that reaches into the bottom band also bounds the hands.
- **Crystal grades share one violet scale** (ash → bright violet) for frames and glows; the old gray→ice→jade→sapphire→crimson→gold ladder is gone (gold is the Flame's). Stones are drawn by CRYSTAL from the sim crystal itself (`CrystalVM.sim`), else from as much of it as the view model carries.
- **Person cards** show Wick · Now · Hearth · Ties under the plain line, then the odds with the top three reasons and "N more".
- **Dusk ledger:** a few ledger rows (owner-coloured values) and the count as a cold-white Council row with its consequence.
- **Hints are shown once:** a hint that was on screen over 1.2 s and yields (to a moment, a higher beat, a phase change) is marked seen and never returns.
- Settings already hold master / music / effects, interface size and reduce motion (shell-owned `src/ui/settings.ts`); an ambience slider is requested from the shell owner.

### HUD — round 6 (builder #6: the bridge feeds the HUD)
- **The bridge (`src/scenes/play/hudbridge.ts`) is the HUD's.** It reads the real sim (`RealSim.g`) directly for what the scene's narrow `PState` does not carry: LIVING's person (line re-voiced by WRITING, hearth, ties), the whisper odds' terms, the sim crystal, the Day film's calendar / weather / effects, threats and exposure. It is unit-tested headless against real sensible-bot states (night 3, Shade) through a recording stand-in for `mountHud`.
- **Enchant is never a dead button.** Every stone is rolled full, so "Add a line" on a full stone becomes "Reroll the weakest line" (a curse first, else the lowest tier) and names the line it replaces; a refusal is always the real reason ("Needs 2 Nether (you have 1)."), never the sim's "every slot is filled".
- **The odds' reasons in plain words** ("How strong you are", "How hard they are to sway", the trait, "Your followers near them", "The town is wary of you", "Asleep: the dream is open"), strongest first; LIVING's part of the bonus is split out and named by the day's own `effectWhy` line.
- **The pending event cannot be missed:** the sim's event opens the HUD's event card; End Night (button or Enter) while it waits brings the card back; End Night's preview leads with "An event waits: <title>. Answer it first."
- **The town's danger is drawn three ways:** three heat bars in the top bar beside the danger word (Temple gold, Council cold white, Public bone; a ring at 50, a pulse at 75), a standing warning per telegraphed faction move in the sim's own words ("At dusk tomorrow: priests ward the well. Answer with …", click → the place), and a warning when followers are exposed while a track is near its line. End Night's preview names what lands at this dusk and who is exposed. Crowd and Elite cards reuse PLAY-SHADE's model (`eliteTargets`, `exposure`, `factionMeters`) so the two town interfaces say the same thing.
- **Light day notes stand at their place too** (briefly, ≤ 3 on screen) when FEEL says where they happened and the Day is not catching up; the feed remains the record.
- **The Day's clock is a word** (The bell, Morning, Noon, Afternoon, Evening, Dusk) the HUD reads from the Day's progress every frame.
- **The grave never scrolls:** the panel is a column whose stone grid takes what is left; crafting is one folded row of chips (line on hover).
- **Ambience volume** is a fourth slider (Settings → Sound, beside the other volumes; key `ambience`, default 80), its own audio bus, applied through `audio.setVolume('ambience', v / 100)`.
