- **The miracle pay box counts what the sim counts.** Each stone shows its burn worth *for this miracle* (affinity-weighted, `payValue`), not its face value; the preselection is topped up with the smallest stone that closes any gap, so the default Pay always goes through. Under Blood Price the box asks only for the stones on hand (Ichor pays the rest).
- **This world's words, at the bridge.** The sim's rules names are rewritten wherever its text reaches the player (events, threats, the count, dawn lines): "Government Suspicion" → "Council heat", "Cult" → "Temple", "Public Suspicion" → "town heat". There is no government here.
- **The scene's top-right controls keep their room.** The top bar measures any `[data-action="camera"]`/`.pl-cam` button each layout and stops 8 px short of it (wrapping the danger group to a second row if needed) rather than covering it.
- **FEEL drives the offering strip by crystal id.** A FEEL beat names the stone; its index is only a fallback, so the scene's list order never has to match FEEL's plan. FEEL's ledger cue is completed from the sim (`duskLedger`: lead line, rows, the count) so one ledger shows both FEEL's lines and the Council's numbers.
- **No raw word key ever reaches the screen.** Counts of one read singular ("1 crystal"); an unknown card kind shows its own name capitalised; the record panel is "The record"; a faction's announced move reads in WRITING's words with its answers named (the answering act of evil by its card name).

### PLAY-CORE — the night's interface and rules wiring (successor, 2026-11-23)
- **The turn (spec §5, §16):** Night → End Night → the Day (FEEL's fast-forward and clock; notes collect in the HUD feed) → **dusk**: the offering strip (each crystal set down and revealed; the world's arrival / reveal effects follow the strip's beats) → a short **ledger** ("Dusk, day N": what the shift gave, the count as *expected · counted · short*, colour gained; Continue / Space) → **the cards are dealt** → the night. The dawn summary box is gone; the watershed's words (stage up) still use the same box once, then that day's dusk follows. The scene's phase is `__hg.play.dayPhase` ('night' | 'day' | 'dusk'), the clock `dayClock` (0–1), the feed size `dayNotes`, the ledger `ledger`.
- **Night 1 has no hands** (it teaches whisper → routine → End Night). The hands are dealt at the end of each dusk from night 2. Hints show only at night (never over the Day, the offering or the ledger) and the grave's introduction waits for night 2.
- **The first whisper** to the sleeper by the grave is ≥ 0.9 (near certain) so the run always starts with a follower; later whispers follow the rules.
- **The first day teaches the strike:** a follower's first swing on day 1 always finds a stone (stand-in rule), so dusk 1 or 2 has something to reveal. **The Council's guess** starts on day 3: on days 1–2 *expected* = what was dug (short = exactly what your followers kept back).
- **Tribute rule (stand-in):** followers on Offer bring everything at dusk; those who Pray may bring their best find of the day unasked (chance 0.35 + 0.12 × devotion, ≤ 0.9); those lying low give theirs to the count.
- **Crafting:** the HUD's native Enchant (Nether by grade; adds a modifier into an open slot, else rerolls the weakest; 8 % curse) and Recombine (two loose crystals → one; 10 % one grade higher). The relabelled graft panel is retired; facilities (facet / forge) wait for the real sim.
- **Stand-in costs:** 3 actions a night (+1 per Divinity level above 1, + set "action" lines); whisper 1, routine change 1, Move 1–2; miracles burn crystals worth their cost; acts of evil are free; devour is always in the evil hand from night 2. The real sim replaces these behind `?standin=sim`.
- **The real rules are live (successor #2):** the play scene runs on the real sim (`src/scenes/play/realsim.ts`: `RealSim` drives the app `Session`, `RealNight` maps the night's verbs to sim actions and the sim's events to the scene's moments). The swap is the one constant `USE_REAL` in `sim.ts`; `?sim=standin` keeps the old stand-in for comparison. Under the real sim the night-1 hand is dealt by the sim but not shown (night 1 still teaches whisper → routine → End Night); stand-in-only rules above (first-swing strike, ≥ 0.9 first whisper, the Council's day-3 guess) no longer apply — the sim's numbers do.
- **Never a black hold for the mine's bake:** `goto` into a scene that draws the mine (cutscene, play, bonus) waits until the shared background bake (started on the title) is done, keeping the current picture with a quiet line "The mine stirs… N %" (at most 25 s; after that the scene bakes the rest itself). Pending story events and level-up blessings are answered at the start of the night through the HUD's pick-one cards before End Night is accepted.
- **The Day ends when FEEL's film says so** (`film.onEnd`), with `dusk: false` until the HUD renders FEEL's own dusk cues (ledger, offering beats); Skip is FEEL's fast catch-up to dusk (≤ 1.2 s; a second press is instant). FEEL's HUD cues go to `hud.cue` untouched (light notes feed-only).

- **Successor 3 (a whole run by play):** (1) **Answer order** follows the sim: a waiting story event first, then a blessing, then a facet pick (`autoplay.ts pendingChoice`); the old blessing-first order made a refused pick re-open forever. An event option the rules refuse right now (e.g. it needs crystals the grave lacks) is marked off with "Not now: <why>"; the card's title, body and labels come from WRITING (`text.event`) when it has the event, the consequence line keeps the sim's exact numbers. (2) **The town is PLAY-SHADE's stage** (its agreed split): on the watershed's last frame (`handback: 'whole'`) the scene mounts `mountShade` — its own Shade HUD, hit-testing in sim ids (Elites first, a crowd member → the crowd), every sim action legal in the town (threat answers, favours, facets) — and routes update / render / input to it; the mine HUD stays hidden. Every town step is adopted by the Session (`Session.adopt`), so the chronicle, the defeat screen and autosave stay whole; once a step is adopted without its action the save carries the state itself (`SaveData.snapshot`, ~0.4 MB) and Continue resumes in the town. A win plays FINALE's ending inside the stage, then the title; a loss goes to FRONT's defeat screen (what killed you, the tally, Title). (3) **The scripted player** (`autoplay.ts nextVerb`) is the game's own: the headless full-run test and the browser full run both read it; it only names the next verb, the player's clicks do it.

### WEATHER
- **One sky state, black-safe by construction.** `src/render/weather` publishes `weather.state` (rain, drop, cloud, dark, fog, wind + direction + gust, cold, wetness, puddles, mine flood, seep, lightning flash, visibility, rain-front cover, audio levels). Everything drawn over the finished picture (the world's `sky` seam) uses only blends that leave exact `#000` black — `overlay`, `soft-light`, `multiply`, `saturation`, `color-dodge` and the host's self-`lighter` flash — so rain, fog lift, grade and lightning show in proportion to the light already there: full over Active, gray over Sensed (all marks neutral), nothing over the Void, and no band at a fog edge. Streaks well inside seen ground (fog `seen` > 0.6 at both ends) add a faint pale `lighter` layer so rain still reads over dark ground.
- **In colour where it belongs.** Ground things (puddles, lamp light smeared down wet ground, eave drips, runoff into the pit) and fog banks are drawn inside the town's colour pass (`inColour` seams) so the town's own Sensed/Void composite covers them like its art; the mine's answer (drips, wet wall glints, the flood, the shaft column) is drawn in the mine's colour pass and weighted by the mine's colour at each mark.
- **Rain is screen-space, panned with the world.** Three depth layers of tapered streaks in tiles of different sizes, offset by the camera's pan (not its zoom, so a zoom never smears the rain); splashes are world-anchored in power-of-two cells so they hold still on a pan. LOD by scale: close (crowned splashes, longer drops) → town → far (< 0.6: streaks fade out; a half-res streak veil + multiply darkening masked by the rain band takes over) → valley.
- **The rain front.** Rain arrives as a band moving along the wind across the town (~7 s) and leaves the same way (upwind clears first); `rainAt(x, y)` and `state.cover` carry it, so people, audio and puddles agree with what is drawn.
- **Weather time.** Wetness, puddles and the mine flood integrate real dt + timeline advance × `unitSeconds` (900 s per unit), so a 15–20 s Day film wets and dries plausibly and a frozen Night still dries slowly. Fields ease (τ 1.2 s) so a jump never pops.
- **Theme (§6, DECISIONS §17):** rain fine, cold and slanted (always leans with the wind); white-violet lightning (bolt + flash + hard building shadows for its few frames); wind shows as drawn gust lines and ochre lichen grit (no grass/leaves); the night grade is capped so forms stay readable (never "lights on black").
- **LIVING's timeline** arrives as `DayWeather` (t 0 = dawn bell … 1 dusk … 2 next dawn; windDir = compass degrees FROM): `weather.set(day | days)` converts it (`fromLiving`, position = `dayIndex·2 + t`, `livingPos`).

- **Successor round (2026-11-23, owner §19: "get something in place"):** cost-driven rework — the grade is two SOLID
  fills (multiply tint incl. the rain band where the view is; pale overlay lift for fog / cold / rain haze); one 1/8-res
  multiply buffer (cloud shadows, the band and its darker front) only at far zoom (< 0.62) or quality 'high'; rain,
  splashes and the far curtain are stroked hairlines (5× cheaper than filled slivers in software rendering) with a faint
  'lighter' twin only where the fog cache says the ground is well seen; fog banks and lamp halos go into one ¼-res buffer,
  one blit, in the town's colour pass. Lightning's hard building shadows are off by default (`weather.hardShadows`): the
  swept footprints read as loose dark wedges.
- **The mine's answer goes through the mine's own seam:** `mine.setAtmos({ wet, water, drip, rain })` every update
  (`weather.atmos()`); the mine renderer draws sheen, water, drips and rain down the shaft inside its colour pass.
  WEATHER's own mine marks stay behind `weather.mineOverlay = true` (post-fog, never in-colour: the world's in-colour
  mine seams cost a full-screen scratch + two void covers + a blit each).
- **AMBIENT link:** `linkAmbient(amb)` calls `amb.setWeather(() => { wetness, windX, windY, wind, visibility, flash: 0 })`
  when it exists (AMBIENT's smoke, flags, washing, cap-tree caps and dust follow our wind; its stand-in rain and mist
  switch off; flash 0 so the lightning is not applied twice), and still zeroes AMBIENT's rain and cloud shadows.
- **Live wiring (landing round):** one Weather per run, owned by the play scene's world (`world.ts`, eager import so it
  exists on the first frame), quality 'medium' with the governor on. The town stage does not make a second one: it is
  handed `world.sky` (`mountShade({ sky })`) and drives it (LIVING's slices via `fromLiving`, position
  `livingPos(turn, Day clock)`, the night holds the Day's last weather, `audio.setWeather` each frame). With no LIVING
  plan the scene uses a seeded stand-in day (`seededDay(seed, day)`: fair 36 % / showers 30 % / fog 20 % / storm 14 %).
  When the linked AMBIENT stops updating (the town stage does not run it) the weather takes its clock light from the
  world's `dayT` and pushes its own darkening into `town.setSky`. The town sky binds on `townReady` when attached early.
  Wind wisps were thickened (line 1.2 → 1.9, alpha +0.1, 14 → 20) after the live check read them as nothing at play zoom.
### LOOP — the playtest harness (successor #3, 2026-11-23)
- **What a pair measures:** a before/after pair (offering, card play, enchant, recombine, socket) counts only the WORLD canvas (`#game`), net of a no-input control over the same span. When the camera moves, the whole-view diff is not counted; a crop around the grave sized in **world units** (220×140, so it covers the same ground at any camera position or zoom) is compared instead, at a quarter weight (≈ 1/16 of the view).
- **Life at every zoom (§16):** during Day 2 the reel wheels through close → mine → pit head → town → valley and takes two 1 s pairs at each. A zoom is alive when ≥ 0.4 % of the seen (non-black) area changes with the camera still, or ≥ 25 % of the people on screen moved (positions taken relative to the grave and divided by the camera scale, so a moving camera cancels out). "Still a whole Day" uses the same grave-relative positions.
- **Weather over Void:** only black pixels that light up and go dark again during a Day count (streaks, drops); a reveal that stays lit is the fog's business.
- **Designed truncation is not "cut off":** text with an ellipsis or a line clamp is recorded as truncated, not clipped; cards fanned in one `[data-hand]` may overlap each other.
- **A blocked run judges nothing downstream:** if the run never reaches night 1, every night/Day rule reads BLOCKED (not FAIL), so the failing rules left are the ones that stopped it.
- **`--from bonus`** enters at `?scene=bonus&seed=N` when the opening is broken; the report says so in its first line. **`--quick`** = no cutscene, 3 nights, half-size capture, `screenshots/reel/quick/`.

### LOOP — the shot loop (successor #4, 2026-11-23)
- **One beat per spec §5 step, three verdicts:** `present` (it happened — the hook or the screen confirms — and the world shows it), `missing` (its UI/hook never appeared), `broken` (it happened but shows nothing in the world, errors, takes too long, or the harness got stuck there). A beat with a console error is never `present`. `not reached` = the run's time budget ran out or the page died.
- **"Shows in the world" = the game canvas alone** (read back at 400×225, no HUD), changed by ≥ 0.8 % (crystal actions, offering) or ≥ 1 % (cards) more than a 1.2 s no-input control; a card whose change is mostly a camera move (< 4 % net with the camera moved) is `broken`.
- **The cutscene is filmed as a time-lapse** (5 moments at ≈3/15/27/39/51 s via `__hg.advance`), then its last shot plays in real time so the hand-over is real; a hand-over is a cut when one frame-to-frame step changes > 35 % of the screen.
- **Shade and the ending are reached by debug jumps** (a 6-minute reel cannot play 25 turns): xp 399.9 + the pit head's Hold 100 written through `__hg.hooks.play.sim.state()` before night 3 ends, so the stage-up runs on the game's own path (fallback `debugFinale('watershed')`); the ending by `debugFinale('ending')`. INDEX.md says so.
- **Never hang:** every wait is bounded, every beat has a budget (a stuck beat gets a `-stuck` screenshot and the run goes on, recovering to the next night like a player: Esc the cutscene, take a bonus, skip the Day, Continue), the run has a hard deadline, and the build retries twice then writes a BUILD FAILED index.

### LIVING — The society
- **Self-contained rules module** (`src/sim/living/**`): pure, seeded functions of (society, dayIndex, facts); imports nothing from the
  sim; the sim plugs in through `planDay` once per turn and `plan.next` (the chronicle travels in the society: chains, lasting effects,
  cooldowns, echoes already made, the bereaved). `adapter.ts` maps the sim's state structurally.
- **Hearths** (theme §3): households are built per zone as ~9-person hearths — an elder or two, a head couple, a grown couple,
  children, and a lodger or two — named "the third hearth in the rows". The old and the children are dealt to the hearths that lack
  them, so ~90 % of homes hold three generations. Soldiers live in the barracks, priests in the temple house.
- **Events as data**: 105 `EventDef`s with calendar / weather / state / chance triggers, a cast, a time window, a fallback line and
  small effects (`[kind, target, value, days, why]`). 16 chains run through the chronicle (a storm puts a wedding off a day, up to
  ten days). Echoes answer the god's doings once per fact (`turn:kind:target` key). LIVING never kills or levies an adopted sim unit
  on its own — only the sim's own `gone` facts end them — so the two never disagree about who is alive.
- **The levy** is an event chain on a fixed rhythm (called on the first day of every second week, marched three days later) rather
  than a calendar kind, so the 7-day week and its tests stay as they are; it takes generated people and crowd members only.
- **Burnings**: every death is followed by a burning at the burning ground; a death with no body (the god's devouring, a sacrifice)
  gets `emptyBurning`, which carries the scandal as suspicion and notice.
- **Feed**: every event, 1–3 weather notes when the weather turns (a dawn note when the day starts differently from how yesterday
  ended), 1–2 calendar notes, then life notes drawn from the actual plans (followers first; one per activity and two per zone on the
  first pass) to 8–10; cap 16 by weight. All in t ∈ [0, 1) so the Day film can show them. textKeys are the writer's families;
  `LIVING_NOTE_ALIAS` maps our ids onto the writer's where the happening is the same.

### THEME — the shared kit (successor, 2026-11-23)
- **Cap-trees (owner ruling §18):** one shared rule `capAlpha(figurePx)` = 1 at ≤16 px adult figure on screen, 0 at ≥40 px, smoothstep between. A cap-tree is three draws in three layers: `drawCapTreeShade` (ground, strength 1 − capAlpha), `drawCapTreeStalk` (y-sorted; `fade = 1 − capAlpha` fades the stalk's upper part so a close view shows a trunk, not a pole), `drawCapTreeCap` (top layer, alpha = capAlpha). Cap width is the size parameter; stalk height 0.78–1.0 × cap width, stalk 0.21–0.26 × cap width (thick, pale). Wind sways the cap only.
- **Props** (`props.ts`) are drawn in the three-quarter top-down view anchored at the ground contact point, three detail tiers picked from on-screen size. Sensed = luminance-preserving gray of every colour; night = mixed 62 % toward `#0a1113` with owned lights (lamp, brazier, crystal) left lit.
- **Extra ramps** (`RAMPS` in palette.ts, additive): capTeal, capAmber, gill, stalk, castCream, terraceTop, terraceFace, lane, vat, beast — sampled from town-target.png and pulled darker per §18. Amber cap-trees are warm ochre and never emit (gold light is the Flame's).
- **Lamp-moths** are drawn from above with swept wings and no wing spots: a pair of dark spots reads as eyeshine, which is how people are found in the dark.
- **Tests** `tests/theme/**` pin the colour code: only the violet family is violet, only red is red, gold / cold / teal / warm hue bands, every ramp steps lit > mid > shadow > ink, grayOf keeps luminance, wick colour = devotion, dead wick = 0, sleep < awake < prayer, capAlpha ends and monotonic easing.

### WRITING round 4
The wickfolk's words (docs/theme.md §10) and LIVING merged. Files: `src/content/{world,days,living}.ts`.

- **Forbidden list.** `tests/content/theme.ts` holds about 180 human-world words: burial, human animals, food, light and building, places, money and dress, body, bells and soldiers, religion and rank, weekdays, trees, farming, and bed phrases.
  - The test scans every string every content module exports.
  - These are allowed: cap-beer, cap-tree, grave (as an adjective), the burning ground, hearth, shard-lamp, chits, knot-cord and stalk-timber.
- **Re-voicing.**
  - Roles: farmer → Gardener, soldier → Guard, gravedigger → Ash keeper.
  - Trades: ratcatcher → skitter catcher, baker → oven keeper, chandler → fuel-cake maker, tailor → wrap-stitcher, pawnbroker → knot-lender, innkeeper → lodge keeper, ropemaker → cord twister, carpenter → stalk-joiner, bell ringer → bowl ringer, soldier → garrison guard.
  - Gem base "Bell" → "Bowl". Crystal name words: Candle → Glim, Saint → Martyr, Wolves → Skitters, Ravens → Wicks, Heaven → Hearth.
  - The UI's "the bell" → "the whistle".
  - The sim's display names still differ; tests map them through `THEMED`.
- **Limits.**
  - An event note is one sentence, two at most: ≤ 130 characters with an 8-letter name at "the market square".
  - A life or work note is ≤ 80.
  - Calendar, weather, effect and line texts are ≤ 60.
  - A week (seed 1, real LIVING feed) never shows a line twice. This is tested.
- **Pool sizes.** The notebook walks every variant of a key before repeating one, so pool size is set by weekly frequency:
  - the eight common activities have 10 lines;
  - dawn and turn weather have 6 each;
  - workday dusk and night have 7 (indexed by day);
  - every other key has ≥ 4.
- **Cases.** One LIVING id can carry two stories (the Council pays, or doesn't). `keyOf` picks a case pool from `slots.outcome`, or else from the fallback text or place, e.g. `event.paySettled.unpaid`, `event.childFound.mine`. A weather note after dawn reads `weather.<kind>.turn`.
- **PLACE_WORDS.** LIVING's human-world place names are re-voiced in notes until LIVING renames them at source:
  - bakehouse → cake ovens, chandlery → fuel-cake yard, pawn shop → knot-lender's;
  - alehouse → cap-beer shed, gate inn → gate lodge, scriptorium → cord room;
  - ropewalk → cord walk, stables → beast pens, graveyard → burning ground.
- **Dread without the god.** Echo notes (strange dreams, the violet showing, the guttering lamps) never name the player. They show warmth, violet and someone "glad to see them" under the rock.

### WRITING round 5
The town's contest (sim v3.3), the real story in the starting bonuses, the opening's lines, HUD kind words. Files: `src/content/{town,hudwords}.ts`, edits across `src/content`.
- **The three voices of the town's moves.** The Council speaks in notices ("Notice. Seal 2 of 4 on the writ … is to be set in two days.", "By order of the Magistrate"); the Temple is warm and certain ("Be glad, little wick: the Flame takes you home"); the crowd is rumour ("Talk at the well", "Somebody's cousin saw violet"). Each move has announce / looms / landed lines, a one-line rule and a dusk-ledger line. Blunted lines are told by the answer that broke the move, and an act of evil by the side it broke.
- **burnTheLedgers is shown as "Burn the Knot-Cords".** The id is kept. The Council records in knot-cords and notch tallies (theme §4), not ledgers. "Tally" is the retired name of the count, so the card is not called "Tally Cords". The two other acts keep their names: Silence the Preacher and Turn the Mob.
- **The starting bonuses follow the opening.** The god was a colossus of violet crystal, and every wick burned for it until the Flame came. It broke, and one shard lies in the dark. "Lingering Throne" becomes **Room in the Shard** ("I was a colossus once. There is still room in me."). "Sweet Voice" becomes **Remembered Voice** ("Every wick here once burned for me. Some still remember."). "Gemcutter" becomes **Old Facets** ("I was all facets once. I know what a stone wants."). Patient Hunger now says "I have starved since the Flame came." `text.bonus(id)` now returns the v3 blessing. The v1 starting gifts had the wrong rules: Lingering Throne gave reach, Cruel Delight gave Divinity.
- **The opening.** Final lines: "Once, every wick in this land burned for me." / "Then the Flame came, and they forgot me." / "I broke. The dark kept what was left." / (Hunger) "And we are so hungry." / "Listen. Someone is digging." The old third line, "I broke. I fell. I lay in the dark.", only said what the picture shows. "Light" became "wick" because the world has a word for it. "Turned to it" became "forgot me" because the picture already shows the turning, and the words should carry the hurt. A test keeps `script.ts` LINES equal to `CUTSCENE_LINES`. Each line has an alternative, and the timing budget is tested: at most 10 characters a second over the window, and fully typed for at least 1.5 s.
- **A stricter word list.** `forbiddenStrictIn` adds ledger, desk, drawer, ink, page, margins, coppers, quill, counting house, Government and Cult. Content was swept: ledgers became knot-cords, desks became the tally bench, coppers became chits. Other theme words were also changed: knelt → covered their wick, buried → saw burned, sleeve → wrap, bread → cap-flesh, boots → goggles, bell → whistle. The report `scripts/writing-forbidden.ts` scans every exported string, every generated string and every string literal in content and in the sim, read-only for the sim.
- **HUD words.** `hand.kind.*` and `card.kind.*` are covered for 14 kinds. "event" reads "Something happens", "bonus" reads "What survived the fall" and "threat" reads "Their move". `text.count(noun, n)` and `text.plural` are plural-safe.
- **The ending tease.** The "any" route no longer repeats its title ("Deepwell is yours" twice). It opens with "Every wick in Deepwell burns violet.", which echoes the opening's first line. The gold light now opens over the city's great dome (theme §6), not over a "highest tower".

### PLAY-SHADE — the Shade stage, playable (builder #1, 2026-11-23)
- **Self-contained stage** (`src/scenes/play/shade/**`, interface at the top of `handoffs/PLAY-SHADE.md`): `createShadeStage(host)` owns the sim state from the watershed's hand-back to the end of Shade; it draws its own plain panels (DOM, logical 1600×900 scaled to the canvas) and ground rings, and calls `host.onStep` / `host.onEnd`. Visuals are §19 "in place": whatever the town renderer draws, plus rings at block anchors (no text in the world).
- **Choices are the sim's legal actions.** Every button commits one exact sim `Action`; a choice the sim refuses is shown dimmed with the sim's own reason (`canWhisper`, `routineWhy`, `movePreview`, `cardView` + `actionWhy` on the exact card action). Picking: click a block on the map (hit-test) → the block card lists who is there (Elites, crowds, named people, your own) with whisper odds → pick one → its card (kind, Resistance, the top odds parts, what you can do).
- **Elite targets** (spec §12 "masters of those buildings") are a staged list derived from the sim, no new rules: *unknown* (their block is dark) → *found* (seen, out of reach or not strong enough) → *studied* (in reach: odds and Resistance shown) → *approached* (whispered at least once; failed whispers wear Resistance down, shown as "worn down by N") → *turned*. Each says what stands between you (the sim's blocker, reach, Resistance, a hunter in their block) and what turning them gives (faction cooling, the vault, the building's craft).
- **Pushback reads as reason + counter-play.** Every `factionAction` id the sim emits has a plain "why" and "what you can do" (`PUSHBACK` in model.ts; a test checks every id seen in 3 bot Shade runs). Pushback notes pulse at their block in the faction's colour (Temple gold, Council cold, Public ochre), slow the Day's film and take the camera; the Dusk ledger lists them again with suspicion from → to. Announced purges ring their block in red until dawn.
- **The Day** is a 9 s fast-forward (×1/×2/×4, skip) of the sim's own `day.events + day.dusk` as notes in time order; the map switches to the Day's outcome at 0.62 (the stain creeps in the renderer). The ledger follows at dusk; Continue deals the night.
- **Ending / loss:** a `won` dusk runs FINALE's `runEnding` (when the host passes `finale`) else `host.onEnd('won')`; a loss shows the reason (Ichor or rooted out) with Begin again (back to the watershed state).
- **Dev entry** `dev/shade.html?seed=3[&at=last][&fresh=1]`: the watershed state is the sensible bot's real mine run stopped at `stageAdvanced` (JSON round trip plays identically — tested), cached in localStorage; `at=last` is the start of the bot's last night (the top of Shade, or the brink).
- **Scripted player** (`autoplay.ts`, e2e only, never acts in play): resolves modals, eats at Hunger 50, vents Malice at 45 with the coolest act, plays an affordable miracle, lies the most watched followers low at heat 45 (≤ half), whispers town crowds / Elites by odds with the gate road preferred. It is weaker than the sensible bot (it does not reach the top of Shade in 40 nights from the watershed); the ending proof therefore starts from the bot's last night.
- **Life, weather, effects (orchestrator notes, BEHAV v4.1 / WEATHER / VFX round 4):** who is where in the town comes from BEHAV's `life.stage(input, t)` over the sim's `state.society` and the Day's `day.plan` (night via `nightClock`, the Day film's clock, dusk 1 → 1.25); sim individuals take their patch, the society around them is drawn (capped 260, outside, in blocks the player can see) and is never selectable. WEATHER attaches at quality `low` behind `?weather=0` (`host.weather: false`), fed the Day's `weather` slices. Effects are the mine stage's kinds (VFX round 5: every wick call carries `feet` and `grave` = the pit head's mouth, so a turned townsperson's flick points to the pit): `wickTurn` on 3–5 members of a crowd turned, staggered 120 ms, `refused`, `wickHood` (Lie low), `wickFlare` (Pray / Offer), `wickOut` (a follower lost), `dayMark` at a Day note's block. Each is a thin wrapped adapter: a failure drops the extra, never the turn.
- **Town tolerance:** the stage reaches the town only through `WorldComposite`'s documented calls; a zone id the town no longer anchors falls back to the nearest landmark of the same kind, a hit on an unknown place id picks the nearest block within 90 px (the town renderer is being rebuilt; the list of exports and ids depended on is at the top of `handoffs/PLAY-SHADE.md`).
- **The Day panel** sets its clock bar in place each frame and re-renders only when its text changes, so Speed / Skip stay clickable while the film runs.
- **Scripted player** keeps two actions for whispers before laying followers low (a night spent only hiding is a dead night).

### PROPS (shared props redrawn in the references' hand, 2026-11-23)
Owner on `theme-props.png`: "Characters are charming. Props are. NOT EVEN CLOSE." Figures untouched; props redrawn.
- **View:** every prop is drawn from the game's steep three-quarter camera: top faces dominant (KD = 0.6 of ground depth), short fronts (KH = 0.88 of height), contact shadow under each; no side elevations. A box shows lid + front (+ a dark sliver of the right side); a cart is an open bed seen from above with its long side as the front; a cap is a broad flattened dome seen mostly from above with a dark gill band under the front rim.
- **Hand:** one teal-black ink (`INK`), wobbled, seeded, of uneven weight — heavier on edges whose outside faces down (×1+under), thinner and sometimes broken on edges facing the upper-left light; four weight buckets per outline. Fills are flat 2–3 tones with hard-edged shadow shapes (no gradients on objects); interior marks are short: plank seams that don't line up, iron straps and rivets, hatch, cracks, chips. Light from the upper left.
- **Value:** muted, low-key materials (new `RAMPS.wood / woodDark / iron / clay`, measured from the crops: cart front #443836, lit lintel #705d3e). Crystals (violet, pale nearly-white tips) and flames are the only saturated things; they cast a separate additive glow (cached glow sprite) onto the ground and into cart beds.
- **Cache:** each draw blits a sprite cached per (prop, variant, tier, seed variant, tone, third-octave zoom bucket rounded up, so sprites only ever downscale); seeds fold onto 24 variants per prop (`SEED_VARIANTS`); animated props cache a few frames (beast walk 8, skitter 6, moth 8, brazier flame 8, lamp lit in quarters); a 28 M-pixel LRU budget; very large on-screen props (> 1800 px) draw directly. No re-stroking per frame, no shimmer.
- **Shard-lamp** is a cold white crystal splinter in a horn cage on a lashed stalk-timber tripod (`mount: 'post'`) or an iron wall arm (`mount: 'wall'`) — not a lantern on a post. **Pit beasts are blind** (theme): no eyes, six stub legs, two long feelers, a hide harness strap.
- **Additions** (additive API): `drawRail`, `drawCrystalCluster`, `drawFence`, `drawCrate`, `drawDrum`, `drawTimberFrame`, `drawAwning`, `drawLadder`, `drawCordRack`, `propCacheStats`, `clearPropCache`, `SEED_VARIANTS`.
- **Round 7 — light (2026-11-23):** every prop is painted in the scene's light. A pass over each cached sprite layer (once per cache miss, `props/light.ts`): a multiply grade from the key light's tint on the side toward it to the ambient (teal-dark) on the far side, a screen lift on the lit side, a thin rim just inside the ink on the lit silhouette edge (the ink itself warmer/thinner there), ambient occlusion toward the ground contact, and coloured spill + rim from up to two nearby emitters. Emissive parts (crystal, shard, flame, cap spots and gills) are drawn outside the lit layer; their spill onto bins, rims and rock is an in-sprite emitter. Light is quantised (8 directions × 3 key colours — warm gold / cold white / violet — × 2 amounts × 3 ambients, + up to 2 emitters × 3 colours × 2 amounts × 9 directions) and is part of the cache key. Default with no light given: warm key from the upper left by day, cold and weaker at night, teal ambient; `flat: true` = the old flat sprite. New optional `PropOpts`: `light`, `ambient`, `glows` (named `glows` because `drawCrystalCluster` already has a numeric `glow`), `flat`.
- **Round 7 — crystals:** raw crystal in the world is luminous (one function, `props/crystal.ts`, for clusters, heaps, cart loads, pens): a violet body brightening up its length, a pale lit face, a soft white core, a bright facet glint, a deep-violet edge (no black ink), bloom. Cart loads and pen heaps are packed fans of prisms standing out of a glowing bed; cart bins take the violet on their inner walls and rim.
- **Round 7 — irregularity:** timber frames are rough logs (lean, different heights, knots, a split, sawn ends with rings, rope lashing, wedge stones); carts are flared, a little skew, with uneven riveted straps, a dent and four iron wheels with hubs; rail sleepers vary in spacing, length, width and angle with a few short or missing; fence rails sag and posts lean; fuel cakes are thick lumpy patties with a thumb dent in an untidy stack. Awnings are hipped cloth tents seen from above-front (back slope, two hips, front slope, sagging ridge, patches) with goods spilling out in front. Cap-trees are stout (stalk ~0.3–0.36 of the cap, base 0.58, warm orange), stand in families (a young tree and a small one at the foot), with gold-lit gills and glowing spots on the cap and a faint glow on the ground; `capAlpha`, the three-layer split and the stalk height contract are unchanged.

### PEOPLE-perf — drawing the people fast, same look (2026-11-23)
- **Cache whole figures, not parts.** On the software-rendered box the cost is raster and blit area (≈ 73 % of the profile), not JS. A miner is painted once per (look, paint, acting state, quantised pose, scale, ½-px origin phase) into a canvas trimmed to the figure and blitted 1:1 after (`src/render/people/figcache.ts`). Two levels: a phase key for steady loops (gait stride in 16 bins, clip loop at ≈ 10 fps) and a pose key (joints quantised to 0.8 device px) that slow idles share. The other facing is the mirrored sprite (not under wind).
- **Exactness rules.** Anything transient (blend, reaction, mid-turn), any weather in play, rotated or skewed transforms and figures over 96 px draw as vector, as before. Wick flicker is frozen per sprite and the fin flick has two states; a hit lands within ¼ px of the vector figure.
- **No hitch.** Each frame paints at most `FIG_CACHE.budgetMs` (6 ms) of new miner sprites and `FolkBank.budgetMs` (8 ms) of townspeople; past it a person shows its last sprite (or a stand-in of the same trade and size) for a frame. The first draw, or one after a pause over 0.4 s, paints everything, so stills and screenshots stay exact.
- **Townspeople below 28 px:** 4 shared looks per trade, and in-place loops keep 4 poses (calm clips) or 8. At that size the variants cannot be told apart, and this keeps the bank small enough to warm.
- **Silhouette tier at ≤ 20 px** (the one look change, and only at speck-adjacent sizes): limbs thicker (×1.3 miners, ×1.35 townspeople) and merged into the body mass under one outline. Inner ink, belt and straps are dropped; head and wick dot are kept. It reads better at that size and draws less. `?sil=0` turns it off.
- **Far zoom specks** are drawn batched in three passes with memoised shades and pooled records (no per-frame allocation).
- **THEME additions (`wick: null`, `tone`, `KIT_LAYERS`) not adopted in this pass:** the cache keys whole figures, so none of them was needed for speed.
- **Not done, by ownership:** the mine renderer repaints a 66×76-unit canvas per person and blits it whole each frame. That blit alone costs 3–4 ms for 62 miners. The fix belongs to `src/render/mine` (draw through `drawGhelm` straight to the screen, which is now a trimmed cached blit, or blit only the used rect).
- **Round 8 — closer to mine.png (2026-11-23, successor 3).** Crystals are **milky and soft**, lit from within: broad blunt prisms (shoulders high, short tip), a pale lit face, a lavender far face, a soft inner core, a faint white bloom at the tip and a thin soft violet-gray edge instead of ink; two ramps sampled from mine.png — `wall` (pale pink-lavender clusters in rock) and `load` (richer violet, cart loads and pen heaps). Clusters are 3–5 chunky prisms; cart loads fill the bin as a mound of chunky prisms; pen heaps are tall upright clusters. **No haze discs**: a crystal's light is a flat pool on the ground at its foot (and spill on the bin / rock), never a disc behind it; the contact shadow is a tight occlusion, not a soft disc. **Timber frames** are squared chunky warm-brown beams (post ≈ a quarter of the opening, lit top plane on the lintel, end grain, iron cleats, visible lean), optional `opening` (a deep dark tunnel mouth behind). **Crates** are never ruler-true (per-corner jitter, sag, planks with gaps, a missing slat or the lid knocked ajar with straw, stain, scuffs, chalk tally, rope); drums, jars and baskets tilt and carry dents / chips / a broken strand. **Sensed props sit dark** (gray pushed 22 % toward black, matching mine.png's gray crates). **Market stalls** are flat sagging cloth sheets on four poles seen from above-front (bright ochre / soft green, scalloped valance, tension folds, a patch, a leaning pole, rope guys) over a counter of goods. **Cap-trees**: flatter-rimmed caps with a bright rim, a gold underside glowing from within (brightest round the stalk), shorter thicker stalks (contract kept: stalkH > 0.75 capW, capRx = capW/2, `capAlpha` unchanged), standing in tight overlapping clumps of mixed size. A brazier's bowl and flame are one cached blit (8 frames).

- **PLAY-SHADE landing (worktree builder, 2026-11-23 ~15:40).** (1) Rooted out = the plain reading of spec §13, as SIM
  built it (`rootedOut(s)`: no follower, no Hold ≥ 10 outside the grave, no one in whisper reach; checked at Day 1.0).
  (2) The loss screen takes the sim's `defeat.why` first: `sealed` → the Council, `rootedOut` → alone (`causeOf`,
  front/adapter.ts); the Ichor guesswork only when there is no why. (3) Mine-worded pushback lines (vigil, bolted doors,
  heretic-hunt, crackdown "ledger", "torches") get town / WRITING words when `stage === 'shade'`. (4) The headless full
  run has two town players: `scripted` (the stage's robot, wins seeds 3/7/11/21) and `idle` (answers only the cards put
  in front of it, never a move: loses seeds 7/11 on Ichor) — the loss path is proven by neglect, not by tuning.
  (5) The browser proof starts from a real save (`lastMineSave`, the Session log at the last mine night's End Night)
  through the title's Continue — no debug jump. `npm run bots` runs `scripts/sim-bots.ts` directly.

## PLAY-MINE (2026-11-23, builder): the mine's turn lands in the browser
- **The Day's camera = the wide + visits to heavy news.** The half-finished round-5 shot list (`src/feel/shots.ts`, every note a cut) is backed out: it broke "follow the day" and the rule that light notes never move the camera. Instead each heavy note is a *visit* (`fastfilm.ts lean`): the camera goes to where it happens — also to a lit zone outside the wide (was: no lean) — centred, ≥ ×1.3 in from the wide, held through the note's slow-down, then back to the wide; dusk brings it home to the grave. Light / normal notes stay on the wide (feed + drift). A player's click / drag still takes the camera.
- **The offering is a dusk beat on the stage's clock.** The sim's `offered` events are mapped to plan time just after PLAN_B (`life.ts toPlanList`, `OFFER_PLAN` 0.912 + 0.006 per stone) instead of the film's 0.92 (plan ≈ 0.84, when bearers used to kneel, rise and leave before the film ended). A bearer then stays at the grave `OFFER_HOLD` (0.2 plan ≈ 3.6 s of dusk) after the stone's moment (`stageEvents.ts`). The dusk stages the SAME day as the film (the Day's state + events; before: a rebuild from the post-Day state without events re-planned the bearers across the mine).
- **Arrival = the ring BEHAV sets stones on** (`world.arrival` radius 24 = `OFFER_R` 22 + lip; was 13.6, inside which the bowing bearer often never stood). The HUD's 3.4 s cap stays as the fallback.
- **The strip closes itself** 1.4 s after the last stone is turned over (+1.0 s when several, so the best find is read), wall clock, via the HUD's `offeringDone`; Continue / Enter still close it at once. One stone: dusk → ledger ≈ 4–5 s.
- **End Night never refuses silently**: a waiting event / blessing → a warn line "First answer the event: …" / "First choose a blessing." + the denied sound + the card back; a refused `endNight` action says the sim's reason. The devour sound plays once (FEEL's devour score at the maw); the click's own `sfx('devour')` only plays when the card has no person.

`dev/ui2.html` (`src/ui/mock2/**`, not loaded by the game): five painted slabs over the mine still — the god (grave crystal lit to how fed you are, rank as lit facets, "Hungry", goal strip), three wicks (flame height = alarm), the pouch (dull / clear / bright), a fan of five painted cards, End Night (violet-lit, moon, "2 left" badge); a clicked miner gets a slab beside her with portrait, one line, Move / Pray / Offer / Lie low. ## 29. UI4 — the town's Night in the approved kit (2026-11-23, awaiting the owner)
`dev/ui4.html` (`src/ui/mock4/**`, not loaded by the game), over a real frame of the town (`town-still.jpg`, grabbed from `dev/shade.html?seed=7&at=last` with the HUD hidden). Decisions made:
- Mock2's own `build()` draws the god, wicks, pouch and End Night; the town only swaps in its numbers (via `NIGHT`) and its hand. No new surface styles.

# 27. PERF: frame cost — the three profiled causes (2026-11-23, PERF builder)
- Props are NOT re-lit every frame: `props/cache.ts` already bakes each lit sprite once per (prop, seed variant, tier, tone, light, zoom bucket). Steady state the light pass is < 1 % of busy time; the profiled ~22 % was the cold fill of the first measured view (≈ 2,000 bakes × ~1.6 ms) plus each zoom jump's new bucket. Change: a lit bake that has a stand-in (the same prop already baked at a neighbouring bucket, drawn at the same place and size) spends at most 4 ms per frame window; a prop with no bake at all is still baked at once (nothing is ever missing). The light pass itself is untouched (the look is unchanged; props are being replaced by their own loop).
- `addOverlay(…, { bounds })`: an in-colour overlay in the mine may give the world rect it can touch this frame; the mine's seam scratch then clears, Void-cuts and blits only that device rect (full screen when any overlay gives none). The grave hoard passes a rect from its seats, stones, flights and drinks (margins for pools, glows, columns; the whole hoard's reach on a frame where the hoard changes).
- Town tiles: a finished detail tile recomposes only its own rect of the Sensed buffer (was: every finished tile rebuilt the whole buffer, `tiles.version` in its key). `tiles.layoutVersion` (preload chunks, flush) still rebuilds it all. The buildings painter also yields after ~1 ms of drawing, so one bake slice is about one building; the coarser level already stands in (stretched) under any tile not yet baked.
- `e2e/perf-check.cjs`: the measuring tool (trimmed env-check: 300 frames per view, load average per view, warm-up until the valley and the queued tiles are done, `STAGES=1` flushed per-stage laps, `PROFILE=` CPU profile with the causes' inclusive shares).

## Mine-look round 1 (terrain builder, 2026-11-23) — proposals awaiting the owner, nothing adopted
- Ground resolution: a sharp-ground pass on the engine's public ground seam (ratio images, multiply + color-dodge, prop mask from the engine albedo), not a bigger bake: +0 shipped bytes, ground at 1 baked px per screen px at the max zoom (3 px/unit). Props/rails stay 2 px/unit (engine-side).
- Albedos are written as the colour seen in the engine at night divided by a measured engine factor, cancelling the teal ambient only in part per material (full cancel = pink albedos that read violet-grey under white lamps).
- The proposal lives in src/render/terrain/look2/ (outside the prebuilt hash) behind dev/terrain.html flags, so the shipped ground and its stale-key test are untouched until approval.

Both are to be adopted into the game. ## 31. The Day and the people (owner, 2026-11-23)
Owner, verbatim: "No day just moves super fast like it ends in 20-30 sec and you have full control. There is 0 "do while zooming" people do stuff 24/7 according to how they would in real life. If they're out of frame they are only data." And: "Props do better that's all".
- The Day is a fast-forward of 20–30 seconds at ×1 (set: 24 s). The camera is the player's the whole time: no camera travel, lean, follow or take-over during the Day or at dusk.
- People live around the clock, night included, on real-life schedules (shifts, meals, sleep, guard rounds, the lift), at every zoom. There is no behaviour that depends on zoom.
- People outside the frame are simulated as data only: not drawn, not animated.
- Props: no choice put to the owner. The orchestrator picks the method and delivers better props; the owner judges the result.

The game's content is NOT that much" · "Just build sensibly and organized" · "Consolidate things and tests and whatnot. Ensure ONLY the tests for changed code runs." · "Make the codebase make sense. It had better reduce to 80k lines or less with this level of content".

- Every system gets one of three verdicts in `docs/vision.md` (to be written from the systems inventory): complete; flesh out into a real system (when the idea is good and the game is thinner without it); remove. The default for a good idea that was built half-heartedly is to build it properly, not to delete it. Nothing stays half-in.

## 36. What a test is for (TESTS, 2026-11-23)
Owner: "80% of them are likely redundant" · "Ensure ONLY the tests for changed code runs."
- A test stays only if it pins a spec rule or formula, determinism (once per deterministic module), a content table's integrity (table-driven, never wording), a silent contract between two modules, or a whole run (`tests/slow/`, excluded from the default run, `npm run test:slow`). The rules and the suite list are in `tests/README.md`.
- Wording lints (banned jargon, British spelling, reading grade, the theme's forbidden words) are not tests: deleted. The approved-figure golden (draw-call hash of `drawFolk`) went with the other drawing tests; if a look must be pinned, its owner adds one fingerprint, as audio does.
- A test that needs a source file as text imports it with `?raw`, so `--changed` sees it; `forceRerunTriggers` (reruns everything) only for tests that cannot (today: the mine's prebuilt-ground freshness check).
- Known gap kept visible: the chronicle neither writes nor silences the V3 events `delved`, `flamePrice`, `hew` (an allow-list in tests/content that may only shrink).
