## UI-BUILD: the approved interface made live (src/ui/play, docs/style.md §7)
- The old play HUD is deleted; `mountHud` and every Hud method keep their names and promises. One stylesheet (`css3.ts`), painted canvases for every surface (`kit/surface`), text stays in the DOM.
- Design space: the root is zoomed by k = clamp(min(w/1600, h/900), 0.86, 1); everything is anchored with a 24 px margin. The guide's marks live on an unzoomed layer because they measure in client px.
- Tonight's draw (owner: "There ARE no standard action cards"): bottom centre shows exactly what the sim deals, 3 miracles and 3 acts of evil, as two separate fans, each with its own Skip ("Skip the miracles" / "Skip the evil"); a played or skipped group leaves. Every other act is a verb plate on the clicked thing's slab (person: whisper, move, pray, offer, lie low; grave: set, take out, eat, enchant, recombine).
- Cards 156x236: painted picture, content name (never clipped: condensed only inside the band no neighbour covers), the FIRST sentence of the content line; cost as crystal pips; an act of evil's alarm as the flame of the power it alarms first (sim `EVILS[id].heat[0]`, tested).
- Blessings use the same card plate with a motif painting per sim BONUS id (20 ids, tested); the effect stands under the card on the slab, the flavour on the card when it fits in three lines.
- End Night badge priority: "1 event" > "Pick a blessing" > "N cards" > "N actions" > nothing.
- The record opens from the goal line (or C); the grave from the god or the pouch (or G). The hand hides while the grave is open. The powers sit below the camera and settings buttons.
- No brackets on the interface: `noBrackets` strips parenthetical asides from sim lines (goal, record, notes, ledger).
- Day notes: one parchment line at a time, placed above the first of anchor / person / place / point that is on screen. Light lines (common strikes, each cart, colour ticks) stay in the record only; repeats of one kind fold into one line ("4 carts roll to the lift").
- "End-of-turn cards" = the moment card plus the fresh deal at dawn.
- Settings: the shared panel (`src/ui/settings.ts`) is restyled in the kit by CSS while the play interface is mounted (`settings3.ts`), not rebuilt.
- Not done here: the starting bonus is the front scene's own picker (`src/scenes/front/bonus.ts`, not this piece); to share the kit it would call `pickCard` from `src/ui/play`.

## 42. The game crystallized: docs/vision.md (2026-11-23)
Owner: "Crystallize and consolidate. Make a refined, high quality vision and execute." · "Flesh them out into actual systems if it's clear the vision is lacking, or implementation is half-hearted, OR just remove it."
`docs/vision.md` is binding. From a read-only audit of the code, every system is one of: stays; built out properly (things people carry, faceting bench, taking a building, Preach and Stand watch, ties, one pushback model, town searches, town story events, the vault as the win, Flame stones, the first night); brought back to the spec (Hunger and Malice at the top, what costs Ichor, Influence as a sum, the watershed trigger, the 24 s Day); removed (crowd units, kindle, gorge, mine tiers II–V, the stand-in game, dead earlier-version state, doubles). The owner can overrule any line.

## 43. Cards: a hand you draw into; three shown in the centre; one card a turn (2026-11-23)
Owner: "The cards should appear one set of 3 at the time at the center of your screen. Not placed at the bottom there. You can draw a card into your hand and use a card a turn. To initiate a draw, there is a button besides your hand to draw a miracle, and another one to draw an evil act."
Replaces the "3 + 3 dealt every night, play or skip each" model (§32 still holds: cards are only miracles and acts of evil; every other act is a verb). Decided where the owner left it open, and the owner can overrule: each draw button can be used once a night; a draw shows three and you take one or none; the hand holds at most five and is kept between nights; a card can be discarded from the hand; one card is played a turn (a miracle or an act of evil); miracles are still paid for by burning stones when played; draws are open from the first night.

§43, amended the same hour. Owner: "Actually let's make it so that you draw from a pool of miracles and evils" · "can use 1 special card each turn". So there is **one pool** (miracles and acts of evil mixed) and **one draw button**, not two; one draw a night shows three from the pool, you take one or none; one special card is used a turn. The rest of §43 stands (hand of five kept between nights, discard, miracles paid in stones when played, draws open from night 1).

§43, second amendment. Owner: "Can use 1 special card each turn for free, next ones have an additional nether fee". So there is no hard limit of one card a turn: the first special card used each turn carries no fee; the second costs a Nether fee, the third twice that, and so on (the fee is one balance key, set to about the Nether a whisper costs). A miracle's price in burnt stones is separate and unchanged.

## TOWN-SIM (2026-11-23): the town's rules as people (docs/town.md, docs/vision.md §4)
- **One person = one unit.** Every living LIVING townsperson is a sim unit with LIVING's id (444 per seed; the adopted t01–t30 / e01–e05 keep theirs). Stats are rolled from a per-person seed, so the run's rng is untouched. Dawn adds LIVING's newcomers and removes those LIVING buries (`town/sync.ts`). No crowd units exist, and their data (`TOWN_GROUPS`) is gone; the twenty retired crowds' creation rolls are still drawn and dropped (`RETIRED_CROWD_DRAWS`), so every seed's miners, Elites and shifts stay as they were.
- **Elites** are the officers (e01–e05) and one master per building: the officer for the council hall, vault, cord room (e01), barracks, drill yard, gate (e02), temple (e03), market (e04); else the titled worker, else the first grown worker. A master is kind `elite` (Shade only), +10 resistance, command = crew size, 20 XP (officers keep 60). A dead master is replaced at Dawn. The well, road, graveyard, wash-place and pit head have no master: yours when their area takes root.
- **Groups** (`town/groups.ts`) are derived, never stored: a building's crew, a household (2+), a congregation (chapel / temple habit), the day and night watch. Elites are never members. Gathered at an hour = ≥ half of the living members (and ≥ 2) at one place. `whisperGroup {group, hour}` (Shade only): one roll on the present members as one target (mean resistance + 5, `influenceCost` with S = present); success turns each present member.
- **Hours.** `whisper` and `whisperGroup` take an hour (night = tonight, default; morning 0.15, noon 0.45, dusk 0.86, evening 1.1 of the coming day). The coming day is LIVING's `planDay` forecast with only turn and stage as facts (deterministic, memoised per society). Reach and odds come from that place and hour; a whisper laid for an hour resolves at once, at night.
- **Translation of the crowd rules** (so the slice keeps its scale, not a tuning pass): an ordinary townsperson stains `(1/stage tier)^0.85` of a named one, pushes back 0.3 of a named watcher, pays 3 XP (xpFollower × 0.6), and Ichor is torn per 10 of them lost over the run. Tithe: your townsfolk tithe per building crew (else household), ceil(n × 0.08) rolls, ≤ 5. The Congregation merge at the watershed is gone (stageAdvanced `congregation: null`).
- **Buildings and the taking rule** (`town/buildings.ts`): a workplace is yours when its master is and more than half of its other workers are (a master alone: with them); lost the same way; a hearth when more than half its household is; burned by a mob = nobody's for 3 days. `s.facilities` and `s.town.held` are caches synced after every action and at Dawn (`buildingTaken` / `buildingLost`); `facilityView` adapts.
- **Gifts** (`content/town.ts` `GIFT`, `town/gifts.ts`): active gifts are `useGift` (1 action + Nether, then rest) — rumour, debt, food (poison/bless, 1–3 days ahead), records, guards; benches make things to carry (below); metal, road, lesson, vault, gate, hearth are standing effects.
- **Pushback through people** (`town/pushback.ts`): each move gets an agent (scribe → pit head; sergeant → the hearth with most of yours; priest → the named one's door; acolyte → temple steps; a ringleader living there → the building; the High Priest → the road) and an hour; `townMove` events at announce / land / stop. New answer `agent`: the agent turned or gone before dusk blunts the move. Card rules unchanged.
- **Vision §4 pass (TOWN-SIM v2).**
  - *Commoners in the mine:* an ordinary townsperson cannot be whispered in the Whisper stage (stage blocker), so the mine's game is unchanged. *Agent guard:* the person carrying an unanswered move resists +20 (`TOWN.agentGuard`), so a move is not always answered for the price of one whisper. *Levy:* takes `ceil(n × levyGroupShare)` of the hearth's commoners, drawn by the run's rng.
  - *One pushback model:* in the Shade the threshold responder fires nothing (`SHADE_RESPONDER`) except a landed crusade's torches; the town's pressure is the announced moves only.
  - *The vault is the win:* `openVault` (1 action) is legal once the council hall is yours (master and more than half its people); it brings one Impossible and the royal stones and the run is won. The level-7-and-gate win and the dusk auto-open are gone; `victoryProgress` says the council hall, then the vault. Kindle is removed.
  - *Searches in the town:* the tithe is carried home in the carrier's stash (0.92), counted at the tally house (0.96: the skim is the shortfall; forgiven `tallyFloor`, ×`overseerTolerance` with the tally house yours, + every worn false weight), searched (0.97: on a search day — the lift's odds — one soldier of the watch, named, searches one row (homes) or one cart line (work) where carriers are; each carrier there is searched with the lift's per-person odds, guard-blind and a follower-soldier there lower it, a looked-away area or lying low makes it 0; a second pocket keeps half; worn things are evidence and the wearer is taken), and what is left reaches the grave (0.975). A carrier's worn strike luck / strike rate helps their crew's tithe rolls.
  - *Things people carry:* lamps (lamp house), clothing (tannery, loom house) and brew (brewhouse, pottery, gardens) are items made at their benches from a loose stone, like weights, relics and draughts; they are no longer area actions. Giving is free (no action), by the hand of any follower (Elites too) who lives or works beside the receiver. Boons: lamp = whispers around the wearer +8 % (+2 a grade); clothing = a second pocket and 15 % less likely searched; brew = +2 faith (+1 a grade) each Dawn for 3 days. Curses: lamp = everyone not yours where they work −8 resistance, Temple +2; clothing = their household −10, crowd +2; brew = tonight the sleepers of their home area +10 % odds, they are sick a day; rings/circlets/weights −25 resistance; amulets/relics spoil an Elite's word; draughts sicken 3 days. Bots make one thing a night, give coats and jewels to tithe carriers, lamps and brew as curses (the cautious bot to its own), and take a thing back from someone named by a move (cautious: also before a likely search).
  - *Town story events:* the six town events have rules about real people (a stallholder, the rows, the Magistrate and a follower who serves, three soldiers of the watch, a robber in the rows, the gate's people) with numbers in `TOWN_EVENT`; `EVENTS.shade` = those six + six mine events that still happen (cave-in, death in the tunnels, informer, omen, letters, victory bowls).
  - *Merge with the card rules (2026-11-23):* the town's card use is on the hand and the draw: an answering act of evil is in the hand or leads the next draw (`answerOptions` says which); the bots' card step is the card builder's; PLAY-SHADE's scripted town player (`src/scenes/play/shade/autoplay.ts`) draws once a night and keeps the answering act, else an act of evil when none is held, else a miracle (throwing back a miracle for an act that matters when the hand is full), plays its first card free and a second only to vent Malice ≥ 85. Kindle stays removed (vision §6) although the card branch still had it.
  - *The council-hall plan:* `hallStep` (`src/sim/bots/hall.ts`) — whisper the hall's master whenever they are in reach, at the hour with the best odds (a whisper at an hour speaks to them where they will be: the Magistrate at the hall among your people), at a floor of its own (10 %); otherwise the hall's crew gathered when affordable, else its likeliest worker; once more than half are yours and the master is still out, `hallWaiting` stops every other whisper (a warm Public talks failed whispers up against the master, and the master's Nether is saved) until the master can be turned; then `openVault`. The scripted town player also keeps and plays a Gilded Tongue when the master is in reach. The sensible and cautious bots and the scripted town player use it (each with its own odds floor). Strategy only, no rule or number changed. `openVault` was never in `legalActions` (only `actionWhy` knew it), so no player reading the legal list could open it: it is now.
  - *The rules' buildings are the drawn town (TOWN-WORLD's plan):* every building of `townPlan.ts` is one of `townBuildings(s)` (and every building of the rules is drawn: `road` as the City Road street); every master and worker is drawn at their building on the week's working days (`tests/town/layout.test.ts`; a storm can keep someone of an open yard under a roof). To get there: a crew works at one place (LIVING's crews of one trade and district take their first member's workplace, so the barracks' and drill yard's guards are not split between a sim place and a drawn one); a trade on rounds starts its day at its own workplace (the carters take the beasts out of the haul yard first); an officer who masters other buildings than their own (the Magistrate: vault, scriptorium; the Captain: drill yard, gate) looks in on each in the afternoon; children, elders and beggars belong to no building (a child's "work" is where it plays or learns, an elder's where they sit, a beggar's where they beg) — the school's children still carry its lesson home.

## 33. CLOSE-PLAY — fixes found by playing (2026-11-23)
- **One clock, one position per person.** A person the stage drives (a stage `pos`, a stage `route` being walked, or riding the stage's lift) is drawn where the stage says, on the layer the stage says. The world's crossing (`render/world/crossing.ts`) no longer walks them to the cage on its own timer, and the town's people (`render/town/people.ts`) follow the stage's point (`stagePos`: the position, or the point at `route.t` along `route.pts`) instead of routing between blocks on their own clock. Once the stage lets go, a person walks home from where the stage left them.
- **The night shift is at work on night 1.** Sim workers on the night shift (`u.shift === 'night'`, awake, posted in the mine) get a BEHAV work stop from `NIGHT_DOWN` (plan 1.08) through the night (`life/stageEvents.ts`). Before this, LIVING's plan sent everyone home and night 1 showed nobody to whisper to. No people were added.
- **The opening view is framed per seed.** `whisperStartCamera(r, seed)` fits the grave plus the mine areas holding the nearest night-shift workers the god can whisper to, until at least `OPENING_WORKERS` (3) are covered (pad 60, max scale 1.4). The cutscene ends on exactly this shot and play starts on it. `pushIn()` only re-frames when fewer than 3 whisperable people are on screen.
- **The town's pick rule.** A click picks the sim unit drawn under the pointer (`scenes/play/shade/pick.ts`). Topmost wins (the largest feet-y, because the town draws in feet-y order). Equal depth goes to the nearest centre. An Elite's ground ring (26 px) counts only where no figure or crowd is drawn. Living-town extras are not targets, so a click on one goes through to whatever is under it.
- **The Day, under the owner's §31 ruling.** This replaces the earlier return glide. `DAY_SECONDS` = 24 in `feel/fastday.ts` is the only Day length; the mine scene and the town scene both import it.
  - The film never directs the camera: `FastFilm.cameraTaken` is always true, so there are no leans, drifts, rise to the pit head or dusk shot.
  - The scene no longer frames the Day, and the town no longer slows down or recentres on a push note.
  - Notes never slow the clock. A rare strike's hold still stops it, and the player panning or zooming ends the hold.
  - The HUD's "Follow the day" button is kept hidden: the film always cues `dayFollow {on: true}`. The HUD builder may delete the button.
  - A click on a feed line still glides there, because that is the player's own move.
- **People out of frame are data only (§31).** Everyone's decisions, walks and place keep advancing in `render/people/life.ts`, but only figures inside the mine camera's view (+80 units) are posed (`Life.inView`, set each frame by the world composite). Nobody is posed when the mine is not drawn. Posing depends only on t, so a figure is exact again the frame it comes into view.
- **Whole world (close-play #8).** While the god knows at most 3 surface blocks, "Whole world" is `frameShaft()`: the descent halfway (scale ≈ 2.33), with the whole mine in full colour through the shaft mouth and the pit head around it. The old surface framing at ×0.75 drew the mine only as the pit's point, which is black on a Void town. The town's far zoom (< 0.75) now keeps every lamp as a small point of light instead of dropping all but shrines.
- **Watershed centring.** From the shaft onward the camera stays on the pit mouth. After the point it pulls straight back onto the hand-back framing, with no detour through `frameAll`, which is mostly black and pushed the point and the known town into the top-left corner. The hand-back (`frameTown(…, pit=true)` / `frameAll(…, pit=true)` via `fitPoseAround`), and play's matching "whole world" pose, keep the pit inside the middle third of the view and refit the zoom so every known block still shows. During the watershed the descent band draws no shaft walls (`WorldView.shaft = false`), so the teal and rust strata rings no longer circle the shrinking mine and it shrinks into its point in plain black. The shaft art itself is unchanged wherever else it is drawn.
- **Defeat sentences.** Every defeat cause the sim can produce has its own plain sentence in `src/content/defeat.ts`: the `why` values (`sealed`, `rootedOut`, Ichor), each Ichor reason, and how the last follower or group head went (`GoneReason`). `scenes/front/adapter.ts killingBlow()` finds the blow from the run's history. A test reads the sim's sources and fails if any `why`, `IchorReason` or `GoneReason` falls through to the default.

§43, third amendment. Owner on Malice: "It's an urge. But while it's higher maybe you get additional bonuses from committing evil." Malice is never a currency. An act of evil played while Malice is high gives more (a stepped multiplier on that act's own reward, about ×1.5 at the top); what it vents and how much it is noticed do not change.

§43, fourth amendment. Owner: "you dont manually draw cards anymore, at the start of each turn it is automatically shown to you what cards you are allowed to use each turn" · "the upgrades that are shown right after the starting animation will not be given, instead showing the cards to be drawn" · "For each major rank increase, you gain an extra card to be drawn from the pool" · "hand is dealt at dusk ... you are only meant to move the timings of the draw".
- **The draw is dealt at every dusk.** `cardsAtDusk` runs the same `drawOffer` the `draw` action runs, after the relics go into the hand, with a full hand too (taking then needs a Discard first, or Take none). The deck, the `draw` action and `drawWhy`, Take, Discard, Reroll and every card and stone that touches them are unchanged; the draw is seeded by (seed, night, reroll) as before, so the cards per night are the ones a click on night N drew.
- **No Draw button.** The deck beside the hand is the stack the draw is dealt from: lit while its cards are on the table, spent once they are answered. The open draw follows the hand's room: a Discard while it is up lets you take.
- **Each major rank (stage) draws one more** (`STAGE_DRAW` in `formulas.ts`, beside the other per-stage tables): the Whisper deals 3 open cards, the Shade 4; Third Hand and the stones' sure miracles and acts of evil add on top.
- **No starting blessing.** A run begins at night 1 with its first draw on the table, where the starting pick was. The roll that made the starting offer is still drawn and dropped, so every seed's town rolls the same. Deep Roots and First Dreamer (start-only blessings) are no longer offered.

## MINE-ADOPT (2026-11-24): the approved mine look is in the game (2026-11-23)
- Zone colouring: `terrain/shade.ts` + `terrain/mine.ts` are the look2 versions; the prebuilt ground is re-baked. `look2/` is gone.
- Sharp ground: built for the play camera's real closest zoom, **2.5 px/unit** (`ZOOM_MAX = 2.5 × K`; 3 px/unit is not reachable in the game), so tiles are shaded at 2.5 px/unit there (1 shaded px per screen px); 3 and 4 only for closer dev views. It turns on above 2.1 px/unit.
- New engine seam (`src/render/mine/ground.ts`): a `MineGroundDetail` drawn inside the engine's screen-cached base layer, right after the lit floor. The engine tells it which albedo pixels are the ground alone (`patch`: the ground's paint + the light-cast tint compared with the albedo; veins and cave-ins cut out), so nobody reads private engine state. Each tile is composited once (lit × ratio) and then only blitted.
- The sharp pass and its seam are deleted (§96): it never shipped on. It failed one done line: a cold zoom-in (view not yet prefetched) took 6.3 s and 6.8 s to sharp against a 2.5 s limit (35 tiles, about 240 ms each, 3 workers, 4-core box). A warm jump took 1.0 s. While panning it cost +6.7 ms median frame time, against +0 at rest. Turning it on needs a faster worker shade, or a whole-mine prefetch before the player can zoom.

## 44. The interface scales with the view and is smaller (2026-11-24)
Owner, on the hosted build at 2560×1440: "It's way too big and there's not UI size scaling with resolution" · "Don't overcompensate and make it microscopic mind you make it sensible". The interface had a fixed design size clamped between 86 % and 100 %. Now: one factor proportional to the window (no floor at 86 %, no cap at 100 %), plates about a fifth smaller by design, an "Interface size" setting (Small / Medium / Large), body text never under 14 px on screen at 1600×900.

## 44. The interface scales with the screen (owner, 2026-11-23)
- One zoom for the play interface, the carved plates and the settings slab: `uiZoom = clamp(min(w/1600, h/900), 0.6, 2) × 0.84 × Interface size` (`src/ui/prefs.ts`). No floor at 0.86, no cap at 1: the interface covers the same share of the screen at every resolution (at rest with a 4-card hand: 17.3 % at 1280×720, 17.1 % at 1600×900, 16.9 % at 2560×1440; before: 26.6 / 22.4 / 8.7 %; `screenshots/ui6-scale.json`).
- Base 0.84, not 0.8: body text set at 17 design px stays 14.3 px on screen at 1600×900 without re-setting every plate; titles and names stay 19 px+ (card names in the hand: 16.6 px at rest, 19 px lifted).
- Interface size: Small / Medium / Large = 85 / 100 / 115 % (was 90/100/115/130 %; an old saved value falls back to Medium). Applied live.
- The hand rests at 0.92 of a card (about 120×182 px on screen at 1600×900) and lifts to 1.1; the three offered in the centre are 1.5 (about 1.65× the hand).
- The guide layer is zoomed with the same k and lays its marks out in design units.

## 41. PROPS 4: drawn big, baked to sprites, behind `?props=4` (builder, 2026-11-23)
Assigned by the orchestrator as the props attempt the owner judges after §39 (the bar: the code-drawn interiors and cards in `src/ui/mock5`, `src/ui/mock2`). Nothing here is the default; the owner decides whether it stays.
- Method: each prop is drawn by a generator in `src/feel-gen/props/` (never imported by the game) at 8 px per world unit with the mock kit's living ink, 3–4 flat tones per material plus a highlight, a contact shadow and a soft cast shadow, then box-filtered (premultiplied) down to 4 px per world unit by `scripts/bake-props.cjs` into `assets/props/<id>-<variant>.png` + `manifest.json` (anchor = ground contact point in px, size in world units, 3 seeded variants, marks drawn, points such as the lantern's flame).
- Ramps are sampled from `docs/refs/mine.png` and kept inside `docs/style.md` §4: rough orange-brown timber with long dark streaks; saturated lilac-violet crystals (#9963af / #d1a5e4 / #edcdfe) with white cores and their own glow; cream fire-lit pottery with thin brown lines; dark board and iron for the cart and the crates.
- Props: cart full and empty (rail point anchor), timber frame and adit (40 × 46 units, stretched to the size of the frame they replace), torch-lantern (a stubby stake in a cairn with a cresset), stacked crates, offerings by the fire. `dev/props4.html` shows them big (`?view=large`), against the reference crops (`?view=vs`) and per prop (`?view=work&id=`).
- Game (only with `?props=4`): `src/render/mine/things.ts` draws the sprites in `shellCart`, `stalkFrame`, `adit` and `shardLampTripod` (not the Council's tally lamp, h ≥ 60, which keeps its cold tripod); without the flag nothing loads and the old drawings run. `mineBuildJob` waits for the sprites before the albedo bake; after it, `props4Lanterns` moves each replaced lamp's flame and light to the lantern head and makes the light warm (#ffb066, radius × 0.9, kind still `lamp` so it burns at night) and the flame a small warm tongue (`warm` mark read by the flame and halo drawing). This runs after the ground provider, so the terrain spec and the prebuilt ground hash are untouched (`dressing.ts` is not edited).
- The carts that roll the rails are AMBIENT's (`src/render/ambient/mine.ts`) and are not replaced; crates and offerings are baked but not placed (no flagged placement asked for them).

## 45. UI-2: the mine interface finished (2026-11-23)
- Cards (§43): the hand sits at the bottom centre with the deck at its left. At rest a card shows its picture and name; its line, fee tag and Discard show when it is lifted, so no text on screen is under 14 px. An act of evil has a blood-inked frame and embers rising from the foot of its picture; a miracle keeps its violet pips. The three are dealt from the deck to the centre at 1.5× a card, over a dimmed world, with "Take none". An open set blocks End Night (the badge reads "Take a card").
- The grave panel is sized to what it holds. Its hero is the picked stone, drawn large, with its name, its kind ("A dull stone of malice") and what it does in plain words (`plainEffect`: no numbers, no brackets). The verbs sit under the stone. "Things to give" lists the things made at facilities, with "Take it back" when the rules allow it.
- The person slab is sized to content. One verb has its odds word beside it. A person out of reach gets one plain line saying where and when they can be reached, never a dead Whisper plate. Give and take-back verbs appear only when the scene sends `carry` (legal actions only).
- Guide marks: one at a time. Each one leaves when its thing is done or after 8 s at most; a click anywhere on it dismisses it. No "Got it". A mark shows only while what it points at is on screen.
- The settings gear and the camera button are carved plates painted with the kit (`src/ui/plate.ts`). The depth mark is a kit gauge in the HUD (`HudVM.depth`), shown once the scene sends it.
- The starting pick uses the HUD's painted `pickCard` (the blessing slab and cards) inside the eye-flare scene. The old DOM picker is deleted.
- One settings screen, in the kit's look everywhere. One words table (`words.ts`) and one guide file (`guide.ts`). No version numbers in file names.

## CARDS (rules builder, 2026-11-23): the hand and the draw in the sim (§43 with both amendments: one mixed pool, one draw a night; the first card a turn free, each more for Nether)
- **State:** `god.hand: CardId[]` (cap `BALANCE.handMax` 5 + Third Hand, kept between nights); `god.offer: { cards } | null`; `god.drew` and `god.cardsPlayed` reset at dusk; `god.rerolls` = rerolls of the open offer tonight. The old `miracleHand` / `evilHand` / `miraclePlayed` / `evilPlayed`, the `skipHand` action and the `dealt` / `rerolled` / `handSkipped` events are gone.
- **Actions:** `draw` (once a night, no offer open, room in the hand; free), `take { card | null }`, `discard { card }`, `reroll` (the open offer), `playMiracle` / `playEvil` from the hand. Ending the night with an offer open takes none. New events: `cardsOffered { cards, rerolled }`, `cardTaken { card | null, from: 'offer' | 'relic' }`, `cardDiscarded`; `miraclePlayed` / `evilPlayed` carry `fee` when one was paid.
- **The draw** (`drawCards`, seeded by seed × night × reroll index, so the same night draws the same cards whatever you did first): leads with relics found by day that did not fit the hand, and with the act of evil that answers a faction's open move (otherwise a 3-card draw from ~30 cards would rarely show it); then the sure extra cards of blessings and stones; then the stage's `STAGE_DRAW` (Whisper 3, Shade 4) open slots, each an act of evil with chance `offerEvilShare = 0.3 + 0.004 × Malice` (0.3 at 0, 0.5 at 50, 0.7 at 100), else a miracle; weighted by each card's `weight`, a card with a target tonight before one without, never a card held, never twice; a reroll avoids the cards just thrown back while others remain.
- **Fee:** the n-th card played in a night costs `(n − 1) × BALANCE.cardExtraFee` Nether (8, about a base whisper: 6 × (1 + resistance/50)); `cardFee(s)` is the next card's fee; an unaffordable fee makes the card illegal ("Not enough Nether (another card tonight costs 8)."). The fee is flat across stages (whispers cost more in the town, so a second card is relatively cheaper there) — balance can scale it later.
- **Remapped effects:** Third Hand (blessing) → each draw shows one more card, always a miracle, and the hand holds one more; Prophet (odd effect) and Prophetic (modifier `miracleDraw`) → each draw shows one more card, always a miracle; Spite (odd effect) and of Bad Ideas (modifier `evilDraw`) → one more card, always an act of evil (Spite keeps its Malice flaw); Second Chance (blessing), Loaded (odd effect) and Loaded (modifier `rerolls`) → reroll the cards on the table that many times a night. Relics found by day go into the hand at dusk (or lead the next draw when it is full). Malice at 100 (the Outburst) and followers' own acts never touched the hand (they pick from the evil pool).
- **Scene:** the draw is open from night 1 (`handsReady` true on the first night). Until UI-2's centre offer lands, the bridge sends the one hand as a single `HandVM` fan and UI-2's `CardsVM` as `cards` on the HUD state.
- **Malice bonus (third amendment):** `evilBonus(malice)` = 1 below 40, ×1.15 from 40, ×1.3 from 60, ×1.5 from 80 (`BALANCE.evilBonusSteps`), read at the moment you play the act (before it vents). It multiplies the act's own reward only: Hunger fed (Devour, Sacrifice, Blood Rite), Devour's and Assassinate's soul-stone luck, Terrorize's fear, Sacrifice's Nether, Bad Water's sick days (rounded), Riot's thinning (capped at the whole crowd), and the share Sabotage and Rob the Cart take from the cart. Never the Malice vented, never the heat. Acts that only remove, break or answer (Desecrate, Frame, Cave-in, Madness, the three answers) have no reward to grow and show no bonus. Followers' own acts are unchanged. `cardView(...).bonus` ("Stronger ×1.3: you are seething") and the bridge's `CardVM.bonus?`; the card's Hunger line shows the grown number.
- **Bots and the bonus:** greedy now holds its acts of evil until the top step (`evilAt` = 80, was 30; Devour when hungry and the answers to faction moves still go early); cautious already held them to 75 (×1.3). Sabotage and Rob the Cart: a share above half takes the rest from the top of what is left (the old alternate-half split capped it at half).
- **Tests that leaned on the old dusk deal's draws from the world's RNG:** the draw has its own stream, so world streams after the first dusk differ from before. Re-seeded or made robust, not loosened: v33 Lockdown test seed 3 → 5 (seed 3's Dawn now draws a clean Inspection that takes 2 off the Council first); life presets pick a searched follower with no find in hand and no prayer to go to; the strike-moment count counts one miner's simultaneous finds once; the invariants accept the town’s two other defeats (writ sealed, rooted out) with ichor left and the vault stone’s ichor (an existing gain reason); the full-run's stand-in town player draws and keeps a card each night (it predates the hand).

## 44. World canvas resolution belongs to the art and a pixel budget, not to the display (ENGINE, 2026-11-23)
- The world canvas' backing store is `render scale × CSS size`, chosen in `src/app/resolution.ts`, not `devicePixelRatio × CSS size`. Measured (PERF.md): a frame is ~2.9 ms + ~19 ms per million backing pixels in the mine at default zoom, so ~90 % of it scales with pixels; DPR 2 on a 16" laptop (6.7 Mpx) cost 4.3× the 1600×900 frame.
- **Render quality** (Settings → Display): Balanced (default) caps the world at 1.52 Mpx (1600×900 + 5.5 %), snapping down to a clean fraction of DPR when one is within 6 %; Sharp = native density; Fast = 1.0 Mpx. Never below 0.5 backing px per CSS px. At 1600×900 DPR 1 Balanced and Sharp give exactly the old canvas (pixel-identical).
- A scene may say what its art can show (`Scene.renderDensity()`: bake density × camera scale, or 'native'); it only ever lowers a dense display towards CSS density, never below it. The play scene does not report it yet (stage 2 wires it with the frame pipeline).
- Canvas text stays at native density: `SceneContext.text()` returns the world context when it is native, otherwise a transparent native-density layer over the world (cleared each frame, faded by the shell's black transitions and the pause veil). The cutscene's dialogue lines and the title's name ink draw there; their glows and the dialogue band stay on the world.
- Known cost of Balanced on dense displays: at DPR 2 the world is upscaled ~2.1× by the compositor, so at the mine's default zoom (art 2 texels per CSS px) it is visibly softer than Sharp. Lifting that needs a cheaper frame per pixel (stage 2), not a bigger budget.

## 34. The town as a place: the plan, the folk, one occupancy rule (TOWN-WORLD, 2026-11-23)
- The town is laid out by hand in `src/render/town/townPlan.ts`: 8 districts in crystal order, named streets, one building per town place (35 buildings for 39 places; rows, highhouses, templesteps and road are streets), and numbered home slots per district. `assignHomes()` gives every household one home in its own district; slots nobody lives in are hidden (`TownRenderer.tiles.hidden`, rebaked through `BlockStates.homes`). The lot-filler, scattered facilities/ground fill and the diggings pit are gone; the heights pad reaches south so the drill yard is walkable (structural only, no restyle).
- People are drawn one by one from the living day plan: `src/render/town/folk.ts` (`TownFolk`, pure in t) and `folkLayer.ts`. Out of frame = data only. No rings, beacons, specks, seals or ground markers anywhere in the town. Size is the only thing zoom changes.
- Town sizes draw from a per-person sprite bank (`src/render/town/folkSprites.ts`: standing, 6 stride and 6 work frames per person, quarter-octave scale steps, 2.5 ms of painting a frame) when a figure is 22 device px or smaller; closer, selected, hovered, Elite and dead figures stay vector (`drawGhelm`). The animator's own figure cache never warms for 500 different walkers.
- One occupancy rule: a person inside a building is not drawn; the building shows it by a lit door glow (stronger at night), lit windows at night only if occupied, and hearth smoke (homes and smoky trades). `TownRenderer.occupancy()` is the sound hook (building id -> count).
- Renderer API for others: `setFolk(input, key)`, `setFolkTime(t)`, `setFolkMarks(map)`, `buildingAt(x, y)`, `buildingInfo(id)`, `occupancy()`. Only the Shade stage calls `setFolk`; other contexts show no people until they do.
- Shade: `ShadeLife` is removed for `scenes/play/shade/folk.ts`. Clock: day t = clock 0..1 over DAY_SECONDS = 24 s; dusk 1..1.25; night 1.25 + sec/300. Sim crowds have no member list, so a crowd's members are the living people of its zone matched by trade, then kin trade, then anyone, up to headcount. Picking: a figure selects the person (or its crowd / sim unit), a building selects the place; the panel head shows name and trade, or the building name and who is inside.

## 46. The frame profiler: one vocabulary of stages, off = no cost (ENGINE, 2026-11-23)
`src/render/profiler.ts`, opened with F3 or Settings → Display → Profiler (off by default). The app's rAF callback is
timed with nested `prof.begin(name)` / `prof.end()`; each row is a stage's **self** time, so the rows sum exactly to the
callback and the root's own time is the "unattributed" row (kept < 5 %; more means a stage is missing). What a page
cannot time is reported as what it is: the rAF interval minus the callback (raster, GPU, compositor, idle), long tasks,
dropped frames; canvas calls, readbacks and blend/filter use are counted; GPU milliseconds are stated as unavailable.
Off, `begin`/`end` return on one boolean and no wrapper or observer is installed. Stage names (`mine: people`,
`life staging`, `mine: relight (lit.step)` …) are the names PERF.md uses for fixes. `e2e/profiler-check.cjs` checks it in
the real game.

## 47. Numbers on screen, a stats menu and a characters menu (owner, 2026-11-24)
Owner, on the live build: "I should see SOME stats the fuck also where is nether?" · "Also where's the menu with my stats? Where's the menu with my characters?"
- **At rest the god's corner shows numbers, not only words.** Nether (the number, and what comes in a night) is always visible; Hunger, Malice and Ichor show their value beside their word; rank and actions left stay. Still few things, still carved, never a spreadsheet.
- **A stats menu** opens from the god's corner (and a key): every stat with its number, what moves it and what it does now (Hunger, Malice with the current bonus on acts of evil, Ichor, Nether and its income, Influence, rank and progress, actions a night, the fee for the next card, blessings held, stones set in the grave and their standing effects, the three powers' alarm).
- **A characters menu** opens from a carved button at rest (and a key): everyone who is yours, one row each (face, name, trade, where they are now, what they are doing, their standing order, what they carry, how firmly they are yours), sortable by place or order; a second list of people you know of (masters, the overseer, inspectors, anyone marked). Clicking a row moves the camera to that person and opens their slab.
- This replaces the "no numbers at rest" reading of `docs/style.md` §7; that file is amended.


## 44. NIGHT-LIFE — the pit works round the clock (2026-11-23)
Owner: "people do stuff 24/7 according to how they would in real life. If they're out of frame they are only data."
- **Two shifts in the pit** (`src/sim/living/society.ts` `PIT_NIGHT_SHARE`, dealt exactly per trade; `plans.ts` `PIT`): about 45 / 40 % of the hewers, haulers, timbermen and cart runners and one of each overseer, pump hand, winder, cook, water carrier, guard pair, sweeper, rail mender, lamp man and shrine keeper work nights; the air-door keeper and the skitter catcher only nights. The mine is 81 people (was 62: more hewers and haulers, a second overseer, a fourth shrine keeper). A person's shift is dealt once and kept; a household can hold both shifts.
- **LIVING follows the rules' people** (the rules are unchanged, byte for byte): `assignLives` rolls its diggers, haulers and sorters onto nights as before (`BALANCE.nightShiftShare`); `simShiftsOf(seed)` (src/sim/state.ts: a state made without LIVING, same rolls) hands those shifts to `createSociety(seed, layout, simShifts)` (via `makeSociety` at state creation and `SocietyAdapter` in the play scene, so the Day and the night show the same people), and the rest of each pit trade is dealt around them to its share. Without the rules' word (LIVING alone) LIVING deals the same share of them itself. The sim's night hands keep to their post all night (meal on the spot, no errands, no doze): the rules have them there, in reach of a whisper. (A first cut had the rules read LIVING's deal; it moved the rules' per-seed outcomes — the opening's night hands, the strike rate, scenario seeds — so it was turned round.)
- **The clock** (plan t 0 dawn, 1 dusk, 2 the next dawn; 0–1 ≈ 15 h, 1–2 ≈ 9 h, `hoursOf`): the day crew reaches the pit head at each hearth's own minute in 0.01–0.12 (far hearths leave first) while the night crew is relieved face by face at 0.10–0.19; the day crew's bell is 0.87 ± 0.045 by face, the night crew reaches the pit head at 0.77–0.85: the crews pass in the cage at dawn and at dusk. The night crew sleeps through the day (≥ 6.5 h), up in the afternoon for the hearth, the market, kin. Everyone sleeps at least a quarter of the 24 h (test).
- **Breaks and errands** (`shiftAnchors`): each crew eats at its own time (by face, at the pit kitchen or on the spot); one to three short errands within two zones of the face — water at the nearest barrel (`water`), the lamp or the pick at the junction / a find to the scales / a word at the overseer's post (`errand`). At night: a meal at the brazier around midnight, an errand, and for about one in six of LIVING's own hands a doze against a prop in the dead hours (`doze`; never the sim's people). The pit clerk closes the book at the post until after the night crew is down. Trades that walk rounds walk them with the meal in the middle.
- **The night shift works the main faces** (non-adopted night hewers at the crystal gallery, the old face, the east face).
- A storm's lift stop: the last stretch below ends in time to walk from wherever it is (a round, a break) to the lift (`mineDay`).
- New plan activities `water`, `errand`, `doze` (additive; the stager maps them to existing clips: water → fill / drink(→eat) / talk; errand → talk / handIn(→putdown) / glance; doze → sleep at a wall spot).
- The stager (`src/life/stage.ts`): a lift trip now leaves when the plan's trip starts (its first walk), not at its last leg; night stops of 0.2+ loop when the night clock holds (was 0.3). `stageEvents` keeps the sim night-shift insert only for someone the sim has moved since.

## 45. PROPS 5: every mine prop as an illustration, behind `?props=4` (builder, 2026-11-23)
- One generator file per family in `src/feel-gen/props/` (containers, timber, lights, tools, furniture, creatures; cart and crates kept), all on `pen.ts`; the shared pieces live once in `parts.ts` (board, post, end grain, lashing, rope, coil, iron band, lathe pot with `wear()`, cloth, stone block, flame, twined weave, shell plate, horn). Generators are never imported by the game; `scripts/bake-props.cjs` bakes them (8 px/unit → 4) into `assets/props/` with the manifest. The old `frame.ts`, `lantern.ts`, `offerings.ts` are gone (replaced by timber/lights/containers).
- Torch: a leaning crooked stake in a stacked cairn, a wrapped rag head scaled up, pitch drips, a charred top, a flame of five tongues inked only on the tongues. Offerings: fewer, bigger, looser (tall stoppered jar, cloth-tied pot, small red pot, food bowl, candle stub on a dish, hide sack). Adit: packed boulders with planes on a dark backing, a pale weathered lintel with end grain, a check and a split, posts with adze bites and wedges, an iron dog on one joint and a lashing on the other.
- Pots wear by `wear()` in `parts.ts` (grime from the foot, drips, scuffs, chips bitten from the silhouette), sized to the pot, so every clay thing (jar, offerings, bowls, braziers, censer, ember pot, hearth pot) ages alike. Baskets are twined (staggered slanted stitches, a darker course every fourth row), not a checker grid.
- Placement (only with `?props=4`, only while the mine's albedo bake draws its props: `P4.on` in `bake.ts`): hooks at the top of the old drawing functions in `things.ts`, `art/props.ts` and `art/props2.ts` (never `dressing.ts`, which the prebuilt-ground hash covers). Each hook returns the same light/flame point the old drawing returned. Variants come from a hash of the place, never from the caller's random stream. Size arguments map onto the sprite's nominal size (basket s/16, canister s/11, jar s/14, offering bowl s/5, rope coil s/6, shed shell s/13, notch board w/50 × (h+4)/40, tally lamp h/64, hide screen w/30, rail stack len/56); rest pallets map their six old felt colours onto three baked felts in turn; picks and scoops are turned by their placement angle.
- Not replaced (kept drawn as before): the lift frame and crystal engine, pump works, boom, cage, punt, counterweights, chute, loading deck, scaffold, duckboard, mooring post, signal bell, switch lever, staff rack, knot cords, castBlock, sort table, offering table, prayer mat, wash trough, trough, votives, hanging censer, flame nook, kiln shrine, shrine steps, pit beast stall, the stalk pile, and the natural dressing (ore and rubble heaps, crystal clusters, stalagmites, split boulder, fungus, ripples, the floor rocks).
- Rolling carts (`ambient/mine.ts`, flag only): the cart is baked twice more, as a body without wheels (its wheels drawn invisibly so the body's random stream, and so its look, matches the whole cart) and one wheel; the ambient draws the two wheels turned by the distance rolled (`sched.d × dirSign / r`), then the body, through `painted()`/`tinted()` so the gray pass and the light apply. The pit beast that pulls the train has four baked walk frames (alternating tripod gait) and a standing pose.

### 47.1 How §47 is built (UI-3, 2026-11-24)
- **The god's corner** (`src/ui/play/rest.ts` `God`, 372×164 design px, was 320×124): the grave crystal, the hunger word on its tab with the number and a carved groove (violet, red once starving; notches at sated/starving), Malice with its number and a blood groove (notches at the evil-bonus steps), Ichor have/most with a drop, Nether with its crystal-violet mark and "+N a night". Always shown, from night 1 (no "shown once met" for these four). Rank stays as the crystal's lit facets, actions left stay on the moon's badge.
- **The god's corner now opens the stats; the pouch opens the grave.** `data-action="crystals"` and the guide's grave/eat/socket anchors moved to the pouch; the god carries `data-action="stats"`. e2e scripts that clicked `.h3 .god` for the grave must click `.h3 .pouch` (fixed in `e2e/hud-shots.cjs`).
- **A people plate** (`PeoplePlate`, 252×66) sits above the pouch: the number of followers, how many more are known. It opens the characters menu, which rises from it.
- **The plate in the rebuilt HUD** (`src/ui/hud/people.ts`, 252×66) sits bottom right over the pouch, drawn from the slab and the baked praying icon; a click toggles the characters menu. The person panel lists Devotion, Strength, Intelligence, Cunning, Health and Resistance (`Person.stats`, `Person.resistance`, rounded) under the bio, and stands 128 design px higher to clear the plate.
- **Keys: `I` your stats, `P` your people**, Esc closes. They are the HUD's own keys; the settings Keys tab appends them to the play scene's list.
- **Values come only from the sim**: `src/scenes/play/menusbridge.ts` (`sheetVM`, `rosterVM`) maps the GameState through the sim's selectors; `hudbridge.refresh` spreads `menusState` into `hud.set` (`sheet`, `roster` on `HudVM`). The corner reads the sheet when there is one.
- **Who is in the lists**: Yours = every living follower, once. Known = living non-followers who are masters (elites), guards, the overseer, priests or hunters and stand where you can see (not the void), plus anyone a threat names, plus the town's hunters (`hunter:<id>`, focused on their block). Nobody is in both.
- **A row click** calls `onFocusPerson(id, focus)`: the mine scene's `pick` verb frames the person (`world.r.personPos`) and selects them (their slab opens); the town selects them on its own panel and moves its camera.
- **The town** keeps its own interface; the same corner, plate and menus mount on their own zoomed layer at its lower left through one call in `shade/index.ts` (`mountTownMenus`), hidden while the stage shows nothing (the ending) and removed with the stage.

Ink is warm (`WINK`) and `inkC` gained a pressure `vary`.

The replaced drawings and the parts nothing uses any more (`coil`, `band`, `post`, `block`, `weave`, `endGrain`) are deleted.
