## 48b. Picture caches share atlas pages, not a canvas per picture (ENGINE, 2026-11-23)
- The prop, figure, head and crystal caches each made one canvas per cached picture: a 3-minute session (pan, zoom, Days) reached 3200–4150 live canvases. Each one had its own backing store and, on a GPU, its own texture.
- `src/render/atlas.ts` gives each cache a few fixed pages instead: props 16 × 1024², figures 9 × 1024², heads 4 × 1024², crystals 4 × 1024². Pages are made as they are needed. The memory caps match the old ones.
- A picture is painted exactly as before: into a scratch canvas with a fresh canvas's state at (0, 0). It is then copied 1:1 into its page (skyline packing, about 75 % full) with a transparent 1 px gutter. A blit uses the 9-argument drawImage. `?atlas=0` puts every picture back on a canvas of its own, for comparisons.
- Eviction works on whole pages. When every page is full, the oldest page is cleared and reused. A picture that is used while it sits in the oldest page is first copied forward to the current page, so only pictures unused for a whole round of pages are lost. That makes it an LRU at page granularity.
- A picture bigger than half a page gets a canvas of its own, capped in pixels. The mine's per-person sprite canvases are resized on zoom instead of replaced, and the canvas of a person who has left is released.

## 49. The merged tree's town and fixtures agree (TOWN-SIM, 2026-11-24)
- **Pit families live in the Rows.** NIGHT-LIFE grew the mine's people (62 → 81), so the society dealt more pit-head hearths than the pit head has homes. By docs/town.md the rows by the pit are the miners' homes, and the society already sends most miners there (`MINER_HOMES`, 7 to the Slag Rows for 3 to the pit head); what was missing was a cap, so the extra pit-head hearths spilled into Rows slots under a pit-head zone. `HOME_SLOTS` in `src/sim/living/society.ts` is the one table of homes per district (minehead 5, slagrows 21, …); `createSociety` deals a zone at most that many hearths, and a full district packs its hearths fuller instead of spilling (bigger pit-head families, not homeless ones). The plan test checks that the plan's home slots per zone equal `HOME_SLOTS`, so the plan and the society cannot drift again: every household one door, no shared slot, nobody homeless.
- **Keep Lane joins the City Road.** Keep Lane (the Tally Hall's street) touched no other street, so walkers from the Tally Hall had no route and `walkRoute` drew them a straight line over the rock (seed 7 day 4: an elder of h049 walking to the well). It now has a vertex where it crosses the City Road (614.5, 164). A new test walks the street graph from one node and expects to reach every node: no lane can be cut off again.
- **HUD fixtures do not lean on a lucky seed.** The bridge test's night-3 state is the first *free* moment of night 3 (no event, blessing or facet waiting: a waiting card blocks every grave verb, which is why "Add a line" did nothing; the rules were right). "Add a line on a full stone" now closes a loose stone's slots to its lines itself rather than hoping the seed dealt a full one. The menus test's Shade state is the first sensible run (seed 7 upward) that crosses the watershed, stopped at a free moment three nights into the town.

## 50. The valley bakes in a worker (ENGINE, 2026-11-23)
- Since TOWN-SIM the valley is made about 25 s into the first night. Its main-thread slices then cost 17 s of frame time in about 56 frames, the worst single slice 1.4 s (software renderer). The land is never in view from the mine.
- The same plan and steps now run in `valley.worker.ts` on OffscreenCanvases (a `document` shim hands art/draw's makeCanvas an OffscreenCanvas). Two bitmaps and the static mask fields come back by transfer, and `Valley.adopt` installs them. The frame path spends 0 ms on it.
- Without a worker, or if it fails, the old slices still run, at most 3 ms a frame. `finish()` bakes synchronously (dev harness).
- The mask fields and live lights come out bit-identical. The art differs only where props are drawn through the shared prop cache (the cap-tree groves). That art was never reproducible: two main-thread bakes in one page differ on 2.8 % of pixels. The cache keys a prop sprite without its size, so the first tree baked in a bucket sets the size for the others, and the main thread's cache already holds the town's and mine's sprites. In the worker the cache starts empty, so the groves come out the same every time. Comparison: `screenshots/engine-valley-worker-grove.png` (left: main-thread bake, right: worker).

## 51. Sound is baked to files; the game is a player (audio consolidation, 2026-11-23)
Follows §38. The synthesiser became a build-time generator; the game only plays files.
- **Where things live.** `gen/audio/**` is the generator: the old engine's oscillators, noise, filters, beds, ambience and recipes, plus the offline renderer `gen/audio/render.ts`. Only `scripts/bake-audio.ts` and `tests/audio/**` import it, and the game bundle does not contain it (checked on the build's chunks). `npm run bake:audio` renders every sound to `assets/audio/**.opus` and writes `assets/audio/manifest.json`. The files are committed. The game loads `src/audio/index.ts` (the player), `automation.ts`, `view.ts` and `types.ts`. At run time it builds only buffer sources, gains, stereo panners, the master compressor and the limiter's wave shaper: no oscillator, noise or filter (a test checks the node kinds).
- **What is baked:**
  - every sfx in 3 variants, rendered with seeds SEED, SEED+1 and SEED+2;
  - burst sounds once per burst position (6);
  - the whisper's close night take;
  - the 9 crystal-material sounds once per affinity (12);
  - the tail find at 1 in 10^4, 10^5 and 10^6, played under the crystal at that crystal's level;
  - the 4 stingers;
  - every bed as a looping file (whisper and shade have night and day layers);
  - every ambience stream as a 28 s loop (market, crowd and tavern once per crowd mood).

  At run time:
  - the player crossfades the phase layers, the beds and the mood loops;
  - the view and the weather set the loops' levels;
  - per-play variety comes from the variant choice (`(seed + plays) % 3`, and the file named by `(SEED + v) % 3` holds the seed-`SEED + v` render, so an engine seeded SEED plays what the synthesiser played), pitch as playback rate, gain and pan.
- **Bake details:**
  - Beds and ambience start 30 ms into their render, the engine's `when()`, with a scheduling pass at once, as the engine pumped after each call. The file starts there.
  - Each bus tap ends in the old master's 18 Hz DC block. It came before the compressor and is linear, so it is baked per bus.
  - Files are peak-normalised to 0.7; the manifest carries the gain that restores the level.
  - Identical renders are stored once.
  - Loop ends are crossfaded (equal power) into the audio before the loop start: beds over 4 s, ambience over 3 s.
  - The FFmpeg encode uses `-bitexact`, so the same generator and the same FFmpeg/libopus build reproduce the same bytes.
- **Pan law.** WebAudio's StereoPanner pans a mono input equal-power and a stereo input by moving the far channel over. The synthesiser's recipes fed it either, and the mix was set with that. The bake records per clip whether the sound's dry signal was stereo (`st`). The player pans a mono-dry sound with its input downmixed to one channel (the mono law) and a stereo-dry sound as stereo. The reverb sends were panned at 0.6× and 0.8× the pan; they now move with the dry signal.
- **Format and weight.** Ogg Opus, 48 kHz stereo, VBR at 64 kb/s for everything. At 48 kb/s libopus dropped the high noise band of short noisy sounds (−17 % RMS on cardBurn, cardDraw and cardDeal) and was often larger than at 64. `assets/audio/` is 27.4 MB: 490 files plus `manifest.json` and `clips.json` (the bake's per-file record, which a partial bake merges into). By folder: sfx 14 MB, ambience 7.7 MB, beds 5.6 MB, stingers 0.5 MB. Only the interface sounds and the stingers (88 files) are fetched at unlock; the rest are fetched as they are first asked for.
- **Loading.** Decoded buffers (stereo float32: 37 MB per 96 s bed, 10.7 MB per ambience loop, about 200 MB for a full town view) are cached across engines up to 512 MB, least recently used dropped first.
  - Beds and ambience decode on first use and start in step when decoded.
  - An sfx waits up to 0.25 s for its file.
  - The interface sounds (tier ≤ 1) and the stingers decode at unlock.
- **Safari before 17** does not decode Ogg Opus. Such a browser plays silence; the player never throws. Accepted for now. A second set in CAF or MP4 Opus, or AAC, is the fix if needed.
- **What is not identical to the synthesising engine.** The engine was pinned before the change (`tests/audio/fingerprints.json`, commit 32974c7). `tests/audio/fingerprint-*.test.ts` play the baked files through the player and compare duration, RMS and spectral centroid at 2 %, and the sample peak at 8 % (Opus rebuilds a transient's phase, so its highest sample moves). The known gaps are listed, each with its measured size and reason, in `tests/audio/pin.ts` `KNOWN`:
  169 of the 217 pinned cases are within those bounds. The 48 known gaps:
  - ambience streams: 25 cases, 10 of them with a crowd mood. A 10–12 s window of random events reads up to 59 % off, 71 % with a mood. The loop is baked at full amount and the player sets its level by a level law the bake measures (`a`: gain = level × (a + (1 − a) × amount)). At the case's level the synthesiser spaced and sized its events differently, so the window holds another take. The synthesiser also eased a new mood in over 4 s; the player starts the mood's own loop.
  - views and mixes over those streams: 7 cases, up to 20 % (the spectral centroid of `view:crossing`).
  - sixteen different sounds in a row (`burst:voiceCap`): 9 %. They shared one random stream in the synthesiser; each baked take has its own seed.
  - pitch as playback rate: 2 cases. The duration follows the rate, ±25 %.
  - the tail find as its own file: 3 cases, up to 7 % in duration.
  - Opus on sharp or noisy transients: 10 cases. RMS within 4 %, sample peak up to 17 %.

  The beds match within 2 % in level and spectrum once they start 30 ms in with an immediate scheduling pass. Before that, their first events were lost (the whisper bed read −24 %). Bursts match once each position follows the earlier ones on the sfx stream.

  Two smaller changes:
  - The reverb sends were panned at 0.6× and 0.8× the pan; they now move with the dry signal.
  - A voice's end is now its last scheduled source stop, not the recipe's returned end.
- **Removed** (nothing in the game played them):
  - sfx: apEmpty, beastLow, cartArrive, chapelBell, crusadeHorn, dayNoteHeavy, eventChoice, pickSwing, shiftWhistle, wickLaugh and the six strike tiers;
  - the sfx alias table;
  - the beds' danger layers (intensity and hunger: tremolo, growl, pulse, heartbeat), with `setIntensity`, `setHunger` and their maps (the game never called them; the beds render byte-identical without them);
  - `setSeed`, `debugMix`, `SFX_INFO`, `SFX_DEPRECATED`, `BED_CUES`, `audioSeedFromRun`.

## 52. Sound ships as a few packs; one-shots decode one at a time, long material streams (audio consolidation, 2026-11-23)

Follows §48 (the section above). The hosted copy allows 511 files (255 per publish), so 490 sound files could not ship, and the
decoded cache of §48 (up to 512 MB, ~200 MB in one town view) added to the lag the owner reports.

- **Packs.** `npm run bake:audio` writes 11 pack files to `assets/audio/` (26.4 MB in all, the largest 5.1 MB, plus `manifest.json` and `clips.json`; the built game is 192 files). A pack is a chained Ogg
  Opus file: each sound is a complete Ogg Opus stream of its own, laid end to end, and the manifest gives its byte
  range (`b`). Taking a sound's bytes and decoding them yields exactly its samples: no codec pre-skip, padding or
  neighbour can bleed, so no silence gaps are needed. Checked in Chromium: a slice decodes to its exact length.
  Packs are grouped by when they are needed: `front` (the interface and the opening's cues) and `bed-front`
  (cutscene, bonus, defeat) at boot; `play`, `crystal` (every material ring), `amb-mine`, `stinger` with either
  stage; `mine`, `bed-mine` with the mine; `town`, `bed-town`, `amb-town` with the town. The player fetches a
  stage's packs when the stage begins (one request per pack, never one per sound) and keeps them as Blobs.
- **One-shots decode per sound, not per pack.** The coordinator's sketch was to decode whole sfx packs and play
  slices (`start(when, offset, duration)`). Measured: the stage-wide sfx are 300–480 MB decoded at 48 kHz (three
  variants of ~150 sounds, plus 10 materials of 12 crystal sounds), so any pack big enough to keep the file count
  under 20 is 30–80 MB decoded and a per-pack LRU under 64 MB would thrash. Decoding a sound's own byte range
  holds only what is played. The cache is least-recently-used under 40 MB (`DECODED_BUDGET`), and a new stage
  drops the one-shots decoded from the other stage's packs.
- **Streams.** Beds, ambience loops and stingers (all over 10 s) are never decoded: they play through
  `HTMLAudioElement` + `MediaElementAudioSourceNode` from an object URL of their byte range. A loop plays its file
  once from 0, then from `loop start − crossfade` each time the playing pass nears its end, the two passes
  crossfading in the graph (quarter-sine in, quarter-cosine out: the equal-power crossfade that used to be baked).
  The next pass is started 1.2 s ahead (more than the 1 s timer period of a background tab) and timed from the playing element's own `currentTime`, so element start
  latency and drift never cut a pass short. The files are now the raw renders; the crossfade is no longer baked.
- **Night and Day stay sample-locked.** The Whisper and Shade beds' Day form is the Night music plus layers; two
  elements cannot be started on the same sample, and a 2 s crossfade between two copies a few ms apart flams. So
  the bed is one four-channel Opus stream (Night in 1–2, Day in 3–4); a channel splitter and two mergers feed
  the two layer gains. Checked in Chromium: the four channels arrive apart through the element.
- **Tails cut at −50 dB** under the sound's peak (was −60): 23 % less decoded audio, nothing a pin can see (the
  energy below −50 dB is under 0.001 % of a sound's), and below any bed it plays over.
- **Measured** (Chromium, the built game, `?scene=play&seed=7`, sound on): decoded audio held 6.0 MB in the mine at night, 8.3 MB in the mine by day and 9.1 MB in the town, 7-8 media elements streaming; with every town sfx then asked for once, 39.9 MB (the budget holds). Each of the 11 packs was requested once in the whole run; zero console errors, zero failed requests.
- The bake runs two workers by default (`BAKE_WORKERS=n`), at low priority when the machine is shared.

## 53. PROPS 7: frame, full cart and torch from the game's raised three-quarter view (builder, 2026-11-23)
- Why: the owner failed loop 6's frame, cart and torch: they were flat side elevations with soft form shading, unlike the passing offerings, gourds and hearth (pen weights, flat tones, hard shadow shapes, ticks, drips, pattern).
- Projection (`src/feel-gen/props/hewn.ts`): an oblique three-quarter view, `pr(x,y,z) = (x + 0.3y, -(0.86z + 0.5y))`. Box members are built from their corners; a face is drawn if it faces the viewer, in one flat tone from the ramp by its angle to the key light (upper left, in front), with a pale arris on the lit edges and the hull inked. No gradients on forms. `form.ts` is bypassed for these three.
- Frame (`frame7.ts`): one hewn set turned −0.3 rad so the posts' right faces and the lintel's end show; the lintel casts a hard shadow on the post heads; knee braces, wedges, earth mounds at the feet. Its idea is a miner's charm (a teal pierced shell disc, knotted cord and a finger bone) hung from a peg, and a tally of shifts cut in the lintel.
- Full cart (`cart7.ts`): you look down into the tub. The rim runs all round, the back walls are lit violet by the load, and three crystal clumps sit on an ore heap. Its idea: one plank was replaced with a pale new one nailed crooked, and a Council chalk mark (square in square) and a load tally are rubbed on the front. The `cartwheel` sprite used by the rolling cart is still loop 6's (shared with the empty cart).
- Torch (`torch7.ts`, still the prop id `lantern`): a clay pitch pot (the offerings' `pot`) bound with cord in the fork of a crooked stake (the hearth's `stick`), burning pitch in its open mouth, pitch run down the pot and the stake, the stake scorched, notches counting shifts, a ring of stones with firelit tops at its foot. `flame` carries no halo, so the pot keeps its tones; a separate glow sits behind.
- Kept: ids, boxes, seeds, anchors, `p4point('lantern','flame')` (now `TORCH7_HEAD`). The adit still uses loop 6's `frame`, and `torch`/`TORCH_HEAD` in `lights.ts` are now unused. The default view does not change.

## 54. A night's stage rebuild runs over frames; the stage on screen plays until it is in (ENGINE, 2026-11-24)
- A stage build (`life.stage` on a cache miss: every sim state the night has, the town's life, a new day) took 250–750 ms in one frame. Every act that changes the sim state (a whisper, a card) froze the game for about half a second, and the town's arrival froze it for 0.7 s (software renderer).
- `buildStageSteps` is the same build cut into steps: one per entity, one per lift batch, one per pass. It yields the very Built that `buildStage` returns. The only world state a build writes, the hearth doors, is put back after every step, so another build may run between two steps.
- With `defer` (the night and the dusk ask for it), a miss steps the build within a budget of a quarter of the time since the last call (4–16 ms) and keeps serving the stage served last. The new stage takes over when its build is in. While one build is under way, a newer request waits for it to finish, so there is always progress. Without `defer`, or with nothing shown yet, the build runs at once, as before. A call without `defer` for the key under way finishes that build at once. The Day needs its bearers' times on its first frame, so it does not defer.
- The life made when the town is baked keeps playing the old life's stage until its own first build is in.
- An empty `dayEvents` list is one cache key. A fresh `[]` each call no longer misses.
- What players see: after an act, the walk it changes starts about 0.4–2 s later (frame rate dependent) instead of after a 0.25–0.5 s freeze. The whisper's own reaction (a sleeper turning) does not wait: it comes from the night events, which every frame reads.
- The build itself is 2–3× cheaper and gives the same Built, byte for byte (fingerprints of three days' builds compared). (1) The slot bank's spiral search carries on from where the last search for the same base cell and nudge set stopped. A candidate that failed once stays failed, because a group's anchor is no longer claimed and then released, so claimed points only grow. (2) The town A* tests a cell's ground when it first reaches the cell, not the whole box up front. (3) The town ground keeps the routes it was asked for, by exact ends, zones and lane, and returns a copy each time. Warm rebuilds went from 220–380 ms to 80–140 ms and cold ones from 680–1210 ms to 430–780 ms (node, one core).

## 55. MINE-RULES: the mine's rules back to the vision (builder, 2026-11-24)
- **Hunger at the top** eats your most devoted follower each night (ties broken by id), with public heat `ravenousPublic`; Hunger goes back to `ravenousResetTo`. With nobody to eat, the god starves: `starveIchor` Ichor, Hunger stays at 100. Strangers are no longer eaten for free.
- **Malice at the top** plays a random act of evil from the evils in the pool (never the three answering acts), on a random legal target, at full notice: its heat skips every cooling (calm, Pale Light, favour blindness). It vents the act's own relief (no reset to a fixed number) and pays no evil XP. Event `outburst` now names the card, target, zone and victims.
- **Ichor is lost only** to lost Influence (`zoneLost`), a hunter standing at the grave (`hunter`, `hunterIchor`), an exposed relic (`relic`, `relicIchor`), starving (`starving`) and the voluntary Blood Price (`bloodPrice`, kept: it is a choice the player makes). Losing a follower (lapse, removal, heads lost) costs no Ichor; `followerLost`, `devotedLossIchor`, `fledglingBelow`, `commonersPerIchor` are gone.
- **Influence = the sum of Hold** (`mapInfluence`); the average stays on `InfluenceView.share` for the HUD's percent.
- **The watershed** keeps the spec's two conditions (§12): the pit head rooted (sticky `surfaceReached`) and Divinity 4. Every text line now says both.
- **One pushback model in the mine** (`src/sim/inspector.ts`): the Council's moves are `Threat`s like the town's (`inspection` at 25+, `crackdown` at 75+ once a stage), announced at Day 0.83 for the next Day, carried out by a named guard (the crackdown: the overseer), with a `route` of up to `inspectStops` galleries (weighted 1 + 3 × your followers there; the grave once the Council is at 50) and an hour at each stop (0.36 → 0.6). At each stop he catches what is really there: stones or a made thing on a follower (searched, taken off, marked; `searchOne` with `where: 'gallery'`), skimming or delving, or someone at prayer (marked; the second mark takes them up the lift). At the grave he sees set stones (Council heat) and, as a hunter at the grave, costs `hunterIchor`. Answers: `agent` (turn him), `hide` (yours on his way lie low), `cool`. The crackdown spends `crackdownCool` of the Council. Removed: the random dawn inspection, the auditor hunter (`HunterKind` is only `cantor`), the Hostile random strikes (lists, heretic-hunt, hymns of fire) and the crackdown sweep, with their balance keys. The Temple's and the miners' crossings (vigils, the Inquisitor, cleansing, the mob, Kindling, Lockdown raids) are unchanged: converting them to announced moves was not in this brief.
- **Preach** (Shade I, `preach.ts`): a person of yours talks to their ties (below). Each Day one listener comes over with the whisper odds × `PREACH.turn` (0.25), else softens by `PREACH.soften`; everyone tied to a preacher gets `TIES.preachOdds` on whispers; Temple heat `PREACH.heat` per preacher (cap 6), the town half that. A preacher gives no Nether.
- **Stand watch** (Shade II, Divinity 5): a person of yours keeps watch where they work; the next levy, ward or burning made there is blunted (`by: 'watch'`), then they pray again and rest `PREACH.watchRest` nights. The writ (signed at the bench) and the crusade (from the road) cannot be watched. The routine id is `standWatch` (the words already used it).
- **Ties** (`ties.ts`): household members, kin / friend / lover ties, and workmates of one place and shift where that place has at most `workmatesMax` (12) hands; from LIVING's society, or (bare states) the people sharing a home area and a work area. A follower's ties get `TIES.odds` on whispers. When someone is devoured by you, their ties harden (+8 Resistance; yours among them −8 Devotion), the town hears (+1 public each, cap 4) and their home loses 3 Hold. When someone is taken or burned by the powers, their ties soften toward you (−5 Resistance) and yours among them feed Malice (+1 each, cap 3). Told as `factionAction` (`tiesHarden` / `tiesGrieve`, faction public) because the renderer's event table is exhaustive over event kinds.
- **Flame stones** (`profane.ts`): an act of evil profanes every hallowed Flame stone where it is done — in the grave (set or loose) for an act at the grave or with no target, or carried by anyone standing in the target's area. A profaned stone keeps its strength but pays no nightly price and no longer quarrels with the set stones. The Temple notices: +4 cult per stone (cap 10), told as `factionAction` `profaned` (cult) with the stones' ids.
- **Removed from the sim** (no rule changes): Gorge (eating no longer heals; only `gorgeNeed`, now the constant `gorgeCrystals`, is left because `src/app/sim.ts` still imports it), mine tiers II–V (`MineTierId` is `1`; vein level = depth 1–3; the ending's tier-II tease keeps its own fallback words), the `influence` alias of `whisper`, the deprecated names (`controlWeight`, `zoneControl`, `PRESENCE_DEVOTION`, `sensedByFollowers`, `INTENT_LINE`, `EVIL_LINE`, `releaseClaims`, `whyCode`, `slipping`), `Unit.strikes` (the inspection event's marks use `caught` and `caughtTaken`, like the inspector) and `Unit.order` / `IssuedOrder`, and every BALANCE key nothing reads. The doubles are one file each: `types3` → `types`, `balance3` → `balance` (`BALANCE3` keys folded into `BALANCE`), content `ledger31` → `ledger`, `powers` → `cards`. Kept: the nine event kinds the sim never emits (`orderIssued`, `orderCancelled`, `orderResolved`, `crystalsChanged`, `soulCrystallized`, `veinExhausted`, `zoneClaimed`, `zoneLost`, `zoneSlipping`), because the renderer's event table (`src/render/fx/sim.ts`) must name every kind; `OrderId` stays for them. `Unit.task` stays because `src/scenes/play/world.ts` reads it.
- **Spec formulas without a test of their own** now have one (`tests/sim/spec-formulas.test.ts`): `burnWorth`, `stageForLevel` / `realmForLevel`, the standing ranks, the 5–95 % odds clamp, `round1`, the vein level, and the event kinds `flamePrice`, `delved` and `hew`.

## 46. PLAY-3: the play scene, cleaned and whole (builder, 2026-11-23)
- **The dead game is out.** The stand-in sim, the old crystal and module views, the canvas depth mark and the scene placeholders are deleted; the play scene runs only on the real sim (`RealSim` / `RealNight`). `src/scenes/play` (without `shade/`) went from 7573 to about 5450 lines. A run that is lost or won is not offered as Continue (`src/app/session.ts`), and a won session goes back to the title.
- **The depth mark belongs to the HUD.** The scene reports the level it shows (1–3, none above ground); the bridge sends `depth` to the HUD only when the level changes. No depth text is drawn on the canvas.
- **People come first under the pointer.** A click hits the person whose body box (at least 28 px tall on screen, whatever the zoom) holds the pointer before it hits the place under them, so a walking miner can be clicked at the default zoom.
- **Out of reach says how to reach them, by the sim's own rule.** The sim hears someone at their night spot or at the mine face they work (never the lift), on your colour or next to it. The card names both places and how far your colour is from the nearer one: "Out of reach. Sleeps at X, works at Y. Bring your colour next to Y (one place further) and they hear you." Up in the town during the whisper stage it says only the household and friends of your followers hear you. It is one line, under 220 characters.
- **Things people carry.** A follower's card lists what they wear (each with Take it back when the rules allow) and what the grave could give them. The grave lists the things it keeps and the things your people wear, with who wears them. Give and take go through the sim's `giveItem` / `takeItem`.
- **The start pick is the HUD's own picker.** When the play scene opens on the bonus phase (normally the front's bonus scene has already made the pick), it deals the sim's three offers as `bonus` cards in the blessing picker ("Choose what survived the fall"), the same `data-bonus` cards the front deals.
- **Bonus words go on the card.** A stronger act of evil shows its bonus ("Stronger ×1.3", with the full sentence on hover) under its line on the card itself.
- **Every act shows in the world, once.** The sim's events for eat, set, unset, enchant, curse, recombine, burn, miracle, evil, devour, soul, give, routine and move go to the VFX router, which owns their effects. While a routed effect plays, FEEL's copy of the same effect kind is muted for 3.2 s so nothing doubles. A miracle, an act of evil and a devouring also move the camera to look at the target. A draw and a take have no picture of their own in the sim, so the grave's mouth flares (nether for a draw or an evil card, blessing light for a miracle).
- **One capture tool.** `e2e/playtest/shots.cjs` is the only capture script for the mine and the run: the reel (every beat filmed) and the full run (the mine to the watershed, the town, the ending) are the same pass. `--until <beat>` stops early. `--from lastmine|lasttown` starts from the headless full run's save through the title's Continue (this replaces `e2e/play-core-fullrun.cjs`). `--png-beats` also saves full-size PNG stills. The old tools (`play-core-fullrun.cjs`, `mine-run.cjs`, `reel.cjs`, `play.cjs` and their libraries) are deleted. The single-purpose probes under `e2e/playtest/` stay.
- **The run plays the current interface.** It clicks the hand (`play-miracle` / `play-evil`: one click plays the card or arms it, then Pay, then a click on the target), the deck (`draw`), the offer (`take` / `take-none`), the grave's tiles and verbs (`data-crystal`), event choices, pick cards and `hint-close`. A new beat clicks a walking person and checks that their card opens.
- **Opening the grave frames the hoard.** The grave's panel covers the left of the screen, so opening it (the button or a click on the grave) moves the camera until the hoard sits to the right of the panel, at a zoom of 4 to 9. The camera stays put when the hoard already shows there. A stone set, enchanted, recombined or eaten is then seen happening in the world, not only on the panel. While the panel is open the HUD's depth mark is hidden, because the panel covers the place where it sits.
- **People stacked under the pointer.** Miners asleep in a row overlap. A second click on the same spot (within 6 px) picks the next person under it, nearest first, and goes round again after the last. Hover always shows the nearest.
- **The reel is filmed in segments.** On a shared, loaded box one run from the title to the watershed holds a browser for 20 minutes or more. `shots.cjs` now films it in segments, each a browser run under 5 minutes. Each segment starts from the save the one before it left (the game's own localStorage save, taken in through the title's Continue), so the nights stay one continuous play. A beat a segment does not run keeps its row from the segment that ran it, so INDEX.md, shots.json and the sheet always show the whole reel. The long mine play carries its nights from one segment to the next and stops cleanly at a night's start when its time runs low. The tool takes any free browser lock and runs at low priority.
- **The Day gets the town's life.** The play scene handed FEEL's Day only the sim's own events. LIVING's feed (a lift that sticks, an inspector at the gate, a story at the well) and its weather were in the session's day data but never reached the notes, and the calendar went in as an object where FEEL reads its kind. `src/scenes/play/livingday.ts` now passes the calendar's kind, the weather's slices and the feed, each line anchored on its zone. On seed 7 the first Day's notes on screen go from 2 to 5, and rain coming in is now a note.
- **The reel reads the act's own effect when people swamp the pixels.** Round the grave, miners walk through the 400×225 window, and on a loaded box their motion can hide a small effect. The debug snapshot now holds the last sim event the VFX router turned into effects (`fxLast`: the event, how many effects it spawned, when). A crystal beat with a small pixel change still passes when that event spawned at least one effect for this act and the grave is in view beside the panel. With no effect, it is still broken.
- **Weather is judged when it comes in.** The weather observer used to look at the first second of the Day, before the drizzle in the plan had started, and it read the Day strip, which draws no weather word. It now waits for the Day's clock to reach the first wet slice, then reads the Day's notes and feed. Drizzle passes when the Day says so; rain and a storm also need the mine's answer (flooding or pumps, spec §5). A sighting that fell short is replaced by a later whole one.
- **A drizzle is seen, not said.** FEEL keeps a drizzle's line light, so it never shows on screen; only rain, a storm and fog become notes (spec §5: "rain coming in"). The reel's weather check therefore accepts a drizzle when the world draws it, with the pit head framed. It reads this from the debug snapshot's `sky`: WEATHER's kind and its rain from 0 to 1. Rain and a storm must still be said on screen and answered by the mine. A long play begun again from an earlier night forgets what it saw on those nights.
- **Bad news leads the ledger, and tribute is named on the strip.** A search at the lift was marked only by a sound at dusk, so a follower could lose their stones with nothing on screen. The dusk ledger's first line now says who was searched, what they lost and whether the Council took them. The offering strip's title adds how many stones came unasked ("The offering · 1 brought unasked", using WRITING's own label), so tribute reads differently from what followers on Offer bring.
- **The reel's segment list only shows what the table stands on.** INDEX.md lists a segment only if one of its rows is still in the table, or if it is a chunk of the current long play.

## 56. The look is too sharp and not stylized enough; crystals and ore (owner, 2026-11-24)
Owner, in order: "Props 7 is OK. A little awkward sharp on the corners and stuff but it's ok" · "Overall the style is too sharp and not stylized enough" · (a crop of the crystal cluster over the grave) "this also needs work" · "Everything tbh especially the crystals scatterd abbout and we need ores in walls and stuff".

What that binds:
- **Everything drawn in the mine moves toward `docs/refs/mine.png`'s stylization**: few, fat, chunky forms; blunt, worn, slightly rounded corners and tips; thick ink with weight that varies and wobbles; two or three flat tones a form and one broad highlight; irregular, lopsided groupings. Out: needle shards, razor-straight machine edges, hairline strokes, symmetric fans, sparkle spam. (Orchestrator's reading of the owner's words against the reference; the pots the owner passed first are already this.)
- **The grave's crystal cluster** is redrawn to that.
- **The crystals scattered about the mine** are redrawn to that: in the reference they are clusters of three to five stubby prisms on a rock base, growing out of the walls and the rock at the edges of rooms, each with a soft glow.
- **Ore and crystal in the walls** is a new thing the mine must have: seams, nodules and crystal growing out of the wall faces, where miners work it.
- Nothing of this becomes the default until the owner has seen it.

## 57. The mine's screen caches scroll; "at rest" means still for a few frames (ENGINE, 2026-11-24)
- During a drag the camera holds still between two pointer events. The cache took a single still frame for "at rest" and rebuilt the whole box (screen + 160 px on each side, 1.6× the screen) for an exact still frame: 172 full rebuilds in a 12 s pan. Now "at rest" means 4 still frames and 120 ms. The exact rebuild still comes then, so a still frame is still exactly a direct paint.
- A pan past the margin no longer rebuilds the box. It scrolls the cache by whole pixels (the origin stays the old one plus a whole number) into a second canvas, then paints only the strips it uncovers. Each strip is painted with the whole box's view under a clip, so it holds the same pixels as a full build at that origin: 0 differing pixels in 8 compared scrolls, base and fog.
- Measured over a 10 s pan (software renderer, 1600x900): full rebuilds 172 → 4 per 12 s, base layer 3.69 → 0.80 ms mean, callback 25.6 → 20.5 ms mean, frame interval p95 50 → 33 ms.
- Still open: the fog composite costs ~11 ms a frame while panning even when it hits. It blends a semi-transparent layer over the whole screen. The idle breath (FEEL camera, ±0.3 % zoom) changes the zoom every frame, so at rest both caches are painted straight every frame.

## 58. The frame has a budget: 1–2 ms. The composite moves to the GPU (owner, 2026-11-24)
Owner, on being shown 17–43 ms medians from the software-rendered container: "Are you stupid? Median ms on my rig should be fucking 1-2ms MEAN" · "Build this shit sensibly. Stop doing stupid shit".

- **Budget (binding):** the game's work per frame is 1–2 ms on the owner's machine at 2560×1440, at rest, panning, zooming and with the Day running. Hitches (a frame over 16 ms) only at a scene change, never from an act, a pan or a zoom.
- **Why the old frame cannot meet it:** it paints with Canvas 2D every frame — about 340 image draws, 360 fills and 70 blend-mode operations at rest, layer repaints whenever the zoom moves, person sprites redrawn every frame. Caches in front of that were patches.
- **The frame from now on:** everything that can be painted ahead is a texture (ground, props, crystals, people's frames, light, fog, influence colour); the frame is a WebGL2 composite — one pass for ground × light × fog × night × influence, one batch for sprites, effects as batched quads. Pan, zoom and the camera's idle breath are a matrix: free. Canvas 2D stays as the tool that paints textures when something changes, never per frame.
- **Measured by counts, not by this container's clock:** at rest 0 texture uploads, 0 canvas repaints, a few dozen draw calls, and the frame callback's own script time ≤ 2 ms even here (a GL frame's script is only the calls). A test holds the counts.
- One renderer at the end: the Canvas 2D frame path is deleted when the GL one has parity; no second path kept.
- Art builders deliver baked sprites and placement data; only the engine builder touches the frame path.
- Machine: at most three builders at once (two restarts in one day from running more).

## 59. The town plays on the mine's interface; a building opens its own screen (UI-4, builder, 2026-11-24)
The Shade stage's own panels (`shade/ui.ts`, the town menus) are gone. The town uses `src/ui/play` through the same `HudBridge` as the mine (`src/scenes/play/shade/hud.ts`, `TownHud`): the god's corner, the grave and pouch, the hand and the draw, the three powers, the moon (End Night, the Day), the stats and characters menus, and the person's slab. A `RealSim` whose session is the stage feeds it, and every step goes through `actionWhy`, then the stage's `apply`. Decisions:
- **A building's screen** (`src/ui/play/building.ts`, view model from `shade/building.ts`) is built to the approved UI5 mock-ups (§35). It shows the interior with its people; the master and the workers with where each is now and a Whisper plate (or when they can be reached); the take rule drawn as lit and unlit figures plus one plain line ("Turn Broth, the master, and one more of the workers"); what it gives; its state (yours / contested / lost / burned / theirs); an announced move against it with its answers; and its bench once yours. It opens three ways: by clicking the building on the map, from "Works at" on a person's slab, or from the Buildings rows of the stats menu. A move's warning (top right) is one short line, "At dusk today: … See its answers.", and a click on it opens the building the move falls on.
- **Every verb on it is one legal sim action.** A verb is shown ok only when `actionWhy` is null. An off verb says the rules' own reason in the screen's status line when clicked. `tests/play-shade/building.test.ts` checks this for every building, both as the sensible bot leaves the town and with the benches held. "Yours" follows the dawn's `town.held` (what benches and gifts obey tonight). People who turned away since dawn make the building **lost**: "Yours until dawn: its people are turning from you".
- **Benches**: the gem workshop clamps the stone, Re-cut deals three cuts on pads, and Cut it keeps one. Each cut shows its sim text and the stone's mods after it. A pending re-cut cannot be walked away from: Esc and End Night bring the workshop back. The forge has the anvil, three forms, the gain line and Forge it. The apothecary, tally house and the chapel's relic use a craft bench (the thing, worn by a follower: its boon; slipped to anyone else: its curse). The council hall and the vault show the keep's door and **Open the vault**, which ends the slice.
- **Give / take on the slab** says plainly what it does: "They wear it. …" for a follower (the boon), "Slipped to them, it curses them. …" for anyone else (the curse); "Take it back" stands under what they wear.
- While a building's screen is open, the guide's hint cards and the Day's notes are hidden, and the hand sits under the veil.
- **Long warnings wrap** onto a second line instead of being squeezed or cut. The grave panel opens under the goal line, never over it. The moon carries a Day strip: the calendar day and the weather, with a chunky weather glyph. The offering names each stone's bearer over its niche, with a tribute (brought unasked) in gold.
- §56 applied to the interface's stones: blunt caps on the prisms, chunkier widths, no hairline facet edges, round motes instead of four-point sparkles.

## 60. Building screens finished on the real game (UI-5, builder, 2026-11-24)
Takes over §59 (UI-4, stopped by a restart). What changed and why:
- **A move's band sits under the interior, not across it.** The red band of an announced move covered the room's people up to their heads. It now hangs between the interior and the rest of the screen, full width, with its answers on the right; everything under it moves down and the slab grows by the band's height (the slab is still centred; at 1280×720 and 2560×1440 it fits with room to spare). The room's people stay in sight.
- **Each cut says what it changes.** The three cuts listed the stone's powers after the cut, which read the same three times ("Your followers strike 5% more often" ×3). Each cut now says what grows, what shrinks and what it cuts away against the stone now ("Grows: …", "Shrinks: …", "Loses: …"; "It comes out as it is now." when the rules' cut changes nothing), and the bench's foot says what the stone holds now. The words are the sim's own `modText`; nothing is computed but the comparison.
- **The master's line is who they are.** LIVING's line opens with their trade at this very building and their hearth, which the screen already shows; the screen keeps the rest (their ties: "Owes Baill four knots.", "Sees Tov in secret.") and their temper (WRITING's trait line). The sim has no "what they want" (docs/town.md §4); this is the nearest true thing.
- **A Whisper plate says only the verb.** Under the master's plate stand the hour, the odds and the price; the price is the sim's `whisperCost` for the person where they will be at that hour (the slab's `canWhisper` cost is the price at night), and "0 actions" (a free whisper) is not said.
- **The town's first hint points at a building** ("Buildings: click a building to see who works there, what it takes to make it yours, and what it gives you"), at the block of one you hold, else one you are taking, else a bench; it is done once a building's screen has been opened. The mine's "The surface" hint is no longer shown in the town.
- **Proof** (`e2e/ui8-shots.cjs`): steps `bonus`, `town` (rest, a warning opening its building, the workshop re-cut and cut, the forge, a jewel given from the slab, the workshop at 1280 and 2560, the keep and its vault opened), `more` (the apothecary's bench used, a building not yours, the town's Day strip) and `mine` (the grave under the goal line at 1600 and 1280, the mine's Day strip, the offering naming its bearers). `ONLY=` picks steps.
- **A building under the pointer is named beside the cursor** (the HUD's aim chip: "The forge · Not yours. Click to open."), worked out once per building hovered. Opening its screen clears the chip, so Esc closes the screen.
- **The orphaned Shade dev entry is deleted** (`shade/dev.ts`; `dev/shade.html` no longer exists) with the start helpers only it used.
- **Rules gaps seen from the screens (not faked):** a re-cut of a stone whose powers are all fixed by the rules (field or action-point powers, and a single power so nothing can be cut away) comes out the same three times and still costs its Nether; the sim has no "what the master wants" (§4), so the screen shows their ties and temper.

## 61. CRYSTALS-2: the mine's crystals and wall ore as baked sprites plus placement data (builder, 2026-11-24)
- **Behind `?crystals=2` only.** Without the flag nothing changes: dressing.ts, the prebuilt ground and its hash are untouched. Making it the default is one change: the `CRYSTALS2` constant in `src/render/crystal/flag.ts`.
- **Sprites, painted once.** `src/render/crystal/sprites2.ts` names each picture with an id: `grave`, `rim:<rx>:<ry>`, `cl:<seed>:<size>:<grade>:<rock>:<base>`, `ore:<kind>:<seed>:<size>:<depth>:<rock>`.
  - `c2Sprite(id)` paints the sprite into the `crystals2` SpriteAtlas at 4 px per mine unit on first use.
  - `drawC2` only blits it.
  - No per-frame painting, gradients, filters or shadowBlur.
- **Placement is a pure function of the layout and the seed.** `crystals2Layout({map, faces, avoid, heart, eye}, seed)` in `src/render/mine/crystals2.ts` returns `C2Place[]`. Each place has kind, sprite, x, y, scale, flip, depth (sort y), zone, glow (x, y, r, colour, intensity, bloom), face id and vein depth.
  - The map is the headless `MineMap`. The faces are the life places tagged `face`, the spots miners strike.
  - Every work face gets ore on the wall foot above it: growth, nodule or seam, richer at vein depth 2–3 (`DEPTH` in sim/content/gems.ts).
  - A face with no wall within reach gets a crystal outcrop on a mound.
  - Clusters stand at wall feet, away from rails, water, set pieces and keep-outs. Sparse seams and nodules are added elsewhere by zone.
  - No sim rules were added.
- **Glow** is a `Light` of kind `crystal` in the existing rig, not paint.
- **The old crystals go at bake time.** The `C2HOOK` in `src/render/art/crystals2hook.ts` skips every old `crystal()` cluster and the grave's mass, replaying their random draws on a no-op context so the bake's random stream and everything else in it stay identical.
  - Old clusters are found by their glint, plus the prop at the foot's y. The unlit ones count too.
  - The old eye fissure and rubble ring are replayed on the no-op context; the rim sprite replaces them.
  - Any other old cluster (the bloom's fresh growth) is redrawn in the new hand.

## 62. CRYSTALS-2: silent glints (builder, 2026-11-24)
- The dressing's glints are both the sparkle stars and the list of "where the crystals are": the bloom grows beside them, and the ambient flow picks faces from them.
- Dropping them with the old clusters would have stopped the bloom in those zones. Instead each new cluster and ore gets a glint of size 0.
- `index.ts` skips size-0 glints in its two sparkle loops: two guards, no change to the default, where every glint has a size.
- The owner's "remove the sparkle star" is met by that skip; the soft ambient wave of light still runs through the clusters.

## 63. CRYSTALS-2: the soft pen and the form (builder, 2026-11-24)
- **The pen.** `src/render/crystal/softpen.ts` (`soften`, `inkSoft`, `lineSoft`, `lump`) is copied from the PROPS 8 branch so that crystals and props share one hand: bellied outlines, ink of varying weight, chiselled corners.
- **The prisms.** Fat prisms with blunt chiselled tips:
  - width 0.42–0.48 of the cluster size; the tip is 0.5–0.66 of the width;
  - 2–3 flat tones per face, one broad stripe highlight, ink `#170d27`;
  - from grade bright up, a tapering inner light column (no blob).
- **Wall ore** draws on the face in three kinds:
  - **growth:** prisms breaking out of a socket;
  - **seam:** a lens-shaped vein packed with faceted crystal ends;
  - **nodule:** a knot of rock broken open on a geode.
- **The grave.** A lopsided crown with a broken giant on the left, set in a ring of overlapping rounded lip stones round the eye.
- **Not done: the stones in the grave panel and the pouch** (optional step 4). The HUD crystals are the inventory's own renderer, and changing them was not clearly better than leaving them.

## 66. PROPS 9: one kit for every prop in the stylized manner (builder, 2026-11-24)
- Every mine prop not on the owner's accepted list is drawn from one small kit in `src/feel-gen/props/chunk.ts` (§56's manner), so there is one way to draw a prop:
  - `blob(outline, ramp, tone)`: a soft form in three flat tones (body, a shadow side, one broad highlight) with thick soft ink.
  - `shape(points)`: a lopsided, rounded, hand-wobbled outline.
  - `tube(line, r0, r1)`: sticks, hafts, legs, feelers and bones, with round caps, a lit band, a shadow band and one streak.
  - `hornS()`: a horn, which is a tube with growth bands and a darker tip.
  - `slab(x, y, w, h, d)`: a chunky block in the raised view, with a front, a top, a side, worn corners and a lit edge.
  - `vat(profile)`: a round vessel with an open mouth.
  - `cord`, `bind`, `rivet`, `wedge` and `groundShade`.
- Each prop carries one likeable idea, named in the header of its file:
  - `vessels.ts`: a bone awl under the bundle's cord; a cup drying on the jar's cloth; the canister's glowing window under a wax seal; one offering fallen out of its bowl.
  - `lights8.ts`: the hook lamp on a beast's horn; the tally lamp's red tally board; the poker left across the cold brazier; the censer's smoke; the ember pot's knucklebone charm.
  - `tools9.ts`: the pick's red wrist loop; grit left in the scoop; the cord coil's bone toggle; an owner's rag on each tool in the rack; the balance's ore pan sinking; one red tally group and a chalk on a cord on the notch board.
  - `camp9.ts`: the pallet's folded blanket and cup; a red hand on the hide screen; the stone still in its lifting sling; a red rag on the spare rail; the shell split at a seam; a spine knuckle among the bones.
  - `beast9.ts`: the pit beast is a working animal, with a madder felt saddle, a cord girth and a knucklebone charm.
  - Baskets: the capped basket holds fat fungus caps with cream gills; the empty one has two violet chips left in the bottom.
- Removed, so that nothing else draws a prop:
  - `form.ts`, `cart.ts`, `cart7.ts`, `creatures.ts`, `furniture.ts`, `tools.ts`, `lamps.ts` and `timber.ts`;
  - the old torch and cairn in `lights.ts`;
  - `shellPlate` in `parts.ts`.
  - The dev page's rails moved into `dev.ts`. What is left of `lights.ts` is the accepted hearth, the candle and STONE, unchanged.

## 67. The minecart is a tub of the baskets' family (owner: "Except for the minecart, very good"; then "Eh sure", 2026-11-24)

  - The body is a short, stout, rounded tub (a superellipse with a belly) in the baskets' raised view.
  - The rim is thick and rounded, in warm iron. The wood has two or three warm tones with one plank seam.

## 68. Prop boxes cover their drawing (builder, 2026-11-24)

- Every box in `index.ts` is grown to that extent plus 1 unit, rounded outward to whole units, and never shrunk. `?view=edges` is now clean for every prop.
- The game places a sprite by its anchor in px (−x0·scale, −y0·scale) and its image size, so a larger box moves nothing. Ids, seeds, files and the lantern's flame point are unchanged in the manifest.

- A bake with `opts.sync` cannot wait for the sprites: a prop whose sprite is not loaded is left out.
- The prebuilt ground's key covers terrain, layout, geometry and dressing code, not props, so it stays valid. `tests/mine-ground` passes.
- Not yet baked, so their painters stay: the lever (`drawSwitchLever`, baked only in the `?props11=1` atlas), the set pieces (shrine, votives, mats, offering table, lift, engine, counterweights, deck, sort table, desk, scaffold, ore and crystal heaps, boulder, pump, trough, duckboard, punt, cache, fungus, hanging censer), the cast block and knot-cords, and the town's own painters (`drawJar`, `drawCrate`, `drawBarrel`, `drawSack`).
- **The view:** the slab depth goes through `hewn.ts` `VIEW` (`DX = VIEW.kx`, `DY = −VIEW.ky`), and the round mouths go through `chunk.ts` `KY` (0.44, hand-set for the 30° view and marked as depending on the view). Nothing is drawn in plan: the pallet, rail stack, cut stones and notch board stand in the raised view with a front and a top.

## 70. The rock's hand and the wall ore stones (builder, 2026-11-24)
- **The one ground.** `ground-1` (`src/render/mine/prebuilt/ground-1.*`) is the mine's only ground. The chunky-hand redraw of the walls (ROCK 2, ROCK 3) was rejected by the owner and deleted, with its bake, its forms sheet and its hash test. `src/feel-gen/rock/stone.ts` (the lumpy boulder in the soft pen, `src/render/crystal/softpen.ts`) stays: the wall ore uses it.

The angle is two constants in `src/feel-gen/rock/view.ts` (`ky`, the share of a thing's depth shown; `kz`, the share of its height shown), 0.71/0.71 (45°). Changing them changes stone proportions, not the walls' height.

The rock colour is the zone family's albedo, the same base the ground uses. In `crystals2.ts`, a work face's ore sits 2–7 cells up the face with a wider size range (seam 26–58, nodule 24–44, growth 25–47). The ids are unchanged, and the placement stays pure.

Owner, on the sheets `screenshots/props8-{1,2,3,4}.png`: "Bundles lamp lantern hook lamp pallets beasts bones cord all fail". Failing, to be redrawn: hide bundle, felt bundle, tally lamp, lantern (the torch on its post), hook lamp, pallets, beast, bones, cord coil. Everything else on the sheets stands. ## 72. A paid re-cut always changes something (SIM-FIX, builder, 2026-11-24)
- A stone remembers the last set of three cuts a gem workshop offered for it (`Crystal.lastCuts`, the cuts' lines as text, order ignored).
- A re-cut is legal only when some cut could differ from the stone as it is (`canFacet`, `src/sim/crafting.ts`): a line whose number can move (round(v × 0.8 … 1.4) spans two values or more; fields and action points never move), or two lines whose dropping gives different stones. Otherwise `facetWhy` says "No cut would change it" (`noSlot`) and the bench does not offer it.
- When it is legal, `makeFacetChoices` rolls the three cuts with the old generator and re-rolls from the seeded rng until the set differs from the last offered set **and** at least one cut differs from the stone (at most 24 re-rolls); if that still fails it changes one cut to the first other cut the stone allows. Deterministic per seed; the odds of each cut are unchanged except that a repeat is refused.

## 73. Bots never put a follower on an empty Offer (SIM-FIX, builder, 2026-11-24)
- Seed 41 with the sensible bot no longer stalls on the current rules (it wins at night 20). The stalled seed-41 runs in `/tmp/sim5/cache` come from the 2026-11-23 rule sets, before TOWN-SIM and MINE-RULES.
- The same symptom was still live in the greedy bot: alive in the town, never finishing (seeds 3 and 15 stalled at night 100; seed 41 took 85 town nights to be sealed). Greedy's `offerAt` of 0 put every follower on Offer and never sent one back to Pray. In the town nobody has a face to fill a stash, so Faith fell to 0 and Nether fell to the Ember plus the grave's lines (about 16 a night), and no whisper was affordable. The rules were not stuck: a player can set those followers back to Pray for 1 AP and 1 Nether each.
- Bot fix (`src/sim/bots/index.ts`, routines): a follower goes on Offer only if they have a stash, work a face, or a stone gives offerings (`offerExtra`). An offerer with none of these goes back to Pray, whatever the bot. Rules unchanged. Diagnostic: `scripts/town-trace.ts`.
- A second cause had the same symptom: seeds 5, 14, 23, 26, 32 and 33 still stalled after that fix. In the town, greedy's whisper floor (0.08 + (Public − 35) / 150) rose above every chance on offer once the Public ran hot (86–99). Greedy then sat on 20,000+ Nether and 9 AP and whispered no one, though the hall's master was in reach every night at 0.1–0.22. Greedy ignores heat everywhere else (it never lies low and plays any act of evil), so it now ignores the Public's talk too: its town floor is a flat 0.08. - **Effect, and what it exposes (balance, not tuned here):** greedy on 60 seeds went from 13 % won / 12 % stalled (before) to 15 % won / 10 % stalled (Offer fix alone), then to **93 % won / 0 stalled**, won by nights 17 / 37 / 63 (p10 / median / p90). Greedy used to lose because the bot defeated itself, not because greed is risky. Once it whispers in the town, its Nether (Faith × 20 at Divinity 7) and its followers carry the hall, and a Public at 86–99 costs it almost nothing. "No strategy dominates" is now unmet. The fix for that is a balance question for the town's pushback against a hot Public, not a bot question. The other option, giving greedy the council-hall plan, was tried: 85 % won by a median of night 19. It was not kept, because it changes greedy's character rather than its blind spot.

## 74. Walls: ground-1's slab faces stay (owner, 2026-11-24)
Owner on ROCK 2's walls: "The fuck happened to the walls in new dude". The redraw had turned each back wall into rows of small dark lumps and lost the wall as a wall. The ROCK 3 redraw (tall lit slabs in the soft hand) failed too (§75, §76). Ground-1's walls (tall lit faces of big slabs, `src/render/terrain/slabs.ts` `addMass`) are the walls; the redraws are deleted (§70).

## 75. The SEEN rule: no crystal or ore on a wall's top or on dark rock (owner, builder, 2026-11-24)
Owner: "Why are there crystals on top of the fucking occluded walls too". Ground-1 is the game's ground.
- **Cause:** the old placement tested anchor points against the life map, never against the drawn box.
  - `onWallFace` assumed a 72-unit face below any rock with floor within 14 cells.
  - Ore heights reached up to 7 cells above the foot, plus the sprite's own height, past the visible face.
  - Seams checked three anchor-row points, and clusters checked only `isFloor` at x ± 0.6·size.
  - Outcrops had no check at all.
- **Rule (`seenOk` in `src/render/mine/crystals2.ts`), judged on what the ground paints:** its masks (`wallFace`: the Mass, Slab and Niche polygons plus the crevice band; `floor`: walkable floor), each at a 0.5 threshold and read bilinearly. Without masks, the life map stands in.
  - A seam or nodule: its whole sprite box, inset 10 % for the glow's fade, lies on a visible face.
  - A growth at a wall's foot: its root is on the face, and its box is on face or floor.
  - A cluster: its footprint (five points across its base, at its foot and a little in front) is surely floor, never the edge of a dark mass, and the rest of its box is on floor or face.
- **Fallbacks when a piece does not fit:** it is tried at its own size and height, then lower, then smaller. A face with nothing visible tall enough gets a growth at its foot; failing that, an outcrop on the floor beside the miner, on either side and up to 40 units out, shrinking down to 16. Every work face keeps its ore.
- **Test:** `tests/render/crystals2-seen.test.ts` checks seeds 1, 2 and 3 against ground-1's mask file, with its own nearest-cell sampler. It allows zero ore boxes off a face and zero clusters off floor or on dark rock.

Every prop in the mine is placed for a reason a player can see (a work face's kit at the face, lights on walls and at junctions, frames across passages, beds in rows in the quarters, nothing lying in a walkway); seeded scatter of props over open floor is out.

## 77. Set features and loose props: a clean mix (owner, 2026-11-24)

Owner: "If you're treating them like actual props why are there 0 angles and variants?" · "Just either make a good set map or make a proper prop set, or a proper mix where some items are just props with variants and others are in-map features. No weird fucking frankenstein".
Decided: the mix, in two classes with nothing in between.
- **Set features** are built into the place and drawn for their exact spot, wall and direction, as part of the map: timber frames and shoring, the adit, wall and post lights, the tally lamp, the bunks, tool rack, notch board, hide screen, balance, hearth, rail stack at a siding, the lever, shrine and grave furniture. Each is specified (a frame's span is its passage's width; a lamp hangs on a named wall), never dropped like clutter.
- **Loose props** are things a person could pick up or that move: vessels, baskets, bowls, offerings, bundles, crates, cut stones, tools, bones, cord, shells; the cart and the beast. Each has real variants (five for the common ones, three at least), long ones four facings, movers eight directions. A loose prop always belongs to a set feature or a work place as its kit; it never stands alone in open floor.
- One kit draws both (`src/feel-gen/props/`), in the approved manner; facings are the eight compass directions on the floor plane, 'S' toward the viewer. Placement is data in `src/render/mine/dress/` with `class`, `facing`, `variant` and a reason for every item.

## 78. Cards say one thing; no hint system; stats as numbers (owner, 2026-11-24)

Owner on the plan for "what is going on / cards / stats": "1 is retarded. 2. Too much fucking info. 3. Yes."
- No hint system, no goal/next-step/result lines added to the screen. Clarity comes from fewer things on screen and from what the player can click, not from more text.
- A card says what it does in one short plain line (plus its cost, already on the card). No target/cost/effect/duration breakdown.
- The stats menu is labelled numbers in a few groups on one screen; explanation only on hover.
- Panels are cut, never added to.

## 79. Clear before clever: what is on screen is real, clickable and owned-or-not at a glance (owner, 2026-11-24)

Owner: "make the game sensible and visually clear first then I can tell you what's wrong because I don't even know what the fuck I'm clicking on. I see some fucking ghosts running around and I click on them and nothing clicks. I click some dudes and I be missing even though I click on them. I have no idea who I own at a glance. There's again a bunch of random fucking phantoms. I see 'can't whisper now' and I don't fucking know why. I click something and it tells me some random fucking tooltip about the region in the mine - I don't care about that. Nothing makes sense".
- A figure on screen is a real person, drawn solid (gray where the god has no hold), and a click on its body selects it. Nothing ghostly, translucent or stand-in is drawn.
- The god's people are told from everyone else at a glance, on the figure itself (colour and the god's violet), with nothing floating over heads. This is the default at once (asked for, not a look to approve).
- The ground, zones, rooms and "the mine" do not react to hover or click and have no tooltips.
- "Can't … now" is replaced by the actual reason in at most four words; a control that can never be used in the stage is not shown.
- This comes before any further art or balance work on the play screen.

## 84. Work the frame does not need now runs between frames
- `src/render/idle.ts`: a keyed job queue pumped as message tasks after the frame, in slices of ≤ 5 ms.
- A prop sprite the frame has not got yet (a walk frame, another facing or light, a neighbouring zoom bucket) shows its nearest sibling for that frame and is painted between frames. Only a prop never seen before is painted in the frame. The mine's relights and the town life's first build run the same way.
- A stage build pauses between a person's stops, so no single step of a deferred build is long.
- The grave hoard's glow pools and light columns are sprites painted once: the frame draws pictures, never gradients.

## 90. The new view, M1 and M2: a retained PixiJS scene of the mine that renders on demand (ENGINE-GL, 2026-11-24)
Per §89 (owner: rebuild the renderer on PixiJS v8, retained, on demand). `src/view/` holds it; `?view=2` or F4 (stored) selects it; nothing else changes when it is off.
- **Mount.** One `WebGLRenderer` (WebGL2 only: the shaders hash with 32-bit integers) on its own canvas *under* `#game`; `#game` turns transparent (2D context with alpha) while the view draws, so the 2D overlays not on the view yet (affordances, person overlays, effects, far point, the god's thread, the world's overlays) still sit on top. A canvas no app frame drew through is hidden. `preserveDrawingBuffer` is on: a frame that issues nothing must keep showing the last picture (headless screenshots showed black without it).
- **On demand.** The world calls `ViewMount.present(update)` from the app frame that shows the mine; `update` writes what changed and says whether anything visible did; if not, nothing is issued (no render, no draw). No second animation-frame callback. Counts are taken at the GL entry points (draw calls, texture uploads) and shown in F3.
- **Retained, from the bake.** The mine is built once from what the mine renderer already baked: the pre-lit layer (albedo × static light, props and crystals in it, exactly what the 2D frame blits) as 8 mipmapped tiles, cut off the thread with `createImageBitmap` and uploaded one tile per idle slice; the night ratio as one multiplied sprite; the Sensed layer and the fog's two small masks under one shader (gray × (1 − void cover), alpha 1 − active: `composeRect` in a shader). The default mine draws its props and crystals into that layer, so the parity reference is that layer; the props11 sprites (and placements as data) become sprites when the default look moves to them.
- **The clock moves things, not the CPU.** Every small additive light (the moving part of the lights, flames, lamp cores, crystal glints, the god's eye, the Kiln's eye, flame halos, the allegiance lamps) is one instanced draw; `rng.ts`'s hash and `noise1` and `lighting.ts`'s `flicker` are ported exactly to GLSL (32-bit integer multiplies wrap the same), so a flame or a glint moves as the 2D frame painted it. A frame writes the camera (when it moved) and one time uniform; instances are rewritten only on the fog's version, the night amount, the god's pulse, the zone flags.
- **People (M2): a figure is baked once per steady frame and the view picks the frame; a walker is never repainted.** A person is two sprites (colour, and the Sensed ground, one picture: `OWN_LOOK` makes the Sensed figure the same as the lit one), each a texture swap away from its next frame.
  - *Frames.* `viewFrame(animator, t, 16, loop)` (render/people/viewloop.ts) names the frame the figure is in (a gait's stride phase from the walked distance, a loop clip's time, or the last pose of a clip that has played out) and leaves its loop in `PState.vloop` (`key`: gait or clip, carry, direction quantised to ¼ / ½, fatigue, the ear-fin and wick channels). `poseStill` poses the middle of bin *b* of that loop from the gait / clip alone (no blend, no planted-feet integration, no weather), facing +x; `figures.ts` paints it with `drawFigure` into its own canvas trimmed to what the figure draws (`measureBounds`, 3 px per unit), one `CanvasSource` + `Texture` per frame, uploaded between frames in the idle slice (`initSource`), so a frame's first sight costs one bake and every later sight is a pointer write. A loop's 16 bins are queued the first time it is seen, the needed bin first and the next ones after it; until a bin exists the sprite keeps its last picture. A person keeps eight loops (the oldest goes; a frame still on a sprite is destroyed when the sprite lets go of it); a change of the paint the frames were drawn in (conversion, devotion) drops them all and they bake again.
  - *Facing.* The frame faces +x; −x is the sprite's `scale.x` flipped about the feet (what the 2D frame's atlas draw does), so a loop is 16 pictures, not 32.
  - *Between loops.* A blend into a new activity (0.32 s), a turn (0.22 s), a reaction, a clip still playing once, or a crest fade is a pose seen once: it is painted as it is (`drawGhelm`) at most every 70 ms into one live texture per person, and `People.note` reads such a figure again every update (a standing figure in a reaction changes nothing else).
  - *Where it stands.* Position is the sim's, written every frame to the sprite (no held position: with 16 bins the stance foot slips under 1 world unit a bin); depth is y; the light on a figure is the sprite's tint; the allegiance lamp is an instance at the wick of the frame shown (`Frame.lx/ly`, from the same skeleton); an effect's shudder or slump (`personOffset`) is the sprite's x, rotation and y scale; the picking box is the 2D box.
  - *Cost.* A walking frame writes a few sprite properties per person that moved and one lamp-buffer commit; the render is 8 draw calls and 0 texture uploads (all uploads happen at bake time, between frames). Not on the view yet: the digger's pick-impact dust and sparks (`drawImpact`, drawn after the sprite on the 2D frame).
- **Picking** is the scene graph's (`eventMode` on the figures, hit areas the 2D box). `WorldComposite.personAtScreen`/`hitTest` answer from it while the view draws. (The play scene's `world.ts` still tries its own body boxes first; making the scene graph the only picker is PLAY-5's call.)
- **Parity** (e2e/view-probe.cjs, same state, app stopped): mine at night mean |Δ| 2.0/255, 1.5 % of pixels > 24 (machines, mine layers, dust motes, wisps, sparks of the shrine and the idle loops are not on the view yet: M2).

## 98. The new view, M3: the god's thread, the people's marks and the far point (RENDERER, 2026-11-25)
- **Thread.** The resting strand is baked offline (`scripts/bake-thread.cjs`, painter `thread.ts` through `threadBake.ts`, `assets/thread/`, 336 kB): four layers as 32-unit tiles, the puff and bead sprites, the strand's samples as data. `src/view/thread.ts` places the tiles (veil and body normal, halo and glow additive; strength = container alpha) and moves 14 smoke puffs and 5 beads along the samples from the clock. It lies over everything the mine draws. A strand still growing (the cutscene) is not on the view.
- **Marks.** Selection and hover rings, the target / inquisitor / apostle sigils, the order glyphs and the far point (black veil, glows, motes, rays) are instanced shapes drawn by signed distance (`src/view/marks.ts`, `far.ts`; one additive mesh and one normal mesh for the glyph's disc). The 2D overlay path no longer draws them while the view does.
- **Probe** (`e2e/view-probe.cjs`, STATIC build, seed 7): 15 draws a render, 4 uploads in 5 s at rest (people frames), 0 panning; parity mean |Δ| 1.19/255, 0.2 % of pixels > 24 (was 2.0, 1.5 %).
- **Night after a Day** (`e2e/night-day.cjs`): night 1 and night 2 are equal on both renderers (luma 21.6/21.5 and 22.6/21.5, same camera); the HANDOFF note names the camera on the dark surface edge. A third night was not reached (the script's Day 2 stalled).
- **Sharp at close zoom.** The lit layer's boot refinement (a coarse bake upscaled, refined at full resolution between frames) was pumped only by the 2D frame, so the view kept the upscale; the view now queues it (`vm.pumpLit`) and the close shot matches 2D. The terrain's sharp pass (`?sharp=1`) is off by default on both renderers and is not on the view.
- **Affordances** (`src/view/afford.ts`): colour-dodge is `dst·(1+src/(1−src))`, which a multiply blend with premultiplied colour `src/(1−src)` and alpha 0 does exactly; one mesh per zone footprint (`zone.foot.c`), the three tints (selectable, hover, selected) multiplied in the shader, seen² from the fog's void cover. They lift Sensed zones, so a forced state shows on the gray ground.
- **Machines** (`src/view/machines.ts`, seam `render/mine/machines.ts`): the lift (cage, rope, sheave, counterweight, ore skip, gate, riders) and the loop's carts with the pit beast, from the ambient engine's own state. Carts and the beast are the baked `assets/props` sprites tinted by the static light at their feet; the lift is rectangles and instanced discs. While the view draws, the engine's colour pass leaves them out (`MACHINES.viewOwns`); its gray (Sensed) pass still paints them on the 2D canvas.
- **Not yet on the view:** the gray pass of the machines, cart-load glints, drips, wet sheen, motes, rats, bats, steam, tendrils, the weather's mine layers, desecration / ward / bloom / incense, effects, haze, pick-impact dust (its position is in figure space, `animator.impactX/Y`).

## 99. The new view, M5: the mine's small moving things, haze, sheen and the weather's drips (RENDERER, 2026-11-25)
- **One batch, no pictures.** `src/view/life/batch.ts`: drops, rings and arcs, puffs, soft glows, discs, boxes (lines), stars and the light shaft are instances of one shader (each a signed-distance shape or the painter's own gradient profile, premultiplied so `normal` blend adds with alpha 0 and covers with alpha), one draw per layer, written from the ambient engine's state each frame the clock moves. No canvas, no texture, no upload. Three layers: ground (under machines and people), above (over people), gray (over the Sensed composite).
- **Ported with the 2D painters' loops and numbers** (`life/ambient.ts`, `life/weather.ts`): pool sheen, drip rings, the grave's breath, roots and moss, falling drops, lamp breath, gutter sparks and motes, the crystal glint wave, the daylight shaft and its motes, steam, grit, bats, rock-face dust, chips and pick sparks, strikes, the hop of a carted gem, cart-load glints, the weather's drips and the shaft's rain and mist; colour and Sensed passes. The 2D painters skip what the view owns (`MACHINES.owned`, `render/mine/machines.ts`).
- **Haze** (`view/haze.ts`): a haze shader on a world quad over two cell textures (noise once; the Sensed/Active amounts only when the fog changes); the clock is a uniform. **Sheen**: the weather's own baked canvas as an additive sprite, re-uploaded when wetness changes.
- **Uploads at rest** (`e2e/uploads-ab.cjs`, counter `uploadBy` in the mount): every upload in the rest window is a person-sized figure canvas (88-108 × 100-156 px) baked between frames, plus the lit layer's tile re-sync once at boot; 62, 49, 29, 8 in four 5 s windows 20 s after load; hiding the people sprites does not stop them (the bake uploads eagerly). It is the people sheet; bake-people replaces it.
- **Night after a Day** (`e2e/night-day.cjs`, view): night 1 luma 22.3, night 2 21.5 (97 %); day 2 of the script stalls on a screen it does not handle, a script limit, not a dark night.
- **Not on the view yet:** the rats, the moths round the lamps, the shrine nooks, the face lamps and the Tally table (baked sprites needed: skitter, moth, gem, brazier, lamp, table), emissive extras (desecration, ward, bloom, incense, god-eye wisps, dust motes in light pools, cave-in dust), effects (`render/effects.ts`), pick-impact dust, the water rising in the lower workings (clipped to the floor), the Sensed (gray) lift and carts and the cave-in's gray sprite, the grave hoard (`scenes/play/grave.ts`).

## UI-6. Cards say what they do in one line; no flavour on any card (owner, 2026-11-24)
Owner on the plan: "2. Too much fucking info." Each card (miracle, act of evil, blessing) shows one short plain line, a handful of words with the single number that matters ("One person: +12 % whisper odds."); its cost is the number already painted on the card. No target/cost/effect/duration breakdown, no second line, no flavour line (the blessing and starting-bonus picker dropped its italic flavour). The line is `line` in `src/content/cards.ts`, numbers as slots from the card's `defaults`, each checked against `src/sim/cards.ts` (`playMiracle`, `applyEvil`) and `mods.ts` / `blessings.ts`; the table is `dev/ui6-cards.md`. Three old texts were untrue and are fixed: Black Dew does not blind guards; Madness does not keep guards at their posts for 2 days (it stops tomorrow's searches); Turn the Mob only breaks the Town's moves. A card in the hand shows its line at rest as well as lifted (a resting card that showed only its name "told you almost nothing"), and a card that cannot be played shows its short reason at rest. The pay slab is wide enough that its sum never sits under Pay / Back, and stands clear of the lifted card's Discard tab. Every hand line fits two rows of the card (all 33 measured); an act of evil strengthened by Malice shows its multiplier as a small "×1.5" tag on the art, the full sentence on hover, so the line stays one line. A card with no fitting target anywhere says "Nowhere to use it" / "No one in reach" (the sim's at-rest `needsTarget`), never "Pick a target".

## UI-6. The dusk report: rows and the count, no mood line (owner: "too much info", 2026-11-24)
The dusk slab keeps its rows (struck by yours, to your grave, carted to the Council; in the town the three heats) and the Council's count as one row ("The count · 6 of 2 expected · Content"). Cut: the day's opening mood line (`duskDay`), the count's second sentence (the same numbers again), and the lines that restate a row ("The mine gave 2 crystals…", "Your followers struck…"); at most two lines remain. Labels lost their filler ("Stones struck by yours" → "Struck by yours"). Before/after: `screenshots/ui9-dusk-before-after.png` (10 lines → 5).

## UI-6. Why a command cannot be used: four words or fewer, from the sim (owner, 2026-11-24)
Owner: "I see 'can't whisper now' and I don't fucking know why." Every dim command (person slab, hand, grave, pay, building screens) shows the real reason in at most four plain words, made by one mapper (`reasonWords`, `src/ui/play/dom.ts`) from the sim's own legality sentence (`src/sim/legal.ts` `*Why`, `town/reach.ts`, `town/gifts.ts`, `selectors.stageTargetBlocker`, and WRITING's plain versions). When the player can fix it, the words say what is missing and how much ("Needs 12 Nether", "Opens at Whisper II", "Ready in 2 nights"). The full sentence stays the tooltip. It replaces the old guess, which said "Not now" for 124 of the 188 sentences and "Too costly" for anything mentioning a crystal. Someone out of reach keeps a dim Whisper plate reading "Out of reach" instead of a two-line paragraph; the building screens no longer print the whole sentence (cut at 470 px). Table: `dev/ui6-reasons.md`; `tests/hud/reasons.test.ts` holds every sentence to four words.

## UI-6. The stats menu is numbers with labels (owner: "your stats is a bomb of text", 2026-11-24)
The stats slab (`src/ui/play/sheet.ts`) is rebuilt as three groups of rows, each row a short label and its number: **Each night** (Hunger, Malice, Ichor, Nether: the number, a thin groove where it has a scale, its change a night and the three largest causes of that change: "Your rank +6", "Your followers +2", "Blessings, stones −2"; Nether's "Praying followers +34", "Your grave +19"; "Acts of evil give ×1.3"), **Your people** (followers, how many on each standing order, crystals they carry, the share of the mine or town in your colour, places coloured; then the three powers watching you, a number and a word each), **Your powers** (rank and progress, actions, the next card's fee, stones set, blessings, then what the stones and blessings add up to, each a label and a signed number; in the town the buildings, each a door). Every explanation is one short line shown only while the row is hovered (an ink tab under the row). Gone: every "what moves it / what it does now / what happens at the top" paragraph, the per-stone and per-blessing lines (their names are the hover of their count), the Influence and Rank paragraphs. The causes are the parts of the sim's own `hungerRise` / `maliceRise` and `netherIncome` (the bridge sums them; `tests/hud/menus.test.ts` checks they add up). It fits one screen at 1600×900 (bottom at 736 in the mine, 820 with six buildings in the town) and at 1280×720, with no scroll and nothing cut.
