## 86. PROPS 10: the nine failed props redrawn as new ideas, and the props' one view at 45° (builder, 2026-11-24)

Not default until the owner has seen it (the orchestrator merges after the verdict). Ids, seeds, frame counts and anchors are unchanged; boxes grew where the new drawings needed it (never shrank, §68).

- **The nine, each a new idea at 45°** (few fat forms, blunt corners, thick soft ink, 2–3 flat tones and one broad highlight, top and front both visible):
  - hide / felt bundle (`bedroll10.ts`): a bedroll lying across the view, its rolled end a spiral facing us; the hide one strapped with two leather bands and pinned with a horn toggle, the felt one cinched with a doubled cord and a wooden toggle, red blanket stripes showing in its spiral.
  - lantern (`lamps10.ts`): a stubby post in a ring of fat stones, bound in two fat cord turns, carrying a clay fire bowl with its coals and a big flame (`LANTERN10_FLAME`).
  - tally lamp: one fat post on a stone cairn with the Council's burnt mark, a squared arm and brace, a fat clay-capped belly lamp hung from it, the red tally tag notched and sealed on the post. Light point unchanged (12, −54.5).
  - hook lamp: an iron wall plate with two rivets, a squared arm and brace, the same belly lamp in small. Light point unchanged (6.5, 11).
  - pallet (`pallet10.ts`): a low bed seen from above-front — timber frame on stubby feet, a quilted felt pad (madder, teal, ochre by seed), a bolster across the head with a stitched end, a striped wool blanket folded over the foot, a clay cup and knucklebones on the floor by it.
  - beast (`beast10.ts`): a round shell-backed grazer with its head end towards us (local skew −0.2), a friendly round head with closed eyes and two knobbed feelers rising off its crown, six stubby legs (tripod gait over the same 4 frames), a red saddle cloth with a knucklebone charm. The thin legs and long feelers that failed are gone. After the orchestrator's first look: the head and legs are the shell's own teal, dulled and paled (one animal, not a grey head on a teal shell), the head pushed out from under a collar plate of the shell; and since at 45° the dome hides the far three legs entirely, they are cheated forward as in a three-quarter drawing — a darker stub and foot each, between and just behind the near feet — so all six can be counted.
  - bones (`floor10.ts`): two fat crossed bones and a skull — a shrine, not a scatter. The skull is a wickfolk's, not a human's (`docs/theme.md` §3, `screenshots/theme-turnaround.png`): a great round globe narrowing to a small chin with no separate jaw, two huge round sockets set low and wide, two nose slits, a small low mouth, fat fans of bone where its ear-fins grew; on its crown the round socket where its wick grew, and the idea: somebody has stood a candle stub in it.
  - cord coil: three fat stacked turns with the tail tucked over the top into the middle, its end whipped with red thread.
- **The 45° constants.** `VIEW` (`hewn.ts`) takes `ky` from `VIEW2.ky` (0.71, `src/feel-gen/rock/view.ts`) and `chunk.ts` `KY` = `VIEW.ky`, so every floor circle, slab top and box-built top is drawn at the rock's angle; one constant, mirrored, not copied. `kz` stays 0.86: as `rock/view.ts` says of the walls, the angle changes the proportions of what lies on the floor, not the screen height of what stands up — the props stand beside people whose height is fixed. That keeps every engine-held point (hook and tally light, lantern flame from the manifest, cart axles) valid without touching the engine.
- **The passing props at 45° (step 2).** Box-built ones (slabs, vats, tubes, baskets, cart tubs, crate and frame tops, every `KY` ellipse) follow the constants with no other change. Hand-set depths that assumed the old view are tied to the same constant: `FLOOR45 = KY / 0.44` (chunk.ts) grows every old contact shadow (`groundShade`, `castShadow` ry) and the hearth's ring of stones, coals and the candle's pool; `LATHE_RY = 0.3 · VIEW2.ky / 0.44` (parts.ts) deepens the lathe pots' mouths, rings and feet (offerings, gourds, hearth pot, candle crater). The hearth's spare stick moved out in front of the deeper ring. Upright things (frames, racks, boards, screens, the balance, lamps, wheels) keep their heights by design (kz). Nothing else needed redrawing; the railstack, pick and scoop still read side-on but passed that way and keep their character.
- **Boxes grown** (§68, never shrunk) to cover the deeper drawings: frame, adit, crates, offerings, baskets, gourds, gourd, jar, canister, brazier, censer, toolrack, balance, notchboard, hearth, hidescreen, cutstones, railstack, shedshell, bones (and the nine's boxes above). `view=edges` is clean for every variant.
- **Re-baked** `assets/props/*` (PORT=5491). Ids, seeds, frame counts, anchors, the lantern flame point (now (0.6, −31.5), read from the manifest), the hook and tally light points and the cart axles unchanged.
- **Proof:** `screenshots/props10-45.png` (the whole set at 45°), `screenshots/props10-45-before-after.png` (jar, basket, brazier, cart, frame, crates: 30° beside 45°), `screenshots/props10-in-game.png` (`?ground=2&crystals=2`, day 1, zoom 11; zero console errors), `screenshots/props10-in-game-quarters.png` (the quarters, still grey in the Sensed light), `screenshots/props10-in-game-2.png` (default zoom); and `screenshots/props10-failed.png` (old beside new, large), `screenshots/props10-failed-game-size.png` (at 2 px/unit, as baked, beside passing props).- **Dev:** `dev/props4.html?view=game&px=2&rows=…` shows props baked down to the shipped sprite at game zoom; `view=board` takes `s=` (fixed px/unit a row) and `imgs=` (scale of image cells).

## 87. PROPS 11: a turning kit; loose props in variants, set features drawn for their facing (builder, 2026-11-24)

Not default until the owner has seen it. Answers §77.
- **One kit turns things** (`src/feel-gen/props/turn.ts`). A prop is modelled once as a few fat 3D forms in its own frame (front toward local −y, 'S' = toward the viewer) and drawn from any facing by turning the frame about the vertical before projecting with the map's own numbers (depth at `VIEW2.ky` 0.71, height at 0.86 as §86 keeps it). The forms are superquadrics (egg → cushion → worn block, per-side half sizes, taper, lumps, open mouths) and turned profiles (lathe). Each is painted in the approved manner: its tone regions are the toon bands of the one key light (shadow t−1, body t, lit t+1, one broad highlight t+2, the floor's dark t−2), each one flat fill, then a softened hand-wobbled silhouette and thick soft ink. The light does not turn with the prop, so a turned prop is shaded as it would be in the place; nothing is mirrored. A `Scene` paints parts back to front by world depth, so the order is right from every side.
- **Marks** (style §3): every form gets hatch groups in its shadow, flecks, and grain along its long axis where asked, scaled to its size.
- **Facings**: loose movers (cart, beast) in all eight; long loose props in four (S, SE, E, NE: both ends alike); bunks in the four axis directions (S, E, N, W: a bunk sits square to its room); wall things for the three walls a viewer can see (S back wall, E left wall, W right wall); round things one.
- **Step 1** (`screenshots/props11-step1.png`, `props11-step1-game-size.png`): pallet (set feature, 4 facings × 5: made, slept in, a bedroll on it, bare slats, kept with a miner's things), cart empty and full (loose, 8 × 3: the tally cart, the mended cart, the old cart; each empty one left differently: a rag, a shovel, crumbs of ore), hook lamp (set feature, 3 walls × 3: horn and slotted pot lamp, Council iron and caged shard, a miner's lashed stub and gourd lamp), jar (loose, 5: the passed jar, a crock with a ladle, a tall store jar sealed, a cracked jar bound in cord, a small jar in a carrying net), beast (8 standing, 8 × 4 walk frames: a domed shell in plates with a skirt, a collar plate, a round blind head with shut eyes, two knobbed feelers, six fat legs splayed so all count, a madder saddle cloth).

## 88. PROPS 11 step 2: the rest of the kit, the atlas, and how the game picks a facing (builder, 2026-11-24)

Not default until the owner has seen it: everything below is behind `?props11=1`; without it the old `assets/props` sprites draw exactly as before.
- **Carts and hook lamps redone** after the owner's step-1 verdict (`screenshots/props11-carts-lamps.png`). Cart: a flared tub with a thick rim and visible depth, on four fat round wheels (end-on, the wheel faces are cheated toward the viewer so they read as round, never as bars; the far pair less); full = a heap of 20+ mixed crystal chunks in the approved crystal manner (`src/render/crystal/inked.ts`) over an ore bed, some spilling over the near rim; empty = hollow with lit far walls and something left (a rag, a shovel, crumbs). Hook lamp: a short stout arm straight out of the wall from a fixed plate (pegged timber, riveted iron, horn wedged with stones), the lamp hanging plumb on a short loop, symmetric bodies (slotted pot, Council cage, gourd), the same three on all three walls, about a third of a person.
- **Side walls are cheated 35°.** A thing on the E or W wall (or a frame across an E–W passage) would be seen exactly edge-on in this view (the map has no sideways skew), so the kit turns it 35° toward the viewer (`wallFrame` in `kit11.ts`). Same idea for the tally lamp: with its arm straight at or away from the viewer (S, N) it is turned 40° so the arm always shows. Flat free-standing things that would be a line when edge-on (hide screen, balance) are drawn square-on and on the two diagonals only; the lookup picks the nearest drawn facing.
- **Set features** (`setf11.ts`): frame (S, E × 3: chalk tally, brace and iron cramp, old wood with a crack, a wedge and a charm), adit (3 walls × 3), lantern post (× 3: fire bowl, cresset, crystal lamp), tally lamp (4 axis facings), tool rack and notch board (3 walls), hide screen (S, SE, NE), balance (S, SE, NE), rail stack (4 line facings), lever (4 × thrown / not), brazier (cold, banked), hearth. Pallets as bunks (step 1).
- **Loose props** (`loose11.ts`): bundles (4 × 5), crates (S, SE × 5), cut stones (S, SE × 3), pick and scoop lying (4 × 3, head toward the facing), bones (4 × 3, one a skull shrine with a candle), rope coil (× 4), spare wheel (× 3). The passed round props keep their drawing and gain real individuals by small options with unchanged defaults: baskets (5 shapes), gourd (5), gourds (three / two / four), ember pot (charm / none / dying), censer (smoking / cold / small), shed shell (one / turned / two), canister (lit / spent / small), offering bowl (ore / chits / crystal), offerings (3).
- **The atlas**: `node scripts/bake-props11.cjs` draws every facing × variant × frame at 8 px a unit, shrinks to the game's 4, and shelf-packs them into 2048-wide pages: `assets/props11/atlas-0.png`, `atlas-1.png` and one `manifest.json` (cells[facing][variant][frame] = page, rect and anchor; named points such as `light` and `flame` per facing and variant). 296 sprites, 3 files, about 4.7 MB. A prop that overflows its drawing box fails the bake.
- **The lookup** (`src/render/art/props4.ts`): `p4(g, id, x, y, { facing, variant, frame, sx, sy, … })`, `p4image`, `p4point(id, name, { facing, variant })`, `p11entry(id)`, `facingOf(dx, dy)` (y south; 'S' = toward the viewer) and `nearestFacing`. Missing facings fall to the nearest drawn one; `variant % count`. Old calls without a pick behave as before.
- **Wiring**: every sprite-backed function in `src/render/mine/things.ts` takes an optional trailing `opt` ({ facing, variant }) and passes it on; the hook lamp and tally lamp return the sprite's own light point. Ambient carts take their rail's direction and the beast its walking direction (`src/render/ambient/mine.ts`, from the mover's angle). The placement code (`src/render/mine/dress/draw.ts`, not touched here) still has to pass a facing for walls and passages.
- When props11 becomes the default, `assets/props` (the 98 single sprites) and its bake can go.

## 89. The renderer is rebuilt, not patched: a retained scene on PixiJS that renders on demand (owner, 2026-11-24)

Owner: "You've said you're optimizing a million times. Actually fucking do it. Rework the entire system. Stop fucking around and wasting time. Stop writing stupid code. Copy good code, there's lots of open source projects everywhere. This is your FUCKING DOMAIN, web development… 1 draw a frame is still excessive, let alone 500" · "YOU DON'T EVEN NEED TO PROFILE TO REMOVE 99% OF LAG" · "LET ALONE PROFILE ON MY MACHINE SPECIFICALLY".
- No more patching of the immediate-mode Canvas 2D painters (§58 is superseded in method; the 1–2 ms budget stands and is tightened).
- The renderer is rebuilt in `src/view/` on **PixiJS v8** (MIT, recorded in `CREDITS.md`): the world is built once into a scene graph from baked images and atlases; game code changes display objects when the simulation changes, and never issues draw commands.
- **Every frame in 1–2 ms, smooth at the display's rate.** Owner: "10 fps is too low. Anything visual needs to render with 1-2ms. That's 500+ fps. Extrapolation frames, no unnecessary draws, culling, shit baked everything, whatever you have to do". Whatever moves is interpolated/extrapolated every displayed frame from the simulation's low-rate states; flames, glows and sheen are time in a shader or blended baked frames; the frame's own work is a few uniform and transform writes for what is moving on screen, with everything else baked, cached or culled; simulation and staging never run inside the frame. When nothing visible changes, no render is issued — but smoothness is never traded for that.
- Light, fog, night and influence are one shader or multiply layer fed by small textures uploaded only on change. Picking is the scene graph's: a click hits the sprite the player sees.
- Budgets: update + render ≤ 1–2 ms with the scene fully animated; ≤ ~15 draw calls, 0 uploads, 0 allocations per frame; 0 renders when nothing visible changes; no task over 50 ms after the title.
- Order: the mine at night (M1), the mine complete and default (M2), town/valley/watershed/cutscene, then the old renderers deleted (M3).
- The hosted page's frame reporter (Versions 23–25) is removed (Version 26): the owner does not want profiling on their machine, and it is not needed to find per-frame waste.

## 79. No phantoms; a click takes the person under it; yours at a glance; the ground is no target (owner, PLAY-5, 2026-11-24)

Owner: "I see some fucking ghosts running around and I click on them and nothing clicks. I click some dudes and I be missing even though I click on them. I have no idea who I own at a glance. There's again a bunch of random fucking phantoms. … I click something and it tells me some random fucking tooltip about the region in the mine - I don't care about that."
- **No phantoms.** A figure on screen is a real person the player can select. LIVING's other people (the rest of the shift in the mine, townsfolk, the crowds) are data only: the stage places them (the dawn, the lift, the dusk walk, who is inside a building) and counts them in `stats`, but a staged frame carries patches for individuals only (no crowd patches, no far-zoom specks) and nobody draws the rest (`src/scenes/play/world.ts`: the scene's view holds the sim's people only). In the town stage only the sim's people and the members of the sim's group units are drawn (`FolkLayer.only`, set by `shade/folk.ts`); the rest of the society fills its buildings and is never drawn or hit.
- **Solid, never see-through.** Figures on Sensed ground are drawn at full opacity (was 0.7: the ghosts); they still sink into the dark at the Void's edge. And no figure is a flat silhouette any more: on lit or gray ground a person is drawn as themselves (the gray pass's `mono` is ignored for people, `render/art/ghelm.ts`).
- **Yours at a glance** (`OWN_LOOK`, `render/people/figure.ts`): the god's people are the only ones in full colour (wherever they stand, gray ground too) and their wick burns violet; everyone else wears gray cloth (14 % of their colour, a little dimmer) and keeps an ordinary gold flame. On the ground under the feet (`src/scenes/play/marks.ts`, the world's post-fog ground seam, both layers): yours = a steady violet pool and a thin violet ring; picked = a bright bone-white ring with four ticks and a soft column of light on the body (a violet pool under it too if it is yours); under the pointer = a pale ring and a faint light on the body. The layers no longer draw their own person rings (the scene passes them places only).
- **A click takes the person drawn under it** (`PlayWorld.personAt` / `peopleAt`, `ShadeStage.figureAt`): each figure drawn this frame owns its body (the mine's rig at its own scale, 33 × 16 mine units, a sleeper 12 × 36; the town's 28 × 18, an Elite half as large again); the front one (feet lowest) whose body holds the point wins; a second click on the same spot takes the next behind; small figures get slack (a box never under 28 px in the mine, 20 px in the town). The hover is the same answer, and the hand cursor shows only over a target. During the Day a click on a person picks them (their card; its acts say "at night").
- **The ground is no target.** A click on the ground, a room, a block or "the mine" itself picks nothing and opens nothing (it lets go of what was picked); hovering it lights nothing and changes no cursor. Only people, the grave (at night), buildings with a screen (town), HUD controls, and places while a move or a card is being aimed, react.
- For the GL frame (ENGINE-GL) to mirror: Sensed figures at alpha `min(1, seen × 1.5) × √sink` (mine) / `min(1, k × 1.5)` (town), never 0.7; people sprites never drawn with `mono`; the people's own selected / hovered rings are not drawn (the scene's ground overlay draws them); the overlay seam 'ground' (post-fog) must run in the GL frame.

## 91. One pointer conversion; the hit box is the drawn figure (owner, PLAY-5, 2026-11-24)
Owner: "it's merely that the hitbox is off 99%" · "or just the hitbox is bad". Reproduced in the owner's pane (1133×620 CSS px, devicePixelRatio 1.875, canvas 1644×925, render scale 1.492) with `e2e/click-everyone.cjs`, which now takes every mine figure's body from its sprite's opaque pixels as the renderer blitted it, and with `?hitboxes=1`.
- **Which it was.** The pointer conversion was right at every density (the cross where the game reads the pointer sits on the mouse at dpr 1, 1.5 and 1.875; the backing store's size never entered it). The hit SHAPE was wrong: the box was 16 × 33 mine units centred on the feet, while a mine figure fills −10…+19 × −41…+3 towards the side it faces (the pick held out in front; the head and wick lean that way too), a walker is blitted where its sprite was last repainted (up to 0.2 s behind its live position) and the box followed the live one, and the smallest box was 28 logical px — 19 CSS px in the owner's pane. A click on the drawn head, pick or forward half missed. (In the Day every click was ignored before §79.)
- **One conversion** (`src/app/pointer.ts`, `stagePointer(canvas)`): client CSS px → the canvas' own pixels (its real rectangle, `getBoundingClientRect`, and its backing size, `canvas.width/height`) → logical px through the drawing transform (`canvas.width / 1600`). Never `devicePixelRatio`. The rectangle is cached and re-read on the app's layout pass, window resize and scroll, the visual viewport, a resize of the canvas, and at the start of every press (a host page can move the game without resizing it). Everything goes through it: the app's pointer, wheel and click (picking, hover, send/move and card targets, the grave, the drag-pan anchor, zoom about the cursor), the town stage's window listeners (figures, buildings, crowds, its pan and zoom), the coach marks and the aim chip (`toClient`), and the pinch. The HUD's scale (§44, `--hgk` zoom on the plates) never touches the canvas or its rectangle.
- **The hit box is the drawn figure** (`PlayWorld.bodyBox`): measured from the renderer's own sprites — standing x −10…+19 mine units (mirrored when facing left) × y −41…+3, walking ±15, lying ±21 × −15…+3, all × the figure's look.scale — at the sprite's blit position (plus a shake offset). Three tiers in the order a click takes them: the person's own body (±9 units round the feet, head to feet), front one first; then the whole drawn figure (their pick), front first; then slack round a small figure, nearest first. The slack box is never under 28 logical px nor under 24 CSS px on the player's screen (`PICK_MIN_CSS`), in the mine and in the town (`ShadeStage.figureBoxes`).
- `e2e/click-everyone.cjs` runs at 1600×900 @1, 1280×800 @1.5 (16:10) and 1133×620 @1.875 and fails on any miss, phantom or console error.

## 92. The prebuilt ground is keyed by data; its drawing code is guarded by a bake run (BAKE)
- **`manifest.key`** is the hash of the terrain spec JSON alone (`prebuiltKey`, `src/render/mine/prebuilt.ts`). The game accepts the asset only when it equals the key of the live spec; the bake writes the same function's value. The terrain's source text is no longer in it (the browser's stored-ground cache in `groundCache.ts` still adds it: that cache outlives a deploy).
- **`manifest.inputs`** is the hash of the spec's plain-data sources (`prebuiltInputs`: the layout tables and the frozen ground light rig). `tests/mine-ground/prebuilt.test.ts` compares it in node. The full spec cannot be computed in node, because the geometry rasterises on a canvas.
- **Not keyed:** the terrain's drawing code (`src/render/terrain/**`) and the geometry / dressing algorithms. Their output needs a browser, so the guard for a change to what they draw is running `scripts/mine-ground-bake.cjs` and looking at the image. A comment edit or an export deletion there needs nothing.

## 93. One clock for the day, night and animation (ONE SOURCE)
- **The hour is one number** (`src/sim/clock.ts`, pure): the night is the player's turn at `NIGHT_HOUR` (0.92); a Day is the replay of the turn's events at progress `p`, hour `DAY_FROM + DAY_SPAN * p` (0.22 + 0.62 p: dawn to dusk); `hourOf(p | null)`. Hours run 0 midnight, .25 sunrise, .5 noon, .75 sunset. `daylightOf(hour)` is the one daylight curve (0 night … 1 day; the sun's height eased: .198 at sunrise and sunset).
- **Every painter of light reads it.** The play scene gives the hour once per frame (`WorldView.dayT`, eased toward `hourOf` so dawn and dusk do not jump); `WorldComposite` hands it to the mine, the town (`setDaylight(daylightOf)`), the land and the seams; the mine's night (`nightK = 1 - daylightOf(hour)`, the banked torches of `NIGHT_BURN`) and the sky down the shaft follow it. A view without an hour is at night. The ambient, the weather and the town stage keep no clock of their own.
- **One render clock** (`src/render/clock.ts`): game seconds, ticked once per frame by `App.step`, frozen while paused, stepped by `App.advance`. What animates or times an effect reads it; frame budgets and the profiler read the wall.

## 94. A new run starts at the shard's fall (INTRO)
- **Owner order:** remove the new-run animation and jump straight to the part where a crystal shard falls and the first upgrade is picked.
- **Reading (reversible):** the opening is the fall (0 to 7.2 s) and the landing in the real mine renderer (to 15.9 s), then the starting-bonus pick on that last frame. The memory and Flame beats, the grave and settle beats, the Hunger line and the title's push into the eye are deleted. The one line left is "I broke. The dark kept what was left." The music bed (`cutscene`, 24 s) and the sound cues run on the same clock; the title's Begin fades to black into it. "Skip the opening" in the settings still starts a run at the pick.

## 95. The lit mine is baked offline and shipped; the boot lights nothing (PERF)
- **Owner order:** "Why is it low res as we're loading in by the way. Things should load essentially instantaneously."
- **The lit layer's mip 0 and the night ratio ship** (`src/render/mine/prebuilt/lit-1.webp`, `night-1.png`, `scripts/mine-lit-bake.cjs`, `docs/art-pipeline.md` Mine lit). The boot decodes them off the main thread and installs them; the quarter-resolution stand-in, the runtime CPU bake, its worker and the first-night ratio bake are deleted. The mine is at full resolution on its first frame.
- **The night ratio holds no zone flags:** it is the fresh run's night. A save resumed with a bloomed or desecrated zone shows that zone's day light changed (a region re-light) and its night ratio unchanged.
- **A mine with no bake of its key stops the load** with an error naming the bake (the key, `mineLitKey`, is data: ground key, placement, lights with their evaluated bases, ambients, constants, `?crystals=2`, `?props11=1`). A flag that re-dresses the albedo is its own bake (`--query`); only the default mine is baked. The dev harness's light tuning (`dev/mine.html` `&lgain=`, `&kt=`, `&ground=`) changes the key, so it needs a bake to be seen.

## 96. The game's switches are its default path (CUTS)
- **Rule:** the path the game runs by default is the only path; the other side of a switch is deleted with its code.
- **Perf caches are the only path:** the sprite atlas, the figure cache, the silhouette tier, the far-zoom speck passes, the pre-sized glows, the valley's worker bake and the weather.
- **Deleted:** the sharp ground pass and the engine's ground-detail seam (off by default), the cutscene start-time switch, the miss and overlay-time recorders, the pick-box overlay, and the `hud` and `townplan` dev sheets.
- **`?fast=1` stays** as a permanent dev switch: `e2e/playtest/shots.cjs --smoke` loads with it.

## 97. The mine's people are baked cut-out parts placed on the animator's bones (BAKE)
- **Owner orders:** nothing draws live, only assets; a bake takes seconds to minutes on the GPU; the people look as the painter drew them (a bake with a visible change was rejected); level of detail holds at the closest zoom.
- **Parts, not frames.** The animator solves the skeleton each frame as before; `src/render/people/cutout.ts` lays the figure out as about 30 rigid parts on it, and each part places the baked variant nearest its bins (`docs/art-pipeline.md`, People). The motion is the animator's own at any frame rate, and the bake is every variant once: 11 456 for the cast, in 12 s on the game VM: each variant is painted into a box measured from the painter's commands, one readback per 4096 px paint page.
- **The light is a multiply.** A figure on the view is lit by multiplying its parts by the light where it stands (`lightMultiply`); the same pose painted and baked, lit, matches.
- **No part is magnified.** `lo` is painted at 1.6 px per rig unit (the painter's own low-detail threshold, below which it is shown); `hi` at 5 px per world unit (the closest zoom on a 2x display).
- **Low detail at boot, full detail per look.** The manifest and every look's `lo` atlas (about 3 MB) load while the mine is built, so no one pops in; a look's `hi` atlas (309 to 478 kB) is fetched the first time it is wanted, `lo` showing meanwhile.
- **One paint stands for devotion.** A follower differs from the neutral figure in the wick, the wick-cap and the tell only, so each part that differs is baked in two paints and fades between them.

## 100. Every opposition move is one threat kind in one table (SHRINK, rb-content)

- **Fourteen moves, one table.** Council: inspection, crackdown, lockdown, writ, levy. Temple: vigil, inquisitor, purge, ward, crusade. Public: bolted doors, mob, riot, burning (`ThreatKind`, `src/content/threats.ts`). A move is called when its power's heat crosses into a level, or announced once heat passes its floor, or both; either way it walks the world and lands unless answered.
- **Merged:** the Rite of Cleansing (level 3) and the Naming (announced) are one move, the purge. The Kindling (level 4) and the crusade are one move, the crusade.
- **Not threats:** searches, guards, occupation, rumours and informers are steady pressure from the powers' heat that the rules apply daily. Nothing announces them and no answer blunts them.

## 103. The moves on the live clock (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **When.** A power's crossing into a level calls that level's move at once (vigil and bolted doors, with no answer, land then); every other move lands at the next dusk, the warning day. At dusk, due moves land, then each power not resting and with nothing pending may announce one move, `warn` days off (`warnHostile` from 75). In the mine the Council announces an inspection (a crackdown from its floor) by `inspectChance` instead of by weight. The crusade also gathers when the god reaches `crusadeLevel` in Shade, `crusadeWarn` days off. `Faction.called` remembers the highest level whose move was called; it falls with the level, so a power that cools re-arms.
- **Answers in one action** (`answer {threat, with}`, 1 action point): an item whose kind answers it; `favour`, the most devoted turned Elite of that power with the favour ready (every move of the power fails, its lockdown or crusade ends, the track is blind `FAVOUR.nights`, the Elite loses `FAVOUR.devotion`, the favour returns after `FAVOUR.cooldown` days); `hide`, which lays low every follower the move falls on for their Direct Nether each. The power's act of evil blunts all its moves. Cooling below the floor, the agent turned, or a follower standing watch on the target blunt a move as it lands.
- **The agent** is a stranger who watches for the power on this stage's side (the mob: up to three strangers where it lands); the world walks them to the target.
- **Dropped:** the vigil's place nearness (the vigil is the Temple level in the resistance rule); the Magistrate tearing the writ and the burning's lost craft wait for the town; the inquisitor no longer reaches the grave (the inspection's walk does).

## 104. The day's doors (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **One reader.** `door(s, kind, on)` in the sim's core folds the weather now, the weekday (a cart day's count too) and the world's events' effects in `s.effects`. Strike, search, countTolerance, notice and crowd multiply; guards, whisper, whisperZone and reach add. Rules work on areas: a door on a place opens its whole area.
- **Where each is read.** Strike: a swing's chance at the digger's face. Search: each search at the lift (the lift and the searched one's work) and the inspection's catch. countTolerance: the count's forgiveness. Notice: every heat gain of that power. Whisper (on a person) adds to their odds; whisperZone adds the strongest of where they stand, work and live. Reach at 1 or more carries whispers a second area out (the wind, the bells). Guards push colour back at dawn as that many watchers in the area (fewer when negative). Crowd scales the strangers an area needs to hold a crowd.
- **Not the rules':** crew (who comes to work) and the lift's stopping are the world's: it keeps the absent home and the shift below when the lift stops. A stopped lift at shift end holds no count and no searches.

## 105. Story events on the live clock (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **When.** Each dawn, with `eventChance`, up to three draws by weight among the stage's stories not resting; the first whose cast is found is told. Telling rests it for its cooldown (`used` on `story:<id>`; once a run: for good), says its body, and either happens at once (then its outcome) or waits as the pending story; the clock holds until the player answers (`story {option}`), then the option happens and its outcome is told.
- **Costs come from the pouch** (cheapest stones first) and are refused while the pouch is short: the rows' cure and the other paid options cannot be chosen without the stones.
- **Readings.** "Resists −n" lowers resistance by n (never below 0); grieving sets the Grieving trait; a death with "a crystal from the soul" leaves a stone in the pouch; a cave-in closes its area's work (`crew` 0) for its days; venting Malice is the act of evil's relief.
- **Waits for the world:** fresh hands (a newcomer needs the world to make a person).

## 106. Discoveries on the live clock (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **When.** What an area hides (content's DISCOVERIES) is found once a run (`PlaceState.found`): at dawn in the area each follower works in and in every area that has taken root. Finding it explores it. The grave starts found.
- **What each does.** A cache: its `crystals` into the pouch, each rolled with `cacheLuck` (the rows count the stones found). A seam: one more seam in the area's vein (`seamBonus` on its face). A danger: its power's heat, once. A relic: its miracle into the hand; while the hand is full the relic stays unfound and waits. Each finding says the area's `discovery.<area>.text` as a `found` event.
- **The loner and the shortcut.** A loner is made by the world where they hide (`World.spawn`, named by `discovery.<area>.loner`, `lonerResistance`). A found shortcut's edge joins the area graph: the graph a state reads is the base graph plus its found shortcuts (`world/graph.ts`), for routes, neighbours, reach and seeing.
- **Dropped:** a relic leading the next deal when the hand is full.

## 107. The HUD is DOM over baked ui cells (SHRINK, rb-hud)

- **DOM, not Pixi.** Each HUD part is a DOM box whose background is a cell of the baked ui atlas (`assets/ui`); surfaces are nine-slices of it. Words and numbers are DOM text in the bundled IM Fell (numbers in the plain serif): Pixi text would paint glyphs at run time and upload a texture on every change. A change writes one style or text node; at rest the HUD writes nothing (`e2e/perf-ui.cjs PAGE=rebuild`).
- **One scale.** Each corner is an anchor 24 px in from the edge; every anchor scales by `min(w/1600, h/900)` times the Interface size (small 0.85, medium 1, large 1.15); parts are laid out in 1600x900 design px.
- **The clock** (bottom right, in End Night's place): the sun's dial by the whole hour from dawn to night, the moon by night; the day and weekday; the weather (none when clear); action points as lit shards over the day's most; a click or Space pauses, and a veil says so.
- **The god** (top left): the grave crystal's fill is 1 − Hunger/100 in 20 steps; rank lights `min(level, 5)` facets; Hunger and Malice grooves notched at their state thresholds; Ichor of its most; Nether and its night's income. The goal under it goes to the pit head, the vault, or the stats menu for a rank.
- **The hand** (bottom centre, over the deal's veil so a full hand can discard): the panels' card plates at 0.92, shrunk as one to fit the 876 px between the pouch and the clock; hover lifts a card 34 px at 1.2×. An act of evil carries the flame of the power it heats and, past Malice 40, its multiplier; after the first card of a dusk each card carries the Nether fee, and one the god cannot pay rests dim. A drag onto the field or 1..9 opens the panels' pay slab (§116).
- **The deal** waits until no panel and no menu is open, then lays its cards at 1.5× over a dimmed world: 1..3 or a click takes one (it flies to the hand), Esc or 0 or Take none declines, Redraw shows while rerolls remain. A full hand says so and refuses a take.
- **The feed** is one note under the goal, never a list: a note stands 3.2, 4.6 or 6.5 s by weight (a life or day line, a gain or loss, a person, a power or a find); at most three wait, a light one is dropped while others wait; a click looks where it happened. Notes stand there, not in the world, until the view gives the HUD a world-to-screen anchor. Under the powers, a strip per announced move with its hours to land, red from 3.
- **Tooltips** are one tip for every `title` in the interface: two lines after 0.35 s, the rest (Details) with Shift held or after 1.6 s; over the world a person or place shows its name only.
- **Hints** (Settings, on by default) teach one thing at a time beside it, for 14 s at most, gone for the run once done or clicked: whisper, actions refilling, a follower's offering, the hand, the goal (days 1 to 3), and Hunger past starving.
- **Menus**: the stats menu hangs under the god's corner (each night, your people, rank, the powers); the characters menu lists yours, those walking against you, and the masters and Elites; settings holds the four volumes, the size and the hints, opened by Esc or the cog left of the powers.

## 109. The land and the fog are baked fields the view reads (SHRINK, rb-bake)

- **The land** is the old Valley's bake, run once offline in its own worker from the town's gate and geometry (seed 1): `assets/valley/far` (the whole land at 0.1 px per unit), `n00`..`n11` (the near ring at 0.25, 2 x 2 tiles cut from one picture, each reaching 32 units into its neighbours) and `mask`, the known-ground mask's static fields as a table (block, edge fade, town cut, distance past the floor, far weight, the road's distance and place along it), with the live lights' places. The hour's light, the flicker and the mask itself are the view's, from the town blocks' fog.
- **The fog** of each place (`assets/fog/mine`, `assets/fog/town`) is the old fog prep's static fields as a table, one texel per fog cell: each cell's three strongest zones with their weights and creep orders, for colour and for seen; the haze's noises; the core zone, the map art and the haze weight (the town's light reach: its cover is one less it). Zone states, fades, the haze's drift and the stain's front are the view's.
- **The stain's order is the view's**, because its seeds are the world's (mouths shared with held zones, the followers' places): it grows over the zone's membership from them with the step cost noise and grain baked in `t`. One noise serves every zone of a place (the old mine drew one per zone); the town's is its own, with its streets in `t` B, where the stain runs at 0.7 the cost. Where the seeds sit is baked (`meets`): where each neighbour's colour enters a zone (the mine's tunnel mouths, the town's street mouths with their head start, the pit for the lift) and where a zone starts with no neighbour.

## 110. The town's buildings (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **The taking rule** (docs/town.md §3). A town building is yours while its master is yours and more than half of those who work there are (with nobody else working there, the master alone). Its hands are the people whose work it is, not Elites and not the trades of a place who do not work there (`TOWN.notWorkers`). A building nobody masters, or whose master is gone, is yours while its area has taken root. Nothing stores it: it is read from the people each time it is asked.
- **Benches.** A bench (content's PLACES `facility`) is open while a building that gives it is yours. `Query.facilities` says, for each closed bench, what each of its buildings still needs: its master and hands to turn, or its ground to root.
- **Told.** A turning, a lapse or a loss that moves a mastered building says so (`building`); rooting or losing an area says it for the buildings there nobody masters. A building left masterless goes with its ground unsaid.

## 111. Made things on the live clock (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **Making.** `forge {crystal, form}` at the jewelry bench and `craft {facility, crystal}` at the others fold a pouch stone into a thing at hand (`Crystal.at {item}`), for `ITEM.makeCost` by the stone's grade (jewelry at `metalForgeCost` with the smelter yours); no action point.
- **Worn** (`give` to a follower; `takeBack` returns it to hand; both free): each line of content's ITEMS row is `value + perGrade × grade step`; a jewel adds its stone's person lines (`MOD_POWER`). Strike luck and rate on their swings, whisper help as the strongest among your followers standing where the target is, Devotion on the day's devotion, guard blindness (at most `blindMax`) and the second pocket (a search finds half) on searches, false weights summed into the count's tolerance. A draught or brew is used up `draught` days after it is given.
- **Given to anyone not yours** it is its row's curse and is used up: resistance, sickness, a power's heat, an Elite's own power cooling, tonight's whisper odds, on the one, their workmates, household or home.
- **Proof.** A search that finds one of yours wearing a thing raises the Council's heat by `exposure × exposureHeat` for each (1 + grade step, + `curseExposure` unless a clean jewel), loses them (a relic found costs `relicIchor`), and the wearer is taken. A follower who lapses or is lost loses what they wear.
- **The vault.** `openVault` in Shade once the council hall is yours: an Impossible stone and `vaultRoyal` Royal ones to the pouch, `vaultCouncil` Council heat, and the run is won.
- **Giving.** A follower wears one thing (an Elite two). A curse goes by the hand of a follower who lives, works or stands in the area beside the one it is for (`give`'s `by` names that hand).
- **The re-cut** (gem workshop, §72): `facet` pays `facetCost` by grade (half with Gemcutter) and offers three cuts as the pending choice (a gentle and a bold reshape of each line ×0.8–1.4, and one that drops a line for ×1.5 on the rest); `chooseCut` makes the stone that cut. The set always differs from the stone's last offered set and from the stone.
- **Profaned.** An act of evil profanes the hallowed Flame stones where it is done (the grave's sockets and pouch, or the pockets of those in its area): no more price or quarrel; the Temple `PROFANE.heat` a stone, at most `heatCap`.
- **Noticed.** An informer heard out (`informerNotice`) and the Inquisitor's song (`hunterNotice`) notice each of yours there who wears a thing with those odds × (1 − Cunning/200): proof, as a search's.

## 112. The panels (SHRINK, rb-panels)

- **What opens over the rest layout** (`src/ui/panels`): the title, the starting gift, the blessing, story and cut picks, paying a miracle, the dusk reveal and ledger, the person slab, the grave, a building's screen, the defeat and the ending. Each is DOM over baked cells (#107), says what it shows as one key and is built again only when the key moves. The first open panel in the modal order answers the keys; a key syncs every panel's key with the state first, because an intent acts at once.
- **The clock holds on a story.** The pump stops while any choice is pending and the sim refuses every other action, so a story is answered where it opens: the old "put aside" had nothing left to do while it waited.
- **The starting gift is the first deal** (rb-sim's bonus phase): take one of the three cards on the opening's last frame.
- **A building's gift** (rumour, debt, food, records, guards; docs/town.md §3) is content's PLACES `gift`, used as `useGift` with balance's `GIFTS`: the screen offers each pick it needs (a power, one who owes, how, when, where), the first taken to start with, and the sim's check says why it is refused.
- **Dusk.** Each stone brought is shown in turn, the rarer the longer; the reveal moves on to the ledger 1.4 s after its last stone (2.4 s with several), the ledger after 15 s unless the pointer is on it. Enter hurries, Escape closes.
- **The defeat** drains the world to grey and darkens it over the HUD (a compositor veil, nothing painted) under the cause, the run's lines and Again, Same seed, Title.

## 113. Gifts, goings and the group whisper on the live clock (SHRINK, rb-sim)

- **A building's gift in the sim** (`useGift`, balance's `GIFTS`): 1 action point and the gift's Nether, refused while no building giving it is yours or while it rests (`used` on `gift:<g>` for `rest` days). Rumour: one power −`cool`, and a rumour tavern's regulars cost `regularsCost` of a whisper's Nether. Debt: one tied to a debtor by `owes` resists `resist`, Council +`council`. Food: laid for a town area 1..`days` dawns ahead, one per area a day; at that dawn, blessed, yours who live there gain `devotion`; poisoned, the rest are sick `sick` days, `deaths` of them die, the town +`public`. Records: a seal must stand; −`seals`, Council +`council`. Guards: no searches until the count, and the watch leaves the named area's colour alone at the next dawn.
- **The hearth** (a home's standing gift): in the town, each household more than half yours adds `GIFTS.hearth.hold` to its home's area at dawn. `Query.hearths` names those homes.
- **Standing boons** (content's PLACES `boon`): each link of the crystals' road of yours (the haul yard, the stables, the cart shed) adds `GIFTS.road.tithe` rolls to the first crew's tithe; with the school yours, in the town, every household with a child there is in reach.
- **The town's tithe** (Shade, at shift end; `tithe.ts`): your crews in the town (by work place, not lying low) skim ceil(n × `titheTown`) rolls, at most `titheMaxPerGroup`; each roll brings a stone with `titheChance` × (1 + strike rate) into the carrier's pocket (the crew's first; their worn strike luck and rate count). The tally house counts the tithe's worth against its tolerance (×`overseerTolerance` with the tally house yours); on a search day the watch searches one row or one cart line where carriers are (none where the guards look away); what is not found reaches the grave (`tithe`). In Shade the lift holds no count and no searches: the Council's eyes are on the town.
- **Burned.** The purge, the crusade's torches and the assassin's work burn a person (`gone: 'burned'`): their ties notice it as a taking (they soften toward you; yours among them feed Malice), and a follower burned feeds Malice as one taken.
- **Fresh hands** come while fewer than `maxShare` (half) of everyone who ever worked the mine are there and not yours: the mine running short of free hands, as 14 of the first mine's 29 meant.
- **The group whisper** (`whisperGroup`, Shade only): a household, a crew of one shift, a chapel's flock or the watch, while gathered (`World.together`). Those of it there, not yours, not whispered today and of a standing the stage reaches are one target: the first of them, resisting as their mean plus `groupResist` (at most `groupResistCap`), of their commonest trait, at a mortal's tier with the open headcount as its size. One roll turns them all or softens each; in reach when the place is or one of them is tied to a follower.
- **What the screens read** (`Query`): `odds` gives a whisper's odds with one reason a term (the formula's terms, then each piece of its bonus by name); `worth` a stone's burn worth for a card and the Hunger eating it takes; `storySlots` the pending story's numbers, who and where; `groups` a person's groups. `GameState.tally` keeps the run's whispers, everyone ever turned and the most followers at once. The grave's seep is told as a `seep` stone at dusk.

## 114. What the old live painters drew is baked as cells and placement data (SHRINK, rb-bake)

- **A shape that recurs is one cell; where it stands is data.** A bake keeps the old painter's numbers and writes the places, timings and tints it used into its manifest, so the view places sprites and never paints. A picture the size of the screen is baked only where nothing smaller can stand for it.
- **The card clips' veins** (`assets/fxmarks/veins`) are the old effects' own networks (makeVeins with each kind's numbers, one fixed seed a network), each grown over 8 frames to its full reach, white inked black for the clip to tint; a racing tip's glow is a point a frame (`tips`), not pixels. Each play shows the same network: the old drew a fresh one per event. The vein sight's wide network was 1 px lines at every zoom, so it is baked at that density (`assets/fxmarks/hair`, band limit 2). The upright marks are the old sigils at the weight they had at their small size (white over black ink), the eye pair as luminance and the prism in its own colours.
- **The shaft's walls** (`assets/shaft`) are world-anchored, log-spaced rings in mouth units, so they are a ring table plus a wobbly disc a ring, stacked outermost first, whose rim is the next ring's ledge ink; three wobble variants serve every ring. The shadowed half is one half plane over them all; cracks, slats and rails are capsules from the table. Its lines grow with the ring under the camera (the old drew them at 1.3 screen px).
- **Beacons** (`assets/beacons`) stay screen-sized: the bloom's two-colour gradient is the sum of two cells the view tints and adds, the rings one flattened ellipse at a ladder of line weights.
- **The warded zones' censers** are points in `assets/fog/mine` (`censers`, `chambers`), each pool's radius kept off the rock where the old pools were clamped; the pools are the life atlas's glows.
- **The title's veil** round the grave is a radial ramp in five breath steps (`assets/title` `veil.*`).

## 115. The held day and the keys (FEEL)

- **Holding the clock 1 s ends the day** (`Query.dayEnd`, the `endDay` intent): by day (dawn to dusk) the clock runs at once to dusk, so the offering and the ledger follow; by dusk and night it runs to the next dawn. It stops early where anything holds the clock (a choice, a film, a phase).
- **Every key the game reads is a bind** (`src/content/keys.ts`): pan and menu up, down, left, right; zoom in and out; hold to skip a film; confirm; back; stats; characters; same seed again; tooltip details; pick one to five; the profiler. Each holds up to two keys, saved with the settings; the Keys tab rebinds one with a click and the next key pressed. Words naming a key name the bound one.
- **Main menu** (settings, in a run) leaves for the title; the dawn save stays, so Continue resumes from the day's dawn.
- **Tooltips** show after 0.1 s; the HUD's own buttons (cog, god's corner, pouch, clock) have none; the world's names show only while the pointer is over the world.

## 116. A card is played or discarded by dragging it (DISCARD)

- **No Discard on a card, no play on a click.** A press on a hand card that moves 5 screen px becomes a drag (mouse, pen or touch): the card follows the pointer, and the discard zone (190x340 design px, the ui atlas's violet surface, "Discard" at its head) shows at the screen's right edge, its foot 36 px above the bottom-right panel; it lights (brighter, a violet glow, as a hovered card glows) while the pointer is over it. The drop decides by the pointer: on the zone it sends `discard`; on the hand's place (its cards' box when the drag began) the card returns and nothing happens; anywhere else, the field, it plays as 1..9 does (the pay slab, or a shake when it cannot). The drag works during the deal too, so a full hand can make room.

## 117. The game is the mine (OVERHAUL, ov-cut)

- **The town and every stage above the mine are cut.** The run has one stage; `state.stage`, the watershed and ending films, the town map, the valley, the surface, the gray town tiles and the town's words and assets are gone.
- **The run is won at rank 4 with the lift rooted.** The god's rank is capped at `BALANCE.whisperLevelCap` until the lift (the gate area) is Hold 100 (`flags.liftRooted`), then at `BALANCE.takenLevel`; at rank 4 with the lift rooted the next dawn ends the run won (`end(c, true, 'taken')`). Phase `won` shows the Ending card in `panels/front.ts`, no film.
- **Everyone sleeps in the bunks, at night only** (`Person.home = 'bunks'`): from the evening until the dawn; by day, dawn to dusk, nobody lies asleep (the day is the player's, played on foot), rest days included, and there is no night shift (`RoleDef.shift`, `nightShiftShare` and the night hands' plan are gone; whispers at night reach sleepers, `sleeperBonus`). The feed's life notes fall in the shift's hours (`FEED.to` 12), when someone is at work. The watch is all up at the dawn count, where only the night hands (30%) and no sleeper pushed back before: `watcherPush` 3 to 1 keeps the dawn's pushback as it was (the bot's run still roots the lift and wins). No households, groups, crews or elites in reach: a whisper reaches one mortal (`standingOf(p) === 'mortal'`), the answer to a threat is hiding only, lockdown lands at once, the lift is a guard post (no ride).
- **The third weekday is the cart day** (the load is weighed twice, carts leave from the lift); it was the market day.
- **Items, gifts, facets, the writ, the levy, the ward and the burning are gone** with the town; a power's set bonuses live in `content/crystals.ts`.
- **A life note is drawn from people who are doing something** (not asleep, not walking), so a day's feed always reaches its 8 to 16 notes.

## 118. No pause, the panels moved (HUD, owner o-922289)

- **No pausing.** Time that is passing (§119) runs whenever a run is live and nothing holds it (a film, a pending choice, the title, a phase but `live`); an open menu, a hidden tab or a key never stops it. Space only holds to skip a film (the `skip` bind); Escape still skips at once. Render time (`vm.time`) never freezes, so the tooltips' delay and the clock's hold time by it.
- **The rest layout** is §123's.

## 119. The night is the turn, the day passes in 3 minutes (TIME, owners o-bca6ec13 and o-e9914f)

- **Action caps are back** ("Put action caps back"): the action points, `apMax` a day (3 plus one a rank), as before §118 took them; Quickening, Restless and the action-point stone lines return. They refill at the dusk, whole, not over the day.
- **No live time.** The sim's clock stands (`GameState.until`; `step` does nothing at it) while the player's turn is on. A run waits at tick 0 (18:00) on the first card and opens at the first dawn when it is taken (§120). The night is dusk to dawn, 550 ticks (`NIGHT_TICKS`).
- **An action fast-forwards its share of the night.** Each action point an action spends moves `until` on so that the night has run `spent / apMax` of its length: three points, a third each; the last reaches dawn. An action that spends none (a card, a stone, an item, a free whisper) moves nothing. The share is `nightUntil` in `src/sim/index.ts`, a function of the points left, so queued actions add up and extra points (Quickening) cost no time until the count is under `apMax`.
- **Holding the clock sleeps**: the `sleep` action takes the rest of the night to dawn (1 s hold, as before).
- **Random events roll in the night's ticks**: a story roll each hour of the night, `nightEventChance` 0.05, beside the dawn's `eventChance` 0.6; each roll is the dawn's rule (`storyRoll`). A choice stops the clock as at dawn. The world's events and the powers' moves roll as the ticks pass.
- **The day passes by itself** (dawn sets `until` to the dusk) and nothing may be done in it but what §120 gives the host (`why.day`, a pending choice excepted). The pump plays the day's 650 ticks in `DAY_SECONDS` = 180 real seconds (3 minutes, the overhaul's day; the turn-based archive's 6 s is replaced), and a night's actions at their own pace, `NIGHT_SECONDS` = 5 for the whole night (a third of the night about 1.7 s), `playTps` in `src/content/clock.ts`. The day card shows at dusk.
- **The day's card** is dusk's ledger with what the day's notes said (the day's noted events but the weather and the day's own line, the last five).
- A save is the dawn's (a resumed run plays its day); the shape's version is 4 (§120).
- **The hand rests by day.** The sim refuses every card action by day (`why.day`, 'Act at dusk'), so the hand (`src/ui/hud/cards.ts`) asks the sim's `check` of a discard whether a card may move (`handIdle`): when it may not, every plate rests dim and says the sim's reason, no card can be dragged or keyed, and its tip puts the reason second. The 'play a card' hint is a night's. At night a card dragged onto the discard zone is discarded and one dragged onto the field is played through the pay slab.

## 120. The god in a host: stats, the day as a possessed follower, marks, the fall (OVERHAUL, ov-core; owners o-272172 o-07b7db o-ffdf62 o-406fcc o-a62b94)

- **The stats.** Every person has Devotion and Resistance (0..100; Resistance stays `Person.resistance`, Devotion in `stats`), Dexterity, Intelligence, Strength, Endurance (1..20, 10 on average) and HP (`stats.hp`). Numbers: `src/content/stats.ts` (`STATS`), the trades' bias in `ROLES[...].bias` (strength, dexterity, intelligence, endurance). A body stat is the roll 5..15 plus the trade's bias, held to 1..20. Max HP = `hpBase` 25 + 2 × Strength + 4 × Endurance (85 at 10 and 10); each hour the living mend `regenPerEndurance` 0.2 × Endurance (`maxHp` in `sim/formulas.ts`, `mend` in `sim/host.ts`). Dexterity's work (movement, mining speed), Strength's damage and Devotion's rarity are the later items' (ov-mine, ov-combat).
- **What the old stats became** (a rule that read one reads its nearest): Strength (0..100, 50 average) → Strength (1..20, 10): the strike chance reads `1 + (Strength − 10) / strengthSpan` (span 250 → 50, the same spread); `strongest` reads Strength. Cunning (0..100) → Intelligence: a carrier's search chance `× (1 − Intelligence / searchWit)` (÷300 → ÷60), an offering's spotting `× (1 − Intelligence / offerWit)` (÷200 → ÷40), the overseer's ledger story `minCunning` 55 → `minIntelligence` 13; each is the old at the average hand. Health (70..100) → HP: the Inquisition's tally, the rail accident (never below 1), the mob's beating (`BALANCE.mobHarm`) and the weakest dying in the tunnels all go through `hurt` or read `hp`. The old Intelligence was read by nothing and is the new one. Devotion and Resistance are unchanged.
- **The run opens at the first dawn.** The take of the starting gift is the god's waking: the clock goes to the first dawn (`NIGHT_TICKS`), the dawn's rules run, and the first miner (`FIRST_MINER`, `g01` Mara, the digger at the grave) is possessed. There is no first night and no Nether before the first dusk.
- **A day is played as a host** (`GameState.host`). At each dawn the god possesses one: the living followers and the first miner, who is always on the list (`Query.hosts`). With only him on it the choice is made; else the clock waits (`Query.picking`) for the `possess` action. At the dusk the possession ends (`release`) and the night is the god's, as §119. The day's clock is `DAY_SECONDS`. §125 holds that someone is always possessed.
- **Marks.** By day the host marks someone near them (the `mark` action; the mark key `E`, bound `mark`, marks the nearest): a person not yours, in the host's area, the host's own place first (`Query.near`; with WASD the distance replaces it). At most `HOST.marksPerDay` 3 a day; a mark holds `HOST.markDays` 3 days, the day it is made counted, so a whisper reaches them in that day's night and the two after (`Person.marked`, `Query.markLeft`). A whisper needs a mark (`why.notMarked`); a follower's routine and move do not.
- **The fall.** `hurt(c, p, n, floor)` is the one way HP is lost. A person at 0 HP dies (`remove(..., 'dead')`); the first miner only falls, and the first miner takes over at once from a host who fell (§125). The first miner is back whenever he is needed, free, at full HP at home, with `FIRST_MINER.stats` for good (weak: 6, 6, 8, 6 against 10 on average; nothing in the game grows a body stat yet). A fallen follower (dead, Devotion left) is revived at night by the `revive` action: `HOST.reviveStones` 3 stones from the pouch (the lowest grades), full HP, back at home, a follower again.
- **A save is shape version 4** (HP, the new stats, `host` and `marked` are in it); a save of another version is dropped. The opening dawn is a dawn like any: its Hunger, Malice and heat rules run, so the first night begins one dawn on from §119's.
- **The bots** pick the host standing among most strangers at the dawn, and each day hour the host marks the nearest in reach whose odds are fair; the numbers are not retuned (the targets are stale).

## 121. The mine on foot (OVERHAUL, ov-mine; owners o-272172 o-07b7db o-ffdf62 o-406fcc o-a62b94)

- **The body.** The possessed host has a body in the mine (`GameState.body`, mine units): where it stands, its heading, the distance walked, the ore it works, the day's tallies. The sim owns it and steps it each day tick (`sim/dig.ts`, `sim/walk.ts`); the `walk` action sets a heading (each of -1, 0, 1) that holds until the next; the view places the baked figure at the body (`ViewModel.hostAt`, the last tick's move shown `alpha` of the way) and the camera follows it, going in once to `HOST_ZOOM` as a day's host begins. WASD (the `up/down/left/right` binds) steer the host by day and pan the camera at night. The save is shape 5 (`body`, `ores`).
- **Walk speed** = `WALK.speed` 60 units a second at Dexterity 10, plus 5 % per point (48 for the first miner at 6). A tick of the played day is `DAY_TICK_S` = `DAY_SECONDS` / the day's ticks (0.277 s); every length of time here counts in it.
- **Hitboxes, one source each.** Walls: the fog table's walkable ground (assets/fog/mine table `o`, red), baked as `src/content/walk.json` (area index per 8-unit cell, 255 rock); the same table names the area under the body, and ground the host stands on is `explored` (the gray the fog and ov-ui read). Props and set pieces: `DRESS` (the grave's pit, the crystal heart's footprint, the shrine's fire, every lamp and brazier) and the lift's shaft. The feet are a circle of `WALK.bodyR` 5; a step meeting rock or a block slides along it. Set pieces with no position in any data (tables, desks, scaffolds, rails, crates) are in the lit picture only and do not stop the host.
- **Ores** are `ORE_SITES`: a seam in a wall face the view shows, the foot of the face nearest each dig spot of the map (`MINING.face`: within 120 units, a cell of the spot's own area's ground under rock that rises 6 cells straight up over it and its two neighbours, a north wall standing up the screen; 28 units clear of every light the lit picture paints, a crystal cluster, a torch or a lamp), 70 units apart (11 sites), tier = the face's depth (1 the upper galleries, 2 the lower workings and the grave, 3 the sump). A dig spot with no shown face in reach gives no ore. Every vein area has ore: the grave, where the first miner works, has two dig spots by its north walls. A mined ore is gone for the day (`GameState.ores` holds the day mined) and stands again from the next. Only a digger works ore (`why.notMiner`).
- **Mining** is a press, not a hold: the `work` key (`F`) beside an ore starts it and it runs while the host stands still; walking, or the ore going, ends it with nothing kept. Click-to-swing stays for ov-combat. Seconds = `MINING.seconds[tier]` (10, 18, 30) over the speed term `(Strength / 10 + Dexterity / 10) / 2`: 10 s for tier 1 at Strength 10 and Dexterity 10, 16.7 s for the first miner.
- **Grades** come from the ore's own table (`MINING.odds`, percent of clear / bright / blazing, dull the rest): tier 1 dull 90 (8.5 / 1.5 / 0), tier 2 dull 85 (12.5 / 2.4 / 0.1), tier 3 dull 80 (15 / 4.5 / 0.5). The grade is forced into the crystal (the rest of the crystal rolls as ever); its `oneIn` counts the grade's odds. Royal and impossible never come. Mining rarity is a person's own value from Devotion, `1 + 0.5 × Devotion / 100` (x1.5 at 100), and multiplies the non-dull odds (`miningRarity`, `oreOdds`).
- **The drop** is the lift's shaft, reached from 80 units. The work key there delivers the lowest stone of the pocket into the lift's cart (`mine.carts.lift`, counted at the count). A miner's day quota is `ROLES.quota` (digger 3 ores; other trades 0 until they have work to count). At the dusk a standing host's day is weighed: each ore of the quota not delivered heats the Public (Town) `POCKET.shortEach` 1.5, each stone pocketed over `POCKET.free` 3 heats it `POCKET.heatEach` 3; what is pocketed then reaches the pouch as tribute. The host digs by hand: the first miner's automatic swings are off.
- **Marks** reach the people within `HOST.markReach` 80 units of the body (each person's place is `xyOf`: their spot, or the point of their route the clock has reached); the mark key marks the nearest.
- **The bots** are carried to each ore, mine the quota and one over, deliver the quota and keep the rest, then are carried to each mark (they do not walk); numbers not retuned.

## 122. The fight (OVERHAUL, ov-combat; owners o-272172 o-07b7db o-ffdf62 o-406fcc o-a62b94)

- **The swing.** A click on a day's host swings the pick along the click (the unit vector from the host's feet to it; the `swing` action, one at a time: `why.swinging`). The swing is the baked clip's own: `COMBAT.host.swing` 0.62 s with its blow at `blow` 0.26 s, both fixed, Dexterity changing neither (the owner's answer). The blow lands on every skeleton within `reach` 34 units of the feet (plus the skeleton's body radius 6) and within the half-arc 60 degrees of the aim, a body at the feet always; damage = `perStrength` 2 × Strength (20 at 10, 12 for the first miner). A swing holds the feet (no walking while it runs) and ends any mining. The sim steps a tick of 0.277 s, so a blow lands on the tick that crosses its second and a swing frees the host on the tick that crosses its length (3 ticks, 0.83 s).
- **Skeletons** (`GameState.mobs`; `COMBAT.skeleton`, rules in `src/sim/fight.ts`) have only Hit Points and a blow: HP 30 + 5 per day after the first, damage 6 + 1.5 per day after the first (`mobHp`, `mobDamage`; day 1 is the first dawn's), both linear. They walk the host's ground and hitboxes at 40 units a second (the host's 60 at Dexterity 10), stop at 26 units and swing (0.95 s, blow at 0.399 s): the blow hurts the host (`hurt`, so a host at 0 HP falls per §120) if the host is still within 26 + 5. They hunt wherever the host is and do not dodge; walking away dodges a blow.
- **They come now and then.** While a host stands by day, each tick a skeleton arrives with chance 0.012 (about one in 23 s) while fewer than 3 stand, on clear ground 240..400 units from the host (out past the lit ground, up to 8 tries). One left more than 700 units behind, or walking 4 s without getting on (rock between it and the host: they do not path-find, they slide along what they meet), is lost in the dark; all are gone at the dusk and when the host falls. They draw from the run's one seeded stream.
- **A slain skeleton** lies 1.8 s (the baked `collapse`), and drops `drop` 2 Nether with chance 0.5 straight into the god's Nether (`nether.slain` in the day's feed).
- **The view.** Skeletons are people `Shown` with the look `s01` (`People.placeMob`; no one picks them); a swing plays `swing:h/u/d` by the aim from the sim's own seconds (so its blow and the damage are one moment); a blow plays the baked `hit-0..5` as clip `hit` at the struck body's chest (0.35 figure heights, bone, normal blend), and a slain skeleton's Nether the clip `drop`. The host the god is in is picked out with the selection ring, ticks and beam the mine already draws for the picked (nothing new baked or drawn live). Click is the swing while a host stands; at night it picks as ever. No sound is baked for a blow yet (`hit` and `slain` cue nothing).
- **The bots** fight what reaches them: a skeleton within reach of the body is swung at (the nearest), the swing waited out; nothing else changes (carried, not walking). The random bot never swings. The save is shape 6 (`mobs`, the body's swing and aim).

## 123. The rest layout (OVERHAUL, ov-layout; owner o-a62b94)

- **Four corners.** Bottom right the day and night clock (dial, day, weather, the night's actions; held, it skips to dawn). Bottom left the pouch with the people plate over it. Top right the cog, then the god's corner (Hunger, Malice, Ichor, Nether; a click opens the stats menu to its left), the three powers' suspicion as three grooves in a thin box under it, the goal strip, and the quests (§124). Top left the host's plate: face, name, trade, HP groove, Strength, Dexterity, Intelligence, Endurance, Devotion, Resistance; always shown once the run is on (§125).
- **The left column's events.** Under the host's plate the day's note line (always there, never over the world; a click looks at where it happened) and, under it, the strips for the moves a power has announced. A decision (a pending story) is the centre panel.
- **The host plate opens the host.** A click shows their panel (all seven stats, HP as `hp / max`) and a click while it is open closes it. By day that panel lists the others on the dawn's list, each a button: the `possess` action is allowed while another is hosting (only the one already hosted is refused, `why.hosting`), so the player may swap hosts through the day. By day the panel has no night acts.
- **The third power** reads `Crews` in the mine (the town is cut).

## 124. What the screen asks and shows: quests, marks, revival, the colour threshold (OVERHAUL, ov-ui; owners o-272172 o-07b7db o-ffdf62 o-406fcc o-a62b94)

- **The quests** (top right, under the goal) are up to three lines of words with no slab, tab or costs behind them, by day while the god is in a body: the trade's quota as ore delivered (`ROLES.quota`, a digger's 3; a trade with none says so), the stones in the pocket as `n of 3 free` (`POCKET.free`), and the marks made of the day's `HOST.marksPerDay`; a line whose number is met turns violet. At night there is nothing. The host's HP is the plate's groove (§123). No task the sim does not count is listed.
- **A mark shows on the person.** A violet diamond (the life atlas's `glyph-diamond-1.2`, added blended, pulsing) floats over the head for each day the mark has left (`Query.markLeft`: 3 the day it is made, then 2, 1), day and night alike; the person's panel says `Marked, n days left` as before. The person panel's day act for anyone not yours is the Mark plate (the mark key's name on its face, `Marked ·` once marked today, the sim's refusal under it otherwise); at night the same place holds Whisper. `Query.marksToday` is the day's count.
- **A fallen follower can be raised on the screen.** The characters menu gains a Fallen list (people who died with Devotion left: `roster().fallen`), each row a Revive plate with its cost (`HOST.reviveStones` stones from the pouch) and, when the `revive` action is refused (by day, too few stones), the sim's reason in its place; the person panel of a fallen follower has the same plate. The plate sends the `revive` action of §120.
- **Colour is a threshold, not Hold alone** (`src/sim/colour.ts`, content `COLOUR`). Gray (`explored`, Sensed) is ground the host has stood on (§121) or that lies beside colour; colour (`PlaceState.coloured`, what `active` and `visibility` read; never lost) comes only when the acts there cross the area's hidden number. Hold (the followers' stain, which still roots the lift and makes a place seen, `explored`) no longer colours and the fog has no stain share or shimmer: its worker works out only active and seen, and `assets/haze` is gone. An act is a stone struck there, a stone delivered at the lift, a person marked there, a skeleton slain there, a whisper to someone there (`PlaceState.acts`); they must fall on at least `COLOUR.days` 2 different days (`actDays`) and reach the area's number of acts: drawn once from `COLOUR.acts` 4..7 by the seed and the area (`keyed(seed, K.colour, idKey(area))`, no stream consumed, the same on every replay), times `COLOUR.ease` 0.7 for each coloured neighbour (never under 1). It is tested at each act and at each dawn (a neighbour that took colour may have brought another over). The player is never shown the number, the count or the days; the colour taking is a feed note (`Colour takes hold at ...`) and the fog fading in. The grave is coloured from the start, and so are the places next to it (Hold 12).
- **The save is shape 7** (`coloured`, `acts`, `actDays`, `lastAct` on every place).
- **Not retuned:** the colour numbers are a first draft. In the decent bot's runs (seeds 7, 8, 9, 25 days) 7 to 8 of the 12 areas are coloured by the end (the ring at the start, then the lift and the face with the most ores in 2 to 3 days, the quarters and the drift in about a week), the rest stay gray; the bots do not play for colour.

## 125. Someone is always possessed (OVERHAUL, ov-revise; owner o-a62b94)

- **A fallen host is replaced at once by the first miner.** When the host in a body falls, the same tick the first miner (back at full HP at home if he was the one down, free, with his own stats) is possessed in a new body (`relieve` in `sim/host.ts`), the skeletons are gone, and the day plays on at its own length; the dawn's pick list does not reopen. The tallies of the day's body (ore dug and delivered) start again with the new one. The first miner who falls as host is up again at once the same way. A follower who fell stays dead (§120).
- **The dawn's pick cannot be skipped or empty.** The first miner is always on the list. With more on it the clock waits for the `possess` action (`Query.picking` is: day and the god in no body yet), and the pick opens on the last host if they are still on the list, else the first miner, who is then the host the plate shows. There is no no-host day and no day tick played with no body.
- **The host plate is always shown** once the run is on, the night too: the god keeps `GameState.host` through the night (the last host; their body is let go at the dusk, `release`), and the plate reads that person, down or not. A night's death of the host leaves their plate at 0 HP until the dawn. The clock's pace is the day's (`DAY_SECONDS`) for every day tick: `Query.hosted` and `playTps`'s second argument are gone.
## 126. The interface's size, the second click, and panels that stand still (UI)

- **A HUD button that opens a panel closes it** when what it opens is already open: the god's corner and the goal (stats), the pouch (the grave, also while a stone is picked), the cog (settings), the feed's notes and the threat strips (their place or person). `toggle` in `src/ui/index.ts` is the one place; a menu opened by its key already toggled.
- **A slab is painted at its size.** The first interface (the stats page) painted each slab at its own size, so its grit, shadow band and hatching were fine at every size. The baked atlas (§114) had one 160x96 slab stretched as a nine-slice, so a panel 4 to 9 times wider drew smeared streaks. The atlas now bakes the slab at seven sizes (160x96, 384x96, 384x192, 384x256, 560x192, 640x384, 880x640; the other surfaces are small and stay at 160x96), at 2 px per CSS px with the same painter, and `surface` in `src/ui/kit.ts` takes the one nearest its box (`sizes` in the manifest), so a slab is stretched by 1.7 at most (`tests/rebuild/app/surface.test.ts`). The atlas page is 2048x6272 (webp 1.7 MB, from 1.47 MB). It is baked from the painters of `442816fc^` (`gen/bake/ui.ts` and `src/ui/play/kit`, restored aside; the tree keeps none): the surfaces' cells are `paintRecipe(g, recipe, w, h, 7)` at each size, named `surf.slab.<w>x<h>`.
- **Nothing is replaced for no change.** A menu fades in once, when it opens (not each time it is built again on an hour, an event or a setting), and keeps its list's scroll; the panels without a tick are built aside and put in only when they came out different.

## 127. One surface at a time on the left, the centre and the menus (OVERHAUL, ov-merge; owner o-a62b94)

- **The feed's notes never float over the world.** `Feed` (`src/ui/hud/feed.ts`) placed each note over the person or place it happened at (the view's `where`), so zoomed out at night a note landed under the god plate or clipped the screen's edge. The note is always the line under the host's plate; a click still looks at where it happened. Only the title's eye and the work key's plate on the ore in reach (§132) follow a world point.
- **Two panels never overlap.** In `src/ui/panels/index.ts` only the first open panel of the list is built; the rest wait (their key is null; focus and menu stay) and come back when it closes. In app's pump a menu and a focus close each other, and the dusk card (event-driven, so it cannot wait) opens alone: the `dusk` event clears the focus and the menu, and a person, the grave or a menu opened over the card closes it. Settings is the exception on both sides: it stands over the rest and leaves them open.

## 128. The deal comes at night, face down; a skeleton says its drop (DEAL)

- **No card at the start.** A run begins at the first dawn, the god in the first miner (`sim.create` runs the dawn): no opening deal, no starting gift, no `bonus` phase (`Phase` is `live | won | lost`), no `tally.gift`, no starting-gift panel. The first deal is the first dusk's (`godDusk`), when the god takes over; the day before it deals nothing (`tests/rebuild/sim/run.test.ts`). Where the hand is dealt is the sim's, not the screen's: by day the hand is empty on day 1 and shows `Act at dusk` over any card kept or found since.
- **The deal rises face down and turns.** The deal's cards rise from below the screen to their places at the centre showing the baked `card.back` (`assets/cardback`, `scripts/art/cardback.py`: a slab with the god's eye, the plate's size and origin), pause `PAUSE_S`, then turn face up (a half-turn of the two faces, back and plate, placed by the DOM). A card can be taken once the last is face up (`Deal.faceUp`). The Take none and Redraw buttons show then too (`hg-wait`), and the panels are framed before the deal so a dusk panel opening in the same frame never lets it be built.
- **A slain skeleton says what it left.** The sim's `slain` event with `nether > 0` puts "+x Nether" (`hud.drop`) over the skeleton, rising 46 px and fading in 1.1 s while the body is shown (`src/ui/hud/drops.ts`); no Nether, no text.

## 129. An enemy's thin red outline (PLAY, enemy-outline)

- **A skeleton carries a thin red line round its whole figure** (owner: "enemies should have a thin red outline around them"). Red is harm (`docs/style.md` 7); the line is a dark, desaturated blood red `#662a25`, dimmer than the interface's harm red, `0.25` rig units wide outside the ink contour, a hairline (half a css px) at the mine's play zoom (owner: "red is too bright, make it dimmer and also make it thinner").
- **Baked, not drawn.** `scripts/art/combat.py` bakes beside each of `s01`'s parts its outline `o:<part>` (every bin: every facing, frame and the pick's 48 angles), the part's silhouette grown by the width, in both detail levels. A look with outlines (`o:tr` among its parts) has the rig lay each part's outline first, under all the parts (`Body.layout`), so only the figure's rim shows; the stamp and the sprite paths place them as any part.

## 130. Nine people in the mine, every one real (PLAY, miners; owner: "NO PEOPLE for just ambience. Only real people, and not a lot")

- **The mine holds nine people in six trades, each one named: `PEOPLE` is the whole roster and the only headcount.** There is no `count` on a trade and no unnamed person at the start (`bear`, `src/sim/world/society.ts`, makes exactly `PEOPLE`, in the trades' order). Every other trade, named person, bio, trade line and trade-only event is gone. Newcomers still come, and are real too: a loner found at the west drift, and fresh hands off the lift (`freshHands`).
- **The list, and what each stays for** (read from the sim, each at the fewest its system works with):
  - digger x4: `g01` Mara (the first miner, wakes at the grave, §125), `g02` Pim at the grave, `g03` Hale and `g04` Wren at the heart vein. The host digger's quota and pocket (`ROLES.digger.quota`, `dig.ts`) are the dusk's Public heat; the owner set "the host plus 3".
  - chaplain x1: `g11` Ilsa, the Temple's watcher. `influence.creep` pushes colour back where a `watches` trade stands, and `threats.agents` sends a stranger who serves the power: without her the Temple has no agent and no eye.
  - pit guard x1: `g20` Osk at the lift, and overseer x1: `g17` Varn (the crackdown's leader, `content/stages`). The Council's watchers (`influence.creep`, `threats.agents`, `mining.councilEye`: the search at the lift); Varn's turning is the count's tolerance (`overseerTolerance`).
