  - hauler x1: `g06` Ket at the cartway; sorter x1: `g21` Brin at the sorting floor. The cart and the count: `skim` (rank 3) is a hauler's or a sorter's, a follower of either carries the stone off.
  One is the fewest every system works with: a watcher turned leaves that power unwatched, as turning the last of several did before; no rule needs a second.
- **Deleted, each for the reason that it only cast events or stood for ambience:** the trades timberman, lamp man, pump man, powder man, cart boy, farrier (beast keeper), water carrier, pit cook, surveyor, pit clerk, winder, track man, pick smith, bone setter, ash boy (sweeper), vent man, rat catcher, with their `role.<id>.*` words; the named `g07` Garr, `g12` Tov, `g18` Jude, `g19` Yara, `g22` Ada and their bios; the events `newArrival` (a phantom stranger's name), `fever`, `feverSpreads`, `propCollapse`, `propInquiry`, `rescueDig`, `sickBeast`, `beastDies`, `beastMoult`, `skitters`, `newSeam`, `injury`, `sickWater`, `badAir`, `cartDerail`, `liftJam`, `powderMisfire`, `runawayTrain`, `lampMoths`, `floodSouthLoop`, `paymaster`, `cartsLeave`, `countAudit`, `lampsIssued`, `festivalPrep`, `festival`, `feastMorning` (each cast a deleted trade, or only follows one that did) and their 100 and more words; the `life.tend` notes; the two weather lines that named the winders now name the lift. The machinery only they used goes too: a trade's `count`, `work` and `rounds` (every person has their own place; a stop-by-stop round), `Stretch.stops`, an event's `names`, `n` and `list` slots, the life notes' by-trade pool, and `familyOf`'s fallback to a random family. The tests of those go with them (a trade's work places, the headcount sum, the walk-the-rounds filter, the unnamed-people name rule, which now holds for the fresh hands).
- **Events and stories left standing.** An event whose cast a trade can no longer fill does not happen, so the events that cast the six trades, a follower or a tie stay (37). Two stories' numbers ride the headcount: `deathInTunnels` asks for more than 3 strangers (it was more than 6), and its tests' followers are every other hand.
- **A story never casts the god's own host.** The possessed first miner is not yet a follower, so the stories' strangers included him: a story's cast of strangers could pick him: with nine people to a thin story list he was dead at the first dawn (tick 550) in 18 of 300 bot runs, and the bots then refused `work` on a fallen host. `strangers` and `caveIn` in `storyRules.ts` leave the host out (`tests/rebuild/sim/stories.test.ts`).
- **The first miner's body wakes at the grave.** `FIRST_MINER.wakes` (1190, 895), the south rim of the grave's pit, clear ground in the grave's area: `spawn` (`src/sim/dig.ts`) puts his body there at every dawn and whenever he takes over from a fallen host (§125). A follower taken as host is where they stand. A mark needs someone within `HOST.markReach` of the body, so the first mark of a day is a walk from the grave to the people (Pim stands at the grave).
- **No number was retuned; the balance moved a lot.** The bots (`python tools/bots.py --timeout 7200 -- 500 all --md`, 500 seeds each, decent / cautious / random), with the host plus three diggers and every other trade at its old headcount (80 hands, then 60), then with 31 hands, then these nine: won 97 % / 57 % / 0 %, then 99 % / 93 % / 0 %, then 64 % / 94 % / 0 %; stalled 1 % / 43 % / 100 %, then 0 % / 7 % / 100 %, then 36 % / 6 % / 100 %; lift day (median) 23 / 22 / none, then 15 / 14 / 77, then 15 / 16 / 93; won on day (median) 25 / 24, then 18 / 18, then 49 / 52; peak followers 20 / 18 / 1, then 18 / 17 / 1, then 6 / 6 / 1; whispers 39 / 32 / 10, then 29 / 25 / 7, then 14 / 11 / 2; threats announced per run 27.1 / 75.4 / 25.0, then 10.7 / 20.9 / 24.7, then 36.6 / 44.8 / 25.3; losses decent 14, then 3, then 0; refused 0. The flock peaks at 6, so a won run takes 49 to 52 days, not 18 to 25, and the decent bot stalls on 36 % of seeds. The numbers are as they fell: the owner says whether the balance contract wants a pass.

## 131. The multiplayer lobbies (LOBBY, lobby; owner o-a62b94 and the owner's "begin will be replaced with single player and a multiplayer button")

- **The title's Begin is Single player, and Multiplayer sits under it** (only in a build with a hub). Continue and Begin anew stay as they are. Logged out, Multiplayer opens the login popup.
- **The hub owns the lobbies** (`app/websocket/lobby.go`), one table, touched only by the hub goroutine. A lobby is its host's: the host's name is its id and its name is `<host>'s lobby`; up to 4 players, no password; a player is in one at a time (hosting or joining leaves the old one). Search lists only the lobbies with room, by host. A join to a lobby that is full or gone is refused with the reason and the fresh list. The host leaving closes it and sends everyone out. A player whose connection drops is out at the next tick; a second tab taking the player over shows the lobby again.
- **Multiplayer is part of the title's menu, not a popup.** It swaps the main entries (Single player, Multiplayer, Settings) for Host, Search and Back, set like them; Back returns. Host and Search draw in the same place (`src/ui/panels/lobby.ts`, built into the title's nav). The title rebuilds only its nav when the screen changes, so the still and the name do not replay.
- **Search and the lobby are centred; their buttons are one row at the bottom.** Search's scrollable list and the lobby's player box sit at the screen's centre, horizontally and vertically, at any window shape (the title's `stage`, centred on the panel layer's origin); their buttons sit in a row centred at the window's bottom (`base`, kept at `Ctx.half` y minus 84). Search has Back, Refresh and Join (dim until a lobby is picked). The lobby has exactly Back, which leaves it, and Start. Start is the host's alone: for a member it is shown dimmed and does nothing, and the line at the foot of the box reads "Waiting for the host to start." Start sends `start`; the hub accepts it only from the host (a lobby is its host's, so anyone else has none to start) and sends it to every member, who see that the host started and Start dims for all. What starts is a stub: nothing yet begins a shared game, and the screen says so.
- **Search's list and the lobby's players are solid panels of the game's own kit.** Each is the baked `slab` (the dusk panel's and the settings menu's) with the parchment name tab ("Open lobbies", or the lobby's name) and `parchDark` rows, the picked row `violet` as the tiles are; nothing is translucent and none of the still shows through. The status (a refused join, "Waiting for the host to start.") is a line inside the slab's foot, and the lobby's count sits right of the tab. The bottom buttons are `violet` plates (`violetDim` when dim) behind the title's type, centred in one row; the layout is the same. All baked cells; nothing is drawn live.
- **The title fills the window.** The panel layer is a 16:9 design box scaled to fit, so the title's menu, login corner and footer sat inside that box and a taller or wider window showed them away from its edges. They are placed at their design offsets from the window's own edges (`Ctx.half`, the window's half extent in design px, read each frame), and the black ground reaches past any window's edge. The still and the name stay centred at the design scale.


## 132. Where to mine (MINE, ore-marks)

- **An ore is a section of wall that looks workable** (owner: "certain sections of the wall, that have some sort of indicator that it is interactable"). Each ore site (§121) is the foot of a shown wall face, and the ore atlas's baked seam runs up that face from it (owner: "seams in the wall"): the rock split open along a jagged line, the cleft packed with violet crystal set in it, a few points breaking out, thin veins of crystal running off into the rock, the rock at its lips paler where a pick bit (`scripts/art/ore.py`, `assets/ore`), longer and wider with the tier, in a soft violet glow on the rock. Nothing of it stands on the floor and no prop marks it: the seam alone is the ore. A mined ore and an ore in the void are not shown. The lit picture's crystal clusters stand on the floor; a seam is in the wall.
- **The ore the work key would mine is picked out**: the seam wears its baked white rim, tinted bone and pulsing as the picked host's ring does, and over it a small plate shows the work key's name (`F` out of the box, the Keys tab's). Both read the sim's `workAt` (the ore within `MINING.reach` 36), so they show exactly when the key works, and go while that ore is being worked (its bar shows then). The plate follows the seam on the screen like the title's eye; it takes no pointer. Left click stays the swing (§122).

## 133. The shared mine: multiplayer play for up to four (MULTI; the owner: "create a world that can handle 4 people and when less people join adjust the world accordingly. They spawn in different areas serving a 'different' god (just make them different color)")

- **The host's Start begins a match** for every member of the lobby (§131 left Start a stub). It is real time, not the run's nights and days: the course asks for a shared space where everyone acts at once and sees the others as it happens. The single-player run is untouched; the match stands over the title while it plays (`vm.match`) and the lobby shows again when it ends. A lobby plays one match at a time and is not listed or joinable while it does (`refused: "playing"`).
- **The hub runs the match** (`app/websocket/match.go`): positions, walls, crystals, blows and the score are the server's, stepped every tick (`TICK_RATE`, 20 by default, was 10) and sent whole to the match's players (`world`). A player sends only the heading held (`input`) and a swing's aim (`strike`), under the name in their JWT. The game shows each player one tick behind, between the hub's last two ticks (`src/app/match.ts`).
- **The world fits the players: three zones a player.** The mine is the one map (`walk.json`, its twelve zones); the match for n players opens `ARENA.layouts[n - 1]` (`src/content/arena.json`, which the server embeds too, one source): 1 player the grave with the cartway and the heart vein; 2 the band west to east (west drift, cartway, grave | sorting floor, heart vein, east works); 3 drop the north-west wing (bunks, west drift, kiln shrine); 4 the whole mine. Every other zone is rock to the feet and void to the eye (the fog closes over it). Crystals on the ground scale too: at most 6 + 3 a seat.
- **Each seat is a god, by lobby order, by colour** (`GOD_COLOURS`): the Violet god (the Hungry One's own violet), the Crimson, the Jade and the Amber. A seat's shrine is where its player spawns, at the far reaches of the open mine and apart from the others (4: bunks, lift, sump, low gallery; 3: cartway, lift, sump; 2: west drift, east works; 1: the grave's south rim), and its three zones are its domain. The player's miner is a digger look washed in the god's colour, standing in its light with a ring of it; the domain's floor carries a faint wash of it; the shrine is an altar of three tall gems in it, a pool and pillar of its light, a ring where crystals are given, and the crystals given heaped round it.
- **The play.** Walk (`speed` 90 mine units a second, the walls the walk grid's, the feet a circle of 5) over a crystal to pick it up (within 18; 5 carried at most, shown as gems over the head), and stand at your own shrine (within 48) to give them: the first god given 20 wins, shown for 6 s, then back to the lobby. A click swings the pick (the host's own clip, 0.62 s, the blow at 0.26 s): the blow lands on anyone within 40 of the feet and 70 degrees of the aim, stuns them 1.2 s, knocks them back 28 and scatters what they carry within 36 for anyone to take (the interaction the course asks for). Nobody is struck at their own shrine, so giving is safe. A player who leaves drops what they carried; their god's score stays.
- **How the view shows it.** The layers read the rules through `matchRules` (`src/view/arena.ts`): in a match the open zones are active and the rest void, it is broad day, and no ore stands; `StateSeen` counts a match beginning or ending as the state moving. The people layer places the players (`src/view/life/players.ts`), the arena's two layers the domains, shrines, crystals, carried gems, the blow's `hit` frames and a stunned head's motes; the panel `src/ui/panels/match.ts` the gods' board, the names over heads, how to play, Leave and the winner. Nothing new is baked: the gems, glows, rings, beam and hit are the life atlas's, tinted.
- **Not tuned by play:** the numbers are a first draft from two bots on the local server (a match to 4 in 17 s with two players).
